(4.2x); strings.js sticky lexer 60-73 / 4-8 / 8-11 ms, a RegExp per remaining chunk 10-16 / 1-4 /
1-3 ms; source-map 1.24 / 9.95 / 3.78 runs/s (3.0x); coffeescript 0.77 / 7.46 / 4.87 (6.3x).

- Octane's RegExp is mostly one pattern: re17, `/(^|[^\\])"\\\/Qngr\((-?[0-9]+)\)\\\/"/g`, replaced
  over 2,257-unit two-byte strings 780 times a run (48.8% of instructions). Its first element
  admits nearly every position, so the matcher starts at each: 158 us a replace, the same on an
  ASCII copy, against at most 2.5 us in Node at either tier. Skipping the replace when its
  required literal is absent (a JavaScript what-if; same checksum) takes a run from 284 to 166 ms.
- Elsewhere fixed per-operation costs dominate. A Djot operation costs ~4,000 instructions (1,250
  matching; Sparkplug-only Node ~180 in all); a source-map one ~8,500, over a third of it guards
  (`normalize` runs `match` and `split` 2.2M times each, creating its `/\/+/` each time, and
  `test` 1.8M).
- Costs the plan's list misses: the matcher's entry and per-attempt resets (Octane 3.7% beyond
  re17, Djot 2.6%, strings 9%); two-byte subject preparation (strings 14%: the prepared-text
  LRU, widening, unit-element binary searches); replace output built as UTF-8 and canonicalized
  (Octane 3.2%; gone with W7's d1ad3c45); match arrays and `d` indices (Djot 2.4%, strings
  18%; W2c/W2d).
- Little or nothing measured: `match` with /g, `search`, `matchAll` (Octane's 2,255 and
  coffeescript's 1,040 global matches are all there are, among 1.36M and 2.6M operations), case
  folding at match time (at most 0.11%), compilation (0.5%), the collector's `regexps`/`gc_pins`
  sweeps (0.2%).

Design (gains are shares of today's instructions):
1. Prefilters: at compile time, the longest literal every match contains and its offset range
   from the match start (re17: `"\/Qngr(` at 0-1); exec finds it with a NEON scan over bytes or
   units and tries only starts in range, and its absence ends the search. The first-element
   scan gets NEON too (byte equality, 128-entry ASCII tables). Octane 1.7-1.9x.
2. A RegExp protector: a realm proof, epoch-keyed like the array protectors, that
   %RegExp.prototype%'s `exec`, flag getters and symbol methods, %RegExp%[@@species] and the
   `constructor` link are the intrinsics. Value writes to those objects bump the epoch too
   (today only structural changes do: the objects are flagged, store caches decline them and the
   slow store path bumps). A receiver is fast when it has the realm's initial RegExp shape and
   prototype. String.prototype's methods then enter the RegExp algorithms directly and
   RegExpExec calls RegExpBuiltinExec without the Get of `exec`. Removes the guards, the @@method
   dispatch and the generic exec call: source-map 12%, Octane 7.8%, Djot 2.6%.
3. Payload and entry: `Exotic::RegExp(PayloadBox<..>)` holds the shared program (P1's Map/Set
   pattern; W2g's RegExp part). `lastIndex` stays an own data property at the initial shape's
   slot, read and written directly under 2's proof; the legacy statics keep the constructor
   cached; the matcher's scratch lives in the Interp, sized per program, capture slots reset
   through the undo log rather than filled per attempt, flags precomputed. Removes the
   `regexps`/`gc_pins` traffic, string-keyed `lastIndex` access and entry overhead: Octane 8%,
   Djot 5%, strings 20%, source-map 4.5%.
4. Subjects in place: the matcher reads one-byte storage as bytes and two-byte storage as units,
   decoding surrogate pairs in /u and /v as it steps, positions in units throughout; ReText, its
   32 MiB LRU and the unit-element maps go (W7's item 5). Strings 14%, Djot 2%, Octane 1.4%.
5. Captures from storage: captures, split pieces and replace arguments as slices of the subject
   at its width (views above V8's 13 units), with no Rust String or UTF-8 round trip. Octane
   2%, Djot 1%, strings 8%.
6. `match` with /g, `search` and `matchAll` without match arrays, on 2's proof: for
   completeness, after 2.
7. Case closure at compile time, with the native tier, which needs closed classes (`v`-mode
   class sets are already closed under `i`, 05e1f798).

Then W5b (Decision 3): the native tier with captures and alternation on both backends, behind a
differential gate (the matcher, Test262's RegExp suites, a fuzzed corpus, the census workloads'
patterns). After W5a it addresses the backtracking interpreter and class tests: about 9% of
Octane's instructions today (a fifth to a quarter of what W5a leaves), coffeescript 3.6% (`clean`'s
`/\s+$/` over a 50,176-unit source restarts inside every whitespace run: 2,150 us a call against
Sparkplug-only Node's 375), strings 14%, source-map 2.8%. The one-byte tier's AArch64 class
emitter has an open release-build termination fault (regex.rs:1156) that W5b fixes or replaces.

Order: 1 (half of Octane, regex.rs alone); 2 (the largest fixed cost on source-map and Octane);
3, as W2g's RegExp part, on 2's shape proof; 4; 5; 6; then W5b with 7. Each series: Test262
identical, difftest, and the census workloads run with each new fast path forced off and compared.

Exit (agreed, Decision 9): W5a: Octane RegExp's score at
least 2x the close-out's, as planned (predicted 2.2-3.1x, as items 2-5 realize 70-100% of their
shares); Djot's RegExp instructions in the 40-pass run halved, the plan's "exec time" made
measurable (5.2e8 today by w5cat.py; predicted 1.7-2.4x); source-map's at most half (5.4e10
today; predicted a quarter to a half); Test262 identical. strings.js's RegExp cases, which W7's
exit left to W5, are recorded (predicted ~4x Sparkplug-only Node; the rest is W2's arrays). W5b's
exit in W5b's section below.

Landed 2026-10-27: items 1 and 2 (958fd73c: the required literal's NEON scan and the
first-element scan; 753d3aa2: the RegExp protector, checked where the algorithms use it, and
String.prototype's methods entering the RegExp algorithms directly). Octane RegExp ran 2.54x
fewer instructions, source-map 10.9% fewer, Djot 2.7% fewer.
Items 3 to 5 (3e5cc406..9524b451, a series by an agent, rebased onto W2a's step 6): 3's
builtins part (`lastIndex` read and written at its slot under 2's proof, the matcher looked up
once), 4 (subjects matched in place by their storage's code units: ReText, its LRU and the
unit-element maps are gone) and 5 (captures, split pieces and replace arguments taken from the
subject's units). Instructions (fat LTO, pinned, pacing off; each against the one before):

| | b352febc | lastIndex, lookup | in place | captures |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Octane | 8.631e9 | -7.0% | -5.5% | -1.8% (7.450e9) |
| Djot, 40 passes | 2.638e9 | -2.2% | -2.3% | -0.3% (2.513e9) |
| strings.js | 1.616e9 | -10.6% | -10.7% | -4.9% (1.228e9) |
| source-map | 1.930e11 | -0.8% | -0.3% | -0.3% (1.902e11) |

Since 15933355: Octane 2.93x fewer instructions, Djot 7.4%, strings.js 24.0%, source-map 11.9%.
Against the exit (no-LTO estimates): source-map's RegExp instructions about 2.2e10, under half
the census's 5.4e10; Djot's about 3.5e8 against 2.6e8, not yet halved; Octane's score waits
for a quiet machine. Not done: item 3's matcher entry (scratch per program, capture resets
through the undo log, precomputed flags) and the `regexps`/`gc_pins` entries at creation,
which need the payload; items 6 and 7 and W5b, which go on alongside W3 (Decision 9). The
native tier now serves one-byte storage only: in-place two-byte units can exceed 0xFF, and the
removed 32-bit-element loads on AArch64 were mis-scaled (only the first element read right).
Views stay at 256 bytes: from 13 units cost Octane 0.5% for 0.1-0.3% elsewhere, and short
views take the JIT's slow `length` and `charCodeAt` paths. Validation: Test262 identical, the
fixtures, difftest, the lib suite, the string difftest over 40 seeds at three tiers (the same
12 known mismatches, seed for seed), GC stress on the RegExp and string-slice tests, zombie's
x86-64 and Windows suites (item 4 edits the x64 native emitter; their one new failure was step
6's I6 test, and step 7's run, which includes the series, shows only the known failures) and
its x86-64 fixtures and difftest; the dumps differ in the interpreter's field offsets only (the
subject cache left it).
Found on the way, older than P2: with RegExp.prototype[@@split] deleted, `'a/b/c'.split(/\//)`
still split by the RegExp, in `nf_string_split`'s own RegExp path, where ECMA-262 and Node
convert the separator with ToString (["a/b/c"]). d476195d removes that path.

Landed 2026-10-28, with W3's first series (Decision 1): W5c, item 3's matcher entry and item 6
(ec28905d..b6689c4b, an agent's). The flags in effect stay out of the modifier stack and are
tested as booleans computed with the program, the matcher's scratch stays with the program, a
failed attempt's captures are unset through its undo records, and a sticky RegExp is tried at
its one start only where a match can begin; exec builds its result without intermediate
vectors, a single-element pattern is matched by its first-element scan, and one-unit ASCII
results come from the shared one-unit Strings. Item 6: a global `match` and `search` without
match arrays, and `matchAll`'s iterator set up without its observable lookups. Instructions
(fat LTO, pinned to the A725 cores, pacing off; against c05fc611, main without W5c): Octane
RegExp -6.07%, Djot's 40 passes -1.91%, strings.js's RegExp cases -7.87%, source-map -1.04%,
coffeescript -1.10%. The largest single change is in class tests: Octane 302M to 32M,
source-map 1.24e9 to 0.28e9, coffeescript 2.04e9 to 0.28e9.

Then W5b's first series (7611cf05..606e33fd, an agent's; its census, design and exit in W5b's
section below, Decision 13): a per-pattern census
(`TRUST_JS_REGEXP_PROF=2`); the matcher keeps no retries for a loop whose shorter runs could only
fail, resumes a scan past a failed leading loop's run, filters the starts of paths that begin
with an assertion, and closes case-insensitive elements over case at compile time (item 7); the
one-byte tier tests an anchor at its own offset in machine code (both backends: x86-64's `$` also
compared with a register the poll can clobber), its corpus runs the machine code, and classes
and anchors compile to it. Against main 02d5bd1b: Octane RegExp -9.96%, coffeescript -2.35%,
source-map -0.62%, Djot -0.48%, strings.js's RegExp cases -0.17%. coffeescript's `clean`
(`/\s+$/` over its 50 KB source) fell from 10.2M instructions a call to 3.1M, 1.54x
Sparkplug-only Node's. Against the exit, the backtracking interpreter and class tests are
62% of main's on Octane, 61% on source-map and 44% on coffeescript (exit: 30%, 30% and 40%),
which wants the native tier with captures and alternation. Validation: the ARM64 gate (the lib
suite, pacing, GC stress on the RegExp tests, the fixtures and difftest as the base's; Test262
53,575 of 53,575, identical), Test262's 2,518 RegExp files with the tier forced on at once and at
64 executions, and zombie's x86-64 lib suite (its known failure only).

### W5b: the RegExp native tier and W5's item 7 (census and design, 2026-10-28; Decision 13)

The first census below is zombie's (x86-64); Crystal's (ARM64) and the counts of W5c and of W5b's
matcher items follow it, under "Crystal (ARM64)". The first series (the matcher items, item 7 and
the one-byte tier's anchors and declines) landed with W5's record above.

Census (provisional): the W5c tip (c9610dfa, branch p2-w5c), a release build on zombie
(i7-6700HQ, x86-64) from p2-w5b, which adds a diagnostic: `TRUST_JS_REGEXP_PROF=2` reports, per
pattern, the matcher instructions dispatched, attempts, first-element scan positions, backtrack
entries, executions, subject widths and the program's features (instruction kinds, groups, first
filter, prefilter). Workloads as W5's census: Octane's regexp.js (the fixed 10-run driver),
Djot's 40 passes, strings.js's RegExp cases, Web Tooling's coffeescript and source-map (their
single-tool bundles). Dispatched instructions count `Matcher::run`'s steps, not its loops' inner
elements (`Many` runs) nor the scans. Data and the diagnostic patch: target/p2/w5b-design (data/,
census-diagnostic.diff); w5cat2.py, beside w5cat.py in target/p2/w5-census, adds the RegExp
functions written since the census (`regexp_builtin_exec`, which a tail call from `regexp_exec`
left unclassified, W5c's `find_single` and `subject_slice`). V8 is cited at c8b709085e21, as
W3's note; its src/regexp/ (common, arm64, x64, experimental) was added to
target/p2/w3-design/v8-src from the same commit. The engine is cited at c9610dfa.

Matcher work by what a native tier needs (dispatched instructions and attempts over all patterns;
the shares, of dispatched instructions with attempts in brackets, over each workload's top 40):

| | dispatched | attempts | core (A) | `i` (B, item 7) | lookahead, backrefs (C) | Unicode mode (D) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Octane (10 runs) | 16.4M | 2.42M | 91.5 (77.6) | 8.5 (22.4) | 0 | 0 |
| source-map | 41.2M | 4.02M | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| coffeescript | 78.1M | 14.1M | 76.4 (91.4) | 2.2 (0.8) | 21.4 (7.8) | 0 |
| strings.js RegExp cases | 2.6M | 0.32M | 0 | 0 | 0 | 100 |
| Djot, 40 passes | 0.1M | 0.06M | 100 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

Core (A): Char, Any, Class, captures (Save, ClearCaps), alternation (Split, Jmp), Many (greedy
and lazy), group loops (SetMark, CheckProgress), `^`, `$`, `\b`, with the g, y, m, s and d flags.
No hot pattern uses lookbehind, counted loops, modifier groups or `v`-mode string sets.

- One pattern each dominates two workloads. source-map's URL pattern
  `/^(?:([\w+\-.]+):)?\/\/(?:(\w+:\w+)@)?([\w.-]*)(?::(\d+))?(.*)$/` is 99% of its matcher work
  (40.9M dispatched over 3.97M executions, 9% matching): on a path without a scheme it scans
  `[\w+\-.]+`, finds no `:`, and retries every shorter run before giving the group up.
  coffeescript's `clean` `/\s+$/` is 62% (48.2M dispatched, 9.5M attempts, 19.7M backtrack
  entries for 1,480 executions): every start inside a whitespace run scans to the run's end, fails
  `$` and retries each shorter run, so a run of k blanks costs O(k^2). Its `/$/m` makes 2.06M
  attempts over 108,560 executions: a multiline `$` gives the first-element filter nothing.
- Octane spreads over 237 patterns: the trim `/^\s*|\s*$/g` (32% of its dispatched instructions,
  1.0M attempts, 0.97M backtracks: no first filter, so every position is an attempt), a URL
  splitter with 11 groups (25%), `/\b\w+\b/g` (9%), `/^\s*(\S*(\s+\S+)*)\s*$/` (5%), ten
  cookie readers `/\bname\s*=\s*([^;]*)/i` (8.5% of the instructions, 22% of the attempts: under
  `i` the required literal can hold no letter, so the prefilter's window admits every start up
  to each `=`), `/([a-zA-Z]|\s)+/`, a 26-way alternation of literals, `/(\d*)(\D*)/g`.
- coffeescript's lexer runs its anchored patterns on every chunk: `(?!\d)`, `(?!\s)`, `(?!:)`,
  `([-+:])\1` and `^(.?)\1*$` put 21% of its matcher work behind lookaheads and backreferences.
- strings.js's sticky lexer `/[A-Za-z_]\w*|\d+|\s+|\/\/[^\n]*|./uy` is Unicode mode over two-byte
  subjects for 75% of its executions.
- The interpreter costs about 20 instructions of dispatch per matcher instruction on x86-64
  (`tick`, the profiling test, the bounds check and a jump table over 56-byte `Inst`s) before the
  instruction's own work: one run of `Save, Class, Save, Match`, a Djot-style scan before W5c's
  single-element path, cost 223 instructions in `Matcher::run` there. V8's native code spends a
  few instructions an element.

Crystal (ARM64, 2026-10-28): Cortex-A725 cores 10-13 (`taskset`), fat-LTO engines from `git
archive` exports in fresh target dirs, `TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0`, `--tier=jit`; user instructions,
least of 3 (`perf stat -e instructions:u`); profiles classified by w5cat2.py, one sample per
500,000 instructions, per 2,000,000 for the two bundles. No timing. Builds: c05fc611 (p2-w3's
tip, main without W5c), b6689c4b (main), and W5b's tip: the design's items 4, 1a-1c and 2
(W5's item 7) on p2-w5b, 2e2cd39e..99f7fb52 on b6689c4b, counted from an export of the identical
tree (4929181f). Data, scripts and binaries: target/p2/w5b-crystal (data/, measure.sh,
census-table.py, w5cat2-table.py, micro-re.py, fault/).

- Octane's gain is the assertion filter's (the trim, `\b\w+\b` and the ten cookie readers: those
  now make 440 attempts for 660 executions instead of 144,760), with atomic loops 0.4 points;
  source-map's is the atomic loops' (the URL pattern's retries); coffeescript's spreads over all
  four (run skip 0.8 points, atomic loops 0.5, assertion filter 0.4, case closure 0.3).
- strings.js's +0.48% is there with every switch on or off: about 17 instructions an execution of
  the sticky lexer (324,000 executions), from the series' fixed bookkeeping and code layout (the
  census switch's gate, the leading-run state, the closed and sorted-range flags), not from a
  path any item takes. Forcing the execution's scan inline made it worse (+1.8%). Djot's +0.14%
  goes with all four switches off (-0.03%) and is within its run-to-run spread.
- w5cat2's "backtracking interpreter" plus "class tests", the proposed exit's measure, as a share
  of main's: Octane 62% (1.59e9 -> 0.99e9), source-map 61% (6.50e9 -> 3.98e9), coffeescript 44%
  (9.56e9 -> 4.21e9); the matcher in all, 74%, 78% and 50%. coffeescript's RegExp share of all
  instructions goes from 4.9% to 2.9%. The interpreter/per-exec-setup split follows inlining,
  which differs between the builds (`exec_impl` is out of line in W5b's), so the matcher's total
  is the steadier measure.

The one-byte tier today on ARM64 (census item 5's baseline: there is no 3a prototype yet; the
census reports executions and machine-code bytes, not compile time):

| workload | patterns | tier-ups tried | compiled | native runs | machine-code bytes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Octane | 237 | 235 | 18 | 491,721 | 196 |
| source-map | 241 | 16 | 5 | 3,329,167 | 0 |
| coffeescript | 290 | 53 | 8 | 25,745 | 0 |
| strings.js | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Djot | 37 | 11 | 11 | 27,926 | 0 |

Almost every native run is the Rust loops' (`find_interpreted`, `find_terminal_one_byte_many`):
the class decline below leaves AArch64 196 bytes of machine code in all. With the declines lifted
(below), Djot's class and anchor programs compile too (212 to 340 bytes each). RegExp
compilation as a whole (w5cat2's "compile": parsing, lowering, filters, closure, tier-up) is 22M
instructions of Octane on main and 35M with W5b, source-map 74M -> 129M, coffeescript 77M ->
125M: under 0.1% of each, the analyses and the case closure W5b adds at compile time.

What the native tier does today (regex.rs at c9610dfa):
- A program tiers up after 64 executions (`REGEXP_TIER_UP_THRESHOLD`, :23; `note_execution`,
  :2695) once a one-byte subject has been seen, never under `i`, `s`, `u` or `v`. One-byte
  storage is ASCII until W7's step S4, and two-byte subjects stay on the matcher: in-place units
  can exceed 0xFF.
- `OneByteNativeProgram::compile` (:1000-1076) takes a program whose body is either one terminal
  `Many` of a one-byte Char, Any or a Class with its 256-entry LUT, run by a Rust loop
  (`find_terminal_one_byte_many`, :1184), or a straight line of such Chars, Anys, Classes and
  non-multiline `^`/`$` with at least one non-literal. Captures, alternation, any other
  quantifier, lookaround, backreferences and `\b` decline it.
- Machine code (`OneByteNativeCode::compile`, :415-470): at most 256 ops, no assertions on
  either backend, and no Class on AArch64 (:1058-1076); the rest runs the ops through a Rust loop
  (`find_interpreted`, :1114). Emitters: x86-64 :586-711, AArch64 :890-992, with their own small
  assemblers, unix only. The candidate loop calls `regexp_native_poll` (:395) at every start
  position (`call rax` :627, `blr x16` :920): a call and `RuntimeInterrupt::current_reason` per
  candidate, where V8 compares the stack pointer at each backtrack.
- In the census the tier was Djot's "native code" (1.6%), almost all of it `find_interpreted`:
  on AArch64 Djot's class programs never reach machine code.
- The AArch64 "release-build termination fault" (the plan's regex.rs:1156 at 56c28010, now
  :1058) was most likely a bug fixed since, by reading. 51fbed49 (2026-09-06) added the emitters
  and, in the same commit, the class decline. Its AArch64 loop branched on the flags right after
  `blr x16` (`b.ne interrupted`, no compare): AAPCS64 leaves NZCV undefined across a call, and
  the poll returned `u8`, whose upper bits w0 does not define. When the flags happened to read
  "not equal", the code added 1 to w0 and returned status 1, "match", with the output still the
  `{0, 0}` the caller initialized: a false empty match at 0. A global replace or exec loop then
  never advances (an empty match at 0 sets `lastIndex` to 0, AdvanceStringIndex moves it to 1,
  and the next exec matches at 0 again), which is "termination lost on a no-match tail" in
  release, where the poll's flags differ from debug's. The same false empty match is the
  "anchored no-match into a false empty match" behind the assertion decline (jQuery's
  vendor-property test). 5967822c (2026-09-10) compares w0 after the call (:921) and returns
  `u32` (:395), with comments naming both causes; the x86-64 loop has tested `al` after its call
  from the start, so that path was sound. The declines were not revisited. Still fragile: `sticky`
  arrives as a `u8` in w3 and the code compares all of w22. Crystal check: a release build of
  c9610dfa with both declines removed, run under the generated harness (regex_native_generated.rs),
  Test262's RegExp suites with `TRUST_JS_REGEXP_TIER_UP_AT=1`, and global replaces over subjects
  whose tails do not match; then the same build with the compare at :921 removed, which should
  reproduce the fault.
  Crystal's check (2026-10-28; two no-LTO release builds of 99f7fb52's tree with both declines
  lifted, the second also with the pre-fix poll: `u8` return and no compare after `blr x16`;
  target/p2/w5b-crystal/fault):
  - With the compare, the class programs are sound. Test262's RegExp suites (2,518 files) pass
    with `TRUST_JS_REGEXP_TIER_UP_AT=1` and at 64; global replaces and exec loops over no-match
    tails give Node's output with 2,796 bytes of machine code. But the engine's own
    `anchors_inside_a_pattern_keep_holding_after_the_pattern_tiers_up` fails: both emitters test
    `^` and `$` against the candidate's start (`cmp x21, #0` and `cmp x21, x25` on AArch64,
    `test r14, r14` and `cmp r14, r11` on x86-64), not the start plus the anchor's offset, so
    `/a^/`, `/a^b/`, `/x$y/` and `/a.^/` match once tiered up. The class decline can be lifted;
    the assertion decline stays until both emitters add the offset.
  - With the pre-fix poll the fault reproduces. `"ab xy".replace(/[ab]c/g, "#")` never ends at
    tier-up 1, nor does the global-replace script; the RegExp suites fall to 2,464 of 2,518 (8
    timeouts, 46 wrong results) and the regex unit tests hang. Disassembly shows why: the no-
    interrupt path of `RuntimeInterrupt::current_reason` sets no flags (`ldarb`, three `tbnz`), so
    `b.ne` reads the loop's own `cmp x21, x25` from the failed candidate (current < last: NE) and
    returns status 1 with the output `{0, 0}`.
  - The generated harness (regex_native_generated.rs) passes in both builds because it never runs
    machine code: `OneByteNativeProgram::find` is `find_interpreted`, and only the engine's
    `find_ascii` enters the emitted code. It should call `find_ascii`.
  Done on p2-w5b: both emitters test an anchor at the candidate start plus its offset (1cf6e70e;
  the x86-64 `$` also compared with r11, which the poll call may clobber); the corpus and the
  tier's differential tests call `find_ascii`, and the corpus gained anchors inside patterns, which
  it fails on the old emitters (299bbdf9); both declines are lifted (606e33fd). Test262's RegExp
  suites pass at tier-up 1 and 64, and the lib tests at tier-up 1 on both backends.

What V8 does (c8b709085e21):
- Single tier by default: a pattern is compiled to native code at its first execution
  (`regexp_tier_up` with `regexp_tier_up_ticks` 0, flag-definitions.h:3512-3539;
  `EnsureCompiledIrregexp`, regexp.cc:837-893), separately for one-byte (Latin-1) and two-byte
  subjects (`CreateNativeMacroAssembler`, regexp.cc:1009-1030). The bytecode interpreter
  (regexp-bytecode-generator.cc, regexp-interpreter.cc) serves jitless and a nonzero tick count;
  even then a subject of 1,000 units or more, or global execution, tiers up at once (regexp.cc:
  1283-1310; js-regexp.h:162). Patterns longer than `kMaxOptimizedPatternLength` compile without
  optimizations; more than 65,536 registers is an error (regexp.cc:1366-1402,
  regexp-macro-assembler.h:37). `--regexp-assemble-from-bytecode` (off by default; flag-
  definitions.h:3544-3546) builds native code from the bytecode one instruction at a time
  (regexp-code-generator.h:19-80), the shape proposed for item 3 below.
- Compilation: the AST becomes a node graph wrapped in capture 0, behind a lazy `.*?` loop unless
  anchored or sticky (`PreprocessRegExp`, regexp-compiler.cc:6469-6512); one pass analyzes it and
  `Assemble` emits through a macro assembler with a native backend per architecture and the
  bytecode generator as another (regexp.cc:1363-1495). A pattern anchored at its end with a
  bounded length starts from the end (`SetCurrentPositionFromEnd`, regexp.cc:1466-1475); global
  execution loops inside the generated code (GLOBAL, GLOBAL_NO_ZERO_LENGTH_CHECK,
  GLOBAL_UNICODE, regexp.cc:1477-1485, regexp-macro-assembler.h:386-391).
- Captures are registers, two per group: on ARM64 the first 16 live in x0-x7, two 32-bit positions
  per register, the rest in the frame (regexp-macro-assembler-arm64.h:202-205, :226-281); w24 holds
  the "unset" value for clearing (:264). Changes are deferred along a Trace, the path being
  generated (regexp-compiler.h:292-505); at a choice point `Trace::Flush` pushes the old value of
  each affected register (RESTORE), or notes it for clearing (CLEAR) or for nothing (IGNORE), and
  emits undo code reached by backtracking: pop or clear, then backtrack on
  (regexp-compiler.cc:511-527, 611-650, 706-880). Loop iterations clear their captures with
  `ActionNode::ClearCaptures` (:981).
- Alternation: a ChoiceNode tries its alternatives in order. Quick checks load up to four
  characters and compare them under a mask to skip alternatives that cannot start here
  (`GetQuickCheckDetails`, :2095; `EmitQuickCheck`, :1965); the unanchored search skips ahead
  with a Boyer-Moore lookahead or SIMD scans (`EmitOptimizedUnanchoredSearch`, :4621;
  `BoyerMooreLookahead::EmitSkipInstructions`, :3976; `SkipUntil*`,
  regexp-macro-assembler-arm64.cc:655-1268), and disjoint alternatives dispatch on one masked
  compare (`regexp_masked_dispatch`, :5393).
- Loops: fixed-length greedy loops step back without pushing a record per iteration
  (`SpecialLoopState`, regexp-compiler.h:507-520). A loop whose retries are futile drops its
  backtracking (`AtomicLoopKind`, regexp-nodes.h:238-252): its continuation is `$` then accept
  (kAtEnd), always succeeds at the greedy extent, as `\D*` after `\d*` in `/(\d*)(\D*)/`
  (kTotal), starts with `\b` over a word-character body (kBoundary), or starts with characters
  disjoint from the body's (kDisjoint).
- The backtrack stack is per isolate, 32-bit slots, 1 KB static, then grown on the heap up to 64
  MB (regexp-stack.h:62-127). Generated code pushes code offsets, positions and saved registers,
  checks a limit kept 32 slots short of the end after its pushes (`CheckStackLimit`,
  regexp-macro-assembler-arm64.cc:2303-2307; `PushBacktrack`, :1946-1960) and calls out to grow
  it. Every backtrack also compares sp with the JS stack limit, which interrupt requests lower
  (`EmitBacktrack`, :195-219; `CheckPreemption`, :2293-2300). An optional backtrack limit counts
  backtracks and fails, or falls back to the experimental linear-time engine, which is off by
  default (`SetBacktrackAndExperimentalFallback`, regexp.cc:1060-1077; flag-definitions.h:
  3622-3630).
- Case independence is compiled in. Classes under `i` gain their case equivalents
  (`TextNode::MakeCaseIndependent`, regexp-compiler.cc:2902-2922; `CharacterRange::
  AddCaseEquivalents`, regexp-compiler-tonode.cc:1743, from ICU's case closure minus the
  characters ECMA-262's Canonicalize keeps apart, special-case.h:20-60), under `iu`/`iv` by simple
  case folding (`AddUnicodeCaseEquivalents`, regexp-compiler-tonode.cc:512-540). A character
  becomes its one to four equivalents (`GetCaseIndependentLetters`, regexp-compiler.cc:1575), and
  two that differ in one bit match under a mask in one compare (`ShortCutEmitCharacterPair`,
  :1077-1103; `EmitAtomLetter`, :1685).

Design (gains are shares of the W5 census's instructions, 56c28010 no-LTO, from the plan's W5b
paragraph: the backtracking interpreter and class tests are about 9% of Octane's, coffeescript
3.6%, strings.js 14%, source-map 2.8%, and in the census Djot 1.5% besides 1.6% in the one-byte
tier; coverage from the table above; native code assumed to remove 75-85% of the matcher work it
covers, counted after item 1's):
1. The matcher's futile retries (Rust only, both tiers, no dump change):
   a. Atomic loops, as V8's four kinds: a greedy `Many` whose continuation is non-multiline `$`
      and then the match, always succeeds at the greedy extent, starts with `\b` over a
      word-character body, or starts with a set disjoint from the loop's takes no Greedy
      record, so it never retries shorter runs. coffeescript's `\s+$`
      (kAtEnd), source-map's `[\w+\-.]+` before `:` (kDisjoint), Octane's `\s*$`, `(\d*)(\D*)`
      (kTotal) and `\b\w+\b` (kBoundary).
   b. Restart past a failed atomic loop: when such a loop, first in the program, ran to `e` and the
      continuation failed, no start inside its run can match (the run from there ends at `e` too,
      and the continuation sees the same `e`), so the scan resumes at `e`. coffeescript's restarts
      inside every whitespace run: O(k) for a run of k blanks instead of O(k^2).
   c. First-element filters for assertion-led programs: a multiline `$` admits line terminators
      and the end, `\s*$` the loop's set and the end, an alternative led by `^` position 0 and,
      under `m`, line starts. coffeescript's `/$/m` (2.06M attempts), Octane's trim (1.0M).
   Gains: coffeescript ~2.3% (`clean` from 2,150 us a call toward Sparkplug-only Node's 375),
   source-map ~1% (its URL pattern's retries), Octane ~1%. Risk: a misclassified loop drops a
   match that a retry would have found; each kind is a proof over the program, tested against
   the matcher with the analysis forced off (a switch per item, as W5a's).
2. Case closure at compile time (W5's item 7, Rust only): under `i`, every Class closes over
   ECMA-262's case relation: Canonicalize's for legacy patterns (simple uppercasing, never
   folding non-ASCII onto ASCII, §22.2.2.7.3), simple case folding for `u` (regex_fold.rs's
   FOLD_CANON and orbits); `v`-mode sets are closed already (05e1f798, `ClassSet::
   close_over_case`, :7337). A Char becomes its equivalence set, compiled as a Char, a two-member
   mask, or a small Class. Matching then tests membership with no folding (`CharClass::matches`'
   orbit loops, :1878-1921, go for compiled classes), the 256-entry LUTs serve `i` patterns, and
   the prefilter and first-element filter scan for case-closed sets: Octane's cookie readers get
   a real prefilter. Backreferences under `i` keep runtime Canonicalize. Gains: case folding at
   match time was at most 0.11% in the census; the value is the filters (Octane's 22% of
   attempts) and 3b. Risk: Canonicalize's exceptions (ß, ẞ, the Kelvin sign, and characters
   whose uppercase is ASCII); a test over every BMP code point compares the closed sets with
   `canonicalize_legacy` and `fold_canon`, as V8 generates special-case.cc.
3. The native tier: compile the matcher's `Inst` program itself, one template per instruction,
   for each subject width (V8's assemble-from-bytecode shape). `Matcher::run` stays the reference
   and the fallback for everything a program uses that the tier does not.
   a. Core, both backends: Char, Any, Class (one-byte LUT; two-byte LUT below 256, else ranges
      searched inline for a few, by helper for more), Save with W5c's undo rules (records above
      the floor, one first-write record per slot at the root floor, 46c93827), Split with the
      first-instruction tests `branch_may_match` makes resolved at compile time, Jmp, the match,
      `^`/`$`/`\b` with their flags resolved statically, greedy and lazy Many (records and retry
      stubs; 1a's atomic loops without), SetMark and CheckProgress, ClearCaps with its snapshot;
      one-byte and two-byte subjects. The candidate loop runs in the generated code with the
      first-element test in line, calling the Rust vector scans for long skips. Coverage: Octane
      91.5%, source-map 100%, coffeescript 76%, Djot 100%.
   b. Item 2's closed `i` programs: Octane +8.5%, coffeescript +2.2%.
   c. Lookaheads (a frame record and the capture snapshot, as `LookFrame`), backreferences
      (inline unit compare; `i` through a helper), counted loops, modifier groups (flags fixed per
      instruction at compile time): coffeescript +21.4%.
   d. Unicode mode (surrogate pairs stepped as one element) and lookbehind: strings.js 100%.
      `v`-mode string sets (StringSet) stay on the matcher.
   Machine state: an entry `fn(*mut NativeMatch) -> u32` with today's statuses plus resource
   exhaustion. Callee-saved registers hold the subject, length, position, the context, the
   capture base and the backtrack stack's base, top and limit (AArch64 x19-x28; x86-64 rbx, rbp,
   r12-r15 and the frame). Captures and marks stay in the program's `MatchScratch` (3708989b).
   The backtrack stack is the scratch's storage taken as 16-byte native records (a resume or
   undo-stub address and an operand), grown by a Rust helper that enforces
   `MAX_BACKTRACK_STATE` (:5859) as the matcher does. A pop jumps to the record's address;
   undo stubs restore and pop again, as V8's undo code (regexp-compiler.cc:861-884). Polling: a
   counter in a register, decremented at each backtrack and candidate, loads RuntimeInterrupt's
   state word at zero; deadlines go through the Rust helper every 2^14 steps
   (`INTERRUPT_POLL_MASK`). Tier-up: after 64 executions as now, or at once for a subject of
   1,000 units or more (V8's rule); each width compiles on first use; one ExecutableBuffer per
   program and width (jit.rs:1564-1600), its code listed in the perf map as today. The one-byte
   tier (`OneByteNativeProgram`, both emitters, `find_terminal_one_byte_many`) goes once 3a
   covers its subset, which also retires the fault above; until then its declines are lifted
   only after the Crystal check.
   Gains beyond 1's: Octane 6.0-6.8% (with 3b; about a sixth of what W5a leaves), source-map
   1.4-1.5%, coffeescript 0.8-1.0%, with 3d strings.js 10.5-12%, Djot under 1% (W5c's
   single-element path took most of its matcher work; its RegExp cost now lies in W2's arrays).
   Risks: two code generators on two backends; every compiled program runs under the
   differential gate below, and the matcher remains the reference. Code memory: an mmap per
   program and width costs a page each, so Web Tooling's hundreds of patterns may need a shared
   arena. Polling must bound the time to an interrupt in every loop. AAPCS64's undefined flags
   across calls and unextended narrow arguments are the fault's lesson: compare results
   explicitly and extend every argument narrower than a register. Windows' x64 ABI stays on the
   matcher, as today's tier does. Garbage collection: matching allocates nothing and runs no
   JavaScript, and the caller keeps the subject rooted.
   Dumps and tests: the JIT's dumps do not change; `TRUST_JS_REGEXP_CODEDUMP=<source>` prints a
   compiled program's words for review, as `TRUST_JS_JIT_CODEDUMP` does; the generated harness
   (scripts/gen-regexp-native-harness.py, regex_native_generated.rs) grows to each stage's
   instruction set; switches force the tier off (`TRUST_JS_REGEXP_TIER_UP_AT=0`, as now) and
   force it on (`=1`).
4. Diagnostics: the per-pattern census (`TRUST_JS_REGEXP_PROF=2`, the patch in w5b-design) as a
   documented switch, with native executions and compiled code bytes per pattern.

Order:
1. 4 and 1 (Rust only, both backends): coffeescript's and source-map's largest costs, and the
   matcher's results unchanged by construction.
2. 2 (item 7): closed classes and case-closed filters, which 3b needs.
3. The Crystal check of the AArch64 fault; lift the declines if it holds.
4. 3a on x86-64 (prototyped on zombie) and AArch64, behind the gate; then 3b, 3c, 3d; the
   one-byte tier goes with 3a.

The differential gate (every stage of 3, before it lands):
- A verify switch, `TRUST_JS_REGEXP_NATIVE_VERIFY=1`: each native execution also runs the matcher
  and compares the result, every capture span and the error kind (interruption, resource
  exhaustion), and aborts with the pattern, flags, subject and start on a difference.
- Test262's RegExp suites (built-ins/RegExp, String.prototype's match, matchAll, replace,
  replaceAll, search and split, annexB's RegExp, language/literals/regexp: 2,518 tests) at
  --tier=jit threshold 8 with the tier forced on (`TRUST_JS_REGEXP_TIER_UP_AT=1`) and verify on, and
  with the tier off; both reports identical to the base's.
- A fuzzed corpus: patterns generated over each stage's grammar plus near misses (programs one
  unsupported instruction away), against one-byte, two-byte and surrogate-bearing subjects with
  line terminators and case variants, sticky and global, from fixed seeds; at least 1M cases a
  stage, with differences reduced to a minimal pattern and subject.
- The census workloads' patterns: every pattern the five workloads compile, with subjects
  sampled from their executions (recorded by the census switch), replayed under verify; and the
  workloads themselves, run with the tier off and on, with identical output.
- Interruption and limits: deadlines and cancellation during native scans and backtracking, and a
  pattern exhausting `MAX_BACKTRACK_STATE` under a lowered limit, behave as the matcher does.

Exit (agreed, Decision 13; Crystal, fat LTO, pinned, pacing off, against W5a's exit build):
- The census's "backtracking interpreter" and "class tests" instructions (w5cat2) at most 30% of
  W5a's on Octane RegExp and source-map, 40% on coffeescript, and, with 3d, 50% on strings.js's
  RegExp cases.
- coffeescript's `clean` within 1.5x of Sparkplug-only Node in the same session (today 5.7x).
- Octane RegExp's score at least 1.15x W5a's (predicted 1.2-1.3x).
- Test262 identical; the gate without a difference over Test262's RegExp suites, the fuzzed
  corpus and the census workloads' patterns; the AArch64 fault reproduced on the pre-fix code
  and absent from the tier that lands.

- 4704806b: RegExp execution takes the interpreter's poll word, and the scan loop splits into

  the native candidate loop) and `run_matcher`. No behaviour change.
- 0fccc7fd: the tier on AArch64. A shared generator emits one template per instruction of the
  program through a macro-assembler interface (V8's shape: code assembled from the bytecode), for
  one-byte and two-byte subjects, with the design's machine state and tier-up rules; it polls

- 53d0bef4: the x86-64 backend (System V; Windows stays on the matcher), its encodings pinned by a
  test whose cases GNU objdump disassembles as intended.
- f9c90215, 1c5afe1d: tighter loops. The scan tests its bound once an element and keeps the class
  table's address in a register; the candidate loop skips up to 16 ruled-out starts in line,
  also for patterns that can start only where `^` holds outside multiline mode (Octane's trims).
- e3d04216: a loop minimum of 2^31 or more goes straight to backtracking (on x86-64 the lazy
  loop's limit had wrapped, failing only by accident).
- 551d7414: AArch64's candidate status branch was a `tbnz`, which reaches 32 KB, so any program
  past 32 KB of code silently stayed on the matcher; a compare and `b.mi` replace it, and a
  code-size estimate keeps every branch in reach (programs past about 800 KB of code stay on the
  matcher, where V8 compiles any size with veneers).
- dda8d69c: strings.js's RegExp cases had risen 2.5% from inlining after the split; `Matcher::run`
  is out of line again, `run_matcher` in line, and tier-up, verify and the scratch's first sizing

- 709e17a9: the fuzzer mixes its seeds with splitmix64 (it seeded with `seed | 1`, so seeds 2
  and 3 ran the same stream).

Notes: the tier counts 2^14 events (a decrement and a branch each) before it loads the poll word,
and a scan loads it every 16K elements, where V8 checks its stack limit at every backtrack; the
time to an interrupt has the same bound. The tier checks `MAX_BACKTRACK_STATE` as its stack grows
and counts one record per loop retry, where the matcher checks every 2^14 steps with two records,
so the two can differ only at the limit (verify treats that as a difference; none appeared).
x86-64's gains are about half of AArch64's on Octane. 3b looks nearly free: case-closed `i`
programs already compare without folding, and outside Unicode mode `\b` is unaffected by `i`; most
of Octane's remaining matcher work is `i` patterns.

Then 3b, the case-closed `i` programs (dfe1c591, 2026-10-29, an agent's): once item 2 has closed
characters and classes over case, the tier takes an `i` program like any other; outside Unicode
mode `\b` is unchanged by `i`, and backreferences still decline (3c). A class of at most two
elements in the subject's width is tested by element, as V8's EmitAtomLetter does: one element is
one compare, two that differ in one bit are one masked compare (ShortCutEmitCharacterPair,
regexp-compiler.cc:1077-1103), and two others on two-byte subjects are two compares. One-byte
code keeps the table for other pairs and inside loops that hold it, where it takes fewer
instructions, and three- and four-member case groups (ǅ, Σ, ι) use the table, since their compare
chains cost more than it on misses.

Then 3c (20579ce7, 227fc7a6, 2026-10-29, the same agent's):
- 20579ce7 (tests only): counted loops made catastrophic fuzz cases common, each costing the 5 s
  deadline, so the fuzzer runs the matcher first with 250 ms and the native code only when the
  matcher finished, still with 4 s, so a native hang remains a difference.
- 227fc7a6: lookaheads run as the matcher's `LookFrame`: the body in line above a frame record
  that holds the position and the choices, its offset from the stack's base kept in a slot, as
  V8 keeps a lookaround's stack pointer as an offset (regexp-compiler-tonode.cc:1463,
  regexp-macro-assembler-arm64.cc:1997-2014); success cuts the stack back to the frame and
  failure pops it. The design's capture snapshot is an unset of the lookahead's own groups, which
  are always unset on entry, as V8's NegativeSubmatchSuccess and PositiveSubmatchSuccess do
  (regexp-compiler.cc:887-915, 5975-5998), so no slots are copied. Element-only bodies
  (coffeescript's `(?!\d)`, `(?!\s)`, `(?!:)`) are tested in place, with no frame.
  Backreferences compare unit by unit in line, polled between stretches, and under `i` through a
  Rust helper. Counted loops keep their count in a mark slot (counts from 2^31 stay on the
  matcher), and modifier groups emit nothing: each instruction's flags are fixed when it is
  compiled. Found before landing: a positive lookahead left an undo record at every success, more
  backtracking state than the matcher's; at the root floor it now leaves one an attempt.

Then 3d and Decision 25 (381c9d4e..b2ec0a93, 2026-10-29, the same agent's):
- 381c9d4e: Unicode-mode programs and lookbehinds. A surrogate pair is one element, stepped as the
  matcher's `Paired` input steps it; the code decodes an element only where a test could match a
  surrogate or a character past the BMP, and tests code units elsewhere. V8 instead splits classes
  into BMP ranges, surrogate pairs and guarded lone surrogates (regexp-compiler-tonode.cc:410-499,
  609-623) because its quick checks and Boyer-Moore tables work on code units; our tier compiles
  the matcher's code-point program and is verified against it, so it keeps the plan's element
  stepping. As V8 does, a backreference must not end inside a pair (`CheckNotInSurrogatePair`,
  regexp-compiler.cc:6064-6067), and a lookbehind's body is compiled reversed and read backward,
  its captures' start and end swapped (regexp-compiler-tonode.cc:1499). Under `i` with `u`, `\b`
  also takes the long s and the Kelvin sign, and backreferences fold through the helper. `v`-mode
  class strings stay on the matcher.
- 1ea59724 (tests only): deadlines, cancellation and the backtracking-state limit inside
  Unicode-mode loops and lookbehinds, forward and backward, on both backends.
- b2ec0a93 (Decision 25): a loop's empty-iteration check and its mark slot are emitted only where
  the body can match empty (assertions, lookarounds, backreferences and a `v`-mode string set
  holding the empty string count as possibly empty), with 39 cases against Node v24.22.0.

-13.6%), Octane RegExp -0.23% (Decision 25 -0.11% of it), source-map -0.14% and Djot's 40 passes

### W3: calls (census and design, 2026-10-27; exit agreed, Decision 10)

Census: the W2a tip (a1535a6f, branch p2-w2a; since then main's code, c4d68127 adding only W2a's
record), a no-LTO release build from a `git archive` export into a fresh target dir, on Crystal.
User-mode instructions (`perf stat -e instructions:u`), perf and the engine pinned to the A725
cores (`taskset -c 10-13`), `TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0`: the engine's repeat runs agree to the
instruction. Profiles: `perf record -e instructions:u --call-graph fp --user-callchains -m 16`
with `TRUST_JS_PERF_MAP=1`, at periods under this kernel's cap of 15,000 samples/s
(`perf_event_max_sample_rate`; shorter periods are throttled and lose most samples). A census
build of the same export, not used for counts of instructions, adds entry-path counters, per-site
reasons for CALLSTAT's misses and a classifier of the call-miss helper's callees
(`TRUST_JS_JIT_CALLSITES=1`). Per-call costs come from microbenchmarks: a loop in a function,
warmed, at 200,000 and 1,200,000 iterations; the difference over 1,000,000, less each engine's own
empty-loop cost (14 instructions; Sparkplug ~100). Node v24.22.0 runs the same files limited to
Sparkplug (`--max-opt=1`) and in full; every output agrees. Beside the fixtures, Web Tooling's chai
alone (its single-tool bundle, target/p2/ext/cli-chai.js; 50 s on these cores) samples the
standing suites: it is call-heavy, and its gap to Sparkplug-only Node was 3.0x at the close-out.
Everything is under target/p2/w3-design: the binaries, the scripts (micro.py, callsplit.py,
fixturecat.py, pathtrace.py, instrument*.py), per-call costs and their parts (data/micro*.jsonl,
data/micro-perf/*.split), the census build's counts (data/callpath, data/callsites), shares
(data/perf/*.cat, data/wtb/chai.cat) and the direct call's traced path (data/direct/prof). V8 is
cited at c8b709085e21 (main, 2026-10-23; its sources in v8-src there).

Calls by entry path (census build; "helper" = the call-hit helper, `jit_call_hit`):

| | instructions | direct sequence | natives via the helper (operand-only + full boundary) | environment or `arguments` entries | other helper entries | call-miss helper | intrinsic helper | cached entries from Rust (`call_jit_cached`) | `Interp::call` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 2.76e9 | 312,804 | 550,038 (245,769 + 304,269) | 23,732 | 4,348 | 616 | 91,733 | 172,475 | 79,783 |
| React | 5.59e8 | 448,177 | 0 | 0 | 683 | 62 | 0 | 62 | 6,749 |
| Djot | 1.01e9 | 127,623 | 62,789 (0 + 62,789) | 208 | 14,551 | 8,363 | 276,060 | 8,385 | 18,157 |
| Web Tooling chai | 1.37e11 | 20.2M | 29.3M (6.2M + 23.1M) | 3.36M (5.72M with Rust callers') | 17,516 | 218,395 | 25.3M | 2.77M | 630,512 |

Share of each workload's instructions in the call path (sampled; a leaf in a generic utility,
hash tables, allocation, thread-locals, takes its nearest caller's class; generated code cannot be
split, so its direct-call part is estimated as the count above times the 257 instructions a
two-argument direct call costs beyond its callee's body, below):

| % | direct calls (in generated code) | native boundary | call helpers | intrinsic helper | compiled frames entered from Rust | environments | generic chain | total |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 2.9 | 4.6 | 2.0 | 0.6 | 3.1 | 0.5 | 0.8 | 14.5 |
| React | 20.6 | 0.9 | 0.0 | 0.0 | 0.5 | 0.7 | 0.8 | 23.5 |
| Djot | 3.2 | 3.0 | 0.9 | 3.7 | 1.7 | 0.4 | 1.5 | 14.4 |
| chai | 3.8 | 6.4 | 3.1 | 4.0 | 1.8 | 1.6 | 0.7 | 21.4 |

- The direct call costs 257 instructions beyond its callee's body. The census traced one
  iteration of `s = add(s, k) | 0` through the code dump and a per-instruction profile (375
  instructions an iteration, all accounted for): at the site, the callee load and the first
  way's identity, epoch and realm compares 31; the gates 30; depth, `FnFrame` push and record pop
  19; argument moves and slot fill 14; the callee context's ten fields 19; saving, clearing and
  restoring `Interp::constructing` and `new_target` with the result push 31; the `blr` 6; the
  finish stub 48 (five clean-frame checks, then the completion move, `FnFrame` pop, record push,
  depth); in the callee, 59: saving x19-x22, d8-d15 and the frame record 16, context loads 4,
  the code-residency counter's increment and decrement 14, saving, setting and restoring
  `Interp::strict` 12, the entry poll 12, the return status 1. The rest is the loop (35), the free-
  name read of `add` (54), argument spills (9) and `add`'s body (20). Sparkplug-only Node spends
  123 instructions on the whole call, its read of `add` and `add`'s body included.
- Per call (instructions, engine / Sparkplug-only Node; full Node inlines most of these): direct
  361 / 123, method 447 / 209, closure 388 / 127, recursion (fib) 384 / 180, a function as
  constructor 1,090 / 422, a class as constructor 3,214 / 329, a getter 1,373 / 246,
  `Function.prototype.call` 1,020 / 285, `apply` 1,574 / 399, spread 4,480 / 231, rest
  parameters 1,602 / 410, `arguments` (`.length` and `[0]`) 2,207 / 406, a body reading only
  `arguments.length` 819 / 559, a callee with one captured binding 1,270 / 230, an arrow capturing
  `this` 1,257 / 322. The call table's time ratios at the close-out track these closely: direct,
  method, closure and fib take 1.65, 1.47, 1.73 and 2.13 times Sparkplug's instructions an
  iteration (its loop included), against 1.9, 1.5, 1.8 and 2.2 times its time.
- CALLSTAT's "all gates pass (direct not emitted at site?)" (Djot's 7,341) is one site, the
  event loop of djot's `parse` (`for(const t of k)w(S,t),E=t.endpos;`, about 667 events a parse,
  11 parses). `parse` runs once a parse, so its loop runs as a borrowed-frame loop fragment, and
  `compile_entry` clears the direct-call bit for borrowed entries (jit.rs:3938): every call there
  takes the helper and `run_moved`. Two other labels mislead: "var force resets" (Vue's 1,343) is
  bit 0 of `CallIc::direct`, which `jit_direct_flags` (bytecode.rs:20599) clears for rest-parameter
  callees, here Vue's array-method wrapper `function(...e)`; and "env entry" counts every
  `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV` callee: Vue's 23,732 are real environments (all one function, Vue's
  `trigger`, whose `let u` an arrow on a rare path captures), while 2,752,053 of chai's 3,355,594
  are bodies with an `arguments` slot and no environment (`flag(obj, key, value)` reads only
  `arguments.length`). "Recompile not settled" is the warm-up: the sequence waits for the callee's
  inline attempt, under 100 calls per callee (Djot 7,170, Vue 2,984, chai 17,229).
- An environment entry costs 882 instructions more than a plain closure call (Sparkplug: 103 more
  for its context): about 490 make the environment (`ActivationPlan::instantiate` copies the
  plan's 24-byte bindings into a vector, allocates the cell, publishes the layout) and about 460
  are the Rust entry that replaces the direct sequence (the helper, `call_jit_env_committed`,
  `run_moved_env`, `run_moved_inner`). A body reading only `arguments.length` builds nothing but
  pays the same entry: 819 against 361 for a direct call. In Vue the environment entries are 0.7%
  of the fixture (23,732 x 882); in chai the `arguments`-slot entries are 0.9% (2.75M x 458) and
  the real environments 0.4%.
- Natives through the helper (instructions per call, engine / Sparkplug-only Node; of the
  engine's, the full boundary and the native's own work, from samples, which skid across
  neighbouring functions, so these parts are approximate; the rest is the call template and the
  helper's dispatch):

| | engine | boundary | own work | Sparkplug |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WeakMap.prototype.get (Vue 245,085 calls) | 541 | 0 (operand-only) | 374 | 216 |
| Map.prototype.get, object key | 668 | 0 (operand-only) | 417 | 222 |
| Array.isArray (Vue 140,523) | 739 | 399 | 42 | 168 |
| Reflect.get, 3 arguments (Vue 78,630) | 1,582 | 258 | 1,082 | 283 |
| Reflect.set (Vue 23,764) | 2,263 | 341 | 1,611 | 865 |
| Reflect.has (Vue 10,724; chai 2.3M) | 1,496 | 276 | 1,004 | 246 |
| Object.is (Vue 23,077) | 827 | 366 | 67 | 180 |
| parseInt (Vue 13,452) | 1,060 | 325 | 418 | 134 |
| Number(x) (Vue 12,085) | 785 | 359 | 140 | 153 |
| String.prototype.slice(start) (chai 12.3M) | 1,013 | 338 | 302 | 309 |
| Math.min, 3 arguments (chai 6.1M) | 451 | 0 (numeric-only) | 97 | 225 |
| Array.prototype.indexOf, packed (chai 4.9M) | 2,082 | 310 | 1,435 | 269 |
| String.prototype.includes (Djot 13,003) | 1,104 | 290 | 582 | 459 |
| String.prototype.substring (Djot 11,041) | 1,027 | 344 | 320 | 360 |
| RegExp.prototype.exec (Djot 27,248) | 4,994 | 308 | 4,399 | 900 |
| codePointAt, two-byte string (Djot's intrinsic helper) | 439 | 9 (the helper's own: 244) | 32 | 231 |

  The boundary (`call_native_committed_with`, interpreter.rs:13898) roots the argument window in
  the registry, which the JIT frame already roots, checks depth and the native stack, polls
  allocation, saves and clears the construct state, polls host interruption before and after,
  activates the Agent for host callbacks and drains pending tail calls: 260-400 instructions,
  more than the whole call costs Sparkplug for Array.isArray, Object.is, parseInt and Number. Only
  the 14 collection methods, Math's numeric functions and DataView's accessors with primitive
  operands skip it today (`INTRINSIC_OPERAND_ONLY`, `_NUMERIC_ONLY`, `_DATAVIEW_*`, bytecode.rs:
  1055-1067, assigned at fill by comparing function pointers, :23908-24025). The intrinsic helper
  (`jit_intrinsic`), which the inline intrinsics fall back to, pays its own version (depth,
  collector check, construct state): 244 of codePointAt's 439. Where a native's own work
  dominates, the boundary is not the problem: WeakMap.get makes four hash lookups across
  `weak_collection_index` and `weak_collection_data` (builtins/collections.rs:738-764); Reflect.get
  converts the key and allocates; Array.prototype.indexOf reads and compares element by element
  (`fast_get_elem`, `strict_equals`); String.prototype.indexOf and includes run Rust's substring
  search, whose setup dominates on short strings (400-580).
- Natives calling JavaScript: a Proxy `get` trap costs 3,304 an access (Sparkplug 448), ten
  forEach callbacks 6,085 (1,666). Each compiled frame entered from Rust (by Proxy traps and
  callbacks through `CallbackCache` and `call_jit_cached`, or by the helper, then
  `call_jit_committed` and `run_moved_inner`) costs Vue about 430 instructions before the callee
  runs (3.1% of the fixture over 200,129 entries).
- Misses (the call-miss helper, then the generic path, within the generic chain's 0.7-1.5%) are
  few: most of Djot's 8,363 come at sites with no way of the current epoch, fresh sites and sites
  the global call-cache epoch emptied (every landed inline recompile bumps it); 848 of the 2,928
  classified come from two sites with all four ways filled, where every new closure evicts a way.

What V8 does (c8b709085e21):
- Sparkplug's call is a builtin call: `BaselineCompiler::BuildCall` (baseline-compiler.cc:1713-
  1729) calls `Call_ReceiverIs*_Baseline_Compact`, which loads the feedback vector from the
  caller's frame and runs `CollectCallFeedback` (ic-callable.tq:91-168: increment the call count,
  then return if the slot holds this target), then tail-calls `Call` and `CallFunction`
  (builtins-arm64.cc:2373-2450, 2944-3024) and `InvokeFunctionCode` (macro-assembler-arm64.cc:
  2884-2948), which pads missing arguments with undefined in place and jumps to the callee's
  dispatch-table entry. The callee pushes its frame (fp, lr, then in `BaselineOutOfLinePrologue`
  context, closure, argc, bytecode array, feedback cell and vector; builtins-arm64.cc:1147-1259)
  and makes one stack check against a limit that also signals interrupts (:1224-1233); the
  interrupt budget is charged at loop back-edges and returns, not at entry (baseline-compiler.cc:
  2638-2720, 2903-2911). new.target travels in x3, undefined for a call (macro-assembler-arm64.cc:
  2908); there is no frame stack beside the machine stack (frame-constants.h:744-785) and no
  ambient strictness. Maglev's `CallKnownJSFunction` skips `Call` and `CallFunction` altogether:
  it pads at the site and calls the dispatch entry (maglev-ir.cc:7434-7470).
- A function with context-allocated variables makes its own context: `CreateFunctionContext`
  (bytecode-generator.cc:8719-8744), in Sparkplug a call to `FastNewFunctionContextFunction`
  (constructor.tq:63-73): one new-space allocation of map, length, ScopeInfo, previous and the
  slots, filled with undefined (builtins-constructor-gen.cc:381-469); bytecode then copies the
  captured parameters (bytecode-generator.cc:8746-8770) and writes the TDZ hole at each
  declaration (:2139-2183). Slots are tagged words; modes and flags live in the ScopeInfo
  (scope-info.tq:101-108), which slot 0 of the context names (contexts.h:545-577).
- Builtins are machine code entered like JavaScript functions (TFJ/Torque): Map and WeakMap
  get/has/set, Array.isArray, Object.is, Reflect.get and has, String includes and substring,
  RegExp exec, parseInt and Number all are. Only C++ builtins (Reflect.set among these) pay a
  boundary: an adaptor and `CEntry`'s exit frame (builtins-internal-gen.cc:1337-1402,
  builtins-arm64.cc:4749-4926). WeakMap.get is one EphemeronHashTable probe by the key's stored
  identity hash, and a key without one returns undefined without creating it
  (builtins-collections-gen.cc:3036-3056, 2742-2760, 2828-2857). The embedder's boundary, API
  callbacks, is the heavier one (an exit frame and `FunctionCallbackInfo`, builtins-arm64.cc:
  5081-5232): the engine applies the equivalent of that boundary to its own builtins.
- A call slot holds a weak target, a FeedbackCell (closures of one literal) or the megamorphic
  sentinel, plus a call count and a SpeculationMode (feedback-vector.h:1115-1118;
  ic-callable.tq:22-50, 134-149). Maglev and TurboFan check the target and reduce known builtins:
  ArrayIsArray, ObjectIs, ReflectGet and Has, MapPrototypeGet, StringPrototypeIncludes,
  NumberParseInt (maglev-reducer-inl.h:1258-1299, 3152-3156; js-call-reducer.cc:5382-5815), and
  use count / invocation count as call frequency (feedback-vector.cc:1158-1167).

Design (gains are shares of today's instructions; per-call counts are the census's):
1. A cheaper direct call (ARM64), from 257 instructions beyond the body to about 140:
   a. The static gates (compiled entry, frame size, the global-body need, a sloppy callee's
      object receiver) fold into one ready bit at fill, and the sequence counts the callee's runs
      itself, so the inline-attempt warm-up gate goes: 30 to ~10.
   b. new.target and the construct flag live in the callee's record, as V8 passes new.target in
      a register; an ordinary call stores Undefined there and the sequence stops saving, clearing
      and restoring `Interp::constructing`/`new_target`; natives that read new.target take it from
      the boundary (2): 31 to ~4.
   c. The callee frame: save only the callee-saved registers the chunk uses, read strictness from
      the running chunk instead of an ambient `Interp::strict` set and restored by every
      prologue and epilogue, and find running code by walking the live frame records when code is
      reclaimed instead of counting residency per activation; one entry check: 59 to ~30.
   d. The finish stub checks one dirty bit that the slow paths set (pending tail call,
      materialized activation, `FnFrame` extra, operands left), not five fields; the callee
      context reads the chunk's constants instead of copying them; the record pool pops and
      pushes by index: 86 to ~45.
   Gains (117 instructions a direct call): React 9.4%, chai 1.7%, Djot 1.5%, Vue 1.3%; on the
   call table, the direct, method and closure cases' instructions an iteration fall to 1.13,
   1.10 and 1.23 times Sparkplug's, recursion to 1.48. Risks: code reclamation must see every
   running chunk (records, Rust entries, borrowed frames: the collector already registers each
   active context); every reader of `Interp::strict` and `new_target` moves. Dumps: every ARM64
   chunk's prologue and epilogue, every call site's sequence, the finish stub.
2. Natives without the full boundary (both backends, the helper):
   a. Native classes recorded at registration, not pointer lists: pure (Array.isArray, Object.is,
      Number.isNaN-style predicates, Math, the collection methods), primitive-pure (no author
      code when the receiver and arguments are primitives: parseInt, parseFloat, Number, String
      and Boolean called as functions, String.prototype's slice, substring, indexOf, includes,
      startsWith, charAt, trim and case mapping; Array.prototype.indexOf and includes on arrays
      the element protector covers; RegExp exec and test while W5's protector, 753d3aa2, holds),
      and the rest. The helper runs pure and primitive-pure natives on the operand window as the
      operand-only path does now; only natives that read new.target keep that clear. A native
      that usually runs no author code but may (Reflect.get, set and has through a getter or a
      Proxy) runs its fast path first and enters the boundary only on the path that calls out.
      Gains: Vue 3.8% (Array.isArray, Object.is, parseInt and Number 2.6%, Reflect 1.2%), chai
      5.2% (slice 3.0%, Array indexOf 1.1%, Reflect 0.9%), Djot 1.7%.
   b. The intrinsic helper's fallback takes the same light path for its pure intrinsics: chai
      ~2.8% (25.3M entries), Djot ~2.5% (276,060), Vue 0.4%.
   c. Templates, after the IC's identity proof (non-speculative, unlike Maglev's reductions):
      Array.isArray and Object.is in line (Vue ~1%), Math.min and max with three or four
      arguments (chai 1.9%), String.prototype.slice with one argument beside the existing
      two-argument intrinsic (chai ~1%), codePointAt on two-byte strings with W7's S4 (Djot
      ~0.3%).
   d. Natives' own algorithms, for their owners: WeakMap get and has as one probe in a payload
      (W2g's pattern; Vue 2.7%), Reflect.get, has and set sharing the property-get path (Vue
      3.7%, chai 2.7%; their key conversion changed with W2a's step 6, property keys as
      PropKeys, d3c66d56), dense indexOf (W1's deferred bulk indexOf now has a workload: chai
      4.3%), the string searcher's setup (Djot 0.5%).
   Risk: a misclassified native skipping the boundary while it can run author code; debug builds
   assert that nothing on the light path reaches `Interp::call`. No dump change apart from 2c,
   whose stubs gain arms.
3. The closure entry: a callee that needs an environment builds it in its prologue, as V8's do:
   allocate the cell its ScopeDesc sizes (a lean helper first, W2e's inline allocation later),
   link the closure's environment as parent, copy captured parameters from the argument slots,
   fill vars with Undefined and lexicals with the empty word, and install it as the frame's
   environment. W2b's descriptors make that a copy of words. Bindings become 8-byte slots (TDZ as
   the empty word; mutability and strictness in the descriptor; deletable eval bindings and
   imports in private descriptors, as W2b planned), aiming at the 64-byte class for one binding.
   Such callees and `arguments.length`-only or lazy-`arguments` bodies (the sequence stores argc,
   or the argument window, as the plan's W3 item has it) then take the direct sequence;
   `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV` keeps prepared entries and mapped arguments. Gains: an environment entry
   ~1,270 to ~430 (~300 after 1); chai 1.3%, Vue 0.7%, React ~0.5% (environments its Rust
   entries build). Risk: every captured-binding path changes layout (the VM, both JIT backends,
   eval and `with`, the collector's tracing); the prologue must initialize every word before the
   next safepoint. Dumps: binding stride and offsets everywhere, environment callees' prologues.
4. Coverage: direct sequences in borrowed frames (the sequence runs the callee on its own record
   and never swaps the caller's context: check that the restriction at jit.rs:3938 is stale;
   Djot 0.3%); rest parameters built in the callee's prologue (the call table's rest case, Vue's
   wrapper); CALLSTAT's labels and the census counters as `TRUST_JS_JIT_CALLSTAT=2`. Risk: a
   borrowed frame's continuation state (resume point, handlers, `ret`) must come through the call
   untouched. Dumps: borrowed chunks' call sites, rest callees' prologues.
5. Natives calling JavaScript: Proxy traps and accessors through a prepared entry that shares
   1b-1d's frame (Vue ~1.3% of its 3.1%); a getter or setter that the property IC proves takes the
   direct sequence from the IC's accessor landing (the getter 1,373 to ~450). Risk: the IC's
   holder and slot proof must still hold when the accessor is called, and the receiver must be the
   original object. Dumps: the property templates' accessor landing.
6. The call table's other forms: Function.prototype.call and apply with a cached target shift
   the arguments and take the direct sequence (V8's call builtin shifts and jumps); spread of an
   array under the iterator protector copies its elements onto the operand stack and takes the
   call template, instead of the generic op helper (`jit_exec`), which also binary-searches the
   operation's source position on every call; a class-constructor bit on the function object
   replaces the `class_info` probes (about 640 instructions of a class construction's 3,214);
   `new` takes a direct construct sequence. Predicted: call ~450, apply ~550, spread ~650, class
   construction ~800, function construction ~550. Risk: spread's fast path must be exactly the
   iteration it skips (a packed array, %ArrayIteratorPrototype%.next and the array's iterator
   unmodified, as the existing protector check proves); the class bit must live and die with
   `class_info`'s entry. Dumps: the `call`/`apply` intrinsic arms, spread and `new` sites. The
   plan's other W3 items wait for a workload: a call cache for the VM's `Op::Call` and
   bound-function entries (the generic chain, all its callers included, is 0.7-1.5% of these
   workloads); mapped arguments stay on the prepared path.
7. W4's call feedback, where W3 needs it: 2a gives every native a builtin id, which the IC records
   instead of comparing function pointers at fill (bytecode.rs:23908-24025) and which W4 exports
   as a native target's identity (what the optimizing tiers' builtin reductions key on); a
   per-site megamorphic bit after repeated evictions stops refilling (V8's sentinel); the ways'
   `func` already plays the FeedbackCell's role for closures of one literal. A per-site call count
   (V8 counts every call) costs ~3 instructions in the sequence; W3 leaves room for it in the
   CallIc and W4 decides within its 2% budget. No dump change unless the count changes the way's
   stride.
x64 has no inline probe or direct sequence (jit_x64.rs:1228, every call takes `H_CALL`): items
2, 3's helper path, 5 and the Rust parts of 6 serve both backends; 1, 2c and 3's template are
ARM64 in this stage.

Order:
1. Diagnostics (4's labels and counters).
2. 2a and 2b (Rust only, both backends, no dump change): the largest share on Vue, chai and Djot.
3. 1 (ARM64; dumps change): React's 10% and the call table.
4. 3 (W2b's descriptors are on main since a1535a6f).
5. 2c, 5, 6 and the rest of 4.
6. 7 with W4.
2d (Decision 10): Reflect, dense indexOf and the string searcher's setup in W3, after 2a and 2b,
which rework those natives' entry; WeakMap's single probe goes to W2g with its payload.

Exit (agreed, Decision 10; the plan's numbers as the starting point, Decision 2): on the call table,
against Node limited to Sparkplug in the same session, the direct, method and closure calls within
1.25x (the plan's; predicted 1.1-1.25x of Sparkplug's instructions an iteration, today 1.5-1.9x in
time), recursion within 1.6x (the plan's 1.25x needs about 160 fewer instructions a call, 40 more
than 1a-1d remove; predicted 1.48x, today 2.2x), the constructor within 1.25x (needs 6), and
`arguments`, rest, `call`/`apply`, spread and the class-and-getter case within 2x (the plan's; today
3.2-14x; spread and the class-and-getter case are the tightest, predicted about 2x). The plan's
"Vue/React elapsed improved by the measured call share" made measurable as pinned instructions on
main at the stage's end: React -8% and Vue -6% (predicted 10% and 8.5%, without 2d), Djot -4% and
Web Tooling's chai -10% (predicted 6% and 14%); elapsed times recorded at the exit measurement;
Test262 identical, no suite slower beyond noise.

Landed 2026-10-28, with W5a's matcher entry (637a944c..b6689c4b; Decision 1): order step 1's
diagnostics, 2a, 2b, 2d (an agent's) and item 6's class-constructor bit.
- 637a944c (2b): the guarded String intrinsics (charAt, codePointAt, slice) run without the
  native-call boundary.
- 1a455cb0 (2a): natives classed at registration run on the caller's operands without the
  boundary when their class proves that no author code runs: Object.is, Array.isArray,
  Number.isFinite, isInteger and isSafeInteger, and Boolean as a function for any operands;
  String, Number, String.fromCodePoint, parseInt, parseFloat and isFinite for primitive
  arguments; nineteen String.prototype methods for a primitive receiver and arguments. Both
  backends.
- 4c3aaa92: a class-constructor kind on the function object replaces the `class_info` probes.
- ed984cb7: CALLSTAT's miss labels by cause, and its counts by call path (`=2`).
- 6dad76db..42756ffa (2d): Reflect.has through HasProperty, Reflect.get's String keys through
  the property ICs, Reflect.set's own data properties written in place, packed Arrays'
  indexOf, lastIndexOf and includes searched in place, short strings scanned directly.
- ffe792dc: Proxy traps receive their keys' atoms (W1's follow-up).
Then (cf3689f7..852a3ec2) the constructor plans' keys, made once (the stage-exit timing's
RayTrace regression), item 1's first steps, and three corrections to 2a and the diagnostics:
- cf3689f7: the Prototype.js forwarder and the constructor plans carry their fields' keys.
- e7d5dee8: an ordinary caller's construct state passes through the direct sequence untouched;
  a constructing caller's is saved and restored as before (1b in the form that moves no
  reader).
- b81c0845: the finish stub hands the completion back in a register and reuses its checks'
  loads; the context install moves field pairs.
- 9cd9e918, ef112472: the strict flag and the call-cache epoch through pointers in the record.
- c8086cea: the static gates as one bit, filled with the call IC (1a without its counting).
- 753e03f0: d8-d15 saved only by chunks that write them, on a 64-byte frame otherwise; numeric
  chains keep their values in d16-d23, and the assembler records SIMD&FP writes as it emits
  them.
- 944acdf7: the finish stub's fast path runs without a frame of its own; the frame pool
  reserves its capacity.
- 3f4a4f80, a57b79b3, 852a3ec2: the call-path counters only in architecture-diagnostics
  builds; light natives leave an ordinary caller's construct state alone; the collections'
  methods run on their operands directly again.

Instructions (fat LTO, pinned to the A725 cores, pacing off, median of 3; base c4464dae):

| | base | W3 landed (ffe792dc) | with item 1's steps (852a3ec2) | stage target |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 2.455e9 | -5.8% | -7.0% | -6% |
| React | 5.221e8 | +0.1% | -3.1% | -8% |
| Djot | 8.401e8 | -3.4% | -6.0% | -4% |
| Web Tooling's chai | 1.210e11 | -9.9% | -10.8% | -10% |

Per call (the loop's 14 removed; Sparkplug-only Node's from the census in brackets): direct
361 to 309 (123), method 447 to 404 (209), closure 388 to 344 (127), recursion 384 to 346
(180), a function as constructor 877 to 854, a class construction 2,567 to 2,378, the class
getter 3,735 to 3,535, call 1,023 to 1,004, apply 1,477 to 1,465. Natives: Array.isArray 697
to 413, Object.is 778 to 486, Number(x) 744 to 468, parseInt 945 to 667, slice(start) 955 to
665, substring 972 to 702, String.prototype.includes 996 to 672, Reflect.get 1,314 to 988,
Reflect.set 1,813 to 1,057, Reflect.has 1,212 to 902, a Proxy get trap 3,103 to 2,413;
WeakMap.prototype.get and Map.prototype.get unchanged (452, 595). RayTrace with a fixed
iteration count: 12.084e9 instructions at w5a, 12.755e9 at ffe792dc, 12.212e9 with cf3689f7.

Validation: the ARM64 gates on b6689c4b and 852a3ec2 (the lib suite, pacing, the downstream
crates, GC stress on the new tests, the fixtures, difftest, Test262 identical: 53,575 of
53,575), zombie's x86-64 lib suite and Test262 for W3 and W5c, and clippy in every
configuration on both architectures. The dumps differ for W3 in an object field's offset (161
to 162, the class kind) and one Djot chunk's inlining, and for item 1 in the prologues,
epilogues and call sequences.

Not done: React's -8% needs item 1's rest (the residency marks, the entry poll, the
frame-record bookkeeping) and items 3-5; the call table's time ratios wait for the stage's
end; item 6's call, apply and spread (an agent's, p2-w3-6) wait for item 1's entry; W5a's
matcher-entry record comes with the W5 agent's Crystal counts.

Then item 1's next steps, the probe and item 6's call, apply and spread (c4bf3b4b..4fcd8a42,
2026-10-28; item 6 an agent's):
- c4bf3b4b: the code cache finds running native code by walking the live activation records
  and the frames the collector registers, instead of counting residency at every entry and
  exit; a direct call marks its callee's code referenced with one store into the code's CLOCK
  word, and the finish stub and a record's release clear the record's code base.
- 27902319: the finish stub stops checking the callee's operand stack (a packed word owns
  nothing) and, outside debug builds, the popped frame's `extra` (only the tree-walker's
  function entry fills one, on the frame it pushed; the helper asserts it). The probe compares
  the callee's word EOR the Object tag with the ways' identities, which no other kind of word
  can match, so the tag check leaves the hit path; way 0 is compared in line. Arguments move in
  pairs, an over-applied call's move goes out of line, and the shared stub returns its status
  in w8, where the finish stub leaves the threw flag.
- dfd1d4b2, 541e2b80, 6976d97c: a site whose callee is made afresh for each pass missed all
  four identity ways, called the secondary stub and missed the overflow cache before the
  code-keyed probe matched, about 80 instructions of probing a call. React's reducer passes a
  new arrow to `map` or `filter` on every call, so most of its calls were such. The code-keyed
  probe records the way it matched in the site (`CallSite::code_hint`), and once way 0's
  identity misses, the site tests that way's function first; a stale hint costs one failed
  test. Testing way 0's function instead measured React +1.01%: warm-up refills the ways round
  robin, and way 0 held another arrow. Emitted at every site, the probe after way 0 grew the
  generated code 7.4% on Vue, 5.1% on Djot and 3.8% on React, so it runs in the chunk's shared
  stub (`CallSecondary`, which already held the secondary probes): React +0.85% against the

Then a direct construct sequence for `new` (ba2c536a..b4b31d81, 2026-10-28, an agent's):
- ba2c536a: a base class without instance elements (fields, private methods, decorator
  initializers) constructs through the construct cache, as a function does; the fill reads the
  class-constructor kind (4c3aaa92). Both backends.
- 9d66739b: on ARM64 a `new` site checks its construct cache in line and runs the constructor
  through a construct form of the direct sequence (a shared stub per arity outside loops). A lean
  helper allocates `this` from the live `prototype` read through the cached slot: it runs no
  author code, never collects, and declines, allocating nothing, when `prototype` is not an
  Object. The body runs with new.target the constructor, an Object completion replaces `this`,
  and stack traces print the frame as `new F`. The instance stays in the native stack's save
  area until the finish stub returns, since the stub's helper fallback releases the record's
  `this`.
- b4b31d81: constructions count toward their constructor's inline checkpoints, as calls do
  (Decision 16): the sequence takes the call form's retry gate from the first run, and the Rust
  path counts while a checkpoint is pending.

Then the safepoint poll in V8's shape (d4c51376..d14387e3, 2026-10-28, an agent's; Decisions 11,
18 and 19; item 1c's one entry check):
- d4c51376: each interpreter has a poll word, an atomic other threads write: one bit the heap's
  (a collection due, the live-object ceiling included), one for host interrupts. A
  `RuntimeInterrupt` handle keeps weak references to the words it controls and writes each bit
  under its lock from the state at that moment, so a request racing a clear is never lost. One
  watchdog thread expires armed deadlines (Decision 18): the first deadline starts it, it serves
  those that follow and exits after 5 s with none armed; a burst's arm and clear costs 462 ns,
  where a thread per deadline measured 36-90 us. The Rust-side polls read the word too.
- ed9f6be9: every native safepoint loads the word and branches: the entry, loop and helper-end
  polls on ARM64 (12 instructions to 3) and x86-64 (10 to 3); the helper-free region guard tests
  the interrupt bit alone, and numeric loops keep their countdown. The allocator compares the
  live count with the ceiling as it counts (3 instructions an object allocation) and sets the
  heap's bit out of line.
- d14387e3: ShadowRealm code is interruptible by the host (Decision 19): its interpreter shares
  its creator's handle, and an interruption passes `evaluate` and wrapped functions in both
  directions, as V8's evaluate returns while execution terminates
  (builtins/builtins-shadow-realm.cc:182-186); ordinary throws still become TypeErrors.
