Then item 5's setters (92874629..40bcadf6, 2026-10-29, an agent's; Decision 17's shape; landed on
targeted tests under the owner's rule):
- 92874629: a store's cache records accessors as a read's does (`IC_ACCESSOR` with the depth and
  the receiver's, intermediate and holder's shapes): an own accessor at the store's own lookup, an
  inherited one once the creation path declines, when every object up to the holder is ordinary
  and plain within `IC_MAX_DEPTH` hops. A hit proves the chain again, so a Proxy anywhere takes the
  generic path, and calls the live [[Set]] with the original receiver as `this` and the value as
  its one argument, its result ignored (ECMA-262 §10.1.9.2 steps 3-7); an accessor without a
  setter fails silently in sloppy code and throws in strict code (PutValue step 3.e). Both
  backends. Profiled stores keep the canonical [[Set]]; `try_ic_set` takes its accessor switch
  as a const parameter and `set_prop_ic` is inlined, so the data stores' Rust path got shorter.
- 41c30af5 (ARM64): the stores' helper stub calls setters at run time, from any store's code
  whenever it was made. It finds the store's setter call cache by pc in the chunk's setter table
  (the getter table generalized: an accessor table whose slots also name a store's property
  cache), which the helper fills the first time it calls a user setter there. A read's template
  validates its accessor at the site; a store carries no accessor code: the stub proves it, as the
  exemplars' shared code does, with the reads' way loop over the store's cache and an accessor at
  the holder's slot. It reads the live [[Set]] from the pair (an absent one matches no callee and
  takes the helper), probes the call cache by identity and runs the direct sequence on [receiver,
  setter, V] with argc 1, dropping the setter's result. `SetProp` keeps V below the window as the
  assignment's value; that form and the `this` and local forms need two operand words above the
  store's depth, which chunks with such stores now reserve (`Cfg::jit_stack_capacity`). A
  declining gate puts the operands back; a throw is placed at the store.
- 40bcadf6: `o.k += v` statements (AppendProp) took the generic op helper; the ARM64 template now
  adds two Numbers in line and stores the sum through the store template under the operation's
  store cache, whose helper takes it as a `SetPropDrop` (and a setter, the stub's sequence).
  Strings, BigInts and objects keep the generic operation.

What the exemplars do (the forks the getters' notes left open):
- V8 (c8b709085e21) calls setters from its store IC builtin, which Sparkplug calls for every named
  store (baseline-compiler.cc:1189-1200): an own accessor's handler is the AccessorPair, whose live
  setter it calls (accessor-assembler.cc:1750-1758); an inherited one's holds the setter weakly
  under the chain's validity cell (:2279-2289). Only callable JSFunction setters are cached; a
  missing setter takes the slow stub (ic.cc:2585-2649). The bytecode keeps an assignment's value in
  a register around the store and ignores the IC's result (bytecode-generator.cc:5164-5181).
- SpiderMonkey (firefox bf75471716c5) attaches CallScriptedSetter after the data-slot stubs
  (CacheIR.cpp:4423-4426, 4776-4815), guarding the receiver's, prototypes' and holder's shapes and
  the GetterSetter (:1089-1127), only for a function setter (:4700-4745). The stub pushes V, the
  receiver as `this` and the callee and calls with argc 1, the result dropped
  (BaselineCacheIRCompiler.cpp:1530-1607); baseline code keeps the right-hand side on its stack
  under the IC call (BaselineCodeGen.cpp:3854-3868).
- JavaScriptCore (WebKit 148206b06ccb) runs one global setter-handler thunk for every site
  (InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:5977-6002): after the handler's structure check it reads the live setter
  from the GetterSetter at the holder's offset, substitutes the strict null setter (which throws)
  in strict code and calls the no-op one in sloppy code, and calls with two parameters (this, V)
  through the handler's own call link info (:5909-5970).
- Taken here: the proof and the call in shared code with per-site data (all three); the live
  [[Set]] read from the pair (V8's own-accessor handler, JSC); a per-site call cache (JSC's
  per-handler CallLinkInfo); V kept on the operand stack below the call (SpiderMonkey's baseline
  stack, V8's register); a missing setter to the helper (V8, SpiderMonkey).

Then item 4's rest (37b61a41, 2026-10-29, an agent's; landed on targeted tests under the owner's
rule): borrowed frames (loop fragments, OSR'd script loops, generator and async slices) take the
direct sequences. `compile_entry` cleared the direct-call bit for them, a restriction from when a
callee shared its caller's context; since item 1 the callee runs on its own pooled record. Calls,
constructions, getters and setters now take their sequences there; the resume point and the
borrowed handlers live in the frame's own record, a callee's throw reaches the VM's handler
routing as any helper's does, and `ret` is always empty at such a call. All three exemplars call
ordinarily from a frame that entered faster code mid-run (V8 converts the interpreter frame in
place, builtins-arm64.cc:5747-5750; SpiderMonkey keeps its BaselineFrame, BaselineFrame.h:212-220;
JSC's loop_osr keeps its call frame, LLIntSlowPaths.cpp:491-528). CALLSTAT's slow calls in
borrowed frames: Djot 7,341 (its parse loop) and chai 199 to none. Per call: a fragment's direct
call 686 to 298, its method call 788 to 416, getter 1,192 to 428, setter 1,106 to 476; an OSR'd
loop's direct call 798 to 396. Fixtures against 3b04fb1a: Djot -0.45%, React and chai flat, Vue
+0.09% (its 386K native calls in borrowed frames now pass the direct sequence's first gate before

fragments, generator slices, handlers, `ret` through finally, constructs, accessors, stack
overflow, collections, re-entry; 9 of 10 fail with the restriction restored), targeted filters,

Item 7 is parked (2026-10-29) on branch p2-w3-i4 (fae31a32, fbb8f73e, marked WIP), with
target/p2/w3-i4/RESUME.txt. Census: sites with live evictions, none on Vue and React, five on Djot,
nine on chai (one with 4,052 evictions over 403 functions), so a megamorphic bit saves at most
about 0.05%. Builtin ids recorded with the class in one lookup measured Vue +0.24%, not yet
explained (suspect: Reflect.apply's target check taking the table's lock on every call); the
megamorphic bit (no eviction after 4, as JSC's limit of 8 callees) has a flaw found but not fixed:
the call-cache epoch is global, so its bumps stale the overflow entries and a stable site counts
as megamorphic (fix: count evictions per epoch). The call count is designed, not built.

Resumed and landed 2026-11-01 (afe58a1e, e12d08ba, 140d2d83, an agent's; on targeted tests under
the owner's rule). A measurement finding first: the collector scans the native stack
conservatively, so two binaries retain different dead objects and Vue's count moves by up to
about 0.4% between any two builds for that alone (address-keyed tables collide differently too);
target/p2/w3-i4/nursery.sh counts a fixture at ten nursery sizes and compares the means, which a
commit's own cost shifts uniformly and that luck scatters. Vue's +0.24% at builtin ids was that.
- afe58a1e: every native gets a builtin id, recorded with its class in the call cache; the one
  real per-call cost, Reflect.apply's lock and lookup (+38 instructions with Math.max as the
  target), takes a one-entry thread-local memo. Neutral (nursery means: Vue -0.03%, React
  +0.01%, Djot -0.06%, chai 0.00%).
- e12d08ba: the Proxy trap caches' first construction is out of line, which had given their
  accessor a ~10 KB stack frame set up on every trap call: a Proxy get 2,320 to 2,270
  instructions an iteration; Vue -0.54% (the same 10.7M fewer at every nursery size), chai
  -0.12%.
- 140d2d83: a call site is megamorphic after eight callees evicted within one epoch, and stays
  so; such a site adds at most 64 callees an epoch to the overflow table (a budget of 16 cost
  Djot +0.17%: its busiest polymorphic site calls 28 functions). SpiderMonkey's megamorphic IC
  stops attaching stubs at its limit (ICState.h:24-29), JSC calls virtually past 8 callees
  (Repatch.cpp:171-184), V8 caches nothing at a megamorphic site; TRust's call without an entry
  pays all its guards, so a budget beats none. chai -0.99% (its churning sites, one calling 403
  functions, stop pinning every new callee in their chunk: weak-cell time 145 to 45 ms over
  1,501 minor collections, 5 major collections to 4); the fixtures within 0.06%.
Parked for the owner (Decision needed): e854636e, item 7's per-way call count (a u32 at CallIc
offset 60; 3 instructions a counted call in the direct sequence, the call-hit helper, the cached
Rust call, the construct helper and fills). React +0.30% (nursery +0.27%), Djot +0.08%
(+0.13%), chai +0.16%, Vue +0.13% (nursery). V8 counts every call per site
(code-stub-assembler.cc:199-211) and SpiderMonkey every baseline stub entry
(BaselineCacheIRCompiler.cpp:233-236), JSC per callee in polymorphic stubs only
(ThunkGenerators.cpp:363-364); in all three an optimizing tier reads the counts, and TRust has
none yet. Recommendation: land it with the first reader of the counts (P3's tier). Unexplained:
Reflect.apply's fast path costs 20-24 more instructions with the count, not 3.

### W3 item 3: the closure entry (census and design, 2026-10-28; decided, Decisions 20 and 21)

Item 3, as the stage's design has it: a callee that needs an environment builds it in its prologue,
as V8's do; bindings become 8-byte slots; such callees and `arguments.length`-only or
lazy-`arguments` bodies take the direct sequence. This note sizes that, and records the design
the owner decided on its questions (Decisions 20 and 21).

Method. The census was measured on 20d78544 (76b05c41's code), and the citations are to its tree.
Main is now 6e3c3ec8, whose code differs by Decision 11's safepoint poll (d4c51376..d14387e3):
its counts, re-measured by the same method on both architectures, follow the census where they
moved, and the predicted gains are against them. A fat-LTO engine from a `git archive` export
into a fresh target dir (rustc 1.99.1), on Crystal: `perf stat -e instructions:u`, perf and the
engine pinned to the A725 cores (`taskset -c 10-13`), `TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0`, `--tier=jit`.
Per-call costs by the W3 census's method (a loop in a function, warmed, at 200,000 and 1,200,000
iterations; the engine's 14-instruction loop removed, Sparkplug-only Node's 99), with the census's
cases and eleven more (micro.py); every output agrees with Node's, and repeat runs agree within 3
instructions an iteration. Profiles: `TRUST_JS_PERF_MAP=1`, `perf record -e instructions:u
-c 400000 --call-graph fp --user-callchains -m 16` on 6M-iteration copies of the cases and on 5-10
runs of each fixture (Web Tooling's chai, target/p2/ext/cli-chai.js, once at -c 2000000), none
throttled; split.py and fixenv.py classify the samples (LTO inlines `call_jit_env_committed` into
`call_jit_committed` and `ActivationPlan::instantiate` into `make_run_env`). A census build of the
same export (census/instrument.py: counters printed at exit under TRUST_JS_ENV_CENSUS=1; release
without LTO; counts only) gives activations by shape, the arguments slot's modes, arguments
objects, prepared entries and derived constructions. x86-64: the same commits built fat-LTO on
zombie, pinned to CPU 2, median of 3 (counts and fixture profiles; zombie has no Node). All of it
is in target/p2/w3-item3. Exemplars: V8 at c8b709085e21 (target/p2/w3-design/v8-src, paths from
its root; the files missing there fetched at that commit), SpiderMonkey at mozilla-firefox/firefox
6bb811006a50 (paths under js/src, js/public named in full) and JavaScriptCore at WebKit/WebKit
260278048486 (paths under Source/JavaScriptCore), both main on 2026-10-28. ECMA-262 at e28783d5:
spec.html is /big/web-standards/repositories/tc39/ecma262/spec.html.

Census

Per call (instructions; Crystal, and the engine on x86-64):

| case | the callee | ARM64 | Sparkplug-only Node | x86-64 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| direct-call | `add(s, k)` | 271 | 131 | 967 |
| closure-call | a closure reading one captured binding | 308 | 129 | 995 |
| env-call | one captured `var` (`() => t` on a path not taken) | 1,044 | 236 | 1,370 |
| env-param-call | one captured parameter | 1,047 | 234 | 1,366 |
| env-let-call | one captured `let` | 1,328 | 239 | 1,667 |
| env3-call | a captured parameter, `var` and `let` | 1,807 | 297 | 2,361 |
| env6-call | six captured bindings | 2,580 | 360 | 3,152 |
| env-this-call | a method whose arrow captures `this` | 1,029 | 327 | 1,601 |
| env-closure-call | makes that arrow and calls it | 2,083 | 420 | 2,978 |
| arguments-length | reads only `arguments.length` (chai's `flag`) | 760 | 559 | 845 |
| arguments-call | `arguments.length + arguments[0]`, no parameters (the call table's case) | 1,702 | 416 | 2,316 |
| arguments-strict-call | the same, strict, two parameters | 1,703 | 396 | 2,314 |
| arguments-mapped-call | the same, sloppy, two parameters | 10,284 | 595 | 10,242 |
| arguments-apply-call | `inner.apply(this, arguments)` | 3,670 | 655 | 4,634 |
| rest-call | `function rest(...xs) { return xs.length + xs[0]; }` (the call table's) | 1,294 | 415 | 2,117 |
| class-construct | a base class, no fields (the construct sequence) | 607 | 335 | 1,058 |
| field-construct | a base class with one field | 3,477 | 564 | 4,154 |
| derived-default-construct | `class D extends B {}` | 2,913 | 569 | 3,502 |
| derived-construct | `constructor(x) { super(x); }` | 8,555 | 562 | 9,414 |

Where env-call's instructions go, against closure-call's (split.py; per iteration, the
14-instruction loop in the first row):

| part | env-call | closure-call |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the caller's code: the loop and call template (closure-call: with the in-line direct sequence and finish stub) | 151 | 240 |
| the callee's code | 72 | 72 |
| shared stubs (the read of the callee's name) | 26 | 11 |
| the call-hit helper (`jit_call_hit`) | 185 | 0 |
| the Rust entry (`call_jit_committed` holding `call_jit_env_committed`, `run_moved_inner`, `JitFrame::release`, `run_pending_tail`, the record's `NativeActivation` drop) | 331 | 0 |
| the activation (`make_run_env` with `instantiate` 214, the cell's allocation 45, copying the plan's bindings 21) | 280 | 0 |
| collections | 12 | 0 |
| total | 1,058 | 322 |

Of the 736 instructions env-call spends beyond a closure call, the Rust entry that stands in for
the direct sequence takes 516 and the activation 292 (with its collection); the caller's code is
89 shorter without the in-line sequence. Sparkplug-only Node spends 107 more on env-call than on
its closure call: a builtin call that allocates and fills the context (below). The other shapes:
- A captured `let` (env-let-call, 284 more than env-call): `instantiate` records the name in the
  record's `lexical_names` (bytecode_activation.rs:185-188), a storage block (52) and an atom push
  (27) per activation, for EvalDeclarationInstantiation's var/let conflict walk (spec.html:30558),
  which no compiled entry can reach: a body with direct eval is a prepared entry. And
  `StoreCapInit` has no template on either backend (jit_regions.rs:3924-3944 and
  jit_x64_names.rs:561-566 serve LoadCap and StoreCap only), so the declaration takes the generic
  op helper (`jit_exec`, 89).
- More bindings (env3-call 763 more, env6-call 1,536): past the first, values spill into a storage
  block (102), which grows (40), and the callee reads its own captured bindings through the name
  stub (113 for env3's three reads).
- `arguments.length` alone: the arguments slot holds argc (`Chunk::arguments_count`,
  bytecode.rs:2637-2639, proven by `lazy_arguments_rewrite`, 8908-8941), so nothing is built, but
  `needs_env` counts the slot (3638-3640): the call takes the helper and the Rust entry, 498 of
  its 760.
- `arguments[i]` (arguments-call): a plain Array of the arguments stands in for the object
  (`lazy_arguments`, 2632-2636), 503 to make (`make_array` 212, `Object::new_with_parts` 164, a
  storage block 75, `arguments_slot_value` 52); `arguments[0]` takes the generic op helper
  (`jit_exec` 125), `lazy_arguments_element` (44) and `fast_get_elem` (39); the entry 557.
- rest-call: rest callees are kept off the sequence (bytecode.rs:20905); the Rust entry (488)
  builds the Array (jit.rs:19290-19296; about 390: `make_array_from_raw` 149,
  `Object::new_with_parts` 135, a storage block 46, the array's length layout and shape 61).
- arguments-mapped-call: a sloppy body with parameters that reads `arguments[i]` fails the lean
  compilation ("mapped arguments observed beyond length", bytecode.rs:7762-7766) and runs as a
  prepared entry: the general FunctionDeclarationInstantiation in Rust, by name (`call_user_inner`
  418, `VarMap::insert` and `share_layout` 439, text-keyed lookups 324, `bind_pattern` and
  `pattern_idents` 177), the mapped object (`make_arguments_object` 518, `mapped_arg_name` and
  `_value` 317) and its address-keyed side-table entry (`gc_pin` 241, the set's insert and rehash
  501, `gc_evict_dead_owners` 229), allocation (`__rust_alloc` 428, storage blocks 571,
  `finish_grow` 166), then `jit::run` (214).
- derived-construct: the derived constructor's Function Environment Record is made by name
  (`%newtarget%`, `this`, `%supercallok%`, `%superpropok%`, interpreter.rs:13446-13474;
  `VarMap::insert` 266, `Names::slot_of_text` 498, `AtomTable::recent` 302), its body runs on the
  bytecode VM by design (13483-13485: `run_vm_inner` 724, `run_inner` 239, `drive_vm` 236), then
  `super()` and the base construction (`prepare_super_call` and `finish_super_call` 227,
  `learned_construct_layout` 205, `init_instance_fields` 229, `Object::new_with_parts` 252,
  `construct_dispatch` 118, `observe_construct_capacity` 105).

The code.
- `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV` (bytecode.rs:1012-1016) is set at fill for every chunk whose
  `jit_no_activation()` is false (interpreter.rs:15215, 15350; bytecode.rs:20839-20841): an
  activation (`makes_env`: captured bindings, a captured `this` or `arguments`) or an arguments
  slot (bytecode.rs:3632-3640); prepared entries are never cached (interpreter.rs:15343-15344).
  An entry with the bit clears bits 0-3, so the ARM64 sequence's first gate routes it to
  `jit_call_hit`, then `call_jit_env_committed` (interpreter.rs:13842) and `run_moved_env`
  (jit.rs:19201-19234): `make_run_env` (bytecode.rs:3654-3750) runs the chunk's `ActivationPlan`
  (bytecode_activation.rs:24-212), whose `instantiate` collects the plan's 24-byte bindings into a
  vector (164), allocates the cell, seeds parameters, records lexical names, makes hoisted
  closures and a captured `arguments` object, and publishes the layout id (210);
  `arguments_slot_value` (bytecode.rs:20573-20586) fills the arguments slot; `run_moved_inner`
  (jit.rs:19246) then enters on a pooled record as the sequence does. On x86-64 every call takes
  `H_CALL` (jit_x64.rs:1220-1224), and environment callees the same Rust entry.
- The prepared entry (bytecode.rs:2622-2625): `compile` (6816-6826) retries a body the lean
  compilation refuses (direct eval, `with`, Annex B or unanalyzable patterns in `CaptureScan`,
  7147-7166; sloppy `arguments` with non-simple parameters, 7131-7140; mapped arguments beyond
  `length`) as a chunk whose caller completes FunctionDeclarationInstantiation in Rust
  (interpreter.rs:15796-15840) and passes the instantiated record, which `make_run_env` returns
  as it is (bytecode.rs:3655-3657).
- Derived constructors compile against a record their caller makes (bytecode.rs:6828-6838;
  interpreter.rs:13439-13510). The construct record (above) lists them, classes with instance
  elements and constructors outside the sequence's gates (an activation among them) as the
  helper's cases.
- The representation (W2b): an environment is a `RefCell<Scope>` (interpreter.rs:2584-2608) in
  the 96-byte class (2669): a `VarMap` (1520-1528: a names word; `BindingValues`, the first
  binding in place and the rest in a storage block, 1962-1964; a generation and a layout id), the
  parent, a `with` object word, three flags and `lexical_names` (2677). Names live apart from
  values: a kept `ScopeDesc` (1586-1594), the record's own list (1705-1710) or one atom (1736). A
  `Binding` (2723-2744) is 24 bytes: the value, `mutable`, `initialized` (TDZ), `import_ref`,
  `imported`, `deletable`, `strict_immutable`.
- Readers: the callee's own captured bindings go through per-chunk cap caches (bytecode.rs:
  20346-20403: a layout-id mode that derives the slot, or a pinned pointer and its generation) and
  their templates (above); free names through `NameIc` (bytecode.rs:239-259) and the shared name
  stubs (jit.rs:12272-12420; deep chains, jit_names.rs:50-160), which check each record's `with`
  word and generation or layout id, the slot's bound, then the binding's `initialized` byte (for
  imports and stores also `imported` and `mutable`), at stride 24 (value.rs:1452-1464,
  1889-1890; x86-64 jit_x64_names.rs:57-62, 311-317). The tree-walker and the VM use `Binding`'s
  fields directly at about 400 sites (`Binding::data` 115, `initialized` 86, `vars.get` and
  `get_mut` 81, `vars.insert` 43, `import_ref` 32, `mutable` 31, `strict_immutable` 10,
  `deletable` 9).
- Tracing: `Scope::trace_fields` (interpreter.rs:2644-2664) reports the storage blocks, the
  lexical names, each binding's value and import, the parent and the `with` word. A record's
  `env_raw` is not traced (jit.rs:1912-1916): its owners are the entry's Rust frame, the callee
  object a direct call leaves on the caller's stack, or a `NativeActivation`, whose base is
  `env_raw` (bytecode.rs:21520-21536).
- Snapshots encode parser output only (snapshot.rs:8-11), and neither the embed API nor the
  downstream crates name an environment type: item 3 touches neither.

Main against 20d78544 (per call, both fat LTO, the same method; the census's counts of calls,
activations and arguments objects do not depend on the poll):

Every call lost the entry poll's 18 instructions on ARM64 (7 on x86-64), and every allocation
gained 3 (the allocator's ceiling compare). The fixtures (pinned instructions, main against
20d78544): Vue 2.255e9 (-0.26%), React 4.644e8 (-1.67%), Djot 7.681e8 (-0.55%), chai 1.0617e11
(-0.29%); x86-64 Vue +0.06%, React -0.35%, Djot +0.06%. The shares below are 20d78544's.

No fixture constructs through a compiled derived constructor; Djot initializes instance fields 55
times. Shares of each workload's instructions (fixenv.py: a sample counts when its leaf, past
generic utilities, is environment work; the owner is the nearest frame that names one):

| % | activations (`make_run_env`) | arguments objects and the mapped side table | general instantiation | block records | name reads in Rust | name-load stubs | Rust frame entries (all) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 0.55 | 0.49 | 0.07 | 0.13 | 7.07 | 8.29 | 2.33 |
