| Djot | 0.07 | 0.15 | 0.63 | 0.23 | 2.02 | 2.60 | 0.66 |
| chai | 1.25 | 0.92 | 0.66 | 0 | 3.33 | 3.01 | 1.55 |
| Vue, x86-64 | 0.11 | 0.34 | 0.04 | 0.05 | 4.24 | (in line) | 3.95 |
| React, x86-64 | 0.03 | 0.06 | 0.01 | 0 | 1.18 | (in line) | 8.14 |
| Djot, x86-64 | 0.05 | 0.09 | 0.21 | 0.11 | 0.98 | (in line) | 3.20 |

- The environment callees are few on the fixtures and many on chai. Vue's are one function
  (`trigger`, vue.js:4:16562, whose `let u` an arrow on a rare path captures), React's are entered
  from Rust (native callbacks), Djot's 208 through the helper. chai has 2.73M bodies that read only
  `arguments.length` (chai's `flag(obj, key, value)`), 3.0M activations, mostly one captured
  parameter, mostly entered from natives' callbacks, and 95,504 calls of chai's
  `assert(expr, msg, negateMsg, expected, _actual, showDiff)`, which passes `arguments` on and so
  runs as a prepared entry with a mapped object.
- The arguments column on Vue and chai is mostly not objects being made: the generic property
  paths probe the mapped-arguments side table by address on every read and write that reaches
  them (interpreter.rs:10146-10150, 10632-10652): Vue 0.36%, chai 0.52%.
- Name reads dominate the environment work (Vue 15.4%, React 7.9%, Djot 4.6%, chai 6.3%): the
  stubs' guarded chain walks and, in Rust, `try_binding_getter` (Vue 3.2%), `get_from_chain`,
  `AtomTable::recent` (the tree-walker's text-to-atom lookups, 0.3-1.4%) and `name_ic_fill`
  (chai 1.0%). Item 3 changes their binding stride and checks only (below); resolving captured
  names statically, as the exemplars do, is Decision 21's new item.

What V8, SpiderMonkey and JSC do (baseline tiers: Sparkplug, Baseline, the Baseline JIT):

| | V8 | SpiderMonkey | JSC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| who needs a record | scope analysis: `NeedsContext()` is `num_heap_slots() > 0` (src/ast/scopes.h:456) | `needsFunctionEnvironmentObjects` (frontend/Parser.cpp:2072-2074; frontend/SharedContext.h:514) | `needsActivation()`: captured variables, eval or with (parser/Nodes.h:1951) |
| who makes it | the callee's first bytecode, `CreateFunctionContext` (src/interpreter/bytecode-generator.cc:1728-1733, 8726-8741); Sparkplug calls the builtin `FastNewFunctionContextFunction` (src/baseline/baseline-compiler.cc:2587-2593); Ignition's handler, Maglev and TurboFan allocate in line | the callee's prologue: Baseline allocates in line from a per-script template, calling the VM only when that fails (jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1507-1606; jit/JitScript.cpp:616); the Baseline Interpreter calls the VM | `op_create_lexical_environment` at entry (bytecompiler/BytecodeGenerator.cpp:1444-1446); the Baseline JIT calls an operation (jit/JITOpcodes.cpp:1861); the DFG and FTL allocate in line |
| what the allocation writes | map, length, ScopeInfo and previous, then every slot undefined (src/builtins/builtins-constructor-gen.cc:381-468) | the template's shape and slots, then the enclosing environment and the callee (BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1566-1581) | the scope and symbol table, then every slot the op's initial value (runtime/JSLexicalEnvironmentInlines.h:43-46) |
| layout | 4 tagged header words (map, length, ScopeInfo, previous; a fifth, the extension, after sloppy eval), then the slots (src/objects/contexts.h:548-570, 737-739, 756-758); 4-byte slots under pointer compression (src/common/globals.h:586-591) | a NativeObject with two reserved slots, enclosing environment and callee (vm/EnvironmentObject.h:535, 615, 624-625), 8-byte Values, 16 fixed slots then dynamic ones | JSCell, butterfly, next scope, symbol table (32 bytes), then 8-byte slots in line (runtime/JSLexicalEnvironment.h:72-91) |
| names and modes | ScopeInfo: per slot mode, init flag, maybe-assigned (src/objects/scope-info.tq:101-108) | a Shape built once per scope (vm/Scope.cpp:125-149) | a SymbolTable per function's code (runtime/SymbolTable.h:427) |
| captured parameters | copied by bytecode after creation, receiver first (bytecode-generator.cc:8749-8769) | copied by bytecode, GetFrameArg then SetAliasedVar (frontend/FunctionEmitter.cpp:371-439); the C++ copy loop went in 2023 (7e3cad0e) | `op_put_to_scope` per captured parameter (BytecodeGenerator.cpp:725-744) |
| TDZ | the hole, stored when the declaring scope is entered (bytecode-generator.cc:2163-2168); explicit checks | a zero-payload magic Value (js/public/Value.h:343-346); function-level lexicals form their own record, poisoned when made (vm/EnvironmentObject.cpp:1069-1072); a check once per basic block (frontend/BytecodeEmitter.cpp:1603-1633) | the empty JSValue (runtime/JSCJSValue.h:499-502); `op_check_tdz`, lifted after the declaration in the same function (BytecodeGenerator.cpp:3302-3313) |
| `arguments.length` alone | an object anyway (Ignition and Sparkplug have no special case) | `JSOp::ArgumentsLength`, no object (frontend/Parser.cpp:11092-11101) | `op_argument_count`, no object (bytecompiler/NodesCodegen.cpp:1101-1102) |
| `arguments` objects | eager in the prologue (bytecode-generator.cc:1778-1781); Sparkplug calls the FastNewSloppy/Strict/RestArguments builtins (baseline-compiler.cc:2622-2636), which allocate in line | eager, a VM call (BaselineCodeGen.cpp:6276); run-time laziness removed in 2021 (bug 1700443) | eager, a C++ operation (JITOpcodes.cpp:1864-1891); the FTL eliminates them (dfg/DFGArgumentsEliminationPhase.cpp) |
| mapped arguments and captured parameters | every parameter is forced into the context, and the map holds context slot numbers (src/ast/scopes.cc:2654-2690; src/objects/arguments.tq:29-33, upstream) | captured formals are forwarded to their CallObject slot (vm/ArgumentsObject.cpp:82-87) | ScopedArguments read the activation through a table (runtime/ScopedArguments.h:97-100); without a captured parameter, DirectArguments are the parameters' storage |
| rest | the FastNewRestArguments builtin | an IC whose fallback allocates in C++ (BaselineCodeGen.cpp:6284-6295) | `op_create_rest`, a slow path (jit/JIT.cpp:270) |
| sloppy eval | every binding of the scope and its parents in contexts; eval's vars in the extension slot's object; outer reads `LdaLookupContextSlot` with extension checks (src/ast/scopes.cc:2630; src/interpreter/interpreter-generator.cc:424-453) | every binding in environment objects; eval's vars as properties of the var object (vm/EnvironmentObject.cpp:4040); outer reads dynamic | every variable captured; eval's vars as properties of the activation; a var-injection watchpoint (interpreter/Interpreter.cpp:1501-1531) |
| `with` | a with context; names inside dynamic (bytecode-generator.cc:8784-8785) | WithEnvironmentObject; names dynamic; Warp declines the script | JSWithScope through a slow path; names dynamic |
| a derived constructor's `this` | the hole until `super()` returns, in a context slot when an arrow captures it (src/ast/scopes.cc:819-825; bytecode-generator.cc:7251) | the TDZ magic in `.this` (jit/BaselineCacheIRCompiler.cpp:3203-3204; frontend/BytecodeEmitter.cpp:2284-2327) | the empty value (BytecodeGenerator.cpp:922-923; NodesCodegen.cpp:1400-1411) |
| instance fields | an initializer function, called at a base constructor's start or after `super()` (bytecode-generator.cc:1827-1830, 7285-7288) | `.initializers`, called likewise (frontend/FunctionEmitter.cpp:356-358; BytecodeEmitter.cpp:11151-11184) | the field initializer, called likewise (BytecodeGenerator.cpp:916-920, 3256-3259) |
| the argument count | passed in a register on every call (InvokeFunctionCode, src/codegen/arm64/macro-assembler-arm64.cc:2884-2916; Sparkplug's prologue takes it, src/codegen/interface-descriptors-inl.h:462-470) | in every frame, whence `JSOp::ArgumentsLength` loads it (BaselineCodeGen.cpp:4701) | in every frame header, whence `op_argument_count` reads it (jit/JITOpcodes.cpp:2002-2010) |

Three points carry over to TRust. Every engine makes the record in the callee, from a static
descriptor: Sparkplug and JSC's Baseline JIT by a call, SpiderMonkey's Baseline in line since 2023
(6f790d09: most of Speedometer's calls to the VM's InitFunctionEnvironmentObjects came from
Baseline code; a micro-benchmark went from 55 to 23 ms with `--no-ion`). So any caller reaches it
through its ordinary call. TDZ is a word no language value can be, never a flag. And no baseline
tier elides an arguments object beyond a lone `arguments.length` (SpiderMonkey and JSC; V8 not
even that): their optimizing tiers do the rest (TurboFan reads elements from the frame, JSC's FTL
eliminates the objects), and TRust JS has one native tier, so it does that work there (20e).

Design (Decisions 20 and 21)

The environment record. A binding is one 8-byte word in its record: its value, or `Value::Empty`
while a `let`, `const`, `class` or a derived constructor's `this` is uninitialized (the word local
slots already use for TDZ, bytecode.rs:16513). The static attributes (mutable, strict, lexical at
this level, deletable, import) live with the names: a kept descriptor's per-name byte, the
record's own list beside its atoms, or two spare alignment bits of the one-name word (immutable,
strict; a single deletable or imported binding takes an own list). An import keeps its
(environment, name) in its record's own list, its slot holding the empty word. A descriptor's
address is the layout identity name caches compare (kept for its heap's life, so never reused
under a cache), which retires `layout_id`; `lexical_names` goes, its use being the lexical
attribute. Every binding is in line, in a cell its descriptor sizes (20b), as V8's contexts,
SpiderMonkey's CallObjects and JSC's lexical environments are:

| bytes | field |
| --- | --- |
| 0-7 | the RefCell's borrow flag |
| 8-15 | names: a kept descriptor, the record's own list, or one name with its attributes (tagged) |
| 16-23 | parent |
| 24-31 | the `with` object word (empty unless a `with` record) |
| 32-39 | slot count and capacity (0: the slots are in line) |
| 40-47 | generation (own lists' structural changes), `under_with`, `var_boundary`, `catch_param` |
| 48- | the slots, one word each |

One or two bindings take the 64-byte class (96 bytes today), three to six the 96-byte class (today
96 bytes and a block of 24 bytes a binding), seven to ten 128 bytes, up to 506 the 4,096-byte
class. A record that outgrows its cell (a sloppy eval's `var`, a tree-walked record's later
insert) moves its slots into a storage block and leaves its in-line words unused, as `InlineVec`
does today (inline_vec.rs:3-7); the collector reads the count from the header. chai's five-binding
activations (111,475) take one 96-byte cell where they take 96 bytes and a 120-byte block today.

Steps, each landing and gated on its own (the lib suite, pacing, GC stress on the environment,
closure, arguments, eval and construct tests, the fixtures and difftest as the base's, Test262
identical, zombie's x86-64 lib suite and clippy; the dumps differ only as each says):

1. The callee makes its environment and the arguments it needs, on today's representation (20a,
   20d, 20e); `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV` retires.
   - The count. Every ARM64 call site passes the argument count in w1, one `movz` (20d). The
     arguments are the caller's top argc words under x20 until it pops them (jit.rs:8504-8507,
     8678-8681), and every prologue already saves the caller's x20 (4080), so a prologue that needs
     the arguments finds them from that word. Rust entries pass the count flagged (bit 31) and the
     arguments' address as the code's second and third arguments; on x86-64, whose calls all enter
     from Rust, the prologue takes both from its argument registers. A prologue that needs them
     keeps them in two new words of its frame record.
   - The environment. The prologue of a non-resumable chunk that makes an activation calls a lean
     helper after its entry poll (jit.rs:4112-4114, the frame's first safepoint), as Sparkplug's
     first bytecode calls FastNewFunctionContext. The helper runs the chunk's plan with the
     closure's environment (`env_raw`) as parent, captured parameters copied from the arguments
     (20c), `this` from `this_word`, hoisted closures over the new environment, then sets
     `env_parent_raw = env_raw` and `env_raw` to it. It reaches no safepoint and collects nothing,
     so every word is initialized before the next safepoint; the frame record's trace reports
     `env_raw`, which the record now owns.
   - `arguments` and rest (20e). Where a compile-time proof shows that every use of `arguments` or
     of a rest parameter is a `length` or element read (`lazy_arguments_rewrite`'s,
     bytecode.rs:8908-8941, extended to rest and to sloppy bodies with parameters: never stored,
     passed, called, captured, written or deleted, in a body that is not resumable), no object or
     Array exists at run time. `length` is the count (for rest, the count less the formals, at
     least 0). `arguments[k]`, k an integer index below the count, reads formal k's slot when k is
     a formal's index: in sloppy code that is the mapped alias (MakeArgGetter, spec.html:
     15130-15148), and in strict code it holds the argument while the body never assigns that
     formal (otherwise the k-th argument word). Past the formals it reads the k-th argument word;
     `rest[k]` reads argument word n_params + k. An index at or past the count is undefined while
     the no-elements protector holds (interpreter.rs:3658-3665: `Array.prototype` and
     `Object.prototype`, where the [[Get]] would go, have no index keys). Any other key, or a lapsed
     protector, materializes the exact object this body would have made into its slot, and every
     later read takes it. A sloppy body with parameters whose `arguments` the proof covers leaves
     the prepared entry. Elsewhere the prologue fills the slot as the Rust entries do today: a count
     body stores the count as a Number (three instructions); an object body (unmapped, or sloppy
     without parameters) and a rest parameter the proof does not cover get theirs from the helper,
     made from the arguments.
   - What goes. The Rust entries (`jit::run`, `run_moved_inner`, `run_moved_oversized`) stop
     building environments, rest Arrays and arguments values, and pass the count and arguments
     instead; `run_moved_env` and `call_jit_env_committed` go; `jit_direct_flags` stops refusing
     rest callees (bytecode.rs:20905). Prepared entries are never cached (interpreter.rs:
     15343-15344), so once activation and arguments-slot callees enter like any other nothing sets
     `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV`, and the bit goes. The ARM64 call and construct sequences admit these
     callees with no change but the count. The VM keeps building environments in Rust through the
     same plan, with its own access to the arguments; borrowed entries resume in a frame that has
     its environment. x86-64's prologue calls the same helper.
   Dumps: every ARM64 call site (the count), the prologues of chunks with an environment, an
   arguments mode or rest, the elided reads, the helper table, the frame record's two words.
2. Binding accessors. Every reader and writer of a binding's fields moves behind `VarMap` methods
   (value or TDZ, attributes, initialize, set), so that the representation changes in one place.
   No behaviour or dump changes.
3. Attributes with the names. `mutable`, `strict_immutable`, `deletable`, `imported` and the
   import's target leave `Binding` for the names, and `lexical_names` goes. Copy on write stays the
   rule: a sloppy eval's `var` (spec.html:30667) or a `delete` gives the record its own list,
   bumping the generation; a kept descriptor never changes. The name stubs check attributes when a
   cache fills (a store into an immutable binding, an import, keep the helper) and drop the
   `mutable` and `imported` loads from the hit path. Dumps: binding stride 16, the stubs' hit paths.
4. TDZ as the empty word, 8-byte bindings, the records above (20b). `initialized` goes: a read
   compares the word with the empty word (two or three instructions where a byte load and a test
   were); InitializeBinding stores the value; `typeof` of a binding in TDZ still throws. Records
   are allocated at their descriptor's size; the tree-walker's records, the VM's block records and
   module records take the same layout. Cached binding pointers (cap caches, `BindingGetterCache`,
   the name caches' pointer mode) become slot pointers, validated by generation, which a spill
   bumps. `trace_fields` reports the slots, in line or spilled, the own lists with their imports
   and the spill block. Dumps: the slot stride and offsets everywhere, the TDZ compare, the
   record's field offsets.
5. Fixed slots, generated stores, TDZ checks per basic block (20c, 20g). In a chunk without
   bridge-op state (`jit_needs_activation_state`, which block records, `with` or eval would need),
   the record's shape is fixed for the call: no direct eval can reach it (such bodies are prepared
   entries) and nested functions' evals hoist into their own records. So LoadCap, StoreCap,
   StoreCapInit (now with a template) and UpdateCap load and store at a fixed offset from
   `env_raw`, storing with the card mark (as `store_cap_ic` does, bytecode.rs:20437-20442), on both
   backends; the chunk's cap caches go. The helper only allocates and fills the header and template
   words, and the prologue's generated stores copy the captured parameters from their slots, as all
   three exemplars copy them. A read of the function's own captured lexical checks the empty word
   once in each basic block (SpiderMonkey's frontend/BytecodeEmitter.cpp:1603-1633); inner
   functions keep their checks. Dumps: the cap templates, the prologues.
6. Mapped arguments alias environment slots, and the side table goes (20f). A sloppy body with
   simple parameters whose `arguments` the proof does not cover, and since Decision 22 one it
   covers, keeps its parameters in its environment, and its arguments object holds that environment and, per mapped index, a slot
   number (V8's SloppyArgumentsElements, JSC's ScopedArguments): `arguments[i]` and the parameter
   share one word, as MakeArgGetter and MakeArgSetter alias them (spec.html:15130-15168), and a
   define or a delete unmaps the index (14960-14992, 15039). The map lives in the object (W2g's
   pattern): the address-keyed side table and its probe on the generic get and set paths
   (interpreter.rs:10146-10150, 10632-10652) go, and such bodies leave the prepared entry.
7. (W2e) In-line allocation of the record in the prologue: cursor take, header and template words,
   as SpiderMonkey's Baseline does.

Outside item 3. Derived constructors and instance elements become a series of their own, after
item 1b's full form puts new.target in the frame record (20h). The design drafted for them here
stands as its starting point: the derived record from a descriptor, `this` the empty word until
`super()` returns; the chunk as machine code (today it stays on the VM, interpreter.rs:
13483-13485); the construct sequence without allocating `this` (spec.html:14013-14014), its
finish per 14028-14033; `super(...)` through the construct sequence with the caller's new.target,
whose `prototype` the lean `this` allocation must read (19977-19982), then BindThisValue
(11277-11293) and InitializeInstanceElements (19988); a class's field definitions as an
initializer chunk called with the instance as `this`, as all three exemplars call an initializer
function. Captured names read by nested closures resolve statically, as (hops, slot) with guards
only under sloppy direct eval and `with`: a new item after the representation change
(Decision 21).

Predicted gains (against main 6e3c3ec8)

Per call (ARM64 unless marked; instructions, loop excluded):

| case | main | after step 1 | after steps 2-5 | after step 7 (W2e) | Sparkplug-only Node |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| env-call | 1,026 | ~600 | ~380 | ~320 | 236 |
| env-let-call | 1,313 | ~870 | ~380 | ~320 | 239 |
| env3-call | 1,794 | ~1,280 | ~400 | ~340 | 297 |
| env6-call | 2,572 | ~2,060 | ~440 | ~370 | 360 |
| env-this-call | 1,011 | ~600 | ~440 | ~380 | 327 |
| arguments-length | 742 | ~265 | ~265 | ~265 | 559 |
| arguments-call | 1,691 | ~280 | ~280 | ~280 | 416 |
| arguments-strict-call | 1,691 | ~285 | ~285 | ~285 | 396 |
| arguments-mapped-call | 10,300 | ~285 | ~285 | ~285 | 595 |
| rest-call | 1,282 | ~280 | ~280 | ~280 | 415 |
| env-call, x86-64 | 1,367 | ~1,320 | ~1,100 | | |
| arguments-call, x86-64 | 2,325 | ~1,050 | ~1,050 | | |
| rest-call, x86-64 | 2,126 | ~1,050 | ~1,050 | | |

The decisions' extra margin over the draft (which predicted against 20d78544): Q4's elision takes
rest-call to ~280 where the draft's helper-built Array left ~900, arguments-call to ~280 from ~330,
and puts the read-only sloppy body of arguments-mapped-call (10,300 today, a prepared entry) on the
same path; Q1's in-line records take env3-call to ~400 from ~520 and env6-call to ~440, the spill
block's 100-140 instructions gone; the poll series took 18 off every call. Per iteration against
Sparkplug-only Node, which the call table's times track (the W3 census): `arguments` and rest
0.57x after step 1 (the stage's exit: within 2x; main 3.31x and 2.52x), an environment callee
1.18x after step 5 (main 3.10x). Every other ARM64 call pays step 1's `movz`. Step 6 takes an
escaping mapped object (chai's `assert`) from about 10,000 instructions a call to ~1,200.

Fixtures (pinned instructions against main):

| | step 1 | steps 2-5 | total | step 6 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | -0.44% (23,732 entries at ~430, less the `movz`) | -0.43% (23,765 activations at ~410: the `let`'s block and `jit_exec`, the build, the cap caches) | -0.9% | -0.36% (the side table's probe) |
| React | +0.10% (the `movz` at each of its 448,177 direct calls, the W3 census's count) | -0.21% (4,480 activations at ~220) | -0.1% | 0 |
| Djot | ~0 | ~0 | ~0 | 0 |
| chai | -1.46% (2.73M count entries at ~475, 619,207 activations at ~430, less the `movz`) | -0.62% (3.0M activations at ~220) | -2.1% | -1.3% (95,504 prepared entries at ~8,500, 0.76%; the probe, 0.52%) |

Against the plan's prediction for item 3 (chai 1.3%, Vue 0.7%, React ~0.5%): chai gains more (its
count-mode bodies), React less (its activations come from Rust callers, whose entries stay). Not
counted: the name stubs' cheaper hit paths (steps 3-4), measured at those steps, and the
collector's work, which falls with the bytes (a one-binding record from 96 to 64 bytes; chai makes
3.0M records).

Risks

Semantic, with the clauses they touch (spec.html at e28783d5):
- FunctionDeclarationInstantiation (14247-14391): the prologue must make what it makes in the
  algorithm's order: parameter bindings (14302-14308), then the arguments object (14309-14321),
  then the parameters' values (14330; with duplicate names the last wins, as the plan's
  `slot(..., replace)` does), vars as undefined (14331-14341), lexicals uninitialized
  (14376-14383), hoisted functions last, closing over the new environment (14385-14388). A
  non-strict function's separate lexical record (14373-14374) is observable only through a direct
  eval's conflict walk (30558), which a compiled entry never runs; prepared entries keep the
  lexical attribute for it.
- TDZ (InitializeBinding 10828-10848, SetMutableBinding 10850-10886, GetBindingValue
  10887-10905): the empty word must never leave a slot as a value; every read path tests it (the
  stubs, the fixed slots, the tree-walker, `typeof`, reflection), and step 5's per-block elision
  holds only where the block's earlier check dominates the read; a derived constructor's `this` is
  the same word (BindThisValue 11277-11293).
- The elided objects (CreateUnmappedArgumentsObject 15059-15082, CreateMappedArgumentsObject
  15083-15168): the proof must see every use on the final operation stream; the argument words
  must stay unchanged for the call (the caller's operand stack under its top, the Rust entries'
  slices) and be read only while the frame runs, so a resumable body keeps its object; in strict
  code a formal the body assigns must read the argument, not its slot; an out-of-range read's
  undefined holds only under the protector; and a materialization must come before any author code
  (a getter on the prototype) can see the object, after which every read takes the object. Step 6
  must keep the map's aliasing exact through defineProperty, delete and freezing (14960-14992,
  15039-15058).
- Direct eval (PerformEval 30442-30535, EvalDeclarationInstantiation 30536-30671): bodies with
  direct eval stay prepared; their records take own lists with the deletable attribute; a deleted
  binding (DeleteBinding 10908) and SetMutableBinding's missing-binding step (10866-10870) must
  keep working once attributes leave `Binding`; a kept descriptor must never change in place, or
  every activation of the function would see another's eval.
- `with` (Object Environment Records, 10974-11040): unchanged; records under `with` keep the
  tree-walker, and the stubs keep testing each record's `with` word.
- OrdinaryCallBindThis (13887-13918) for a captured `this`: sloppy callees with non-object
  receivers keep the sequence's receiver gate (`CALL_IC_DIRECT_READY`).

Implementation:
- The collector: a prologue's environment is reachable only through the frame record's `env_raw`
  until a closure takes it, so the frame record's trace must report it (step 1), and the helper
  must reach no safepoint. Records sized by their descriptor (step 4): the trace and the marker
  read the count from the header, and a spill must set the count and capacity before the next
  safepoint.
- About 400 sites move in step 2; the tree-walker's records, module imports and the global
  declarative record are the cases with own lists.
- Cached binding pointers become slot pointers, which a spill moves: the generation must bump on
  every spill, as on every structural change today.
- Generators and async functions keep Rust-made records (their coroutine owns them); the prologue
  step is not emitted for resumable chunks.
- Every ARM64 call site grows by one instruction (20d).

Decided (Decision 20; the exemplars' answers kept)

Q1. The environment record keeps every binding in line, in a cell its descriptor sizes (20b), not
the draft's 64-byte record with two slots in place. [All three in line: V8's contexts,
SpiderMonkey's fixed slots (16, then dynamic), JSC's variables after a 32-byte header.]
Q2. Step 1 first, on today's representation; the representation change follows as its own series
(20a). [No exemplar bearing.]
Q3. The helper copies captured parameters during step 1, and generated stores take over as soon as
bindings are fixed slots: step 5, not W2e (20c). [All three copy in bytecode after creation;
SpiderMonkey removed its C++ copy loop in 2023 for that.]
Q4. `arguments` and rest arrays are elided where a compile-time proof shows every use is a length
or element read, with no run-time placeholder: a formal's index reads its parameter slot, an
out-of-range read goes through the prototype (20e, step 1). [Baselines elide only a lone
`arguments.length` (SpiderMonkey, JSC); TurboFan reads elements from the frame and JSC's FTL
eliminates Direct and Cloned arguments and CreateRest; SpiderMonkey removed run-time lazy arguments
in 2021. The exemplars leave this to their optimizing tiers; TRust JS has one native tier.]
Q5. Mapped arguments alias environment slots and the side table goes (20f, step 6). [V8 forces the
parameters into the context and maps context slots; JSC's ScopedArguments read the activation;
SpiderMonkey forwards captured formals to the CallObject.]
Q6. TDZ checks on a function's own captured lexicals are elided after the first in each basic
block; inner functions keep theirs (20g, step 5). [V8 elides per block by a bitmap; SpiderMonkey
checks once per basic block; JSC lifts the check after the declaration within the function and
keeps it in inner functions.]
Q7. Derived constructors and instance elements are a series of their own, after item 1b's full
form (20h). [All three: `this` is the TDZ word until `super()`, and fields run in an initializer
function.]
Q8. Captured names resolve statically, as (hops, slot) with guards only under sloppy direct eval and
`with`: a new item after the representation change (Decision 21). [All three resolve statically;
V8 falls back to `LdaLookupContextSlot` with extension checks, JSC to var-injection checks behind
a watchpoint, SpiderMonkey to dynamic name ops.]
Q9. Every ARM64 call site passes the argument count, one instruction (20d). [V8 passes it in a
register on every call; JSC and SpiderMonkey keep it in every frame.]

Step 1's record (afca0db6, b9241ae6, 2026-10-28, an agent's; Decisions 20a, 20d and 20e):
- afca0db6: a callee builds its activation, rest Array and arguments value in its prologue, as
  V8's CreateFunctionContext, SpiderMonkey's Baseline prologue and JSC's
  op_create_lexical_environment do. A helper (`bytecode::jit_frame_setup`), called after the
  entry poll, runs the chunk's plan, installs the activation and fills the slots; it runs no
  author code, and its call ends in the explicit poll. Every ARM64 call site passes the argument
  count in w1 (one `movz`), the arguments lying under the caller's operand top; a Rust entry
  passes the count with bit 31 set and the arguments' address (x86-64: the count and the
  address). A prologue that needs them keeps both in its record (`JitCtx::argc`, `JitCtx::args`),
  and the record's trace reports its environment. `CALL_IC_NEEDS_ENV`, `run_moved_env` and
  `call_jit_env_committed` go: environment, rest and arguments callees take the direct sequence.
- b9241ae6: Q4's proof (20e) for unmapped `arguments` (strict, or sloppy without formals) and for
  rest parameters. A body that reads them only by `length` and element makes neither in a fresh
  native frame and reads the caller's argument words instead (ArgLength, ArgElem, RestLength,
  RestElem, with templates on both backends). An index past the count is undefined while the
  no-elements protector holds; any other key materializes the exact object into the slot, which
  every later read takes. The bytecode VM, coroutines and borrowed frames keep the object or Array.

Per call against 6e3c3ec8 (ARM64, loop excluded): env-call 1,026 to 669 (predicted ~600; the
helper's ABI and its closing poll), env-this-call 1,011 to 670, env3-call 1,794 to 1,437,
arguments-length 742 to 283, arguments-call 1,690 to 302 and rest-call 1,282 to 302 (predicted
~280; Sparkplug-only Node 416 and 415), arguments-strict-call 1,692 to 316; other calls +1 (the
count). arguments-apply-call rose 42: its sloppy callee uses `this` with an undefined receiver, so
it still enters through the call helper, and now also calls the prologue's helper. Step 5's
generated stores remove that, and x86-64's +21 for environment callees, which enter from Rust and
now make their activation in a helper call of its own. x86-64 (zombie): arguments-call 2,325 to
963, rest-call 2,126 to 966, arguments-length 838 to 747, other calls -14.

Fixtures (ARM64): Vue -0.64% (its runs spread up to 0.25%; another session of main gives -0.71%),
React -0.08%, Djot +0.47%, chai -1.26%. x86-64: Vue -0.13%, React -0.31%, Djot -0.25%; its
bytecode VM +0.12% on Vue and React (the new ops share the existing arms), ARM64's unchanged.
Djot's rise is one major collection. Main ends with 21 minor collections and no major, 12 KB under
the 8 MiB budget after the 20th; there Part A is 6 KB over it (it keeps 1 to 21 more objects
alive at each minor collection, of about 27,700) and Part B 33 KB (it also promotes 42 KB more at
the 16th), so their next collection is major. Not a leak: the collector traces active frame
records only. With a 16 MiB floor Djot is -0.14%.

Validation: the ARM64 gate for each part (the lib suite; pacing with its known failure, and for
Part B two tests that failed at a load of about 13 and passed on re-run: the watchdog's burst test,
which saw two watchdog threads, and the test that a dropped realm which ran the last RegExp is
collected, at the tree-walking tier; GC stress on the elided-arguments, arguments, rest,
activation, call-guard, construct and thousands-of-formals tests; clippy in its five
configurations and the workspace; the downstream crates; the fixtures and difftest as the base's;
Test262 53,575 of 53,575, identical; the dumps differ by the count at 615 call sites, the new
prologue in five Djot chunks and the record's field offsets) and zombie's x86-64 lib suite (its
known host_buffer failure) and clippy, Windows clippy as its baseline.

Not done: sloppy bodies with formals, which Q4 put in step 1; arguments-mapped-call stays at
10,258. A formal's index could read its parameter slot, but a non-index key or a prototype getter
needs the exact mapped object, which must alias the parameters while the frame runs, and step 1
keeps them in frame slots: step 6 takes these bodies (Decision 22). Found on the way: the ARM64
encoders check their immediates in debug builds only, so a template that misses its range guard
mis-encodes silently in release. The first version of afca0db6 stored the count wrongly in a body
with 4,096 or more slots (`arguments.length` read undefined with 5,000 formals); it was fixed with
an indexed store and a test, and Decision 23 closes the class. And `perf stat ... taskset -c N
cmd`, taskset inside perf, can add a scaled count from the other core type's PMU (about 80M on
Djot): taskset goes outside perf.

Steps 2-5's record (1a15e2b4..3290f1f4, 2026-10-29, an agent's; Decisions 20b, 20c, 20g and 24):
- 1a15e2b4 (step 2): every reader and writer of a binding's fields goes through VarMap's views.
- c771a7bb, 92bfe73e (step 3): binding attributes live with the names and are checked when a cache
  fills, so the name stubs' hit paths lose the import byte's load and branch, and stores and
  updates the mutable byte's (Vue 138 `cbnz` fewer, Djot 578); an own list's attributes sit in its
  name words, one word an entry.
- 6ecba8ff (test only): the host buffer test scrubs the stack before its collection: a stale word
  under the conservative scan had kept its object, on zombie at main too, where it now passes.
- f7762afb (step 4, 20b): bindings in line in records their descriptors size, a 48-byte header and
  8-byte slots, TDZ as the empty word.
- 73667de9 (step 5, 20c and 20g): a fresh frame's LoadCap, StoreCap, StoreCapInit and UpdateCap at
  fixed offsets from its environment, stores with the card mark, the prologue's generated stores
  copying captured parameters and `this`, and TDZ checks once a basic block (a parameter's LoadCap
  from 12 instructions and three stub calls to 4). Not as designed: code entered from a borrowed
  frame (a function the tree-walker started, which a loop fragment continues) runs in the
  tree-walker's activation, whose slots lie elsewhere, so it keeps the validating cap caches, and
  so does the VM. All three exemplars share one environment layout across their tiers; ours
  belongs with Decision 21's static addressing, which needs it too.
- 3290f1f4 (step 4's follow-up): step 4 alone made a one-binding activation 77 instructions
  dearer (a run-time-sized memset to zero the cell, a memcpy of the template, the size class
  found on every allocation). The plan keeps its size class, the cell is not zeroed (every slot the
  record counts is written before the next safepoint, as V8 writes a fresh context once,
  src/heap/factory.cc:1647-1660), small templates are copied with stores, and construction is
  inlined.

Per call against 50530f50 (ARM64, loop excluded): env-call 669 to 542, env-let-call 956 to 546,
env3-call 1,436 to 594, env6-call 2,217 to 644, env-this-call 670 to 639; derived-construct -62;
arguments-mapped-call +128 (+1.2%), derived-default-construct +0.1% and arguments-apply-call +0.2%
(Decision 24). x86-64: env-call -125, env-let -420, env3 -848, env6 -1,585. Against the design:
the multi-binding drops as predicted (predicted 490, 880 and 1,620), the one-binding drop 127
against 220, and every level 160-200 above the predicted ~380-440: main after step 1 sat about 70
above its estimate, and making an activation still costs about 190 (the helper and its
allocation), which step 7 (W2e) removes. Fixtures (ARM64): Vue -0.60%, React -0.25%, chai -0.78%,
Djot -0.03% (predicted -0.43%, -0.21%, -0.62%, ~0); x86-64 Vue -0.41%, React -0.39%, Djot -0.28%.
Smaller records: React 18 minor collections to 17 (1.40 MB promoted to 1.31 MB); Djot's generated
code 0.9% smaller.

Validation: the ARM64 gate on d52094b2 (the lib suite, pacing with its known failure only; the
downstream crates; GC stress on 19 filters, among them activation, binding layout, closure, TDZ,
arguments, construct, fixed activation, function tiering, OSR and borrowed parameters, and the
eval tests without the hour-long tail test; the fixtures and difftest as the base's; the dumps
differing as steps 3-5 say; Test262 identical) and zombie's lib suite and clippy; then the head
over W5b's 3d (3290f1f4): clippy, the lib suite (2,658, pacing as before), the fixtures,
difftest and the dumps as d52094b2's, Test262 identical, and zombie's lib suite (2,471, none
failing) and clippy.

Then step 6 (04fb922a, 2026-10-29, the same agent's; Decisions 20f and 22; landed on targeted
tests under the owner's rule):
- The ParameterMap lives in the object: the arguments exotic holds the Function Environment Record
  and a slot per mapped index (V8's SloppyArgumentsElements, JSC's ScopedArguments), and gets,
  sets, defines and deletes go through it. The address-keyed side table, its pins and its probes
  on the generic [[Get]] and [[Set]] paths go; a per-thread flag, set when the first mapped object
  is made, keeps the remaining probes (define, getOwnProperty, apply's list, prepared references)
  free until then, and the get path tests the exotic kind in the borrow it already takes (without
  both, Vue measured +0.36%).
- The tree-walker makes the formals' bindings before the arguments object that maps them
  (FunctionDeclarationInstantiation steps 21-22), so the map holds slots.
- A sloppy body with simple formals that reads `arguments` beyond `length` compiles without a
  prepared entry, every formal in its activation. Under the proof (Decision 22) no object is
  made: an index below the count that maps a formal reads its binding (at a fixed slot in a fresh
  frame's native code, on both backends), any other index the argument word, and a
  materialization makes the object over the activation; duplicate formals keep the last
  occurrence. Added, not designed: function OSR from a tree-walked call into such a body enters a
  prepared variant compiled on first need (`FunctionRuntime::osr_code`), since a parked AST frame
  cannot hand over a chunk that keeps its formals in the activation.

Against 6c1d62c4 (fat LTO, pinned, randomization off), per iteration: arguments-mapped-call
10,382 to 598 (Decision 24's regression gone), derived-construct -43, derived-default-construct
-41, field-construct -48 (the side table's probes and pins), arguments-apply-call +8.5 (+0.2%,
from code layout only; minor). Fixtures: Vue -0.26%, React -0.21%, Djot -0.09%, chai -2.01%,
collections unchanged. Tests: mapped_arguments_tests.rs (aliasing both ways, duplicates,
unmapping by define, delete and freeze, an arrow capturing `arguments`, apply over `arguments`,
a prototype getter that materializes, keeps and writes the object); the entry tests now expect
lean entries for mapped, duplicate, captured, interleaved, define and escaped bodies; the lib
suite (2,666, with step 7 over it); GC stress on the mapped-arguments, arguments-set and entry
tests; zombie's lib suite (2,477) and clippy.

Then step 7, W2e's first use (59196aea, 2026-10-29, the same agent's): a fresh frame makes its
activation in line. Where: the prologue of a chunk whose activation is only its record (fixed
slots, no hoisted function, no `arguments` object or rest Array to make,
`Chunk::jit_inline_activation`); every other chunk keeps the frame setup helper, which is also
this path's slow path. The allocation is W2e's machinery for any small cell of one descriptor and
size class: `gc_core::InlineAllocSite` fixes the heap's address, the cursor's index and the cell
size at compile time (chunks belong to one heap), and `emit_inline_alloc` (ARM64 and x86-64)
follows `Heap::alloc_small_uninit` and `Cursor::take`. Three guards branch to the slow path with
nothing changed: the heap allocates fast (not collecting, no stress interval, no collect-on-alloc),
the cursor's free word is not zero, and the bytes fit before `due_check_bytes`. Then about 30
instructions: charge the bytes, take and clear the lowest free bit, set the page's allocation bit,
bump the cursor's counters, and form the cell's address (the heap now keeps `cursor_base`, the
cursor vector's buffer, for generated code). The 48-byte header comes from compile-time words, the
outer record from the closure's environment, and the plan's template words are stored in runs from
one register; then the activation is installed, step 5's copies of captured parameters and `this`
run without card marks (the record is young and no safepoint has passed), and the explicit poll
follows: allocate, then poll. `TRUST_JS_JIT_INLINE_ALLOC=0` turns it off.

Grounding: SpiderMonkey's Baseline does this, making the CallObject in line from a per-script
template (js/src/jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:1506-1606, createGCObject at 1567-1571) and redoing the
whole job in the VM on failure (1589-1602), with the stack check after it as the poll (7059-7080);
its `freeListAllocate` (MacroAssembler.cpp:362-406) is the closest match to our cursor, and
`initGCSlots` (1035-1105) fills runs from one register. V8's Sparkplug calls FastNewFunctionContext
(baseline-compiler.cc:2587-2614), which allocates in new space in line and stores the header
without barriers (builtins-constructor-gen.cc:381-469). We keep SpiderMonkey's fallback shape (the
slow path does the whole job and rejoins), since the helper existed. One difference: neither
exemplar charges bytes per object (SpiderMonkey per arena, Allocator.cpp:483-504; V8 at each
refill of its allocation area, main-allocator.cc:279-294); ours stays exact per cell, so the
allocation that spends the budget makes the collection due. Charging at a cursor's refill would
change every allocation's accounting, so it belongs to W2e's general form.

Per iteration against 6c1d62c4 (ARM64, fat LTO, pinned, randomization off): env-call 555.9 to
367.0 (-189), env-let -190, env3 -189, env6 -204, env-this -193, env-closure -190,
arguments-mapped-call 598 to 407; the other cases unchanged. Per call, loop excluded, against the
design's figures after step 7: env-call 353 (~320), env-let 356 (~320), env3 405 (~340), env6 441
(~370), env-this 446 (~380); Sparkplug-only Node 236, 239, 297, 360 and 327. Fixtures, step 7
alone: Vue -0.08%, React -0.17%, Djot -0.01%, chai -0.58% (steps 6 and 7: -0.34%, -0.38%, -0.10%,
-2.59%); collections unchanged. x86-64 against 19fe9dc5: env-call -194 (1,070 a call, under the
~1,100 predicted after steps 2-5), the environment cases -192 to -198, arguments-mapped-call
-197, derived-construct +8 (+0.08%); fixtures within 0.06%. Tests: 250,000 activations made in
line across collections they make due, with closures keeping records alive, against Node; GC
stress on the fixed-activation and activation tests (under stress the heap counts each allocation,
so the fast path steps aside); `TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY`; the lib suite 2,666 on ARM64 and 2,478 on
x86-64, where the new test ran the in-line path; clippy on both. With step 7, W3 item 3 is done.

### Immediates out of an instruction's range (design and record, 2026-10-29; Decision 23)

The ARM64 encoders check their immediates with `debug_assert!` only, so a template that misses
its range guard mis-encodes silently in release builds: the field overflows into the opcode bits
(`ldr_imm`, `str_imm`, `add_imm`, `cmp_imm_w`, the pairs) or wraps (`ldur`, `stur`, `ldur_d`,
`stur_d`, `ldurb` and the D pairs mask their field without even a debug check). Step 1's first
version stored `arguments.length` wrongly in a body with 5,000 formals. Decision 23: the encoders
accept any offset, as the exemplars' macro-assemblers do. The owner's question: does that require
a permanently reserved scratch register across every template?

Method. Exemplars: V8 at c8b709085e21 (target/p2/w3-design/v8-src, paths from its root;
register-arm64.h fetched at that commit), SpiderMonkey at mozilla-firefox/firefox 6bb811006a50
(paths under js/src; MacroAssembler-vixl.cpp from the copy fetched for this note, main on
2026-10-29), JavaScriptCore at WebKit/WebKit 260278048486 (paths under Source/JavaScriptCore;
MacroAssemblerARM64.h from the copy fetched for this note). Encodings checked with llvm-mc 23
(`llvm-mc -triple=aarch64 -show-encoding`). The census is of main a187b1f8.

How the exemplars do it

V8. `MacroAssembler::LoadStoreMacro` (src/codegen/arm64/macro-assembler-arm64.cc:936-959) emits
the scaled immediate form, else the unscaled one, else `LoadStoreMacroComplex` (961-989), which
takes a register from a `UseScratchRegisterScope`, moves the whole offset into it and uses the
register-offset form; out-of-range pre- and post-index forms move the base with an `add` before or
after the access. Pairs (`LoadStorePairMacro`, 991-1028) add the offset to a scratch register.
`AddSubMacro` (839-885) moves an unencodable immediate into a scratch (`MoveImmediateForShiftedOp`,
789-837, pre-shifting it where that saves an instruction). The pool is `DefaultTmpList()`, ip0 and
ip1, that is x16 and x17 (macro-assembler-arm64.cc:41; an FP pool beside it), and
`UseScratchRegisterScope` (macro-assembler-arm64.h:2564-2651) lends and returns them, nesting by
snapshot: `AcquireNextAvailable` is a release `CHECK` that the pool is not empty (2644-2650).
x16 and x17 are outside the register allocators' set (src/codegen/arm64/register-arm64.h:32-46,
`ALLOCATABLE_GENERAL_REGISTERS`), and hand-written code that names them takes them out of the
pool first (`temps.Exclude(x17)` around `Call(x17)`, src/builtins/arm64/builtins-arm64.cc:1994-1999;
the warning at macro-assembler-arm64.h:621-649). Sparkplug, V8's template-like baseline compiler,
wraps the same scope and adds x14, x15 and x19 to it for baseline code
(src/baseline/arm64/baseline-assembler-arm64-inl.h:18-41); the RegExp macro-assembler's register
map leaves x16/x17 to the assembler ("Very volatile", src/regexp/arm64/
regexp-macro-assembler-arm64.cc:45).

SpiderMonkey. vixl's `MacroAssembler::LoadStoreMacro` (jit/arm64/vixl/MacroAssembler-vixl.cpp:
1217-1305) takes a scratch from its `UseScratchRegisterScope` and prefers adding the offset's high
bits to the base and encoding the low bits in the access (1260-1270), which avoids materializing
the whole offset. When the pool is empty it recycles: it pushes two volatile registers that alias
neither the base nor the transfer register, lends them to the scope, adds 16 to an SP-based
offset, and pops them after the access (1241-1253, 1277-1280). Pairs add the offset to a scratch
(`LoadStorePairMacro`, 1316-1352), out-of-range pre- and post-index forms move the base, and
`AddSubMacro` (1069-1114) is V8's. The pool is ip0 and ip1 (MacroAssembler-vixl.cpp:36-40,
`tmp_list_(ip0, ip1)`, `fptmp_list_(d31)`), which `NonAllocatableMask` keeps from the register
allocator (jit/arm64/Architecture-arm64.h:196-201); `AcquireNextAvailable` is a `VIXL_CHECK`
(MacroAssembler-vixl.cpp:2082-2088).

JSC. `load64(Address, RegisterID)` (assembler/MacroAssemblerARM64.h:1849-1856) tries
`tryLoadWithOffset` (7949-7959: the unscaled form, then the scaled one), else sign-extends the
offset into `memoryTempRegister` and uses the register-offset form; stores are the same
(`store64`, 2383-2390), and `add64` with an unencodable immediate goes through
`dataTempRegister` (326-342). The two are x16 and x17 (56-57), reserved from the register
allocators (jit/GPRInfo.h:370-379, `reservedRegisters`); a region can forbid their use, and a
macro instruction that needs one there is a release assertion (`scratchRegister()` and the cached
temp getters, MacroAssemblerARM64.h:64-68 and 7635-7653). JSC caches memoryTempRegister's contents
to skip rematerializing an offset.

Why they do it, and whether the reason applies here. All three serve register allocators
(TurboFan, Maglev and Liftoff; Ion and Wasm's; DFG, FTL/B3 and the baseline JIT's register
assignment): any allocatable register may hold a live value at any instruction, so the only
register a macro instruction can always clobber is one the allocators never use. That is why the
scratch registers are reserved for good, and why hand-written code must take them through the
scope before naming them. TRust JS's ARM64 JIT has no allocator: templates name registers, and
every register has an owner somewhere. x0-x15 are temporaries and residents (the loop regions
allocate x0-x8 to resident values, `I_UNIVERSE`, jit.rs:16125); x16 and x17 are temporaries in
517 emitter calls in jit.rs and its siblings, the intra-procedure-call registers of the helper
calls (`ldr x16, [x21, #idx*8]; blr x16`), and the loop regions' first two receiver bases, live
across whole loop bodies of slot loads and stores (jit.rs:16655-16662); x19-x22 hold the context,
operand top, helper table and slot base; x23-x27 are the regions' pins and x28 their poll
countdown; x29/x30 the frame. No register is free for the encoders to own.

The answer to the owner's question

No. A permanently reserved register is what the exemplars need because an allocator may put a
live value in any other register; it is not what accepting any offset needs. Most out-of-range
forms need no scratch register at all:

- a load into a general register uses its destination as the address register, which the load
  overwrites anyway: `add x9, x22, #hi, lsl #12; ldr x9, [x9, #lo]` (SpiderMonkey's add-the-high-
  bits form, with the destination in the scratch's place);
- an add or subtract adds the high part shifted by 12 and then the low part, into its destination:
  `add x20, x20, #hi, lsl #12; add x20, x20, #lo`;
- a store, a SIMD&FP load or store, or a pair whose base is not SP and not a register it
  stores moves the base and moves it back: `add x22, x22, #hi, lsl #12; str x9, [x22, #lo];
  sub x22, x22, #hi, lsl #12` (V8 and SpiderMonkey move the base themselves in their out-of-range
  pre- and post-index forms; here the move is undone after the access);
- out-of-range pre- and post-index pairs move the base before or after the access, as V8's and
  SpiderMonkey's do.

What remains needs a register for a value with no destination to borrow: a compare (or flag-
setting add) whose immediate fits neither 12 bits nor 12 bits shifted, a store whose base is SP
or is the register it stores, and an add of 16 MB or more onto its own source. For those the
encoder recycles a register as SpiderMonkey's does when its pool is empty: it pushes x16 (or x17,
x15 or x14, the first the instruction does not name), uses it, and pops it, adding 16 to an
SP-based offset: `str x16, [sp, #-16]!; mov w16, #5000; cmp w1, w16; ldr x16, [sp], #16`.
The push and pop leave NZCV alone. It is safe everywhere the JIT emits code: every SP adjustment
in jit.rs and jit_regions.rs is a multiple of 16, so SP is always 16-byte aligned, and no
template keeps data below SP.

So no template's register convention changes, nothing is reserved, and no legalized form depends
on which registers a template holds: each is correct by construction, whatever is live. The cost
falls only on out-of-range operands, which ordinary code never has: every in-range operand
encodes exactly as today.

The design

Each encoder keeps its signature and, for an operand its own form encodes, its exact word, after
one mask test (`off & !(4095 * size) == 0` for a scaled access, a biased mask for imm7, one compare
for a 12-bit immediate), inlined at the call site, where it folds away for a constant operand; the
legalizing paths are out of line and cold. That keeps the compiler's own cost, which the fixture
counts include, to about 0.6 of an instruction per word emitted (see Measurement). Out of range, an
encoder legalizes by this ladder, taking the first rung that applies:

1. the instruction's own form (unchanged);
2. the other single-instruction form: scaled for an unscaled encoder whose offset is aligned and
   in the scaled range, unscaled (LDUR/STUR, -256..255) for a scaled encoder; for a compare or
   flag-setting add, the 12-bit immediate shifted by 12 when the low 12 bits are zero (identical
   flags);
3. no scratch: the destination as the address register (loads into a general register, LDP of
   general registers, ADDS/SUBS whose destination is not a source); the destination itself for
   ADD/SUB below 16 MB, through `#hi, lsl #12` then `#lo`; the base moved and restored (stores,
   SIMD&FP accesses and pairs whose base is not SP and not a stored register); the base moved
   before or after the access for pre- and post-index pairs;
4. a recycled register (push, use, pop), for what is left.

Offsets split as SpiderMonkey's do: the high part, a multiple of 4,096 below 16 MB, goes in an
`add` or `sub` with `lsl #12`, and the low part in the access's own field (scaled when aligned,
unscaled when the remainder is within -256..255); an offset that splits neither way is moved whole
into the address register and used through the register-offset form (into the recycled register
when the address register is the base itself).

Operands that are field widths, not offsets or values (shift amounts, bitfield positions, MOVZ and
MOVK's 16-bit lanes and their shifts, a logical immediate's prepared field), have no legal
meaning past their range: an out-of-range one is a template bug. Debug builds panic, as today;
release builds fail the compile instead of emitting a wrong word (the `ubfx` and
`orr_low_ones_x` encoders keep their release assertions and tests). A compare of SP with an
immediate past 12 bits and an add onto SP of 16 MB or more, which no template emits, fail the
compile in every build.

Failing a compile. The assembler records its first failure (`Asm::failed`); `checkpoint` and
`rewind` save and restore it, so a discarded optional region takes its failure with it. The
chunk compiler and the shared-stub assembler check it before finishing and return `None`, so the
function stays on the bytecode VM; `finish_with_offsets` asserts that nothing failed. The
conditional-branch fields checked after branch relaxation become a release assertion, like B's.

Fixed shapes. The JIT patches no code once installed. The sequences whose length other code
relies on are the prologue's first eight words and the epilogues' restore groups, which
`skip_unused_callee_saved_fp` rewrites in place; their operands are the frame constants, so
compile-time assertions pin them in range and no legalization can change their shape. The direct
call's position word follows branches only. The RegExp tier patches its branches, ADRs and literal
loads in `finish`, which already returns `None` when one does not reach.

The RegExp tier (regex_native_a64.rs, landed with W5b 3a). Its register map already reserves x16
and x17 as "the intra-procedure scratch", V8's convention, and its `add_any`, `cmp_any` and
`load_elem` legalize through x17. Its encoders are free functions returning a word; each field
past its range now returns UDF #0 (`UNENCODABLE`), which no instruction it emits ever is: `emit`
notes it and `finish` returns `None`, so the pattern stays on the matcher. Its slot loads and
stores (8 bytes a slot) legalize as the JIT's do, a load through its destination and a store
through x17 (no store's register or base is x17), and `retreat` goes through x17 past 12 bits, as
`advance` does through `add_any`. The single-bit operands (`orr_bit` and `tbnz` on LOGGED, below
64 when the plan is not wide) refuse the program past 63. Its program-level guard (MAX_SLOTS,
4,000, "AArch64's scaled 12-bit offsets reach 4,095") stays: it chooses the matcher for patterns
with that many captures, not a cheaper path, but lifting it changes which patterns run natively.
This design does not touch the code-size cap (MAX_CODE_UNITS: conditional branches without
veneers); the JIT's `relax_branches` is the shape to port when that is taken up.

Weighed and not chosen

- V8's scope over a small pool, with templates that use pool registers taking them through it.
  Reserving x16 and x17 means 517 emitter calls take them through scopes, and the loop regions'
  receiver pins (x16, x17 live across whole loop bodies) move to other registers or exclude the
  pool for the region. It is a permanent change to every template's register convention for a
  benefit the scratch-free forms give without it, and it would touch the cap templates the W3
  item 3 agent is changing. A template could also lend a register it knows is dead (V8's
  `Include`, Sparkplug's x14/x15/x19) to get two-instruction forms for stores; no template has an
  operand that reaches rung 3 in ordinary code, so nothing would use it today. When P3's
  allocating tier arrives, its allocator can exclude x16 and x17 and lend them to the encoders for
  its code: the ladder has a place for a lent register before rung 3.
- Forms with no scratch only, failing the compile where they do not apply. Simpler, but compares
  of variable immediates and SP-based stores would then fail where SpiderMonkey's recycling
  accepts them; recycling costs two instructions and two stack accesses only past the range.
- A debug checker that panics when an emitted instruction names a pool register the scope lent
  out, or one the template did not take. It turns the audit of 517 calls into a test-driven one,
  but it is the audit the pool creates: with no pool there is nothing to check, because no
  legalized form assumes anything about which registers are live. The tests check the legalized
  forms themselves instead: decoded words, and execution on Crystal.

Census (main a187b1f8)

Encoders with immediate fields: 44 of the 134 in `mod asm` (and the five patched branches), called
about 1,900 times from jit.rs, jit_operations.rs, jit_regions.rs, jit_names.rs,
jit_typed_array.rs, jit_exec_values.rs and jit_storage.rs (tests included). Their immediates come
from three places:

- struct and layout offsets (`offset_of!`, `JitLayout`, `InterpLayout`, the context constants),
  in range by construction; several carry compile-time assertions already (jit.rs:1861-1893);
- frame-slot, operand-stack and table offsets that grow with the body: slot*8 from x22, depths
  from x20, constant-pool and cache indices. Their templates guard them by hand: 16 range checks
  in jit_operations.rs (`(*slot as u32) * 8 + 8 < 4096` and `skip >= 4096`), 19 in
  jit_regions.rs, 3 in jit_names.rs, `fits_scaled` in jit_storage.rs, and in jit.rs `in_range`,
  `slot < 4096`, the call sequences' `receiver <= 256` (a SUB and an LDR past LDUR's reach) and
  the inline caches' admission checks of layout offsets. The 4,096-byte bound is stricter than an
  8-byte load needs (the scaled form reaches 32,760 bytes) because some of those templates also
  read a byte at the offset;
- compare immediates: 184 calls, 98 with literal immediates below 4,096; the rest name constants
  (tags, cache kinds, intrinsic ids, way counts) or bounded values: `argc` below 256 at the
  direct-call sequences, `k_imm12(..).unwrap()`, and a region compare already guarded
  (`(0..4096).contains(&value)`, jit_regions.rs:1773).

`movz(1, pc, 0)` at nine helper-call sites relies on the chunk limit of 65,535 operations
(jit.rs:4028, "op index must fit one movz"); the checked MOVZ makes it a compile failure instead
of a silent wrap if that limit ever moves.

Hand guards: they stay, with three exceptions. Each chooses which code a template emits (a fast
path or the helper) for bodies past about 510 slots, and lifting them changes that code; what they
protect against is now caught by the encoders. Removed, because the legalized form is what the
template wanted:
- the prologue's hand fallback for `arguments.length`'s slot past 4,095 (`fmov`, `movz`,
  `str x10, [x22, x9, lsl #3]`, jit.rs:4161-4169), now `str_d_imm`'s legalized store (three
  instructions either way);
- the release assertions of `emit_exec_word_load`, `emit_exec_word_store` and
  `emit_exec_number_store` (jit_exec_values.rs:43-59 and 133-138), whose unscaled branch is the
  legalizing `ldur`, `stur` and `stur_d`;
- the slot and formal-count guards of step 1's elided `arguments` and rest templates (ArgLength,
  ArgElem, RestLength, RestElem; `u32::from(*slot) * 8 + 8 < 4096` and `skip >= 4096`,
  jit_operations.rs:792-859), the family whose missing guard surfaced the problem: past slot 510
  or 4,095 formals they took the helper only for the encoders' sake. Ordinary code is unchanged,
  and the thousands-of-formals tests now run their fast paths with legalized operands.
Lifting the other slot guards is a measured change of its own for later (it gives bodies past
about 510 slots native fast paths, which changes those bodies' code).

x86-64. jit_x64.rs states its rule (jit_x64.rs:414-416, "Always use a full disp32 addressing
form"): every variable memory operand is disp32, every branch rel32 with a checked conversion in
`finish`, every ALU immediate imm32. The narrow forms are constant byte sequences, plus
`add_sp(amount: i8)`, called only with +8 and -8, which would encode -128 as `sub r13, 0x80`, an
add of 128: it gets the one-byte form's full range. One silent range hazard of the same class
is real: `Op::New` passes `pc | argc << 16` to `jit_new`, which takes the pc as `packed & 0xFFFF`,
and x86-64 compiles chunks of up to u32::MAX operations (ARM64 stops at 65,535), so a `new` past
operation 65,535 would run another operation's site. Such a site takes the generic helper. The
RegExp tier's x86-64 encoders (regex_native_x64.rs) pick imm8 or imm32 by range, use disp32 and
rel32 (checked in `finish`), and take their counts below 2^31 from the compiler (regex_native.rs
filters loop bounds to `m < 1 << 31`); `bt`/`bts` take LOGGED's bit as an imm8 modulo 64, below 64
only while the plan is not wide, and `set_logged` truncated a mask past 32 bits: a bit from 64 on
now refuses the program, and a wide mask is moved whole.

Tests

- Unit tests (jit_asm_imm_tests.rs, aarch64): every encoder's in-range operands at their
  boundaries give the former formulas' exact words and legalize nothing; each legalized form, 39
  sequences, matches llvm-mc's encodings; the refusals (SP compares and moves, a rewound region's
  failure); field widths past range panic in debug builds. Probes run the legalized sequences on
  the CPU, loading x0..x28, d0, d1, SP (a probe stack) and NZCV and storing them back: ten
  single-register accesses at offsets at and past each boundary (0, 255/256, 4095/4096, 4096
  times the size and one more, misaligned ones, -256/-257, -4096/-4104, 40,000, near 2 MB) with
  14 register roles (the base stored, a recycling candidate named, SP); pairs with 8 roles and 14
  offsets, and pre- and post-indexed SP moves; adds and subtracts past 12 bits and 16 MB onto
  themselves and SP; and CMP, CMN, ADDS and SUBS past 12 bits at the flags' edges. Each checks the
  bytes accessed (and that nothing else was written), every register, SP and NZCV.
- RegExp tier: its legalized slot accesses and retreat against llvm-mc, and the refusals; on
  x86-64, the wide mask's and LOGGED bits' encodings (GNU objdump 2.47) and the refusal past 63.
- End to end (tests.rs): bodies with 5,000 formals and locals read and write slots past 4,096 in
  every tier against Node's output, the JIT tier's compiled bodies legalizing their operands
  (counted); a construction past operation 80,000 in every tier (x86-64's packed pc).
- No checker test: the design has no pool to check.

Measurement (2026-10-29): fat-LTO engines of main 709e17a9 (W5b 3a landed) and of this series'
tip 50530f50, from `git archive` exports in fresh target dirs (target/p2/asm-imm/builds), on
Crystal: `taskset -c 10-13 perf stat -e instructions:u`, `TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0`, `--tier=jit`.

word for word apart from their literal pools' helper addresses, which move between two runs of
one engine (ASLR): 29 programs in djot, and the micro case's.

within 1.2 instructions (their runs overlap), and two lower with identical generated code:

### W3: static name addressing (census and design, 2026-10-29; Decision 21)

Census (main a25e56c5, an agent's; fat LTO, ARM64, address randomization off; perf record -g
--user-callchains on instructions:u, one sample per 250K instructions, 1M for chai, each sample
counted where it lands; scripts in target/p2/w3-i3b/names). Share of user instructions spent
resolving names:

| | total | shared name stubs (deep-chain mode) | in-line name code | Rust IC | tree-walker |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 11.4% | 8.5% (5.2%) | 2.4% | 0.5% | ~0 |
| React | 11.7% | 4.3% (none; fixed-layout mode 0.8%) | 3.75% | - | 3.5% |
| Djot | 4.5% | 2.2% (0.75%) | 1.5% | 0.4% | 0.4% |
| chai | 7.9% | 3.0% (1.75%) | 0.35% | 4.4% | - |

The stubs include their mode classification (Vue 2.2%); chai's Rust share is refills after JIT
LoadName misses (name_ic_fill 3.2% inclusive, the deep IC's WeakRef pins 1.26%). Global-object
names are almost absent: nearly all of it is function and block scopes. Per iteration: a closure
call 261 with no outer read, +43 for one read one hop out, +39 for each more, +109 for one read
two hops out; a let reads as a var; a function's own captured var in a frame the tree-walker
started and a loop fragment continued 59 a read (step 5's deviation); a store to an outer var 99,
`++` 90, `this.v` in an arrow 70; outer reads in loops about 1 (the region compiler keeps them in
registers). A static read is 1 + hops + 1 loads, and a TDZ test of 2 for let and const: about 3-6.
Static addressing is worth about 9-10% on Vue and 6-7% on chai, and part of React and Djot.

The exemplars (V8 c8b709085e21, SpiderMonkey bf75471716c5, JSC 260278048486):
- V8: Scope::Lookup (src/ast/scopes.cc:2224-2302) puts captured or eval-visible variables in
  context slots (MustAllocateInContext, 2614-2631); hops count only scopes with a context
  (ContextChainLength, 1560-1583); LdaContextSlot is depth loads and one more, unguarded
  (interpreter-assembler.cc:209-233; Sparkplug unrolls it, baseline-assembler-arm64-inl.h:656-664).
  Past a sloppy-eval scope a reference becomes kDynamicLocal or kDynamicGlobal, keeping its slot
  behind per-hop extension checks (code-stub-assembler.cc:15860-15893); eval's vars go to a side
  extension object and never move a slot (runtime-scopes.cc:440-459). `with` is dynamic
  (scopes.cc:2311-2344); TDZ is a hole test where not proven (scopes.cc:2441-2543).
- SpiderMonkey: closed-over bindings get environment slots (Parser.cpp:894-965); an
  EnvironmentCoordinate is (hops, slot) over scopes with an environment (NameAnalysisTypes.h:19-67,
  Scope.h:399-413); GetAliasedVar is unguarded and "can't be used when there's a dynamic scope (due
  to non-strict eval or with) that might shadow" (Opcodes.h:2910-2911); such names are dynamic,
  GetName with an IC (EmitterScope.cpp:594-599, 837-839, 940-941); TDZ checks unless proven
  initialized (BytecodeEmitter.cpp:1583-1647).
- JSC: resolve_scope and get_from_scope (BytecodeList.rb:514-563), linked against the closure's
  real chain (CodeBlock.cpp:579-629, JSScope.cpp:58-108), valid for every instance since closures
  of one function share symbol tables; ClosureVar walks depth links and loads the slot
  (LowLevelInterpreter64.asm:2854-2866); past a sloppy-eval scope, one realm watchpoint fired by
  the first var-declaring sloppy eval (JSScope.cpp:106-107, Interpreter.cpp:1530-1531); `with`
  resolves by name.

Why they can, and TRust cannot yet: each engine has one scope analysis that every tier follows,
so a record exists with the same slots in every tier and a (hops, slot) proven once holds for
every closure. TRust builds environments twice: compiled tiers by ActivationPlan (captured
bindings only, and a record only when the function makes one), the tree-walker always (every
binding, in insertion order, spilled past two slots). Which tier made a record changes its slots
and whether it exists (a middle function without captures has an activation under the tree-walker
and none compiled; Vue's module functions run once in the tree-walker), which is why the deep IC
guards every hop and chai keeps refilling it. SpiderMonkey's rule applies directly: static (hops,
slot) where no sloppy-eval function or `with` lies on the path, today's name ICs (the guard)
otherwise. V8's extension checks and JSC's watchpoint would keep static slots under eval; the
census has neither eval nor `with`.

Design:
1. A plan per function (CaptureScan, cached on the Function node): Opaque (direct eval or `with`
   inside, its nested functions included, as the scan walks them; parameter expressions; more
   than 506 bindings; new.target observed by the function or its arrows, which the tree-walker
   resolves by name; script, module and eval code), Private (no captures, no lexical `this` or
   `super`, which the scan treats as `this`, no captured `arguments`, no mapped formals; never a
   generator or async function), or Public (a prefix of the captured function-level names, and
   `this` when an arrow reads it, in sorted order). `super`'s home object lives in the method's
   own environment, made with the closure, which skipping an activation never hides.
2. One activation layout: the tree-walker makes a Public function's activation at the plan's size
   with the prefix first, which FunctionDeclarationInstantiation then initializes in place; the
   compiled ActivationPlan orders the same prefix first, and a fresh-frame chunk that would
   disagree does not compile.
3. Private records: the tree-walker flags a Private function's activation, and closure creation
   (`make_function_named`, every tier's) takes the nearest non-private record as [[Environment]],
   which nothing can observe; every closure chain then has the same records in every tier.
4. A static outer context on each Function node, innermost first: the enclosing Public
   activation, the self-name record, barriers (blocks, catch, for-heads, class and parameter
   scopes until they conform, `with`, module, script, global), then the enclosing function's own.
   The compiler resolves a free name through it to (hops, slot) when only planned records lie on
   the path, otherwise emits today's LoadName and IC (as SpiderMonkey's
   ScopeContext::searchInEnclosingScope, Stencil.cpp:1262-1419).
5. Static outer reads, calls' reads, stores (card-marked), updates and `typeof`: the VM walks the
   hops; native code loads [[Environment]], the hops and the slot, with the empty-word test for
   lexical bindings.
6. Borrowed frames use the fixed-slot cap templates when their activation is a Public planned
   record and the entry environment.

Steps, each landed when it improves: 1, plans, the tree-walker's planned activations, the
compiled prefix order and fixed caps in borrowed frames; 2, private records, the closure rule,
outer contexts and static outer reads (the main gain: Vue's and chai's deep chains); 3, static
stores, updates, `typeof` and calls' reads; 4, blocks, catch and for-heads conform; 5, class and
parameter scopes; then the tree-walker resolving its own names by coordinate (React's 3.5%), as
the three exemplars' interpreters do. Risks: CaptureScan once per tree-walked function with a
nested function (cached; compiled functions pay it already), and one flag test per closure made.

Step 1's record (6e45c153..c62fbbc5, 2026-10-29, an agent's; landed on targeted tests under the
owner's rule, at the session's pause):
- 6e45c153: every function's environment is planned once (`bytecode_scope_plan.rs`), and a free
  name read from an enclosing function's planned record is read by static address, (hops, slot),
  with no guard, on the VM and ARM64; closure creation skips the records compiled code never
  makes, so the address holds in every tier. Names a sloppy direct eval or `with` could shadow keep
  the name caches.
- 10d1b030: x86-64's static loads, and tests across tiers (scope_plan_tests.rs).
- 6af5df25: a spliced callee's free names read at static addresses (LoadNameIn).
- 4c335c15: every function that runs gets a plan; `TRUST_JS_SCOPE_LOG=1` prints each plan and why
  a name stays dynamic.
- c62fbbc5: functions whose parameter expressions cannot observe their own record share one
  activation.
Against a25e56c5 (fat LTO, ARM64, randomization off; measured before the rebase over 3b04fb1a and
item 4's rest): Vue -8.83% (2.184e9 to 1.991e9), chai -5.04% (the name caches stop refilling),
Djot -1.55%, React +0.13% (its closures sit inside a `switch`, whose record the tree-walker makes,
so it pays the planning alone; minor). Collections unchanged. Per iteration: a call of an outer
function 237 to 214, `this.v` in an arrow 84 to 65, a read two functions out 370 to 304; stores and
a function's own captured reads in tree-walker-started frames unchanged (later steps). Tests: the
targeted filters (closure, eval, `with`, arguments, generator, activation, scope, TDZ, name, OSR,
fragment, self-name, home, capture, default, parameters, destructuring), clippy and fmt; zombie's
clippy and scope_plan tests; on the combination with item 4's rest, the scope_plan and
borrowed-call tests. Not run: GC stress and the full validation, due at the stage's end. One test
changed: it removed a binding from a planned activation, which only an eval could do, and an eval
keeps the function unplanned.
Found on main, not fixed: in compiled tiers a default parameter that reads a name the body
redeclares as a captured `var` reads undefined (`var x = 'outer'; function f(a = x) { var x =
'body'; var g = () => x; return a; }` returns undefined; the tree-walker and Node return 'outer').
Parked on branch p2-w3-names (old base a25e56c5; target/p2/w3-names/RESUME.txt): a54f487f, the
compiler reusing the plan's capture analysis and straight-line numeric code reading static
addresses (built, not counted), and d229d095 (WIP), loop, switch and catch records made only where
they bind, which React needs (fixture outputs as main's in every tier, otherwise untested). Next:
classes, stores, loop fragments and preambles, borrowed frames' caps, the tree-walker's reads.

Resumed 2026-10-31. Three bugs found on the way, fixed first (e77750d0..9b5c7049; landed on
targeted tests under the owner's rule):
- e77750d0: generators' and async functions' plans are opaque again, as the design above has
  them. A compiled coroutine makes an activation of its own, in declaration order, below the
  record its call's FunctionDeclarationInstantiation made (`Chunk::make_activation`), so under
  step 1 a closure made in it read its own names at the plan's slots and outer names one hop
  short. With a compile threshold of 0, reentry_rooting_tests' two async-disposal tests hit the
  static read's debug assertion ("the static address of \"holder\" names another binding"), and
  in release a method of an async function read undefined for an outer name. Step 1's targeted
  filters had not included them.
- 67448f43: a `for (using x = ...; ...)` head's binding, when a closure captures it, lives in the
  loop's own record: the capture scan bound no names for `using` heads, so a closure in the loop
  read an outer or global `x` once compiled (older than P2). The resource is disposed once, after
  the loop (ForLoopEvaluation, spec.html:22617); Node v24.22.0 disposes it 4 times in the test's
  two-iteration loop (a V8 deviation; not reported).
- 9b5c7049: compiled parameter expressions no longer see captured body bindings (the bug found at
  step 1, and its relatives). With parameter expressions, FunctionDeclarationInstantiation
  (steps 30-32 and 37) gives the body's vars, functions and lexicals records of their own; a
  fresh compiled frame keeps the captured ones in its one activation from entry, so a default
  naming one read the body's binding (undefined for a var, the function for a body function or
  for one that redeclares a parameter, a TDZ error for a let or a homed block let). Such a list
  now leaves the function to the tree-walker, as one that reads a later parameter does. The
  exemplars give the body a record of its own: V8's varblock scope for non-simple parameters
  (parser-base.h:4869-4875, 4945-4969 at c8b709085e21), SpiderMonkey's extra body VarScope with
  parameter expressions (Parser.cpp:2071-2084), JSC's separate var environment for non-simple
  lists (BytecodeGenerator.cpp:633-634, 1251-1274); that is Decision 21's parameter-scope step.
Against 8ed756bc (fat LTO, ARM64, pinned, randomization off): Vue +0.04%, React 0.00%, Djot
+0.02%, chai +0.04%.

Then the parked commits, rebased onto the fixes (f454126e, e53f2fa7):
- f454126e: compiling a function reuses its plan's capture analysis (one scan, not two), and
  straight-line numeric chains read free names at their static addresses.
- e53f2fa7: the tree-walker makes a `for` head's, a for-in/of round's, a `switch` block's and a
  `catch` block's record only where the statement declares something block-scoped, as compiled
  code does and as V8 drops such scopes (Scope::FinalizeBlockScope, scopes.cc:930-940; switch,
  catch and for scopes at parser-base.h:6469, 6581, 6920); the capture scan's record barrier
  keeps only Annex B.3.4's bare function declarations.
Against 8ed756bc, measured before the rebase over the fixes (fat LTO, as above), each step
against the one before:

Collections unchanged. Tests: the targeted filters (scope plans, closures, eval, `with`,
