The census after e53f2fa7 (its parent's build, as the step 1 census; share of user instructions
resolving names, before step 1 in brackets): Vue 6.00% (11.44%), React 3.45% (11.66%; 3.03% of
it the tree-walker's own lookups), Djot 3.41% (4.51%), chai 4.55% (7.9%). Stores are 0.09% of
Vue. What stays dynamic, by the compiler's scope log: on Vue, reads in class members (33 sites,
behind the class barrier: its reactivity classes' methods read module-level helpers) and
globals (33); on Djot, class members (103) and globals (104); on chai, 756 sites inside lodash's
`runInContext`, whose activation is over the 506 in-line slots ("activation too large", most of
chai's 2.09% of name-cache refills and 1.31% of deep-chain stubs), and globals (381).

Then class records (2daffbe5, 2026-10-31; design step 5's classes). TRust makes three records
for every class in every tier (`Interp::begin_class_evaluation` and `finish_class_evaluation`,
which the VM's class ops share): the class-name record (the name, in its TDZ until the class is
defined), the private-name record (`#`-named keys) and the instance and static records (the home
objects and `%`-named bindings), so a constructor's, method's or accessor's outer context now
holds those three, the class's name the last one's, in place of the class barrier. As the
exemplars do, the coordinate is fixed by the scope analysis: V8 gives a class scope a context
only when a variable in it is context-allocated (Scope::NeedsContext, scopes.h:451-457),
SpiderMonkey an environment only when the scope has one (EmitterScope.cpp:415, 489 at main,
2026-10-31); TRust always makes the records, so their count is constant and nothing needs
deciding. Two findings on the way: a compiled frame does not lower a class (outside coroutines)
but evaluates it through a projection of its slots (`eval_expr_with_slots`), a record the
tree-walker never makes, so the class's records now close over the frame's own record once the
heritage, computed keys and decorators (the projection's only readers) are done, before any
member exists; and an explicit constructor was cloned at each evaluation to carry the class's
source text, which the parser had already given it, so each evaluation compiled a new
constructor without an outer context (it now keeps the parsed node). A class in a private
function (whose activation only the tree-walker makes), static blocks, field initializers,
closures in heritages and computed keys, and nested classes keep the barrier.
Against 12e917c8 (fat LTO, ARM64, pinned, randomization off): Vue -2.75% (1.989e9 to 1.934e9),
React -0.01%, Djot -1.29% (7.196e8 to 7.103e8), chai -0.20%; collections unchanged. Read sites
left dynamic on the fixtures: globals (Vue 33, Djot 81), a few with no outer context (Vue's module
loader), lexical blocks (8). Tests: scope_plan_tests' class cases in every tier at thresholds 0
and 8 (members reading outer and class names, the barriers, the slot projection with a static
field calling a static method during the definition), the class, method, accessor, super,
private, static, field, decorator, construct and toString filters with the earlier list; fmt,
clippy in three configurations; zombie's x86-64 clippy and the scope plan and class tests.

Then static globals (2a71f547, 2026-11-01; not in the design's step list, which stopped at the
planned records). After the class step, what the fixtures' compiled reads still resolved through
the deep name cache, validating every record on the way, were globals (Vue's reactivity traps
read `Reflect`, `Map`, `Object` from functions four and more records deep). Every exemplar
proves such a name unbound on the way and searches the global environment directly: V8's
LdaGlobal with its LoadGlobalIC (a property cell, or the script context table's slot),
SpiderMonkey's GetGName, JSC's GlobalProperty and GlobalLexicalVar resolve types. Here
ScriptEvaluation now plans the Script's own inner functions (`scope_plan::plan_script`, a shallow
scan: their barriers, not their bodies), whose contexts end at the realm's global environment and
pass that end to theirs; a free name no planned record on the way binds then compiles to the
number of records to the global environment (`Chunk::name_globals`), and the read walks them and
probes its name cache there, in the cache's existing modes (a script-level lexical's binding, a
global object property), in the VM, the ARM64 templates (with numeric chains and loop preambles)
and x86-64. A script-level `with`, blocks with lexicals, classes and the other barriers apply; a
Script's direct eval can only add global bindings, which the search finds; eval code, module code
and `new Function` bodies are not planned. Frames over records the tree-walker made (prepared
entries, mapped activations, loop fragments) read some of the function's own bindings by name,
`arguments` among them, so their names keep the caches; the first build missed that, and the
arguments and entry tests caught it. A full scan of the Script cost Djot 0.26% (its whole AST once
more), hence the shallow scan.
Against 2daffbe5 (fat LTO, pinned, randomization off; means over ten nursery sizes, as the item 7
note explains, then least of 3): Vue -0.44% (-0.16%), Djot -0.14% (-0.13%), React -0.01%
(0.00%), chai -2.62% (one run). Tests: scope_plan_tests' global cases in every tier at
thresholds 0 and 8 (script-level var, let, const and function reads, a global getter, an
eval-added global, late and deleted globals, a dead-zone read, a script-level `with`, numeric

script, realm, ShadowRealm, arguments, entry and earlier filters; fmt, clippy in three
configurations; zombie's x86-64 clippy and 495 targeted tests.

Tried and parked (branch p2-w3-large, 432e48df): large activations. A plan counting more than
506 bindings stays public, its activation marked spillable in its children's contexts, and their
static reads find its slots through the record's spill capacity (SpiderMonkey's coordinates
address a CallObject's dynamic slots so): chai's 756 read sites in lodash's `runInContext`
become static, and the tests pass on both architectures, but nothing measurable changes (chai
+0.15% in one run, its name resolution 2.32% to 2.41% sampled; the fixtures +0.02-0.03%, Vue's
uniform across nursery sizes). After the static globals and W3 item 7's megamorphic sites,
chai's name resolution is 2.32% (from 4.60% at the class step), deep-chain stubs 0.02%: those
reads were not hot. Not landed.

Then step 3's stores and updates (769afe06, 2026-11-01). An assignment, compound assignment or
`++`/`--` of a free name whose binding a planned record holds, and which is mutable (planned
bindings now carry that: not a `const` or `using` binding, a class's own name, a function
expression's self-name or `this`), writes the binding in place: the VM walks the static hops
and writes through the record's mutable borrow, the write barrier; ARM64 takes the
own-activation store and update templates with the hops walked first (the TDZ test of a lexical
binding's old word, the card mark unless the word is a Number). A store in the TDZ and every
immutable binding's keep the checked path, which throws; the VM fills the site's name cache once
for x86-64's probe, as a static read does. Against 0422d291 (fat LTO, pinned; nursery means,
then least of 3): Vue -0.73% (-14M at every nursery size; -0.73%), Djot +0.00% (-0.02%), React
+0.01% (+0.01%), chai -0.00%. Tests: scope_plan_tests' store case in every tier at thresholds 0
and 8 (outer var and let stores, compound assignments and updates, objects stored through the
barrier, const, class-name and self-name stores, a `valueOf` reassigning the variable during
`++`, stores and updates in the TDZ), the targeted filters with update, assign and store, fmt,
clippy in three configurations, GC stress on scope_plan_tests; zombie's x86-64 clippy and 280
targeted tests. Read sites left dynamic on the fixtures after this series: Vue 12 of 322, Djot
74 of 1001 (mostly globals under functions no plan reaches: class field initializers' closures,
encoded bodies).

Found on the way, for the owner (no change made): the tier-up threshold is the largest lever
left on React and Djot. The fixtures run at the default of 8 calls before a function tiers up to
bytecode; React's `reducerBatch`, called twice, stays in the tree-walker (its name lookups, 3% of
React's instructions, are the tree-walker's, which Decision 21's last step, coordinates for the

Octane 2 (median of 3): Score 3099 -> 3138 (+1.3%; t1 3253, +3.7%). EarleyBoyer +36.5%, RegExp
+35.3%, Typescript +15.6%, Box2D +13.6%, Gameboy +13.5%, PdfJS +12.9%, zlib +10.5%, RayTrace

node-sp 1968).
- NavierStokes (5193 -> 1749): a numeric loop's preamble (emit_loop_chain) found each free name
  it pins, a bound or an operand, through the read's name cache, and ran the original loop when
  the cache was empty. Since outer names are read by static address (6e45c153), code compiled
  straight from the tree-walker never fills those caches, so a loop bounded by an enclosing
  function's binding never entered its numeric region: the solvers' `for (var i = 1; i <= width;
  i++)`, `width` a FluidField var, left it on every entry. Fixed (1d3b64b8, below): 5801 after
  the fixes, +11.6% on c446.
- CodeLoad (15006 -> 14096): user instructions at a fixed 40 iterations of each of its two
  benchmarks (fixed-driver.js, target/p2/w3-exit/codeload; two runs within 0.01%), c446 3.122 G,
  main 3.579 G (+14.6%), main with TRUST_JS_SCOPE_PLANS=0 3.257 G (+4.3%). Decision 21's plans
  are 70% of the growth: plan_script's scan of each Script before its body runs and each
  function's plan (CaptureScan's expr, fn_statements and reference, and the hoisting collections
  the plans run: collect_hoist_stmt, hoisted_vars_stmt, collect_annexb_funcs, collect_hoist_ops).
  Most of the rest is the RegExp engine: CharClass::matches_raw2 2.3 times its c446 samples (the
  interpreter's class test; b63d060e closes case-insensitive classes over case at compile time,
  which lengthens their range lists: likely, not verified), and three new compile passes
  (filter_starts, close_over_case_sharing, mark_atomic_loops). CodeLoad compiles two libraries
  per iteration and runs little of them, so planning is all cost there. V8 walks no syntax tree
  for it: the parser collects each scope's unresolved references as it parses (Scope's
  unresolved_list_, src/ast/scopes.cc:1326), a function's compile resolves and allocates them over
  the scope tree (DeclarationScope::Analyze, scopes.cc:762-800; ResolveVariablesRecursively,
  2570), and a lazily parsed inner function's allocation is saved with its preparse data and
  restored at its compile (AnalyzePartially and SavePreparseData, scopes.cc:1840-1907; 792-797).

uglify-js -3.3%, babel-minify +3.6%), every tool faster (chai +44.1% to esprima +9.8%). The

ARES-6 (ms, one run): summary 317.0 -> 280.6 (-11.5%; t1 +0.4%); steady states Air -16.5%, Basic
-15.6%, Babylon -20.7%, ML -3.6%. w5a -> c446: summary -2.9% (ML steady -7.6%).
- Its minor collections spent 29.2 s clearing weak references (the GC log's `weak` step)
  against c446's 4.5 s: 1.7 ms a minor collection in Air's first round, 5.4-9.5 ms from the
  fourth, and their `sweep` grew with it (ML 3.9 -> 8.5 ms a minor collection; c446 0.8-1.5). An
  agent bisected it to 2daffbe5 (target/p2/weak-cells). Air's Liveness.prototype.localCalc
  declares `class LocalCalc` and constructs it on every call; since 2daffbe5 shares a class's
  constructor node, each fresh class fills the `new` site's construct cache, and the fill also put
  the constructor's weak pin in the chunk's call pins, which last the chunk's life (only a compile
  prunes them). Each dead class stayed reserved and its weak cell in the young list every minor
  collection reads: about 62,000 a round, 125,000 while two rounds' chunks lived (the young list
  censused by creation site). Fresh constructor functions took the same path before W3 (208,875
  cells after 200,000 `function F() {}; new F()` at c446). Fixed (02ac6393, below): after the
  fixes the step takes 0.79 s over the run (c446 4.31 s in the same session), 127 us a minor
  collection on average and 499 us at most. The exemplars hold such targets weakly and let the
  collector clear them: a V8 construct feedback slot holds one weak target or the megamorphic
  sentinel (src/builtins/ic-callable.tq:227-276), cleared after a full marking
  (mark-compact.cc:4166-4199); SpiderMonkey unlinks a stub whose weak field died
  (BaselineIC.cpp:184-202); JavaScriptCore unlinks a call to an unmarked callee
  (CallLinkInfo.cpp:172-219). Left for the owner: a megamorphic state for construct sites
  (localCalc still refills on every call), per-way pins at call sites (an evicted way's pin stays
  for the chunk's life, up to 4,096 a chunk: most of c446's dead cells), and, as the exemplars
  do, a collector that clears entries naming dead callees, so that no address stays reserved.

IIFE (a loop fragment's private bindings, 280.3 M) falls to 18.9 M. The snapshot's trees and their
plans belong to one Agent (Interp::shared_snapshots), and the capture summaries of its encoded
bodies (a3cb1cbf) live only while one bootstrap runs (lib.rs:1449), so each page pays again. TRust's
desktop runs each navigation in a new Agent on its own thread but warms a spare one in idle time
(src/page_spare.rs: it evaluates the prelude once in a throwaway Realm, and the claimed page's
bootstrap reuses that Agent's table): there the cost mostly leaves a navigation's critical path,
which the replay harness, making its engine per page, keeps on it. With TRUST_JS_SCOPE_PLANS=0
main's event-loop page costs 852.4 M (base 864.1, plans on 955.5); the plans still save 192 M a page
on dom-reconcile (7968.8 against 8161.3 M) and cost 21.5 M on todomvc. At t1 event-loop's
instructions inside the timer double (58.8 -> 116.4 M), nearly all compilation (native 20.7 -> 78.5
M): 313 chunks and 3.99 MB of code a page against 102 and 1.15 MB, TRust's platform functions that
run two to seven times a page (dispatch, 241 KB of code; moNotify, 180 KB) compiled again for every
page; no page is cheaper at t1. Event-loop's +1.7 ms inside the timer at main is not explained (+1.2
M instructions).

Octane through TRust (the spike's eight benchmarks, at its own threshold 0), two rounds: 2440 and
2442 -> 2323 and 2388 (-3.5%), all of it NavierStokes (9202 and 9211 -> 2561 and 2633, -71.8%);
the rest faster (RegExp +64.4%, EarleyBoyer +37.8%, DeltaBlue +5.1%, RayTrace +4.9%, Richards
+3.6%, Splay +2.5%, Crypto +0.4%). On 02ac6393 (below) 2777 and 2760, +13.2%.

Three fixes after the exit timing, on main since 2026-11-01 (02ac6393), each with a test that fails
without it and counted against its parent (fat LTO, user instructions pinned to CPUs 10-13,
median of 3; Vue also as the mean over ten nursery budgets, nursery.sh):
- 828ec3f6 indexes an array's named entries once it holds a far index (W2c's census, item 6;
  its repro 93.8 s -> 124 ms, below): Vue +0.035% (+0.035% at every nursery budget), React
  -0.034%, Djot -0.018%, chai -0.010%.
- 1d3b64b8 pins a loop's free names from their static addresses (NavierStokes, above): Vue

- 02ac6393 keeps one pin per construct site (ARES-6, above): Vue +0.061% (+0.061% over the
  budgets), React +0.122%, Djot -0.020%, chai -0.041%.
The fixtures' collections are unchanged by all three.

  M, the outputs identical; W3's exit stands as measured.

NavierStokes and the threshold (an agent's, target/p2/navier-threshold: user instructions on
fixed work with the fat-LTO 02ac6393 and c446 on CPU 19; its scores under load). Its steady
state is the same at every threshold, 94.2 M instructions a run at thresholds 0 to 8 (c446 99.3
M); the difference is the calls that run on the tree-walker before a function's calls pass the
threshold. lin_solve's a=1 path, `for (k < 20) { for (j <= 128) { for (i <= 128) ... } }` inside
an if/else, 327,680 inner iterations a call and two calls a run, takes 3.11 G instructions a call
there against 21.6 M compiled (144x; 58% of the tree-walked samples resolve names by string,
Names::slot_of), and nothing tiers such a call up during it: a loop's admission counts 129 visits
afresh at each execution of the loop (Admission, bytecode_loop_fragment.rs:92-143; eval.rs:1439),
which a 128-iteration loop reaches only at its failing test; inner loops' backedges count toward
neither the enclosing loop nor the function; and a frame transfers only at a loop that is a
direct statement of the body (function_osr_site, bytecode.rs:7254, and its use at 16503-16516),
where lin_solve's sit in an if/else. Over the first 20 runs threshold 1 costs 3.9 G more than
threshold 0, 2 7.1 G, 4 10.5 G and 8 20.2 G (six cold calls); c446 likewise (its first three runs
at 8: 8.25, 7.43 and 7.36 G). Octane's timed warmup absorbs the slow runs up to threshold 6; from
7 the third run is the first measured, and the score falls (standalone: 9678 at 1 and 2, 9441 at
4, 9137 at 6, 5572 at 7, 5810 at 8; c446 9550 at 1 and 5188 at 8). t1's Octane gain is this gap
meeting Octane's measured window, and where the score falls moves with the machine's speed. The
exemplars count loop iterations toward a function's tier-up and can enter compiled code at any
loop header, nested ones included: V8 charges each backedge to the function's interrupt budget
(JumpBackward, src/interpreter/interpreter-assembler.cc:1179-1256) and checks OSR at every
JumpLoop against its loop depth (interpreter-generator.cc:2472-2541;
control-flow-builders.cc:81-91); SpiderMonkey bumps the script's warm-up counter at each loop
head and enters the Baseline Interpreter there (vm/Interpreter.cpp:2048-2091; 10 calls or loop
iterations, jit/JitOptions.cpp:209-222); JSC adds 1 for a loop iteration and 15 for a call to one
counter, against 500 (OptionsList.h:369-376), and enters Baseline at the loop (loop_osr,
LLIntSlowPaths.cpp:491-536). For the owner: tree-walked backedges counted toward the function's
tier-up, so that a function compiles once they pass a budget (one cold call at most, what
threshold 1 pays now), then a transfer at nested loop headers as well, so that not even the rest
of that call stays on the tree-walker. DIAGNOSTICS.md's "can tier up after 128 executed
backedges" holds per loop execution. Not investigated: standalone and under load, threshold 0
scores 3.4% below threshold 1, with the same instructions and CPU time a run in the fixed-work
drivers.

The plans' compile-time cost (for the owner). Two parts:
- Snapshot bodies (TRust's prelude: about 210 M of the bootstrap plan's 309 M a page): every
  Agent decodes and scans each still-encoded nested body again for its capture summary, though a
  summary depends on its static encoding alone. Kept process-wide by that encoding (TRust gives
  each navigation its own thread, so a thread's cache would not carry over), or written into the
  snapshot when it is compiled (TRust compiles it once a process), no Agent after the first pays
  it. V8's equivalent travels with its snapshot: a lazily compiled function's preparse data (its
  inner functions' variable allocation) is saved with the function and restored at its compile
  (src/ast/scopes.cc:792-797, 1840-1907), so no context analyzes it again. Recommended: in the
  snapshot.
- Fresh sources (CodeLoad +10%): plan_script's scan and each function's plan walk the syntax tree
  again after parsing. V8 collects each scope's unresolved references while it parses
  (scopes.cc:1326) and resolves them over the scope tree at compile (DeclarationScope::Analyze,
  762). Folding the collection into the parser is the larger change: for the owner to place.

### W2c: elements as 8-byte words (census and design, 2026-11-01)

Census: main d74d9c0b, fat-LTO engines built from `git archive` exports into fresh target
directories (target/p2/w2c-census/builds/main; rustc 1.99.1), ARM64 on Crystal, pinned to CPUs
10-13 (the A725 cores) with setarch -R; instruction counts at TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0 (least of 3,
fixcounts2.sh's convention), everything else at the default pacing. A census build of the same
export (census-src: patch/census.diff, then patch/census2.diff) adds TRUST_JS_ELEM_CENSUS=1: after
each collection, every live object with element storage (and every array) classified, its bytes
counted by size class and modeled under W2c; the reasons that take a map's elements off "all
plain", each counted once per map in a census word; entry counters in the ARM64 element
templates; and the marker's element trace out of line, so that profiles can attribute it. Its
Props carry the extra census word (120 B instead of 112; the object cell stays in the 192 B class),
and it is never used for instruction counts. Profiles: perf record -g --user-callchains on
instructions:u, one sample per 250K instructions (1M for chai), TRUST_JS_JIT_MAP=1 and
TRUST_JS_PERF_MAP=1, attributed by scripts/elemcensus.py. The machine carried other work
(a validation gate, builds): counts and bytes are exact, times indicative. Data and scripts:
target/p2/w2c-census.

Splay is Octane's splay.js alone (base.js, the shells' driver; target/claude-closeout/ext/octane),
whose live heap the W2 exit names; chai is target/p2/ext/cli-chai.js; "sustained" is
benchmarks/sustained-allocation.js at --tier=jit.

1. Arrays in the live heap. Splay and the sustained workload at their largest major collection;
the fixtures (which run no major collection) and chai at the minor collection whose heap held the
most element bytes, where old garbage still counts. Bytes are size classes: the array's object
cell and every storage block its elements use (sidecar, packed deque and its box, dirty bitmap,
slot map, mirror, and for classic storage the named entries and layout that hold the index keys).
"W2c saves" is the model below (Design 1) applied to the same objects.

| | live bytes | arrays | elements | array bytes | of live | W2c saves |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Splay (major 18) | 246.2 MB | 256,045 | 2,560,384 | 114.7 MB | 46.6% | 41.0 MB (16.6%) |
| chai (minor 414) | 11.27 MB | 4,137 | 55,287 | 3.45 MB | 30.6% | 1.86 MB (16.5%) |
| React (minor 17) | 1.71 MB | 92 | 6,984 | 261 KB | 15.3% | 133 KB (7.8%) |
| Djot (minor 11) | 7.42 MB | 1,561 | 20,406 | 1.08 MB | 14.5% | 534 KB (7.2%) |
| sustained (major 10) | 43.98 MB | 3 | 200,002 | 5.25 MB | 11.9% | 2.62 MB (6.0%) |
| Vue (minor 18) | 718 KB | 65 | 113 | 30 KB | 4.2% | 13 KB (1.8%) |

- Splay's arrays are its payloads' `array: [0, 1, ..., 9]` (splay.js, GeneratePayloadTree): 256,038
  arrays of exactly 10 elements, all plain, all int32, every one in the sidecar's inline slots:
  192 B of cell and 256 B of sidecar each, 448 B for 80 B of values. The rest of the live heap is
  512,000 other object cells at 192 B (98.3 MB) and about 33 MB more, 128 B for each of the
  256,000 leaves' strings of about 46 units (not examined further).
- chai's arrays are small (lengths: 0 266, 1 563, 2 346, 3 266, 4 169, 5-8 1,091, 9-16 919,
  17-32 253, 65-256 263); 2,074 of its 4,137 live arrays carry a numeric mirror (232 KB, 6.7% of
  its array bytes). React's are the reducer's 70 state arrays of 100 todos (heap deques,
  215 KB of values); Djot's 1,561 are mostly 1-2 elements.
- Arrays made over each run (allocation, not live): chai 1,893,642, Splay 645,128, sustained
  400,002 (the `[point, i]` pairs, 448 B each today, 224 B under the model), Djot 49,751, Vue
  38,198, React 4,511.
- Non-array objects with element storage are few: Vue 5 (21 KB; 3 frozen), Djot 12 (15 KB),
  chai 290 (212 KB; 254 frozen) and chai's 470 live arguments objects (276 KB). Ordinary objects
  with more than eight named keys and no shared layout index carry a whole 256 B sidecar for one
  index pointer: 20-75 objects per workload, 5-19 KB.
- Peak RSS (main, MB, three runs each, identical): empty script 13.1, Vue 23.9, React 15.1, Djot
  40.7, sustained 93.5, Splay 1006.4 (about four times its live heap: V8's growing factor), chai
  989.1, which is not its heap (its live bytes stay at or under 11.3 MB; not attributed here).
  Element bytes are a fraction of a megabyte of the fixtures' RSS.

2. What takes elements off "all plain". The reasons each array (or other map) ever took, from
the census word: no array in the acceptance set ever took an accessor element, an element with
non-default attributes, an integrity level (freeze, seal), an index far past its elements, or
classic (named-entry) storage.

| | arrays made | ever any reason | holes (padded first write) | holes filled | new Array(n <= 8) | length set past the elements | other reasons |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chai | 1,893,642 | 222,102 (11.7%) | 216,321 | 222,068 | 5,748 | 5,781 | none |
| Djot | 49,751 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 0 | 0 | none |
| Vue | 38,198 | 3 | 0 | 1 | 0 | 0 | length made read-only 2, delete 1 |
| Splay, React | 645,128; 4,511 | 0 | | | | | |
| sustained | 400,002 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | a length past 100,000 (the retained array) |

- chai's holes all come from a first write past index 0 on an empty array
  (`start_packed_elements`), sizes 1 (2,161), 2 (23,480), 3 (26,160), 4 (34,240), 5-8 (96,920),
  9-16 (31,560), 17-64 (1,800): arrays filled from the top down, then filled completely (222,068
  maps filled a hole); the live census holds no holey array in any workload.
- Non-array maps that ever held an index key: chai 6,981 (6,496 with non-default attributes,
  2,485 in classic storage, 484 padded), Djot 12, Vue 8. The 6,496 are String wrapper objects,
  whose code units box_primitive materializes as own index properties (below).

3. Element operations in generated code. Entries into the ARM64 templates (census2's counters),
the operations that then reached a JIT helper (TRUST_JS_JIT_OPSTAT=1, main), and the templates'
share of user instructions (perf, self samples of their code; chai's helper paths inclusive):

| | element reads (stack, local) | read helpers | element stores | store helpers | map/filter appends | `k in O; O[k]` pairs | share of instructions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chai | 104.5M (37.3M, 67.1M) | 11.1M | 12.9M | 12.9M (99.9%) | 55.7k | 89.3k | 7.4% in templates; store helper 10.0% incl., its dense path (fast_set_elem) 4.6%, read helper 5.8% incl. |
| React | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 446.5k (40.3k helpers) | 448.7k | appends 7.6%; the pair inside the loop test's 7.6% (not separable) |
| sustained | 1.03M | 0 | 34.9k | 34.9k (100%) | 0 | 0 | reads 2.7%; array creation 4.8% |
| Vue | 275k (113.6k, 161.4k) | 116.8k | 61.8k | 61.8k (100%) | 58 | 63 | 0.25% in templates |
| Djot | 93.1k | 15.7k | 198 | | 0 | 0 (`in` 8.0k) | 0.4% in templates |
| Splay | 15.7k | 0 | 0 | | 0 | 0 | 0; array creation (MakeArray's helper) 6.8% |

Instructions per execution, from the code dumps (TRUST_JS_JIT_CODEDUMP; dumps/ in the census
directory): the stack form of GetElem spends 25 on the receiver tag, the key's u32 round trip,
the payload and the exotic and `ic_plain` bytes, then 39 in all through the numeric mirror, 46-48
through packed storage (heap deque, inline slots) and 51 through classic storage; the fused local
form is 3 more. About 11 loads on the packed path (the receiver word twice, the key, three flag
bytes, sidecar, packed pointer, inline length, the descriptor's flags, the value). The store
templates (SetElem*, SetElemLocal*) store only Numbers over an existing writable element (56 with
no mirror, about 75 maintaining one); every other value, every append and every store into a hole
calls H_SET_ELEM, hence the 99.9-100% above. The map/filter append (CreateDataPropertyOrThrow,
emit_define_elem_inline) measures about 75 an append, 21 of them finding and checking `length`
through its slot memo.

4. The collector. The census build's element trace (out of line), inclusive of its edges'
marking, against all marking:

| | element trace, of marking | of all instructions | slots traced |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Splay | 9.1% | 5.05% | 9.76M (majors and young arrays), all Numbers |
| sustained | 30.5% | 2.42% | 0.25M full, 3.01M remembered (the retained array's dirty blocks) |
| chai, Vue, React, Djot | | 0.2% or less | |

Collections (main, TRUST_JS_GC_LOG): Splay 40 minor (881 ms, 754 of them tracing; it promotes
1.07 GB) and 4 major (481 ms); sustained 21 minor (32.7 ms) and 2 major (26.5 ms); chai 444 minor
(1,157 ms, of which tracing 29 ms: its minors are side tables, stack and weak handles); the
fixtures 12-18 minor, 3.6-15 ms. A Splay payload is 47% array bytes, as its live heap is.

5. Whose saving is whose:
- W2c alone (the model): Splay -41.0 MB (-16.6% of live), each 10-element array 448 B to 288 B;
  chai -16.5%, React -7.8%, Djot -7.2%, sustained -6.0% of the live heap, and the sustained
  workload's pairs 448 B to 224 B of allocation (90 MB fewer over the run); the mirror (chai 232 KB
  live, 0.7% of its instructions in upkeep) and the classic slot map go.
- W2d (the slimmer cell): 64 B more per array at 128 B (Splay 16.4 MB, 6.7%), and 64 B per other
  object (Splay 32.8 MB more). With W2c an array is 224 B, half of today's 448 (the plan's "halves
  arrays"); Splay's live heap 246.2 to about 156 MB (-36.6%). Halving it also needs one of: the
  elements in the cell for small arrays (SpiderMonkey's fixed elements; -24.6 MB), copy-on-write
  literal elements (Splay's payload arrays are never written; V8 shares them; -24.6 MB), or
  smaller leaf strings (the 33 MB above).
- W2g: the String wrappers' materialized code units (chai's 6,496 maps; the cliff below), and
  arguments objects' exotic state (chai 470 live, 2.4% of its live bytes); typed arrays are not
  elements here (their data lives in buffers).

6. Found on main (the first fixed since, outside W2c):
- An array that ever has an index more than 256 past its elements, or whose first write lands
  past index 256, pays a linear scan of its named entries on every later absent-index insert,
  and a packed one on every read past its packed prefix: filling and reading back 200,000
  elements after `a = [1, 2, 3]; a[1000] = 0` takes 106.8 s against 33 ms without the far write
  (Node 19 ms);
  20,000 elements after `a = []; a[300] = 0` 1.1 s, 40,000 4.4 s (Node 1-3 ms; quadratic). With
  `has_far` set, Props::find can no longer prove absence from the slot map and falls back to
  `entries.keys().iter().position` (value.rs:9155), because array maps never index their index
  keys (should_build_index, value.rs:8594), and appends at the packed frontier stop going to
  packed storage (Props::insert, value.rs:9662). Repro: target/p2/w2c-census/edge/farcliff.js and
  farcliff2.js. No acceptance workload hits it. Fixed outside W2c (828ec3f6): once such a map holds
  more than eight entries its key index counts every key, as after a truncation, so the scan is a
  probe: 93,789 -> 124 ms (20 ms without the far write, Node 15 ms), and 959 -> 11 ms and 3,789
  -> 20 ms for the 20,000 and 40,000 (ed0427e6 against 828ec3f6 on CPU 5, an X925 core, after
  W3's exit timing). The elements stay
  named entries until W2c's sparse limit (Design 3)
  keeps them in the words, as V8 does: the rest of the gap to Node.
- ToObject of a string materializes every code unit as an own index property with a fresh key
  and a one-unit string (box_primitive, builtins/mod.rs:12569-12582): 200 `Object(s)` of a
  10,000-unit string take 502 ms (Node under 1 ms). V8 keeps wrappers in
  FAST_STRING_WRAPPER_ELEMENTS (src/objects/elements-kind.h:93-171), SpiderMonkey and JSC answer
  indices from the string; it belongs with W2g.
- No correctness bug in today's element handling showed up: 54 edge cases (holes under indexed
  prototype properties and setters, read-only length with push, shift, unshift, splice, pop,
  reverse, sort and fill, non-writable and non-configurable elements in sort, reverse, fill,
  shift, pop and truncation, accessor elements in copyWithin, slice and map, frozen, sealed and
  non-extensible arrays, far keys and their order, huge indices, descending fills, deletes in
  shift, reverse, splice and sort, holes in concat, spread, copyWithin and filter, arguments,
  String wrappers, coercions that shrink the array mid-algorithm) give Node's output in the
  tree-walker, the VM and compiled code, hot, at thresholds 0, 1 and 8 (edge/edge.js, edge-hot.js).

Today's representation (d74d9c0b):
- An element is a `Property` (value.rs:5429): a 16-byte pair of the value word and a meta word
  holding the four attribute bits and, for an accessor, its pair cell. A hole is
  `Property::plain(Value::Empty)` (the PACK_EMPTY word, value.rs:304).
- `Props` (value.rs:7056, 112 B) holds the elements behind `elems: DenseStorage`
  (value.rs:6309), a nullable pointer to the element sidecar plus the extras pointer. The sidecar,
  `DenseBuffers` (value.rs:5887; 248 B, a 256 B block), holds five things: `inline_packed`
  (value.rs:5794: up to INLINE_PACKED_CAPACITY = 10 Properties in place, value.rs:5792, with the
  "all plain" proof); `packed` (value.rs:6086: a box with a DenseDeque<Property> that keeps front
  room for shift, `traced_len` and a dirty bitmap of 64-element blocks, PACKED_DIRTY_BLOCK at
  value.rs:6074, for minor collections); `elems`, the classic slot map (u32 entry slots, NO_SLOT
  a hole, value.rs:7152); `mirror`, the numeric view; and `index`, a named-key hash for large
  non-array maps. Props adds `mirror_flags` and `mirror_holes`, `has_far` (an index lives only in
  the named entries) and `elem_mode` (an array's map: index keys never transition its shape),
  value.rs:7076-7102.
- Three element representations. Packed (arrays only: can_start_packed_elements requires
  elem_mode, value.rs:8250): made by creation (packed_array_named, value.rs:7927;
  reserve_dense_exact, 8092) or by a first write at an index up to 256 on an empty array, which
  pads holes (start_packed_elements, 8261); it holds any Property, so an accessor or a
  non-default attribute stays in place (Props::insert writes the slot, value.rs:9644-9661).
  Classic: index keys as named entries (a PropKey in the layout, the Property in the fields) found
  through the slot map, with the mirror; used by every non-array map with index keys and by arrays
  whose first write is past 256 (or with TRUST_JS_DENSE_ELEMENTS=0, value.rs:7177). Far: an index
  more than 256 past the frontier lives in the named entries alone and sets `has_far`
  (note_inserted, value.rs:8555; insert, 9662-9676), cleared only by truncation or by a bounded
  rescan after deletes (recover_dense_coverage, value.rs:9880).
- The "all plain" proof is `InlinePacked::packed_plain` (value.rs:5797), a cached fact that every
  packed slot is a plain element (writable, enumerable, configurable data, not a hole;
  is_plain_element, value.rs:5728), recomputed by a scan when false (packed_all_plain,
  value.rs:6505) and cleared by every mutable escape (packed_mut, packed_slice_mut,
  packed_index_mut, value.rs:6425-6470). shift, unshift, splice and the bulk edits depend on it
  (shift_dense_array 8839, unshift_dense_array 8995, plain_elements_mut 8810); generated code does
  not: it tests each slot's flags.
- The mirror flags W4 is to read (value.rs:7117-7133): MIRROR_OK (a coherent f64 copy), NO_HOLES,
  ALL_I32, PACKED (the copy parallels packed storage) and PACKED_FAILED (a preparation that
  failed). They describe a numeric copy rather than the elements: an array of objects, a holey
  array and one never prepared all read 0. The copy is made for numeric literals over 32
  elements, numeric reserve_dense_exact and hot numeric loops (prepare_packed_numeric_mirror,
  value.rs:8146, from jit.rs:2204, bounded to 4,096 elements), and dual-stored by
  set_index_value (value.rs:8489) and generated stores (emit_mirror_store, jit.rs:14412).
- Sizes in the release build: Object 168 B, the RefCell<Object> cell 176 B in the 192 B class
  (value.rs:4179); Props 112 B; DenseBuffers 248 B (256 B class); InlinePacked 168 B;
  PackedBuffer 80 B (96 B class); Property 16 B; DenseDeque 32 B. An array of 1 to 10 elements is
  448 B; past 10, a 96 B box and 16 B a slot more.
- Who touches elements:
  - Props (value.rs): get_index 8392, set_index_value 8489, try_append_element 8615,
    try_define_dense_element 8649, pop_last_element 8729, packed_array_elements 8795 (searches),
    plain_elements_mut 8810, shift_dense_array 8839, unshift_dense_array 8995, find 9074 (an index
    through the slot map), insert 9644, remove_indices_from 9743, remove_key 9801, ordered_keys
    9911, freeze_descriptors 9491, highest_nonconfig_index_from 9975, length_property 8281.
  - Interpreter and VM: fast_get_elem and fast_set_elem (interpreter.rs:9850, 9890),
    plain_own_element_present (9840), array_prototypes_unshadowed (9784, the indexed-prototype
    protector), make_array and make_array_from_raw (8424, 8565); the VM's GetElem/SetElem family
    (bytecode.rs:18359-18405) through get_computed_element and set_computed_element (25570,
    25631), the JIT helpers jit_get_element (25678) and jit_set_elem (25001).
  - ARM64 templates (jit.rs): GetElem 14237-14410; the fused local and keyed forms 15194-15457;
    SetElem 14496-14627 with emit_packed_element_store 14629 and emit_mirror_store 14412;
    UpdateElem 11229; the map/filter append 14855-15131; `in` 14686-14853; computed method calls
    7792; self-hosted `k in O; O[k]` (emit_local_own_dense_data_guard and
    emit_local_assumed_dense_data_value, 15458-15600); numeric loop chains and linked scans over
    the mirror (emit_chain 16344, emit_loop_chain 18635); the gates 14155-14229 and the offsets
    in JitLayout (value.rs:1367-1420). Dispatch: jit_operations.rs:357-404, 676, 865, 916;
    jit_regions.rs:2223-2225, 2494-2513, 2650-2754, 3812-3861.
  - x86-64 has no element template: GetElem and GetElemLocal call H_GET_ELEM (jit_x64.rs:1279),
    every other element operation H_EXEC (jit_x64.rs:1373).
  - Builtins (builtins/mod.rs): push 6204 and its intrinsic, pop 6266, shift 6367, unshift 6418,
    the Array constructor 7314 (reserve_small_holes 7328), the bulk edits 7381, searches 7501,
    try_define_dense_element 9239, ArraySetLength 5658-5760, SetIntegrityLevel 2157; JSON
    (json.rs:499); spread and argument lists (bytecode.rs:17425).
  - The collector: Object::trace_gc_fields (value.rs:3759) reports the blocks
    (DenseStorage::trace_storage, 6354), then traces packed Properties
    (PackedBuffer::trace_elements, 6199, only dirty blocks and the untraced tail in a minor
    collection's remembered trace) and
    classic elements with the named fields; finish_packed_mark_trace 6256; the owner's card
    (gc_core.rs:112) through its mutable borrow and PackedBuffer::note_index (6147) record
    writes.
  - Snapshots: snapshot.rs encodes ASTs only, never heap elements; nothing changes there. The
    managed-memory accounting reads packed values and the blocks' bytes (memory.rs:1058-1119).

The exemplars (V8 c8b709085e21, SpiderMonkey bf75471716c5, JSC 260278048486, as in W3's static
names; paths under src/ for V8, js/src/ for SpiderMonkey and Source/JavaScriptCore/ for JSC; the
files read are in target/p2/w2c-census/exemplars):
- Element words. V8: `FixedArray` slots of tagged words, 4 bytes with pointer compression, and
  unboxed float64 in `FixedDoubleArray` (src/objects/fixed-array.h:320-322,
  fixed-primitive-array.h:111-166); the kind lives in the map (code-stub-assembler.cc:4551-4552).
  SpiderMonkey: 8-byte barriered Values after a 16-byte ObjectElements header of flags,
  initializedLength, capacity and length (js/src/vm/NativeObject.h:270-287, 839-845), never null
  (a shared empty header, NativeObject.cpp:38-63), possibly inline in the object (NativeObject.h:
  826-831). JSC: 8-byte slots after an 8-byte {publicLength, vectorLength} header, Int32 slots
  boxed and Double slots raw (runtime/Butterfly.h:140-150, 200-202; IndexingHeader.h:120-132), the
  shape in the cell's indexing byte (IndexingType.h:37-79).
- Kinds and holes. V8: PACKED and HOLEY x SMI, DOUBLE, ELEMENTS plus NONEXTENSIBLE, SEALED, FROZEN,
  DICTIONARY and the arguments and string-wrapper kinds (src/objects/elements-kind.h:93-171), a
  one-way lattice ("never ... backwards in the elements kind manifold", map.cc:1142-1144); the hole
  is the_hole, or a NaN pattern in double stores (fixed-array-inl.h:379-389, globals.h:2195-2204).
  SpiderMonkey: holes are a magic Value and NON_PACKED a one-way header flag ("guaranteed to
  contain no hole values" while clear, NativeObject.h:234-236, set at NativeObject-inl.h:56-64);
  no number kinds. JSC: Undecided, Int32, Double, Contiguous, ArrayStorage, SlowPutArrayStorage,
  one-way (the least upper bound is `std::max`, IndexingType.h:217-225); a hole is the empty value
  0, or PNaN in Double (JSCJSValue.h:367-368, ButterflyInlines.h:311-330). All three keep holes in
  the fast representation; none goes back from holey to packed.
- A non-plain element. V8: the whole array goes to DICTIONARY_ELEMENTS with requires_slow_elements
  set (accessor: lookup.cc:927-953; a new index with attributes: js-objects.cc:5683-5684 and
  elements.cc:1817-1851; reconfiguring one: lookup.cc:575-581). JSC: the whole array enters
  dictionary indexing ("sparse") mode, every value copied into its SparseArrayValueMap
  (JSObject.cpp:3052-3096, 1189-1219). SpiderMonkey: only that index leaves, as an ordinary property
  in the shape's property map, its dense slot becoming a hole; the object is marked `Indexed`,
  which stops dense growth past capacity (NativeObject.cpp:1398-1476, ObjectFlags-inl.h:18-31,
  NativeObject-inl.h:390-397).
- Integrity levels. V8: the array keeps fast elements under the NONEXTENSIBLE, SEALED and FROZEN
  kinds (Smi and double kinds boxed first), dictionary only when the map can take no transition
  (js-objects.cc:4683-4770, map.cc:1952-2007). SpiderMonkey: dense elements stay, with
  NOT_EXTENSIBLE, SEALED and FROZEN header flags and shape flags that let a shape guard exclude
  frozen elements (JSObject.cpp:506-590, NativeObject.h:216-232). JSC: dictionary indexing mode
  for every array, even an empty one (JSObject.cpp:2906-2966, JSArray.cpp:197-268).
- Non-writable length. V8: the properties normalize, the elements kind stays; growing stores are
  refused by MayHaveReadOnlyLength (lookup.cc:954-971, objects-inl.h:874-898). SpiderMonkey: a
  header flag and the invariant capacity <= length, so the JIT's capacity check refuses stores
  past it (NativeObject.h:150-155, ArrayObject.h:31-35). JSC: dictionary mode with LengthIsReadOnly
  on the map (JSArray.cpp:197-208).
- Where length lives: V8 in the JSArray (js-array.h:161), SpiderMonkey in the elements header,
  JSC in the butterfly's header; none keeps it in the general property table, as TRust JS does.
- Sparse indices. V8: a gap of 1024 past capacity (kMaxGap), or past 500 slots (5000 young) a
  growth to at least nine times the dictionary capacity the used elements need, sends the array
  to dictionary (js-objects-inl.h:1255-1286, js-objects.h:961-974, dictionary.h:480-482); a
  length up to 32 Mi stays fast (objects.cc:4518-4524).
  SpiderMonkey: past index 1000 when fewer than 1/8 of the indices would be filled
  (MIN_SPARSE_INDEX, SPARSE_DENSITY_RATIO, NativeObject.h:1877-1884; NativeObject.cpp:555-589).
  JSC: past index 1000 beyond the vector, or 100,000 at under 1/8 density (ArrayConventions.h:
  57-106, JSObject.cpp:3278-3332).
- Back to fast. V8: when the dictionary "only saves 50% space", checked only as an index is added
  (js-objects.cc:5602-5629, 5685-5689), never with requires_slow_elements. SpiderMonkey: when the
  sparse indices fill 1/8 of the range and are all plain, checked only when the slot span reaches
  a power of two to avoid quadratic work (NativeObject.cpp:592-659, 1490-1503). JSC: never
  (StructureTransitionTable.h:92-115).
- The dictionary itself. V8: NumberDictionary, (key, value, details) triplets with open addressing
  and a max-number-key word whose low bit is requires_slow_elements (dictionary.h:382-386,
  459-478; property-details.h:17-27). SpiderMonkey: property-map entries with full PropertyInfo,
  merged into index order when enumerated (PropMap.h:18-60, Iteration.cpp:316-450). JSC: a
  separate SparseArrayValueMap cell of {attributes, index, value} (SparseArrayValueMap.h:43-93).
- Baseline element reads and writes. V8's Ignition and Sparkplug inline nothing: KeyedLoadIC and
  KeyedStoreIC handlers check the map, load elements, bound against length, load, and test the
  hole under NoElementsProtector (accessor-assembler.cc:3987-4018, 2693-2770, 768-778); stores add
  a 4-instruction ARM64 barrier (code-generator-arm64.cc:1290-1295). SpiderMonkey's CacheIR dense
  load is a shape guard, the elements pointer, an unsigned initializedLength check, the packed
  flag or a hole test and the load: 9-10 x64 instructions and 6 loads past the operands' tag
  tests (CacheIR.cpp:3018-3040, CacheIRCompiler.cpp:5278-5316); its in-bounds store about 16 with
  the pre and post barriers (CacheIRCompiler.cpp:7312-7355). JSC's LLInt get_by_val loads the
  indexing byte (no structure check), the butterfly and publicLength, then the value and the hole
  test, about 40 interpreter instructions with profiling (LowLevelInterpreter64.asm:1840-1896);
  its stores into holes need no check while the prototype chain is sane (JSArray.cpp:1953-1956).
  Every exemplar stores any value in its baseline store path; TRust JS stores only Numbers.
- The collector. V8 scans FixedArrays and skips double stores; old-to-new stores set a bit per
  slot in the page's SlotSet (objects-body-descriptors-inl.h:1061-1066, 2027-2034;
  basic-slot-set.h:24-29). SpiderMonkey traces [0, initializedLength); a nursery value stored into
  an element records a SlotsEdge range when initializedLength exceeds 256, else the whole cell
  (Tenuring.cpp:876-897; CacheIRCompiler.cpp:9190-9244). JSC scans publicLength of Contiguous
  storage, never Int32 or Double storage, and remembers whole cells (JSObject.cpp:84-145,
  HeapInlines.h:87-121).
- Holes and prototypes: V8's NoElementsProtector (protectors.h:15-30, isolate.cc:7006-7042),
  SpiderMonkey's arrayProtoHasNoIndexedPropsFuse (RealmFuses.h:328-352, Array-inl.h:40-59), JSC's
  arrayPrototypeChainIsSane watchpoint and havingABadTime (JSGlobalObject.cpp:3320-3368,
  2682-2847): TRust JS's array_prototypes_unshadowed (interpreter.rs:9784) is the same proof.
- shift: V8 moves up to 100 elements, then left-trims (array-shift.tq:11-40, heap.cc:3118-3229);
  SpiderMonkey keeps up to 2047 shifted elements in the header (NativeObject.h:177-196); JSC keeps
  an index bias in ArrayStorage, to which it converts queues past 128 elements (JSArray.cpp:
  1487-1555, 1766-1769). TRust JS's deque front room is the same idea.
- Copy-on-write literals: V8 shares "simple and shallow" boilerplate stores (ast.cc:815-821,
  runtime-literals.cc:636-641), JSC shares constant butterflies (NodesCodegen.cpp:442-512),
  SpiderMonkey removed its COW elements (NativeObject.h:199-243).

Design:
1. Representation. An array's elements are one storage block of 8-byte Value words, a hole the
   PACK_EMPTY word, behind a 16-byte header {length used: u32, capacity: u32, shifted: u32,
   flags: u32}, as SpiderMonkey's ObjectElements; Props holds one word pointing at it, never null
   (a static empty block, as SpiderMonkey's and V8's empty stores), where `elems` is now. The
   sidecar's inline slots, the packed Property deque, the slot map and the mirror go; the named-key
   index moves into the extras or a word of its own, so a large ordinary map stops carrying a
   256 B sidecar for one pointer. A Number word is the double itself (TRust JS's Values box
   everything but Numbers), so no separate double store is needed. Shift keeps its front room as
   the `shifted` count (SpiderMonkey's numShiftedElements). A block over 64 words keeps a dirty
   bitmap of 64-word blocks, as PackedBuffer does, in a side record its owner reports.
2. Kinds. A byte in Props (where mirror_flags is, already read by generated code) holds facts
   about the words: WORDS (else the dictionary below), NO_HOLES (no hole below the used length,
   and for an array the used length is its length), NUMBERS (every word a Number) and INT32 (every
   word an exact int32: MIRROR_ALL_I32's meaning), and, as open question 2 decides, SEALED and
   FROZEN. Stores keep them exact: a hole clears NO_HOLES, a non-Number clears NUMBERS and INT32, a
   fraction INT32. Unlike the exemplars' one-way kinds they may be proven again by a bounded scan,
   as packed_all_plain and the mirror's preparation do today (open question 3): chai fills 216,321
   arrays from the top down, and a one-way bit would keep them off the packed-only paths for life.
   Generated code never relies on NO_HOLES (it tests the hole word); builtins re-check it under
   the cache rules they use today. This byte is what W4 records for elements kinds.
3. The dictionary. A non-plain element (an accessor, or attributes other than writable,
   enumerable, configurable) and an index past the sparse limit leave the words for the named
   entries, as SpiderMonkey does: that index alone, its word becoming a hole, with `has_far`
   (SpiderMonkey's `Indexed`) set (open question 1 has V8's and JSC's whole-array form). Index keys
   in the named entries keep the map's key index once there are more than eight (828ec3f6's rule),
   so a dictionary lookup is a hash probe. The sparse limit follows V8: a gap of
   1024 past capacity, or a growth to nine times the dictionary capacity the used elements need
   (open question 5); and the way back follows V8's rule (words when they would take at most twice
   the dictionary's room), checked as SpiderMonkey does only when the dictionary's count reaches a
   power of two, and only when every dictionary index is a plain data property.
4. Specification equivalence of the transition. The abstract state of an array is its own
   properties (each key's value or accessor pair and attributes), their [[OwnPropertyKeys]] order,
   [[Extensible]], [[Prototype]] and `length` with its writability. Both representations encode
   that state: a word w at index i is the property {[[Value]]: w, [[Writable]], [[Enumerable]],
   [[Configurable]]: true} (with SEALED or FROZEN clearing the bits they clear), a hole is no
   property, and a dictionary entry is its key's Property. Every internal method computes from
   the abstract state alone: [[GetOwnProperty]], [[DefineOwnProperty]] (§10.4.2.1 for arrays,
   §10.1.6.3 ValidateAndApplyPropertyDescriptor), [[HasProperty]], [[Get]], [[Set]] (§10.1.9.2,
   where a hole consults the prototype chain in both forms), [[Delete]], and [[OwnPropertyKeys]]
   (§10.1.11.1: integer indices ascending first, which ordered_keys produces by sorting the
   indices of both stores, then strings and symbols in creation order, which index keys never
   enter). The transition maps each word to exactly that property and each hole to absence, and
   changes nothing else; it runs inside the internal method that needs it, after that method's
   observable steps (ToPropertyKey, ToPropertyDescriptor and their author code) and before it
   returns, runs no author code and allocates only storage blocks, which never collects (collections
   run at safepoints). Hence no program can tell whether or when it ran. Three consequences must
   hold in the code and are where the risk lies: ArraySetLength deletes from the top and stops at
   the first non-configurable index over both stores (§10.4.2.4; highest_nonconfig_index_from
   already scans both); a for-in enumeration in progress must not re-visit or resurrect an index
   the transition moved (EnumerateObjectProperties; arrays' keys are not cached today,
   enumeration_layout returns None for array maps, value.rs:8346); and the indexed-prototype
   protector moves whenever the transition gives a prototype an indexed accessor (the epoch bump
   note_structural already makes for marked prototypes). The way back maps each plain dictionary
   entry to its word, and is legal only when no entry is an accessor or has other attributes, so
   the same argument covers it.
5. Generated code (ARM64 only; x86-64 calls helpers for every element operation and changes only
   where Props' or Object's offsets move). A read becomes: the receiver tag, the key's u32 round
   trip, the payload, the exotic and `ic_plain` bytes (21 instructions), the elements word, its
   used length, the bound, the word itself, the hole test, the commit: about 33 instructions and
   8 loads against 46-48 and 11 through packed storage and 39 through the mirror. A store takes
   any value: it tests the kind byte for FROZEN or SEALED (if they are kinds) and updates NUMBERS
   and INT32, stores the word, and card-marks the owner when the value is a cell (the named-store
   barrier, emit_card_mark at jit.rs:11809), calling the helper for a block with a dirty bitmap;
   an append within capacity bumps the used length and `length`. The map/filter append, `in`,
   UpdateElem, computed method calls, the self-hosted pair and the numeric loop chains read the
   same words (the loops lose the mirror's separate pointer and test NUMBERS/INT32 instead of the
   mirror flags). Dump checks change in exactly these templates (the element sequences, the
   elements and kind offsets) and in every template whose Props or Object offsets move; typed
   arrays' templates do not change.
6. The collector traces a block's used words, not its capacity, and skips a NUMBERS block
   entirely, as V8 and JSC skip double and int32 stores; a minor collection's remembered trace of
   a large block keeps today's dirty bitmap.
7. Builtins, interpreter, VM: the dense paths that take `&[Property]` (searches, shift, unshift,
   splice, reverse, sort, fill, copyWithin, concat, slice, join, JSON, spread, argument lists,
   push and pop and their intrinsics) take `&[Value]` words, searches as word compares; the
   creation paths (make_array, make_array_from_raw, packed_array_named, templates) copy words into
   an exact-size block instead of initializing a 256 B sidecar (Splay spends 6.8% of its
   instructions creating its payload arrays, the sustained workload 4.8% creating pairs).
   Non-array maps keep index keys in the named entries (open question 7), arguments objects with
   W3's argument work.

Order, each series behaviour-preserving, with Test262 identical, difftest, the fixtures' output,
and the dump checks changing only as stated:
1. The census in the tree: TRUST_JS_GC_LOG's element counts and bytes per collection, and the
   kind byte's facts computed beside today's storage (no layout change, no dump change).
2. Words: packed storage becomes the word block (the inline slots and the Property deque go), a
   non-plain element leaves for the named entries, JIT reads and the existing Number stores read
   words. Dumps: the element
   templates' sequences and offsets.
3. The mirror goes: NUMBERS and INT32 in the kind byte, numeric chains over words, the classic
   slot map gone (non-array index keys through the key index).
4. Stores of any value and appends in line on ARM64, with the card mark (the 12.9M helper calls
   of chai's run).
5. The collector: NUMBERS skips, used length only.
6. The sparse limit and the way back to words (farcliff's gap to Node goes here).
7. As the owner decides: SEALED and FROZEN kinds, `length` in the header (open question 6).

Predicted gains, against main d74d9c0b:
- Bytes: the model above. Splay's live heap -41.0 MB (-16.6%) and its peak RSS by about as much
  in proportion (V8's sizing scales its limit with the live size): about -160 MB of 1006; chai,
  React, Djot and sustained -6% to -16.5% of their live heaps; Vue -1.8%; the fixtures' peak RSS
  within a megabyte (Djot about -0.5 MB). The sustained workload allocates 90 MB less.
- Instructions, from the shares and per-operation counts above: chai -4% to -6% (reads about -12
  of 46-51 each over 104.5M reads, -1.4%; its 12.9M stores in line instead of a helper path that
  takes about 300 instructions a store, -3% to -4%; no mirror upkeep, -0.7%); React -3% (the
  self-hosted pair about -17 and the append about -12 over 0.45M iterations each; -5% with
  `length` in the header); sustained -3% to -4% (creation halved, the element trace halved);
  Splay about -8% a run (the element trace, 5%, gone; creation halved); Vue and Djot -0.2% to
  -0.7% (most of Vue's element operations reach helpers, 69% of its stack reads and all of its
  stores, likely through its reactive Proxies, which W2c does not change).
- Collection time: Splay's marking -9% from skipping its numeric arrays, and its minor collections
  by about the 16% fewer bytes they promote; the sustained workload's marking about -15% (half the
  bytes per element traced); the sustained workload's minor collections fewer by the pairs'
  bytes. The finalizer share of the W2 exit was W2a's and is met.

Risks:
- The JIT's element templates on ARM64 change (reads, the new stores with their barrier, the
  numeric chains); the dump checks and difftest must hold, and GC stress on the element tests
  because stores of cells into old arrays are new to generated code.
- The transition must be exactly specification-equivalent (Design 4); the census found no array
  in the acceptance set that takes it, so Test262's Array and Object suites, the edge battery
  above and difftest are its tests.
- The kind byte's facts are proofs generated code and the collector trust (NUMBERS skips tracing):
  a missed clear is a use-after-free. Debug builds verify the facts at every collection, as heap
  verification checks the barrier.

Exit (proposed, within W2's): Splay's live heap at or below 206 MB (-16%; the rest of the halving
is W2d's and the open questions'); live element bytes (arrays' cells and storage) at least 35%
lower on Splay, chai, React and Djot; the fixtures' peak RSS at or below today's (Vue 23.9, React
15.1, Djot 40.7 MB) and the close-out's (27, 19, 40; Djot's 40 against today's 40.7 wants both
binaries measured together, since peak RSS follows page-cache state); pinned instructions chai
-3% or better, React -2% or better, Vue and Djot no worse than noise; one 8-byte load per element
read and no descriptor test in generated code; the far-index cliff gone (farcliff.js within 2x of
Node); Test262 identical; dump checks unchanged apart from the element templates and moved
offsets.

Open questions for the owner:
1. A non-plain element: that index alone into the named entries (SpiderMonkey), or the whole array
   into a dictionary (V8, JSC)? Recommended: SpiderMonkey's. TRust JS's `has_far` already is
   SpiderMonkey's `Indexed`, the move is O(1), the other elements keep their words, and the
   equivalence argument is the same; no acceptance workload takes either.
2. Integrity levels: SEALED and FROZEN as kinds over the words (V8, SpiderMonkey), or the
   dictionary (JSC)? Recommended: kinds, as V8 and SpiderMonkey do: reads do not change, stores
   already read the byte, and frozen arrays occur outside the acceptance set, which has none (a
   tagged template's strings array is frozen by the specification).
3. NO_HOLES (and NUMBERS) one-way, as all three exemplars' kinds and flags are, or re-provable by a
   bounded scan, as today's proof and mirror are? Recommended: re-provable. The exemplars' tiers
   specialize on kinds and avoid invalidation by never going back; TRust JS's templates test the
   hole word and its builtins re-check the proof, and chai's 216,321 top-down fills would
   otherwise stay holey.
4. Remembered-set precision for stores into large blocks from generated code: helper calls for
   blocks with a dirty bitmap at first (as above), or the bitmap update in line now? V8 sets a
   bit per slot, SpiderMonkey records ranges past 256 elements and whole cells below, JSC whole
   cells. Recommended: the helper first; in line with W2f, which touches the same barrier.
5. The sparse limit: V8's gap of 1024 and its density rule, SpiderMonkey's 1000 and 1/8, or
   JSC's 1000 past the vector or 100,000 at 1/8? Recommended: V8's, the engine whose sizing P1
   already follows.
6. `length`: keep it an ordinary named entry, or move it into the elements header with a
   LENGTH_RO kind bit, as SpiderMonkey and JSC do (V8 keeps it in the JSArray)? It costs 21 of the
   map/filter append's ~75 instructions and every `length` read's named lookup, but touches every
   `length` path (the named ICs' array cases, ArraySetLength, ordered_keys, descriptors).
   Recommended: the header, as W2c's step 7, after the words have landed.
7. Non-array maps with index keys (ordinary objects, arguments): words too, as every exemplar
   gives all objects elements, or the named entries with their key index? Recommended: the named
   entries for now (the census finds at most 290 such live objects and nothing hot), arguments with
   W3's argument work, String wrappers made exotic with W2g.
8. Copy-on-write literal elements (V8, JSC; SpiderMonkey removed them) or elements in the cell for
   small arrays (SpiderMonkey's fixed elements)? Either takes Splay to about -47% with W2d's
   128 B cells (-24.6 MB each, against the exit's halving); neither is W2c's. Recommended: decide
   with W2d's cell size, measuring both on Splay and on the fixtures' allocation.

### Tiering: loop iterations toward a function's tier-up (design, 2026-11-01)

The owner's go-ahead (2026-11-01, after W3's stage end): close the tiering gap NavierStokes
exposed, as the exemplars do. Today a function compiles once its calls pass the threshold
(interpreter.rs:16888-16902), and a tree-walked call leaves the tree-walker only at a loop that
runs 128 backedges in one execution (Admission, bytecode_loop_fragment.rs:92-143): at a
body-level for, while or do-while loop by moving its whole frame into the compiled function
(try_function_osr, bytecode.rs:16488), elsewhere into a compiled fragment of that loop, which is
cached per site (fragment_cache) but entered only after 128 more backedges of a later execution.
Backedges count toward nothing else, so a call whose loops each run 128 times or fewer stays on
the tree-walker throughout, and so does every call before the threshold's: lin_solve's six cold
calls at threshold 8, 3.11 G instructions each.

The exemplars:
- One counter per function, charged by calls and by loop iterations. V8's interrupt budget lives
  in the function's FeedbackCell; each JumpLoop charges it the loop's bytecode length
  (JumpBackward, src/interpreter/interpreter-assembler.cc:1179-1256), and a budget is
  invocations times the function's bytecode length (ScaleInterruptBudget and
  TieringManager::InterruptBudgetFor, src/execution/tiering-manager.cc:187-190, 268-291; fetched
  at c8b709085e21 into target/p2/tiering/ext-v8), 8 invocations to the first tier-up (feedback
  vectors and Sparkplug, flag-definitions.h:1110). SpiderMonkey's warm-up counter takes a call
  or a loop head alike, 10 to the Baseline Interpreter and 100 to the Baseline JIT
  (vm/Interpreter.cpp:2048-2091; jit/JitOptions.cpp:209-222). JSC's execution counter takes 15
  an entry and 1 a loop iteration, 500 to Baseline (OptionsList.h:369-376).
- A running activation enters compiled code at its next loop header once the function has it:
  V8's JumpLoop checks for baseline code at every backedge (interpreter-generator.cc:2472-2541),
  SpiderMonkey's LoopHead enters the Baseline Interpreter there, JSC's loop_osr enters Baseline
  at that loop (LLIntSlowPaths.cpp:491-536). Every loop header is an entry, nested ones included;
  V8 orders its optimizing OSR by loop depth (control-flow-builders.cc:81-91).

Design, in three steps, each landed with its own test and counts:
1. One budget per function. A call weighs 16 and a backedge on the tree-walker 1
   (FunctionRuntime gains `backedges`), and the function compiles once calls × 16 + backedges
   pass threshold × 16: at the default, 8 calls or 128 backedges in any mix, today's two numbers
   made one budget. That is JSC's proportion, nearly; SpiderMonkey's 1:1 would make a backedge
   worth a call, and V8's weighting by size needs sizes the tree-walker does not keep. Admission
   follows the function: once its budget is spent at a header visit, or whenever the function
   already has code, the next header visit of each of its tree-walked loops is admitted, so the
   call leaves the tree-walker there, by a frame transfer where one exists and otherwise into the
   loop's cached fragment. The budget compiles the function at that visit, where the call path
   would (not a derived constructor, a closure under `with`, a generator or an async function;
   threshold 0 and self-hosted code unchanged). A function that cannot compile and code outside
   functions keep today's per-execution admission. Expected: lin_solve's first call leaves the
   tree-walker within its first inner loop, and its later calls run compiled at any threshold.
2. Frame transfers at nested loop headers: a FunctionOsrEntry for each for, while and do-while
   loop whose enclosing statements are blocks, ifs, labels and other such loops, under the
   conditions body-level entries meet now (no try handler but runtime lexical scopes' own, no
   arguments object, inline depth 0). Excluded for the enclosing state they would need: try,
   catch and finally, with, switch, and for-in and for-of (their iterators live in the
   tree-walker's Rust frames). The tree-walker's enclosing statements receive the call's Return
   completion and run nothing more, as above a body-level transfer now. One compile then replaces
   a call's fragments; measured against step 1 before it lands.
3. Code outside functions (Scripts, modules, eval): admission by each loop site's backedges over
   all its executions, as V8 and SpiderMonkey count top-level code like a function's.

Measures: NavierStokes's fixed-work drivers (target/p2/navier-threshold) at thresholds 0, 1, 2, 4
and 8 for each step; the fixtures, chai and CodeLoad by instructions against main; Octane at 8 and
1 and TRust's replays on a quiet machine after step 1, since more functions compile (a call that
loops 128 times compiles its function even if nothing calls it again, which TRust pays per page).
DIAGNOSTICS.md and the CLI help state the new rule.

Tests: lin_solve's shape (loops nested in an if/else, 128 iterations each) at threshold 8, its
first call's loops entering fragments once the budget is spent and its second call compiled (step
1), and no fragment at all (step 2); a function that cannot compile and a Script's loops admitted
as now (step 1); shadowing, dead zones, captured block bindings, labeled break and continue and
per-iteration copies across a nested transfer (step 2).

### W2c step 2: words (design, 2026-11-01)

Step 1 landed (e92696a5): the census in the tree. The kind byte, which the design computed
beside today's storage in step 1, comes here, kept by the word block's own operations. A survey
of the change surface (an agent's, read-only, at e92696a5) sets this step's shape:
- Every element read hands out `&Property`: get_index (15 production sites, 13 outside
  value.rs), get, get_key, get_walking and get_with_slot_walking for index keys (`get` has 85
  production callers, index and named keys mixed and not separable statically), values,
  packed_values and indexed_properties, packed_array_elements (`&[Property]`, the searches) and
  plain_elements_mut (`&mut DenseDeque<Property>`, reverse and splice). A word has no Property
  to point at.
- The words must hang off Props itself. Nested in today's sidecar (DenseBuffers) and a
  PackedBuffer box, a 10-element array would take three blocks besides its cell, about 480 B
  against today's 448 and the model's 288.
- Packed storage also holds arguments objects' elements and ForInKeys' read-only entries
  (eval.rs:77-84), and non-plain descriptors (Props::insert over a slot, freeze_descriptors for
  tagged templates' arrays, SetIntegrityLevel through define_own_property_ordinary).
- Shift rotates words through shared slices (PropWord cells) after forcing a full trace; three
  numeric-chain stores index the classic slot map bounded by the mirror and are safe only because
  the packed exclusion is emitted first (jit.rs:16685-16693, 16761, 19295); twelve ARM64 sites
  hard-code the 16-byte element stride (`add_shifted ... 4`, gated on property_size == 16).

Four parts, each landed with its tests, and the dump checks where templates change:
2a. Element views, behaviour-preserving: index-key reads return an `Element<'_>`, a word's value
   or a named entry's `&Property`, with Property's read methods (value, accessor, getter, setter,
   writable, enumerable, configurable, number_value, is_plain_element) and to_property; `get` and
   its walking forms return the same view for every key (a named key's is always an entry); the
   slice accessors give way to value operations (the searches over values, reverse and splice
   over a value sequence). Storage, behaviour and dumps unchanged.
2b. The word block: one storage block of a 16-byte header {used, capacity, shifted, flags} and
   `capacity` words of Value bits, as SpiderMonkey's ObjectElements (vm/NativeObject.h), the
   pointer at the first word and the header before it; a static empty block for maps without
   elements, so generated code never tests for null; front room as `shifted`
   (numShiftedElements); a block over 64 words keeps its traced length and dirty bitmap in a
   side record the owner reports, as PackedBuffer does; the kind byte (NO_HOLES, NUMBERS, INT32)
   in the header's flags, kept by the block's operations, and verified against the words at each
   collection in debug builds. Unit-tested alone.
2c. The switch. DenseStorage gains the block as a word of its own (Props 112 -> 120 B; the cell
   stays in its 192 B class); the sidecar keeps the classic slot map, the mirror and the key
   index until step 3 removes them. InlinePacked and PackedBuffer go. A non-plain element (an
   accessor, or attributes other than the defaults) leaves the words for the named entries, its
   word a hole and `has_far` set (open question 1, SpiderMonkey's `Indexed`); until step 7's
   integrity kinds, a frozen or sealed array's elements leave the same way (tagged templates'
   strings arrays). Arguments objects keep words (their elements are plain); ForInKeys' entries,
   read-only, go to the named entries. ARM64 templates: an 8-byte stride (`lsl #3`), the hole
   test on the word, no meta word, bounds from the header's used length, and define-elem's
   append writing the word and bumping `used` and `length`; the collector traces used words.
   x86-64 calls helpers for every element operation and changes only where offsets move.
2d. Measures against the parent: the census's element bytes (Splay, chai, React, Djot,
   sustained), fat-LTO instruction counts, and the dump checks (the element templates only).

### Capture summaries in the static snapshot (design and record, 2026-11-01)

The plans' compile-time cost, as the owner chose at W3's stage end ("in the snapshot"), by an
agent on a branch, reviewed, rebased and landed as 9325b9c0. Snapshot version 6 begins each
function body's encoding with that function's capture summary: after the body's length, a LEB128
summary length, a byte of effects (0 when walking the function fails, else 1, plus 2 for an outer
`this` through its arrow chain, 4 for a direct eval, 8 for a `with`), and the free names, counted
and in ascending order; then the statements as before. The encoder computes each summary from
the parsed function with the CaptureScan that reads it, once a function (a memo for one
encoding gives each enclosing function its inner functions' summaries). A plan's or a
compilation's scan applies the stored summary of an inner function whose body keeps its
encoding, without decoding it; the per-bootstrap cache of a3cb1cbf and its guard are gone. A
mutable access to a body drops its encoding (FunctionBody's DerefMut), so an edited body has no
stored summary. Validation: both decoders check a summary's structure with its body, so the
first static decode accepts and rejects what an eager decode does; its content is trusted as the
statements' are (this build's encoder wrote it; snapshots never outlive a build), test builds
check every applied summary against walking the function, and a corrupt but well-formed summary
can only misresolve names within that snapshot, never address past a record (a plan's prefix
holds only its function's own bindings). V8 saves a lazily compiled function's preparse data
with it and restores it at its compile (src/ast/scopes.cc:792-797, 1840-1907).

TRust 78435d0's replays, user instructions a steady page against 15ff3892 (median of 3, outputs
identical): event-loop 961.5 -> 640.4 M (-33.4%), dom-reconcile 7,929.3 -> 7,608.9 M (-4.0%),
todomvc 2,763.3 -> 2,441.6 M (-11.6%): the prelude's plans 360.4 -> 55.6 M a page,
decode_function_body 218.5 -> 50.1 M. A process's first page pays 126 M once (summaries at TRust's
encode, 114.5 M; their checks at the first decode, 16 M); the prelude's snapshot is 2.86 MB, 390 KB
of it summaries; the runtime CLI's empty script +0.55% (its eager glue decodes check summaries
they do not use; inferred). Tests: every function's stored summary equals walking it (web and
node glue, the corpus, TRust's assembled prelude through TRUST_JS_SUMMARY_SOURCE, 7,965
functions); plans from stored and computed summaries and from the parsed source are equal; a
bootstrap's plan decodes no inner body. Checks: rustfmt, clippy in the three landing
configurations, the lib suite in full on Crystal (2,715) and zombie (2,524), TRust's tests (4,072
with three wall-clock tests rerun alone under the machine's load).

Found, not fixed (for the owner):
- No prelude function gets static name addressing, before or after: a plan applies an inner
  function's summary but never records it as a child (only walk_inner_fn does,
  bytecode.rs:5307), so no prelude function nested in another has an outer context; an
  event-loop page's 470 compiled name reads are all dynamic. Fixing it turns Decision 21 on across
  the prelude, which wants its own validation and measurement.
- About 12.5 M a page of other temporary decodes remain (scan_flags when object-literal methods
  are created, 9.2 M, and on nested arrows, 3.2 M), syntax-only and storable the same way.
- The one-time 114.5 M encode, and the plans' own 55.6 M a page, are the "fresh sources" part:
  folding reference collection into the parser, as V8's preparser does.

### W2c step 2c: the switch (record, 2026-11-01)

Five commits on p2-w2c-words, landed together: 97b9dfaf keeps array elements as words, c1fe1a65
moves the ARM64 element templates onto them, abea113b checks the block's size arithmetic (a 32-bit
target's large reservation could pass usize), 8100d1fe splices words in place, and 90512397 builds
literal words in one pass and keeps empty element storage cheap. The last two answer Vue's +0.20% at
c1fe1a65 (its JIT code unchanged, so Rust paths), found by microbenchmarks (target/p2/w2c, scratch
copies of the loops below), parent -> c1fe1a65 -> 90512397: a loop of `[i, i]` literals +9.10% ->
+0.20% (each word was pushed with its capacity check and kind upkeep into a block the growth path
made), object literals +1.60% -> -1.36% (DenseStorage's out-of-line clone and the record check at
creation), closures +1.49% -> -0.11%, a Proxy set trap +1.27% -> +0.66% (the call to insert's split
half, below), constructors (`new C(i)`) +0.48% -> +0.58%, a Proxy get trap +0.31% -> +0.31%, WeakMap
gets -0.85% -> -0.86%. Splitting Props::insert's named-entry half into a function (for the test-only
mutable escape) cost property stores a call; forcing it back in line (inline(always)) left a stale
pointer in a native frame, which the conservative stack scan kept alive
(payload_cell_tests::a_long_string_held_only_by_a_property_lives_and_dies_with_it and
jit::rooting_tests::context_return_slot_is_a_root failed, on zombie and once on Crystal), so
90512397 merges the halves back as they were before 2c and the escape moves a word with a
non-configurable define instead.

Against the design above, five choices were made in the code; each is the owner's to overrule:
- ForInKeys' keys are plain words, not named entries: no script reaches that object, so its
  descriptors are unobservable, and a word is half an entry.
- The remembered record (traced length and a dirty bit per 64 words, for blocks over 64 words)
  lives inside the block, after its last word, instead of in a side record the owner reports:
  one block, and every write through Words::set marks its block. Growth that keeps indices
  keeps the record, as PackedBuffer's deque growth kept its own; shift, unshift, reverse,
  clones and whole-map moves trace everything next. Numbers and holes never mark a block.
- Repeated unshifts keep front room of the used words (SpiderMonkey's tryUnshiftDenseElements
  reserves ahead the same way; P1's deque prepend reserved max(len, 8)): exact room would have
  made them quadratic.
- get_index answers a named element the slot map records (a non-plain element beside words,
  a frozen array's elements), not only words: copy_numeric_array on a frozen array regressed
  without it.
- The loop regions' mirror mode becomes words mode. The preamble proves the receiver's words in
  use, Numbers and hole-free (and int32 for int reads) by their kind bits, or else by a scan,
  as P1's packed mirror preparation scanned (Props::reprove_words); a new KIND_SCANNED bit keeps
  a failed proof from rescanning on every entry until a write could make a fact true again.
  Region stores canonicalize NaN (a word is a Value's bits; a NaN payload must not read as a
  tag). Classic storage (the TRUST_JS_DENSE_ELEMENTS=0 ablation, first writes far past the end,
  frozen arrays, ordinary objects with index keys) runs its loops outside the region; step 3
  removes the classic slot map and mirror anyway.

Two bugs the tests found, fixed before landing: the chain's word read moved the word into the
key's register before its number guard, so a bailout replayed `x[NaN]` (a string element read
as NaN); and get_index stopped at an all-holes block beside named elements (frozen arrays).

Checks at 90512397: rustfmt; clippy in the three landing configurations on Crystal and zombie
(x86-64), and zombie's aarch64 cross clippy; the lib suite in full on Crystal (2,720) and zombie
(2,529); the census, dumps and outputs were taken at c1fe1a65 (no later commit changes a template,
JitLayout or a block's size); Octane at five runs and the fixtures give the parent's outputs (zlib's
run count prints its heap array, which varies from run to run in the parent too); the classic
ablation (TRUST_JS_DENSE_ELEMENTS=0) runs the fixtures with the parent's outputs.

Element bytes (TRUST_JS_GC_CENSUS, each run's collection with the most array bytes; fat-LTO
engines, Crystal):

| chai (minor) | 3,300, 2.69 MB | 3,207, 1.44 MB | 1.29 MB |

Instruction counts, fat-LTO engines from `git archive` exports (w3-i3b/build.sh), pinned to
