Vue, Raytrace and zlib are the regressions, all small: Vue's JIT code is unchanged (its chunks
dump identically), so its +0.25% is in Rust paths, of the kind the constructor and Proxy
microbenchmarks above still show (each object's storage word more to initialize, copy and check);
step 3, which retires the sidecar's slot map and mirror, works on the same paths. React and Djot
run fewer collections (React 17 to 10 minor, Djot 20 minor and a major to 17 minor).

Dumps: DenseStorage's third word moved Props' fields (rustc reordered them: the named entries from
offset 0 to 0x48), so every chunk that reads a named field changes its immediates and the gate's
hashes differ (React 12 of 12 chunks, Djot 155-160 of 163; Vue's 56 are identical). With
load/store and add offsets masked as well (target/p2/w2c/maskcmp.py), React's chunks with element
sites (6 of 12) and Djot's (72 of 163) differ, and all but two shrink (67,602 to 66,687 and
676,596 to 675,862 words); Djot's two longer chunks grew in every instruction kind, calls included
(another compile's content, not a template's).

### W2c step 3: words for every object; no slot map, mirror or sidecar (design, 2026-11-01)

The owner's decision (2026-11-01), revisiting open question 7: ordinary objects get element words
as arrays do, as V8, SpiderMonkey and JSC give every object element storage. The question came
back with step 3 because the classic slot map is what gives an ordinary object with integer keys
(`{0: 7, 1: 8}`) inline element reads, stores, `in` and updates in ARM64 generated code today
(jit_update_elem_tests.rs:105, jit_native_operations_tests.rs:415 and jit_count_guard_tests.rs:43
assert they stay native); keeping those keys in the named entries would have sent them to the
helpers.

Step 2 left the classic storage beside the words, on paths the census finds almost no live data
on: at each workload's largest collection chai keeps 286 objects with a slot map (4.4 KB) and a
mirror (4.4 KB), almost all of them frozen ones, Djot 8 objects (4.7 KB of slot map), the others
none; no array keeps either (target/p2/w2c/census, c1fe1a65). What still uses them:
- The classic slot map (DenseBuffers.elems: index to named-entry slot, NO_SLOT a hole) finds an
  index key in the named entries: every index key of an ordinary object, an array's non-plain or
  far elements, a frozen array's elements (freeze_descriptors moves its words there), and every
  element under the TRUST_JS_DENSE_ELEMENTS=0 ablation.
- The numeric mirror (DenseBuffers.mirror, Props::mirror_flags and mirror_holes) shadows the slot
  map's Number elements for the ARM64 templates' classic read paths, which also chase the slot map
  to the entry and test its descriptor (51 instructions a read against 33 through words).
- The sidecar (DenseBuffers) carries both and the named-key index.

The exemplars keep the indices outside their dense storage in a hash table: V8's
NumberDictionary, SpiderMonkey's shape table (an `Indexed` object's sparse indices are ordinary
properties), JSC's SparseArrayValueMap; and all three keep an ordinary object's dense indices in
the same element storage as an array's (V8's JSObject elements, SpiderMonkey's NativeObject
dense elements, JSC's butterfly), without a `length`.

Design:
1. Words for every map: a word is a plain data element and the rules are step 2's (a non-plain
   element, an index past the sparse frontier, 256 for now and step 6's limit, and a frozen
   object's elements live in the named entries). For a non-array nothing couples the words to a
   `length`: appends, `length` and the array builtins' storage paths stay Array-only (elem_mode).
   Which maps that reaches (the survey of the exotic kinds): ordinary objects (plain objects,
   functions, the global object, DataView and ArrayBuffer), Map and Set, the primitive wrappers,
   errors and iterators, arguments objects (already words; a mapped one's parameter aliases stay
   in its exotic methods) and ForInKeys. String wrappers keep their characters as named entries
   (non-writable, non-configurable, so never words), their indices past the length are ordinary.
   Typed arrays, proxies, module namespaces, Web IDL indexed hosts and document.all intercept
   index keys before the map, so they never hold one.
2. Lookup. Props::find stops consulting a slot map: an index key in the named entries is found as
   any key is (the shared layout's index, the map's own index past INDEX_THRESHOLD keys, else a
   scan of at most eight), and `has_far` comes to mean SpiderMonkey's `Indexed`: some index key
   lives in the named entries, on any map. While it is clear, an index key that is not a word is
   absent with no lookup (find's and get_key's shortcut today). should_build_index counts index
   keys like any other, so a frozen array's elements are a hash probe, not a scan.
3. Prototype and indexed proofs. The proofs that a chain holds no index key
   (array_prototypes_unshadowed, read by the elided `arguments` reads, the searches, shift,
   unshift, push, fill, the iterators and spread) already read words and named index entries
   alike (Props::indexed_properties), and a word added to a marked prototype bumps the creation
   epoch through insert's note_structural, so they stay sound when Object.prototype or a class
   prototype keeps words. The named read cache could not see words: a canonical index name never
   fills it now (c294f96b, which fixed `const {"0": v} = ["a"]` reading a prototype's element in
   the bytecode and JIT tiers).
   Object-literal templates place their values by named position (instantiate_plain), so a
   literal with an index key (`{"0": a, b: 1}`) stops qualifying for one and builds its keys one
   by one.
4. Shapes: index keys leave the shapes of ordinary objects, as they already have for arrays'
   (shape transitions track named keys only): `{0: 1, a: 2}` and `{a: 2}` share `a`'s slot.
   Literal templates keep their index keys as words (instantiate_plain clones them).
5. The mirror goes: mirror_flags and mirror_holes leave Props, the mirror leaves the sidecar,
   with every upkeep call (mirror_sync, mirror_grow, mirror_invalidate's callers, MIRROR_*). The
   words are the f64 view.
6. The sidecar goes: DenseStorage's first word becomes the key index itself
   (Option<StorageBox<KeyIndex>>), so a map past eight named keys keeps one block for its index
   instead of a sidecar wrapping it (design 1's "a word of its own"). The creation probe
   (jit.rs:11717) and set_prop_inlinable read that word now. DenseDeque goes with its last users.
7. Generated code (ARM64): the element templates keep their word paths and accept every receiver
   that has words; a map with no words in use goes to the checked operation. The classic paths
   (slot map, entry chase, descriptor guards) and the mirror paths (get-elem's and elem-local's
   mirror reads, the chains' mirror pins and mirror-slim stores, emit_mirror_store) go; the
   chain's typed-array marker (mlreg = usize::MAX) gets a mode of its own. JitLayout loses the
   deque and mirror fields and the probes that served them, so Interp's offsets move and every
   chunk's dump changes; the element templates shrink.
8. The census and diagnostics keep their columns (sidecar, slot map, mirror at zero) so the exit
   compares with step 1's lines; retained_requested_storage_bytes counts the Symbol map without
   a sidecar too (it counts it only beside one today).

Equivalence: a word is the property {[[Value]]: w, writable, enumerable, configurable}, a hole
absence, as for arrays (W2c's design 4); for an ordinary object the transition between words and
named entries is the same argument without `length`. [[OwnPropertyKeys]] already sorts every
integer index first (ordered_keys reads words and named index keys alike).

Risks: an index key that reaches the named entries without `has_far` would read as absent
(find's and get_key's shortcut, eval.rs 3133-3188, bytecode.rs 25757-25760), so every path that
places one sets it; code that read "no sidecar" or `elems.len() != 0` as "no index keys"
(named_data_keys, observed_instance_capacity, try_copy_named_data_from) reads words and `has_far`.
Test262 (the Object, Array, String and arguments suites), difftest and GC stress on the element
tests are the checks, with the three tests above as canaries for the native paths.

Order, on a branch, landed together after the counts, the census and zombie:
3a. The mirror goes (design 5, the templates' mirror paths of design 7).
3b. Words for every object and the slot map goes (designs 1-4, the classic paths of design 7, the
    ablation).
3c. The sidecar goes (designs 6 and 8).

### W2c step 3: words for every object (record, 2026-11-02)

Five commits on p2-w2c3, landed together: 15f7b113 retires the numeric mirror (3a); 7fd5b8ee
keeps every map's plain index keys as element words and retires the classic slot map with the
TRUST_JS_DENSE_ELEMENTS ablation (3b); 686b44ef keeps a map's key index in DenseStorage's first
word instead of a sidecar around it (3c); 0ddef2cb copies literal words in one pass and tests
their kinds without branches (a codegen regression the counts found at 3b, below); bb51e2ff
documents the census columns. Choices made in the code, each the owner's to overrule:
- An ordinary object's index key in its named entries (a non-plain element, one past the sparse
  frontier, a frozen object's) still transitions its shape, as SpiderMonkey's shape keeps an
  `Indexed` object's sparse indices as properties: its slot precedes later named keys', and an
  ordinary object's inline caches trust a shape's slots (an array's caches re-check the key, so
  its named index keys stay out of its shape as before). Words never enter a shape (design 4).
- An object literal with an index key (`{"0": a, b: 1}`) keeps MakeObject without a template:
  its keys insert one by one (design 3). The creation cache already refused digit names
  (try_ic_create), so no path places an index key in the named entries without `has_far`.
- With an index among the named entries, later indices join it there until a truncation past
  them (the classic slot map kept such an array's later elements addressable in generated
  code). Densification belongs to step 6, as SpiderMonkey's maybeDensifySparseElements and V8's
  ShouldConvertToFastElements.
- The element templates need no "no words" test: the static empty block's used length is zero,
  so the bounds compare misses every index of a map without words.
- The JitLayout Vec probes (vec_ptr_off, vec_len_off, vec_cap_off and the Vec<u32> and
  DenseDeque checks) served only the slot map and went in 3b, DenseDeque with them; the
  population census's `sidecars` column now counts maps with a key index of their own.

The counts at 3b found two regressions, located by building each step (fat LTO, user-mode
instructions, median of 3; Octane at a fixed 5 runs under the corrected driver, below; zlib's
first run alone in the last row):
| | c294f96b | 3a | 3b | 3c | 0ddef2cb |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Splay | 1,898.6 M | 1,896.2 M | 1,911.1 M | 1,911.1 M | 1,890.4 M |
| zlib | 166,308.8 M | 166,197.5 M | 167,325.4 M | 167,325.5 M | 167,325.2 M |
| zlib, one run | 34,356.4 M | 34,328.8 M | 34,548.8 M | | 34,548.6 M |
- Splay: no generated code changed (every chunk within 4 bytes); array literals cost five more
  instructions a word. Words::from_values' source is unchanged, but with Words::with_capacity
  inlined into make_array_from_raw, LLVM no longer merged the iterator's bound and `take` into
  one count. The literal paths (the JIT window, make_array's Vec) now copy through
  Words::from_words, whose loops have the slice's single bound: up to eight words a scalar loop
  (a volatile store keeps LLVM from splitting it into a memcpy call and a vectorized fold, whose
  fixed cost is more than a short literal's), past eight that memcpy and fold out of line; and
  word_kind (on every element write as well) tests a Number's tag with two range compares and
  builds the kind bits without branches, checked against Value::number's tag table for every
  16-bit tag. Against 3a: literals of 1, 3, 10 and 30 elements -2.67%, -1.59%, -2.22% and
  -12.18%, Splay's payload pattern -0.60%, object literals +0.24%.
- zlib: +0.68% at 3b against 3a over five runs, the first run +0.64% and each later one +0.69%
  (the old driver showed +0.40% against 3a and +0.35% against c294f96b, diluted by 20.9 G of
  its own work, below). A register region that the profitability test
  (Emission::profitable: emitted bytes at most twice the ops' fixed baseline charges plus 1 KB)
  refused at 3a fits at 3b, because its element templates shrank: zlib's hottest function (a8,
  Emscripten's deflate loop) compiles to 353 KB instead of 273 KB with 136 name sites in
  regions instead of 30, and runs about 12% more instructions. The step only exposes it; why
  that region is slower than the templates it replaces (its name-element fallbacks and the
  flushes around them), and charging the baselines from the templates' real sizes, are
  follow-ups for the region tier.

### Octane's fixed-run driver, corrected (2026-11-02)

The fixed-run driver behind Octane's instruction counts since W3's exit
(target/p2/w3-exit/codeload/fixed-driver.js) measured two benchmarks wrongly, and the count
scripts hid it: they piped perf's output and dropped each run's exit status.
- zlib. The driver kept its run count N and its loop indices in globals. zlib's first run
  assigns its own global N, a 16M-element Float64Array (Emscripten's heap view), so the loop
  test after that run converted the array to a string, read NaN and stopped, and the "ran"
  line converted it again (which W2c step 2's checks, and this step's before this correction,
  read as heap words that vary between runs). Every earlier zlib figure at a fixed N is its
  load, one run and two 16M-number joins: 55,252 M at c294f96b, of which the joins are 20.9 G,
  against 34,356 M for the load and one run alone. Counting calls to each benchmark's run
  function found every other benchmark at its N runs.
- RegExp. The driver ran each Setup without BenchmarkSuite.ResetRNG, which Octane's RunStep
  calls at each suite's start, so Math.random was the engine's, seeded afresh per process.
  RegExp's 32,958 draws build its input strings, and its sum missed 1666109 at the end of
  setup's own run, which threw and ended the process (exit 1). Its figures are its load and
  that run, on other strings: random strings cost about a quarter of the strings Octane's
  generator makes, in Node as in TRust (ten runs 78 against 325 ms in Node 24, 300 against
  1,222 ms in TRust), so the old 314 M was about a ninth of the corrected 2,885 M (setup and
  five runs). Splay's 8,400 keys were random per process too (its runs spread 0.7 M; now
  within 6 K).

The corrected driver (target/p2/octane-fixed): prelude.js, loaded after base.js, calls
ResetRNG before the benchmark files load (Crypto draws 128 numbers for its pool at load, which
spread its runs by 1 M; now within 8 K); driver.js resets the generator at each suite's start
as RunStep does, keeps its state inside a function, and prints a completion line last. The
count scripts (w2c/counts.sh, w2c3/steps.sh, w3-i3b/fixcounts2.sh) print FAIL for a run that
exits nonzero or lacks that line, and outputs.sh marks it INCOMPLETE. Octane's timed runs use
upstream run.js, whose RunSuites resets the generator and keeps its state in closures, so they
were never affected; only Crypto, RegExp, Splay and CodeLoad (15 draws) use Math.random.

Earlier records' zlib and RegExp figures at a fixed N stand for that other work: the tiering
steps' zlib -4.6% and RegExp +2.8%, W2c step 2's zlib +0.02% and RegExp -0.89%. Against the
old driver, the function-scoped driver adds 0.16 M instructions to Richards and 0.69 M to
EarleyBoyer, and the prelude 0.06 M and 2.5 M (an empty script in its place adds 0.16 M). What
loads before a benchmark moves some counts by itself: declaring one unrelated global before
earley-boyer.js lowers EarleyBoyer's by 12.6 M (0.35%), and PdfJS's and Gameboy's step-3
changes moved by 0.16 and 0.08 points between the drivers. Changes that small on those
benchmarks are within that band.

### W2c step 4: stores of any value, hole fills and appends in line (design, 2026-11-02)

The owner's go-ahead (2026-11-02, after the fixed-run driver's correction). The ARM64 store
templates (SetElem, SetElemDrop, SetElemLocal, SetElemLocalDrop) overwrite an existing element
with a Number and send everything else to H_SET_ELEM, about 450 instructions a store (2M appends
of a 500-element fill: 1,121 M against 219 M without the stores, CPU 0-3, w2c3d).

What reaches the helper (a census build of 7ebe314b, every jit_set_elem call classified by key,
receiver, value and where the index lands; target/p2/w2c4/census; Octane at five runs):
| | stores | overwrite, not a Number | overwrite into a large block | append with room | hole | no words | full | string key |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chai | 12,901,012 | 325 | 0 | 7,205,487 | 1,238,792 | 1,513,600 | 9,015 | 2,933,695 |
| Vue | 61,829 | 61,765 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 64 | 0 | 0 |
| Gameboy | 1,496,814 | 0 | 0 | 1,496,222 | 0 | 40 | 552 | 0 |
| TypeScript | 1,396,650 | 391,795 | 74,421 | 471,753 | 123 | 191,903 | 1,975 | 264,663 |
| PdfJS | 1,046,008 | 53 | 0 | 1,031,846 | 412 | 2,928 | 5,939 | 4,625 |
| NavierStokes | 228,252 | 0 | 0 | 228,006 | 0 | 12 | 234 | 0 |
| Box2D | 51,931 | 23,018 | 13,725 | 10,436 | 3,365 | 1,314 | 73 | 0 |
| Crypto | 15,878 | 0 | 0 | 6,479 | 8,501 | 409 | 329 | 0 |
Every u32-keyed store lands on an Array (bar TypeScript's 15,920 into ordinary objects without
words). No Number overwrite reaches the helper (the template takes them). The appends are
Numbers and cells alike (chai 6.1 M Numbers, 1.1 M cells; Gameboy 0.8 M and 0.7 M); chai's holes
are its 216,320 top-down fills (each starts as "no words" past index 0, then fills down); "no
words" is an array's first element, which allocates its block; a "large block" keeps a
remembered record (more than 64 words of room) and the index lies below its traced length;
appends into large blocks (Gameboy, PdfJS, NavierStokes, every one) lie past it. chai's string
keys are named stores through a computed key (`o[k] = v` in its for-in loops), outside W2c.
React and Djot reach the helper 0 and 200 times.

What the exemplars do (V8 c8b709085e21, SpiderMonkey bf75471716c5, JSC 260278048486; the files
in target/p2/w3-design/v8-src and target/p2/w2c4/exemplars):
- None inlines an element store in its baseline tier. Sparkplug calls KeyedStoreIC
  (baseline-compiler.cc:1214-1225), whose handlers are one shared builtin per store mode
  (builtins-definitions.h:517-527; EmitElementStore, code-stub-assembler.cc:14985-15163);
  SpiderMonkey's baseline tiers run CacheIR stubs shared between sites by their IR ("allows us
  to share IC code between caches", CacheIR.h:36-41; BaselineCacheIRCompiler.cpp:2057-2108);
  JSC calls a VM-wide handler chain (InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:620-623). Maglev, V8's inlining
  tier, emits the map check, the bound and the store in line, its growth too
  (maglev-graph-builder.cc:5494-5609, 5523-5582). The baselines' call per store leans on a later
  tier that inlines it; TRust JS has one tier, which inlines its templates as Maglev does.
- Any value over an existing element: SpiderMonkey's stub has no value-kind logic
  (CacheIRCompiler.cpp:7312-7355); V8 and JSC change the elements kind in the runtime, because
  their double kinds store unboxed doubles (code-stub-assembler.cc:15067, 15094;
  InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:2592-2615). TRust's words are NaN-boxed whatever their kind, so the
  kind bits clear in line, as SpiderMonkey's NON_PACKED flag does.
- Appends: V8 grows a packed array only when the key equals `length` (CheckForCapacityGrow,
  code-stub-assembler.cc:15165-15228) and refuses a read-only `length` when it installs the
  handler (ic.cc:3142-3144); SpiderMonkey checks extensibility, a writable `length` and the
  prototype chain when it attaches AddAndStoreDenseElement (CacheIR.cpp:4956-5030), and at run
  time only the shape and the capacity (CacheIRCompiler.cpp:7357-7382; prepareOOBStoreElement,
  MacroAssembler.cpp:10278-10360); JSC stores past publicLength within vectorLength
  (InlineCacheCompiler.cpp:2636-2643). TRust's sites have no attach step, so each store checks
  extensibility and `length` itself.
- Holes: SpiderMonkey shares the append's operation and guards Array.prototype and
  Object.prototype with a realm fuse, one load and a branch (CacheIR.cpp:804-809;
  CacheIRCompiler.cpp:11868-11880), popped when either gains an indexed property or its first
  dense element (Watchtower.cpp:162-193, 348-361). TRust's elems_protector is that fuse, an
  epoch-keyed proof. V8 installs the handler only while no prototype holds read-only elements,
  with a validity cell (ic.cc:3165-3175); JSC converts every array when a prototype gains an
  indexed accessor (haveABadTime, JSGlobalObject.cpp:2765-2798).
- The barrier: V8 emits none for its Smi and double kinds and skips Smis before its page-flag
  tests (code-generator-arm64.cc:1290-1295); SpiderMonkey skips values typed as non-GC and tests
  the value's tag first (CacheIRCompiler.cpp:9197-9206), recording the whole cell, or past 256
  elements the slot (VMFunctions.cpp:1052-1076); JSC filters only on the owner's cell state
  (JITPropertyAccess.cpp:262-265). TRust's card records the whole owner, as SpiderMonkey's and
  JSC's whole-cell entries do, and is skipped for a Number, as V8's number kinds skip theirs;
  its 64-word dirty bitmap is SpiderMonkey's per-slot entries past 256.
- Growth and the first element: in V8's shared builtin (code-stub-assembler.cc:7172-7248), in
  SpiderMonkey's pure ABI call one past capacity (addDenseElementPure,
  MacroAssembler.cpp:10295-10312), in JSC's C++ slow path; not in this step.

Design (ARM64; x86-64 has no element templates and keeps the helper):
1. One store sequence for the four forms, after today's receiver, key and plainness guards
   (x9 the index, x10 the cell, x11 the Object):
   - Overwrite (index below the used words, the word no hole): a Number as today (kind bits
     through emit_word_number_kind). Any other value clears NUMBERS and INT32, which is then
     exact, so KIND_SCANNED stays; a NUMBERS block is never traced (Words::trace_values), so
     the clear is what keeps a stored cell alive. The owner's card is marked (emit_card_mark,
     as every other generated store of a reference). A block with a remembered record (room
     past 64 words, front room included) takes the helper, whose Words::set marks the dirty
     block: the W2c design's decision 4 (in line with W2f's barrier work); TypeScript's 74,421
     and Box2D's 13,725 are the only such stores measured.
   - Creation (a hole below the used words, or the index equal to the used length with room):
     [[Set]] finds no own property, so OrdinarySet consults the prototype chain and then
     CreateDataProperty on the receiver (ECMA-262 §10.1.9.2, §10.4.2.1). The sequence proves
     what fast_set_elem proves in Rust: an Array, extensible, not itself a marked prototype (a
     structural change to one must bump the creation epoch: note_structural), no index among
     its named entries (has_far clear, so the index is absent), its prototype the active
     realm's %Array.prototype%, and the protector proof Interp::elems_protector live at the
     current PROTO_EPOCH (the canonical Array.prototype -> Object.prototype chain has no index
     key, no exotic hop, and every structural change to either bumps the epoch). A stale proof
     takes the helper, whose array_prototypes_unshadowed renews it. A realm switch resets it.
     An append at or past `length` also needs a writable data `length` (its slot memo, as the
     map/filter append) and sets it to index + 1; an append below `length` (after
     `new Array(n)`, or a length store) and a hole fill (below the used words, which never pass
     `length`) leave it. The kind bits: an append keeps NO_HOLES and KIND_SCANNED (no cleared
     fact can become true) and clears NUMBERS/INT32 as the value breaks them; a hole fill may
     make NO_HOLES true again, so it clears KIND_SCANNED, with NUMBERS/INT32 as an overwrite.
     A value that is not a Number marks the owner's card; an append lies past the traced
     length, so it needs no dirty block (the map/filter append's site E); a hole fill of such
     a value into a block with a record takes the helper.
   - Everything else keeps the helper: an index past the used words by more than one (a gap),
     a full block or none (growth and the first element allocate), an ordinary object's
     creation (its chain is not the protector's), string and other keys, and the TDZ marker
     as the value (it would become a hole).
2. Every check precedes the first write; the commit is the word, the kind bits, the card, the
   used length and `length`, with no safepoint between them.
3. The sequence is in line at every site, as the existing templates are and as Maglev inlines
   its element stores; about 60 instructions more per store site, about 1% of the fixtures'
   code (Vue 30 store sites in 610 KB, Djot 144 in 3.8 MB, React none).
4. A truncation (`a.length = n`) that removes only words keeps the named entries' slot memos,
   key index and shape (Props::remove_indices_from reset them all, a leftover of the named index
   keys step 3 retired), so an `a.length = 0` refill appends in native code from its first word
   instead of finding `length` through the helper.
5. The region size test. Emission::profitable admits a register region when its bytes are at
   most twice its operations' fixed charges plus 1 KB (jit_regions.rs baseline_bytes: 1,536
   for every element and property operation, 2,048 for calls, 768 for names). The charges
   stay fixed while the templates change, so step 3's smaller element templates admitted
   zlib's a8 region (+0.61% at five runs) and step 4's larger stores could refuse others.
   Regions are emitted before their baseline code, so the test cannot read the baseline's
   real bytes. The candidate: charge each operation family the bytes its template emits,
   measured once per process into a scratch assembler. Today's charges are several times the
   real sizes (an element read's template is a few hundred bytes against 1,536), so real charges under the
   same rule would refuse most regions; the rule would need its own calibration. Measured
   after the stores land, over every workload; it lands only if no workload regresses, and
   otherwise zlib's region stays a region-tier follow-up (its slow paths' cost in the work
   estimate, not its size).

Predicted, from the census and about 350-400 instructions saved per store (450 for the helper
against 50-100 in line): chai -3% to -4% (8.4 M stores), Gameboy -2%, PdfJS -4% to -5%,
NavierStokes -12% to -15%, TypeScript -0.5%, Vue -1%, Box2D and Crypto small; Richards,
DeltaBlue, RayTrace, EarleyBoyer, Splay, Mandreel, CodeLoad and the other fixtures unchanged.

Not in this step: the first element and growth (chai 1.5 M, TypeScript 0.19 M), which allocate;
a pure call that grows the block without the helper's dispatch (SpiderMonkey's
addDenseElementPure) would save part of it. String-keyed stores (chai 2.9 M) are named stores.

Risks: cells stored by generated code into old arrays (the card) and the NUMBERS clear (the
collector skips NUMBERS blocks): GC stress over the new tests and the element tests; the
protector (Array.prototype or Object.prototype gaining an index, a setter, a proto change, a
realm switch, another realm's arrays): tests that change each after the sites have run; the
dump checks change in every chunk with a store site.

### W2c step 4: stores of any value, hole fills and appends (record, 2026-11-02)

Seven commits on p2-w2c4: 2ddae859 the design; 23b33a70 keeps the named entries' slot memos,
key index and shape when a truncation removes only words (Design 4); b53f122b stores any value,
fills holes and appends in the ARM64 element templates; 9805f6f2, 796e5ca4 and 6ca7fab2 settle
where the sequence lives (below); 808839e6 tests stored cells across explicit collections (it
fails without the owner's card mark, in the collector's old-to-young verification).

Where the sequence lives (Design 3 changed). In line at every site (b53f122b) it grew
Mandreel's code 7.1%, Gameboy's 5.7% and zlib's 11.7% (5,772 store sites, typed-array stores
almost all) and moved region admissions both ways: zlib's deflate region, which step 3
admitted, grew past the 128 KB per-function region budget (129 to 143 KB) and was refused (zlib
-0.67%, back to step 3a's count), while ten Mandreel decisions flipped (+0.44%). The creation
sequence as a shared stub outside hot loops (9805f6f2) and everywhere measured alike: these
sites are almost never in code the emitter marks hot. The sites now keep only the Number
overwrite they had and send every other store to one shared stub, SharedStub::ElemStore, three
instructions a site (6ca7fab2), as SpiderMonkey's and JSC's baselines share their store
handlers: code +0.02% (Vue), +0.03% (Djot), React unchanged, +0.30% (zlib), +0.64% (Mandreel),
+0.21% (Gameboy), and one region decision moves in zlib and one in Mandreel. The call and its
re-derived checks cost about ten instructions a store: chai -3.39% against -3.54% in line,
NavierStokes -12.55% against -12.87%.

chai's store helper calls 12.90 M -> 4.46 M (SetElemDrop 9.98 -> 2.63 M, SetElemLocalDrop 2.92
-> 1.82 M), the 8.44 M the census put in line; the rest are string keys (2.93 M), first
elements (1.51 M) and growth.

Counts against 7ebe314b (fat LTO, Crystal; user-mode instructions on CPUs 10-13, setarch -R,
TRUST_JS_GC_PACING=0, median of 3; Octane at a fixed 5 runs):

The predictions held: chai -3% to -4%, PdfJS -4% to -5%, NavierStokes -12% to -15%, Gameboy -2%,
Vue -1%, TypeScript -0.5%. The regressions are small: Mandreel +0.27%, one region (function
@1746839, head 3400) growing from 3,820 to 3,832 bytes past the size test (twice its
operations' fixed charges plus 1 KB) and refused; Djot +0.04%, DeltaBlue and CodeLoad +0.03%,
React and Richards +0.01%, in the templates (Djot's +0.08% at b53f122b bisected to them, not to
23b33a70, with unchanged region decisions). zlib +0.06%: its step-3 region is admitted again.

Measurement: builds that differ only in a template's layout move Gameboy's count by 1.3%
(22,521 M against 22,819 M, with identical region decisions): its named inline caches re-derive
and its typed-array length path run at different rates (Interp::ic_get_rederive 0.51% against
1.31% of instructions, array_buffer_byte_length 5.99% against 4.14%), as caches indexed by heap
addresses and shape ids collide differently. Changes that small on Gameboy are within that band.

The region size test (Design 5): not changed. Djot's baseline templates (w2c3d) measure 176 bytes
for an element read against the 1,536 charged, 328-468 for property reads, 312-537 for calls
against 2,048 and 68 for a name read against 768, while a strict comparison takes 568 against
192 and a peeking jump 260 against 128: real charges under the same rule would refuse or admit
regions throughout, a new heuristic rather than a correction. The flips above (12 bytes
refusing a Mandreel region; zlib's region crossing the budget) say the admission rules decide
on proxies that any template change moves, and zlib's step-3 region is slower in its work (its
name-element fallbacks and flushes), which the work estimate does not see: a region-tier item
of its own.

Checks: rustfmt; clippy in the three landing configurations on Crystal and zombie (x86-64), and
zombie's aarch64 cross clippy; the lib suite on Crystal (2,595) and zombie (2,525);
TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1 on the 13 store tests and the value, dense-slot, index-name,
numeric-array, update-element, native-operation, count-guard, array-shift and array-length
tests; Test262 in full at --tier=jit threshold 8 (53,575 of 53,575, the report identical to
7ebe314b's), difftest (138 agree, 12 skipped, as at 7ebe314b), the fixtures' masked outputs and
Octane's at five runs as 7ebe314b's.

### W2c step 5: the collector (record, 2026-11-02)

The collector's part landed with step 2 (32df6f1f, 97b9dfaf): Words::trace_values reports a
block's used words, never its capacity, and nothing for a NUMBERS block, and a minor collection's
remembered trace of a block over 64 words reports its dirty runs and the words past its traced
length. A trace census of main 86b0cd4f (every element block the marker's traces visit, by
purpose; target/p2/w2c5/census): Splay's full traces skip 665,225 NUMBERS blocks (6.65 M words)
and report 12 blocks of one word each, where the W2c census found 9.76 M words traced, all
Numbers (5.05% of its instructions). The blocks a trace reports whose words are in fact all
Numbers (a NUMBERS bit cleared and never proven again) number at most 95 in any workload
(EarleyBoyer, 589 words), so the marker does not prove the bits again as it scans; V8 and
SpiderMonkey never take a kind back either.

What step 2's design promised and did not land: the kind bits verified at each collection in
debug builds. a5f34fda: Words::trace_values asserts in debug builds, at every trace, that each
kind bit set holds of the words, since the collector skips a NUMBERS block's words and
generated code trusts all three bits. Checks: rustfmt; clippy in the three landing
configurations on Crystal and zombie (x86-64), and zombie's aarch64 cross clippy; the lib suite
on Crystal (2,595) and zombie (2,525) with the assertion on; TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1 on the store,
value, dense-slot, index-name, numeric-array, update-element, native-operation, count-guard,
array-shift, array-length and word-block tests (108). Release builds are unchanged.

### W2c step 6: the sparse limit and the way back to words (design, 2026-11-02)

The owner's go-ahead for W2c's remaining steps (2026-11-02, "all approved"), with the W2c
design's open question 5 answered as recommended: V8's limits. Today an index more than 256 past
the used words, or a map's first index past 256, goes to the named entries (`has_far`), and
every later index joins it there until a truncation past them. The far-index repros
(target/p2/w2c-census/edge, main 86b0cd4f, CPU 17, a loaded machine): filling and reading back
200,000 elements after `a = [1, 2, 3]; a[1000] = 0` takes 117-119 ms against 8-9 ms without the
far write (Node 24: 11-16 ms); 20,000 after `a = []; a[300] = 0` 11-12 ms, 40,000 23-25 ms (Node
1 ms); 20,000 after a first write at 100,000 12 ms (Node 13 ms, which goes to its dictionary as
well).

What the exemplars do (V8 c8b709085e21, SpiderMonkey bf75471716c5, JSC 260278048486):
- V8, going slow (ShouldConvertToSlowElements, js-objects-inl.h:1266-1286): an index below the
  capacity stays fast; one at least kMaxGap = 1024 past it goes to the dictionary
  (js-objects.h:966); otherwise the new capacity NewElementsCapacity(index + 1), (index + 1) *
  1.5 + 16, stays fast up to kMaxUncheckedOldFastElementsLength = 500 for an old object and
  kMaxUncheckedFastElementsLength = 5,000 for a young one (js-objects.h:970-974), and past that
  only while it is under kPreferFastElementsSizeFactor (3) times the dictionary that would hold
  the used elements (ComputeCapacity(used) * kEntrySize 3; ComputeCapacity rounds 1.5 * used up
  to a power of two, at least 4: hash-table-inl.h:88-95; dictionary.h:385, 482).
- V8, going back (ShouldConvertToFastElements, js-objects.cc:5602-5630), on every element added
  to a dictionary: never once an element needed slow elements (an accessor, an index past
  2^29 - 1; requires_slow_elements, dictionary.h:458-466), never for arguments objects;
  otherwise fast when the dictionary saves less than half the space: 2 * capacity * 3 >= the new
  capacity, an array's length or the largest key + 1, and at least index + 1.
- SpiderMonkey (NativeObject.h:1878-1884, NativeObject.cpp:555-650, 1489-1503): dense below
  MIN_SPARSE_INDEX = 1000 slots, sparse past it when under 1/8 of the required capacity would be
  filled; after adding a sparse index it densifies when the object is extensible, every indexed
  property is a default data property and at least 1/8 of the span is filled, measuring only when
  the slot span reaches a power of two (an O(n) count amortized; bug 1782487's cliff).
- JSC (ArrayConventions.h:67-104, JSObject.cpp:3285-3300, 3384-3396): a vector below index
  100,000 (MIN_SPARSE_ARRAY_INDEX), and past it while 1/8 dense; a sparse map for an index at
  least 1000 past the vector length; back to a vector on a put once 1/8 dense.

Design:
1. One admission rule for an index the words do not hold yet (Props::insert,
   try_define_dense_element, can_start_packed_elements): V8's. An index below the capacity
   takes words; one 1024 or more past the capacity takes the named entries; otherwise the
   grown capacity (Words' growth is V8's NewElementsCapacity already) takes words up to 5,000,
   and past that while the named form would not save a factor of 3 (3 * ComputeCapacity(used)
   * 3 > grown, with `used` the words that are no hole: the used length when NO_HOLES holds,
   else a count). V8's 500 for an old object needs an age TRust JS's objects do not carry
   (its pages are young or old, not its cells), so every object gets V8's young limit, 5,000:
   at most about 40 KB of words before the density test applies.
2. The way back, when an index key joins the named entries: V8's density test with
   SpiderMonkey's measurement. When the named entries' count reaches a power of two, count the
   index entries; if every one is a plain element (V8's requires_slow_elements, SpiderMonkey's
   default data properties) and the named form saves less than half the space (2 * 3 *
   ComputeCapacity(words that are no hole + index entries) >= the largest index + 1), move them
   into the words (holes between), drop them from the named entries, rebuild the key index and
   the slot memos, and clear `has_far`. An ordinary object's index keys are in its shape (step
   3), so it moves to a fresh shape, as a removal does; an array's shape holds no index key.
3. Specification equivalence, as for every transition of W2c: an element's descriptor is plain
   in both forms, OrdinaryOwnPropertyKeys lists indices in ascending order before strings
   whichever store holds them, and `length` does not change.
4. Tests: both transitions at their thresholds (1023 and 1024 past the capacity, the 5,000
   and density limits, the power-of-two densify), non-plain entries never densified, property
   order and descriptors across both, ordinary objects' shapes; the far-index repros against
   Node; Test262's Array and Object suites, difftest, GC stress over the element tests; counts
   over every workload, which no far index reaches (the W2c census).

Predicted: the far-index cliff gone (farcliff's 117 ms to about its 9 ms without the far write;
farcliff2's writes at 300 like the empty start); a write at 100,000 densifies once the named
entries reach 16,384, close to V8's (which converts at each add once dense enough); the
workloads unchanged.

### W2c step 6: the sparse limit and the way back to words (record, 2026-11-02)

Two commits on p2-w2c6: abddb903 bounds a loop chain's proof of its receiver's words; c38b4f3c
admits far indices and densifies sparse arrays by V8's limits (Design 1-2, as written).

The bound, found by the repros: with holes up to 1023 past the capacity admitted, filling a large
holey array in a chained loop scanned all its words every iteration. A failed preamble proof runs
the iteration in plain code, whose back edge enters the preamble again, and each hole the loop
fills withdraws the scan: 20,000 stores after a first write at 100,000 took 721 ms, 98% of it in
jit_reprove_words. The preamble now proves at most 4,096 used words by a scan (REPROVE_MAX_WORDS,
the mirror's bound, the W2c design's "bounded scan"); a larger receiver is proven by its kind bits
alone. A test counts the scans: 3,619 without the bound, none with it, and a small receiver's
still happen.

The far-index repros (fat LTO, CPU 17, a loaded machine; Node 24 on CPU 18): filling and reading
200,000 elements after `a = [1, 2, 3]; a[1000] = 0` 119-121 ms -> 9-10 ms (9-11 ms without the
far write; Node 11-16 ms); 20,000 after `a = []; a[300] = 0` 13 -> 1 ms and 40,000 23 -> 1 ms (Node
1 ms); 20,000 after a first write at 100,000 11 -> 9 ms (Node 13 ms): the named entries densify at
16,384, where V8 converts at the first add once dense enough. The W2c exit's "farcliff.js within
2x of Node" holds.

Choices made in the code, each the owner's to overrule:
- Every object gets V8's young limit (5,000 words before the density test), as the design said.
- The admission and the way back can disagree near the limits, as V8's do: an index refused for
  its grown capacity can densify at once (the way back compares the named form with the largest
  index + 1, V8's with the length, not with the grown capacity), as V8 converts back on its next
  add.
- Two tests' representation expectations moved past the new limits: dense_slot's classic mode
  starts at index 2^32 - 2, beyond any span the way back takes, and the far-key index test
  writes at 1,000,000.

Counts against 2f17b229 (fat LTO, Crystal; Octane at a fixed 5 runs), which no far index reaches:
| | 2f17b229 | step 6 | change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 1,889,573,675 | 1,890,629,815 | +0.06% |
| React | 341,816,769 | 341,904,502 | +0.03% |
| Djot | 676,110,454 | 676,690,091 | +0.09% |
| chai | 83,477,489,018 | 83,504,978,087 | +0.03% |
| Richards | 141,717,141 | 141,717,778 | +0.00% |
| DeltaBlue | 295,087,446 | 295,114,658 | +0.01% |
| Crypto | 747,662,346 | 749,028,776 | +0.18% |
| RayTrace | 1,059,241,303 | 1,059,240,123 | -0.00% |
| EarleyBoyer | 3,616,489,874 | 3,616,519,167 | +0.00% |

agree, 12 skipped, as at 2f17b229), the fixtures' masked outputs and Octane's at five runs as
2f17b229's.

### W2c step 7: integrity kinds and `length` at slot 0 (design, 2026-11-02)

The owner approved W2c's remaining steps (2026-11-02, "all approved"), with the W2c design's
open questions 2 (SEALED and FROZEN as kinds) and 6 (`length` in the elements header) as
recommended. The second does not transfer as written; below.

What the exemplars do (V8 c8b709085e21, SpiderMonkey bf75471716c5, JSC 260278048486; the files
in target/p2/w3-design/v8-src, target/p2/w2c4/exemplars, target/p2/w2c7/exemplars):
- Integrity. V8 keeps frozen and sealed elements dense as elements kinds in the map
  (PACKED/HOLEY_{NONEXTENSIBLE,SEALED,FROZEN}, elements-kind.h:109-119; Map::CopyForPrevent-
  Extensions, map.cc:1942-2008), each element's attributes implied by the kind (lookup.cc:
  1438-1443); its store ICs take the frozen kinds slow and never grow the others
  (builtins-handler-gen.cc:197-208, ic.cc:2915-2945). SpiderMonkey keeps them dense with
  NOT_EXTENSIBLE, SEALED and FROZEN in the elements header and FrozenElements in the shape
  ("to simplify JIT code"; NativeObject.h:199-243), leaves its shared empty header unflagged
  (NativeObject.cpp:106-158), and shrinks capacity to the initialized length when an array becomes
  non-extensible or gets a read-only length, so the append's capacity test refuses it ("in JIT
  code every check counts", NativeObject.cpp:1087-1093; Array.cpp:915-931). JSC moves every
  element into a sparse map (enterDictionaryIndexingMode, JSObject.cpp:2949-2966), which is
  TRust JS's present way: a frozen array's elements leave the words for named entries, so
  reads go through the key index and freezing is a define per element.
- `length`. V8 keeps it in a JSArray field with descriptor 0 always `length`, which it relies on
  ("nonconfigurable, hence ... guaranteed to stay the first property", array.tq:66-85;
  MayHaveReadOnlyLength reads descriptor 0, objects-inl.h:873-898). SpiderMonkey's elements header
  holds it (NativeObject.h:245-282) and JSC's IndexingHeader its publicLength (IndexingHeader.h:
  120-127): both can, because every array with a nonzero or read-only length owns its header
  (SpiderMonkey's arrays have fixed elements, ArrayObject-inl.h:35-46; JSC allocates storage
  for any nonzero length, JSArray.cpp:1307-1323). TRust JS's arrays without elements share one
  static empty block, which cannot hold `new Array(10)`'s length: the header does not transfer.

Design:
7b. `length` at slot 0 (V8's descriptor 0). Every Array's first named entry is its `length`, as
   ArrayCreate and %Array.prototype%'s setup already make it; it is non-configurable, so it is
   never removed, and a removal before it cannot happen. The invariant is asserted where the
   slot is resolved (Props::length_property, debug builds), and the two appends in generated
   code (the map/filter append and step 4's) read the entry at slot 0 directly: no memo load,
   no empty-memo branch, no slot arithmetic, and no accessor test (a non-configurable data
   property stays one). Its value is always a Number (ArraySetLength), which the template's
   test keeps. The census put 21 of the map/filter append's ~75 instructions in finding and
   checking `length`.
7a. SEALED and FROZEN as kinds over the words (SpiderMonkey's header flags, V8's kinds): two
   bits in the header's flags, set on a block the object owns (never the static one; an empty
   array has nothing to overwrite or delete, and non-extensibility, which both imply, refuses
   every creation). SetIntegrityLevel sets them in one step for an object whose index keys are
   all plain words, instead of a define per element; the named entries (with `length`) take the
   per-key defines as before. A word's descriptor reports them (Element: writable unless
   FROZEN, configurable unless either). Writes keep them: the value facts' updates mask only
   those bits (Words::note, reprove), and every path that changes a word other than by an
   overwrite (pop, shift, unshift, splice, truncation, reordering, the batch edits behind
   plain_elements_mut) stands down for either bit, so the generic, descriptor-checked algorithm
   runs; an overwrite stands down for FROZEN, in Rust (set_index_value) and in generated code
   (the Number overwrite at the site and the shared stub, one bit test; a loop chain's
   preamble refuses a frozen receiver it stores to). A define that changes one element's
   attributes moves that element to a named entry with its implied attributes written out (the
   V8 13.6 bug the research observed: a sealed array normalized with default attributes, so
   isSealed answered false and a delete succeeded). TestIntegrityLevel reads the bits. Integrity
   is one-way: the bits are never cleared.
   SpiderMonkey's capacity trim is not needed for correctness here (the creation sequence
   already tests extensibility and `length`'s writability) and is left out.

Order: 7b, then 7a, each measured against its parent. Tests: arrays from every creation path
(literals, ArrayCreate, species, Array.prototype, subclasses, JSON) keep `length` at slot 0;
frozen and sealed arrays' reads, overwrites, defines, deletes, truncation, every Array.prototype
method that writes (sort, reverse, fill, copyWithin, splice, push, pop, shift, unshift) in every
tier, descriptors and key order, and the re-define of one element; Test262's Object.freeze,
Object.seal, Object.isFrozen, Object.isSealed and Array suites, difftest, GC stress.

Predicted: React about -1% to -2% (its appends' `length`), the map/filter appends elsewhere a few
instructions each; freezing an array O(1) in its elements, and a frozen array's reads as fast as
an unfrozen one's (outside the acceptance workloads, which freeze no arrays but tagged templates'
strings).

### W2c step 7: integrity kinds and `length` at slot 0 (record, 2026-11-02)

Seven commits on p2-w2c7. bfc5e7fe is 7b and 9819f66f and 54ee3c55 are 7a, as designed;
bd6d5b2f, 39a9a4d4, 673d92c4 and 64f7ecdd take back 7a's cost on workloads that freeze nothing.

7b, as designed: the map/filter append and the element store's append read `length` at slot 0.
Against c38b4f3c (step 6's code): React -1.18% (its appends), Gameboy -0.47%, NavierStokes
-0.42%, PdfJS -0.15%, chai -0.09%; the rest within 0.11%.

7a, as designed, plus two changes in 54ee3c55:
- A loop chain refuses a frozen receiver only when it stores to it. A chain that only reads
  keeps running, since reads ignore the level.
- SetIntegrityLevel defines only the named entries after sealing the words. No trap observes
  their order.

Its first counts against 7b moved broadly: chai +0.66%, Vue +0.55%, Gameboy +0.54%, Crypto
+0.33%, PdfJS +0.33%. A measurement-only build without the generated-code guards kept every one
but part of Crypto's. Two tools found the causes:
- SPE sampling: `perf record -e arm_spe_0/load_filter=0,store_filter=0,branch_filter=0,
  jitter=1/u` with `perf report --itrace=i1i`. It records exact PCs; PMU sampling's skid had
  moved about 1% of the samples between JIT code and Rust.
- The disassembly.

1. The design's Element::Word(Value, u32), a word with its block's integrity bits, gave that
   variant two scalars. Option<Element> stopped being a scalar pair (its size stayed 16 bytes),
   so get_index, get_key, Props::get and get_walking returned it through a hidden pointer that
   their callers reloaded, named lookups included. One 8-byte field per variant (Word,
   SealedWord, FrozenWord, Entry) returns it in x0/x1 again (bd6d5b2f).
2. The gates' loads tipped fat LTO into calling push_packed_property out of line from
   try_append_element and start_packed_elements, together with Words::reallocate_with_room.
   `#[inline]` restores the inlining (673d92c4).
3. The generated-code guard cost a Number overwrite three instructions (load, and, cbnz) just
   before the kind update loaded the same flags. The update now runs before the store and tests
   FROZEN within its own tests (39a9a4d4). FROZEN moves to bit 3, between INT32 and SCANNED (now
   bit 4; SEALED takes bit 5). INT32|FROZEN and FROZEN|SCANNED are then each one logical
   immediate, so the guard costs one compare on either path. The store site, ++/-- and the
   chain's element store use it; the shared stub keeps its guard for the other values.
4. Selecting a level in get_index cost five instructions. Every level sets SEALED, so one
   test-bit branch identifies a word without a level, and the sealed and frozen elements are
   built out of line (673d92c4). Reads that need no attributes use Props::data_element, which
   answers a word without an Element (64f7ecdd).
5. try_append_element and try_define_dense_element create an element. Every caller refuses a
   non-extensible object first, and every level implies non-extensibility, so their level tests
   become debug assertions (64f7ecdd).

What remains comes from two sources. In generated code, the compare per Number overwrite:
Crypto +0.18%, NavierStokes +0.10%. In Rust, a test bit per element read and the four-way match
of its callers: Vue +0.11%, and 0.07% or less elsewhere. Gameboy's +0.15% is layout noise.
Gameboy reads no clock. 25.7M of its 34.7M is a hash lookup keyed by object addresses (a
SideSlotTable's HashMap<usize, ...>, FxHasher), whose probes follow the addresses, and those
move with the binary's size. That lookup takes 2.1% of Gameboy, a lead for later.

Frozen arrays (fat LTO, CPU 17 on a loaded machine; Node 24 on CPU 18), before 7a and at its tip:
- 2M reads of a frozen 100,000-element array through a called loop: 98-103 ms -> 8-9 ms, the
  same as an unfrozen array (9-10 ms before). Node: 27-28 ms frozen, 6-8 ms unfrozen.
- Freezing that array: 31-32 ms -> 0 ms.
- A nested loop bounded by `x.length` over the frozen array: 141-146 ms -> 38-39 ms, the time
  that loop takes on an unfrozen array in both builds. Node takes 25-26 ms frozen and 9-11 ms
  unfrozen; that gap predates step 7.

PdfJS reads the clock. CanvasGraphics_executeOperatorList yields after a 15 ms slice of
Date.now() (pdfjs.js:2305-2346), so its count follows the machine's speed and load. With
TRUST_JS_TIER_LOG=1, one build compiled that function on its call count in one run and on its
loop budget in the next. octane-fixed/clock.js makes the count deterministic: loaded after
prelude.js, it advances Date.now 1 ms per read. With it, PdfJS goes 8,130,813,730 ->
8,118,917,400 (7b) -> 8,124,276,355, which is -0.15%, +0.07% and -0.08% overall, as the real
clock's figures show. The counts scripts keep the real clock, as earlier records used.

### Region admission (design, 2026-11-02)

The follow-up W2c steps 3 and 4 left: the native tier's optional register regions (a loop's or a
bounded acyclic span's SSA copy, jit_regions.rs, with the baseline templates kept as its bailout
target) are admitted by tests that template changes flip, and zlib's a8 runs slower with its
region than without it. Measured here on main 5d40ee64; the tools are in
target/p2/region-admission.

The rules today (jit.rs:5083-5150, jit_regions.rs:859-863):
1. Work: the units the region saves (transfers, owner operations) times 16 for a loop or 1 for an
   acyclic span exceed its entry's, 1 + 2 per numeric home + 1 per tagged home.
2. Size: its bytes are at most twice its operations' fixed charges plus 1 KB.
3. Budget: a function's optional copies take at most 128 KB in all, granted in pc order.

What decides in practice. A measurement-only build (variants/policy.py) picks the rule by
environment variable and refuses or keeps single regions by function and head. Counts against
the current rule (fat LTO, Crystal, user-mode instructions on CPUs 10-13, median of 3; Octane at
a fixed 5 runs with octane-fixed/clock.js):
| | no regions | loops only | acyclic only | no tests | no size test | work test only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | -0.40% | -0.57% | +0.34% | -0.00% | -0.00% | -0.00% |
| React | +12.28% | -0.46% | +12.77% | +0.00% | +0.00% | -0.00% |
| Djot | -0.28% | -0.51% | +0.23% | +0.05% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| chai | -0.04% | -0.06% | +0.02% | +0.01% | +0.03% | -0.02% |
| Richards | -0.36% | -0.26% | -0.11% | +0.02% | +0.00% | -0.00% |
| DeltaBlue | -2.62% | -0.34% | -2.28% | +0.08% | -0.00% | -0.00% |
| Crypto | -1.20% | -0.23% | -0.96% | +0.03% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| RayTrace | +0.13% | +0.29% | -0.17% | +0.00% | +0.00% | -0.00% |
| EarleyBoyer | -2.14% | -1.05% | -1.08% | +0.02% | -0.00% | -0.00% |
| RegExp | +1.63% | +0.66% | +1.00% | +0.10% | +0.00% | +0.10% |
| Splay | -0.60% | -0.37% | -0.23% | +0.01% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| NavierStokes | -11.30% | -0.03% | -11.26% | +0.02% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| PdfJS | +1.82% | -0.23% | +2.04% | +0.03% | +0.00% | +0.04% |
| Mandreel | +11.03% | -0.19% | +11.22% | -0.07% | -0.00% | -0.11% |
| Gameboy | +8.08% | +3.39% | +4.99% | +0.00% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| CodeLoad | -0.20% | -0.09% | -0.11% | +0.02% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
| Box2D | +1.18% | +0.16% | +1.05% | -0.42% | -0.00% | -0.43% |
| zlib | +16.72% | +0.11% | +16.58% | -5.78% | +0.00% | -5.78% |
| TypeScript | -0.46% | -0.04% | -0.42% | -0.03% | -0.00% | +0.00% |
("Loops only" refuses every acyclic span, "acyclic only" every loop; "no tests" admits every region
the builder plans. Gameboy moves up to 1.3% between builds that differ only in layout. On the
step-1 build below, the first two columns agree with these within 0.3 points elsewhere.)
- The size test refuses nothing in any workload today: dropping it changes no count. It decided
  regions only when a template's size moved under its fixed charges (W2c steps 3 and 4).
- The work test refuses regions worth at most 0.1% (no tests against the work test alone).
- The budget decides in large functions (zlib, Mandreel, Box2D, RegExp, TypeScript). Granted in
  pc order, it reaches a function's later loops last: in a8's full compile the loop at pc 252
  takes 129,716 bytes and the loop at pc 1452, 70% of a8's instructions, is refused. Without the
  budget: zlib -5.78%, Box2D -0.43%, Mandreel -0.11%, RegExp +0.10%, PdfJS +0.04%, and code +10.7%
  (zlib), +8.9% (Mandreel), +12.7% (RegExp), +4.4% (Box2D), +2.4% (TypeScript). Regions cost
  92 bytes an operation in zlib's a1 and 250-290 in RegExp's and Box2D's largest functions, so a
  byte budget admits very different programs very differently.
- Regions as a whole help where no test decides: without any, React +12.3%, zlib +16.7%,
  Mandreel +11.0%, Gameboy +8.1%, PdfJS +1.8%, RegExp +1.6%, Box2D +1.2%. They also cost:
  NavierStokes -11.3%, DeltaBlue -2.6%, EarleyBoyer -2.1%, Crypto -1.2% without them. Refusing
  only the acyclic spans helps ten workloads (EarleyBoyer -1.05%, Vue -0.57%, Djot -0.51%, React
  -0.46%) and costs Gameboy +3.4%, RegExp +0.66%, RayTrace +0.29%.

No test sees the regions that lose. Four causes, each measured:
1. Compile time. RegionIr::remove_trivial_params walked every block's successors for each
   parameter of each block, up to once per block; every block has a parameter for every local,
   so the pass was cubic in the region's size: 9.2% of DeltaBlue's instructions, 1.2% of
   CodeLoad's, about 1% of Djot's and EarleyBoyer's. Landed as step 1 (below).
2. Speculation that keeps failing. A census build (variants/census2.py) counts each region's
   entries, bails and exits in generated code, by bail site. 81% of the bails are a region's
   ordinary end at a return, which regions do not lower (jit_regions.rs:2982-2986). The rest
   are guards that fail on most entries. Each such entry runs the region's entry, part of the
   work, the bail, and the rest of the iteration in baseline code, whose back edge enters the
   region again:
   - DeltaBlue's Plan.execute loop, with `c.execute()` inlined for one constraint class, misses
     its inline guard on 94% of entries. Refusing that region saves 1.69%.
   - React's reducerBatch loops speculate a Number Add on string concatenations (`'Todo ' + i`,
     `'changed-' + i`) and bail on 99% of entries. Also:
     PdfJS's hottest loop on an Add (100%), zlib's loops on a local's Number guard (99%),
     Mandreel's on a store to a numeric home (100%), Splay on an Add (52%; refusing it: -0.35%),
     TypeScript on inline guards (96%) and a call's result guard (68%).
   Refusing every region whose speculative bails reach half its entries: DeltaBlue -1.79%,
   Splay -0.35%, Box2D -0.27%, RegExp -0.11%, but React +0.43%, Mandreel +0.41%, PdfJS +0.06%:
   those regions do more work before the failing guard than the bails cost. The speculation at
   one site fails, not the region.
3. Lowering worse than the baseline's.
   - NavierStokes's advect. As a region its inner loop runs 35% more instructions than as
     baseline code; refusing its two regions saves 11.05%, all of NavierStokes's loss. The
     region pushes its operands to the frame's operand stack around each static name read
     (`width`, `rowSize`: Decision 21's coordinates) and reloads them with a Number check. The
     baseline's numeric chains keep them in registers.
   - The fused access to a typed array named by a free name (NameElementPlan). Stub:NameElement
     proves the name through the generic name cache before the element access. In one zlib run
     it takes 11.2 G instructions; the same accesses unfused cost 9.4 G (a static name read,
     Stub:TypedElement and their templates). With the fusion off: zlib -9.14%, RegExp -0.97%
     (whose fused sites find no typed array: 511,908 fallbacks), Mandreel +8.12%. Each fused
     site also carries its own copy of the name and element templates as its fallback. That
     doubles a8's regions (loop 252: 61 KB unfused, 130 KB fused) and spent a8's budget.
4. Small acyclic regions that end at a return lose in Vue (ve, `return _e(e)?e:ye(...)`, about
   200 instructions a call more with its region; ke, 29), EarleyBoyer (an nboyer term compare,
   37 a call) and Djot: refusing them saves 0.40% and 0.17% of Vue, 1.04% of EarleyBoyer and
   0.19% of Djot. Vue's profile with ve's region shows 4.3 M more instructions in the exact-PC
   helper (jit_exec); the bail targets a region publishes also stop the baseline's fusions at
   those pcs. The rest is not yet explained.

What V8, SpiderMonkey and JSC do (target/p2/region-admission/exemplars.txt; V8 c8b70908,
SpiderMonkey 6bb81100, JSC 26027804):
- They admit optimized code by counted hotness and never estimate the work it saves:
  TieringManager::ShouldOptimize compares budgets and has no cost or benefit term
  (tiering-manager.cc:434-474); SpiderMonkey compares a warm-up counter with thresholds
  (JitOptions.cpp:211-249); JSC multiplies its thresholds by 0.83 + 0.06 x sqrt(bytecode cost)
  (CodeBlock.cpp:2512-2607).
- Their size limits are on input. V8: TurboFan stops at 60 KB of bytecode (pipeline.cc:699-702)
  and Maglev at 512 KB. Maglev peels only innermost loops under 400 bytecode bytes, 900 per graph
  (maglev-graph-builder.cc:17775-17800), its nearest kin to an optional copy of a loop. TurboFan
  inlines up to 920 bytecode bytes in all (js-inlining-heuristic.cc:373-384). SpiderMonkey's Ion
  stops at 100,000 bytes (Ion.cpp:1964-1988); JSC's DFG at 100,000 of bytecode cost and its FTL
  at 60,000, with inlining budgets of 2,500 and 12,000 a compilation. None compares optimized
  machine code with baseline code, and none budgets machine code per function. Only
  process-wide executable memory counts: SpiderMonkey's 8 MB headroom gate
  (ProcessExecutableMemory.cpp:904-917) and JSC's pressure-scaled thresholds
  (ExecutableAllocator.cpp:1146-1163).
- They count exits, and the next compile drops the speculation that failed, not the code
  around it. SpiderMonkey recompiles after 10 bailouts of the fixable kinds
  (IonScript.h:336-341), and particular bailout kinds set script flags that remove just that
  speculation (BaselineBailouts.cpp:2014-2189). JSC jettisons after 100 exits (5 from loops); it
  records each frequent exit site by bytecode index and exit kind in the baseline block's
  ExitProfile, which the next DFG compile consults per speculation, and doubles its thresholds
  per retry (CodeBlock.cpp:2399-2417, 3093-3123, 2737-2770). V8 marks a deopting call or loop
  site non-speculative (translated-state.cc:3006-3024).
- Nothing times optimized code. They keep it from being slower upstream: they compile after
  feedback settles, speculate from the baseline's profiles (SpiderMonkey transpiles its CacheIR),
  and turn off-profile paths into counted exits.
- None makes an optimized copy of a straight-line span. They compile whole functions with loop
  entries and enter outer loops first.

Design: the exemplars' rule within one tier. Admission by input caps, failed speculation by
exit sites, and region code no worse than the baseline's for any operation. Each step is
measured and lands on its own:
1. The region IR in linear time (landed, below).
2. Input, not output. The size test goes: it refuses nothing today and decides only when a
   template's size changes. The 128 KB of output becomes a budget of region operations per
   function, the unit the exemplars cap, which no template change moves. Its size comes from
   counts at 1,024, 2,048 and 4,096: the smallest that admits a8's two loops (about 1,360
   operations in its full compile) without growing RegExp's code for nothing. The work test
   stays as the builder's test that a region has something to do; it moves no count by more
   than 0.1%.
3. Exit sites (JSC's ExitProfile, SpiderMonkey's bailout flags).
   - Each speculative bail site of a region counts its bails in generated code, on the bail
     path only. Returns are not counted: they are a region's ordinary end.
   - At a threshold the chunk records the site and discards its code, so the next call compiles
     it again. JSC jettisons at 100 and SpiderMonkey invalidates at 10; the threshold is chosen
     by measurement.
   - That compile lowers the recorded sites without their speculation. A Number Add becomes the
     generic add inside the region. An inline guard's miss continues in the region through the
     call alternative. A local whose guard failed stops being a numeric home.
   - Expected from the census: DeltaBlue, Splay, Box2D and RegExp keep their regions' work
     without the bails. React, PdfJS, zlib, Mandreel and TypeScript lose their bails, and no
     region is refused.
4. Lowering parity.
   a. Static name reads stay in registers inside a region (advect; NavierStokes's 11.05% is the
      bound).
   b. The fused typed-array access reads its name the way the baseline does, through Decision
      21's coordinates when the name has them. It is not fused where the element site's cache
      has seen no typed array (RegExp). All its sites share one cold path per chunk instead of a
      copy each (a8's 130 KB).
   c. Find what costs the small acyclic regions that end at a return (Vue's ve, about 200
      instructions a call), and fix or refuse that shape.
5. With W4's value classes, regions speculate from the baseline's feedback rather than from SSA
   demand alone, so most failing speculations are never compiled (all three exemplars speculate
   from profiles). This is part of W4's remainder.

Not proposed: compiling a region only once its loop is hot, with entry into the new code.
That is how the exemplars' optimizing tiers work and is Phase 3's tier structure. Removing
acyclic spans (no exemplar has them; they cost ten workloads 0.2-1% and save Gameboy 3.4%) is
re-measured after steps 3 and 4.

Order: 2, then 4b (zlib, RegExp), 4a (NavierStokes), 3, 4c, and 5 with W4.

Step 1, landed (b36823b4): remove_trivial_params and live_entry_locals read each block's incoming
edges from one array built once (RegionIr::incoming_edges), in the order the old walk met them.
The replacements and live locals are the walk's, and the generated code is byte for byte the same
in all 18 workloads (dumpsum.py of TRUST_JS_JIT_CODEDUMP=*). Counts against 5d40ee64:
| | 5d40ee64 | b36823b4 | change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 1,892,202,993 | 1,890,472,393 | -0.09% |
| React | 337,925,219 | 337,599,005 | -0.10% |
| Djot | 676,741,073 | 674,784,793 | -0.29% |
| chai | 83,474,424,604 | 83,464,925,442 | -0.01% |
| Richards | 141,835,953 | 141,871,245 | +0.02% |
| DeltaBlue | 295,499,698 | 266,079,848 | -9.96% |
| Crypto | 750,424,199 | 749,201,873 | -0.16% |
| RayTrace | 1,059,324,946 | 1,055,880,862 | -0.33% |

| TypeScript | 60,124,451,263 | 59,779,722,321 | -0.57% |
Richards' +0.02% and RegExp's +0.01% come from outside the changed code: the generated code is
the same, and fat LTO compiled two functions the change does not touch differently
(Interp::make_arguments_object is one instruction shorter, bytecode::finish_chunk seven).
Octane here runs with clock.js, so PdfJS's count is deterministic; the records before this one
used the real clock. Checks: rustfmt; clippy in the three landing configurations on Crystal and
zombie (x86-64), and zombie's aarch64 cross clippy; the 84 region and IR tests, then the lib
suite on Crystal (2,735) and zombie (2,530).

### Region admission: steps 4b (first part), 2 and 4a (record, 2026-11-02)

Five commits, landed one step at a time (main 6ba8756b, 91cab361, 805fac44 and 32f3fd3f).

Step 4b, first part (6ba8756b): the fused typed-array access reads a name that has a static
address (Decision 21) where it lives, as the baseline's LoadName reads it. Before, the fusion
proved the name through its name cache at every access. With Decision 21's scope plans off
(TRUST_JS_SCOPE_PLANS=0), the fusion saved zlib 7.8%; with them on it cost 4.3%, because every
other read of those names had become a load from their address. A site with a static address now
walks to the binding and enters the shared routine past the proof (SharedStub::NameElement's
`resolved` variant); its cold path reads the name the same way. A lexical binding in its TDZ
still throws through the full LoadName. Mandreel's names have no static address and keep the
probe. Counts against b36823b4:

zlib's -15.85% is more than turning the fusion off (-9.14%): the fused element access stays.

The design's other two parts of 4b move:
- Not fusing where the element site never sees a typed array (RegExp: 511,908 fallbacks) needs a
  record of what the site saw, and GetElem has no cache of its own. It becomes one of step 3's
  exit sites: the fallback counts like a bail, and the next compile does not fuse that site.
- One shared cold path per chunk instead of a fallback copy at each site waits until those
  misses are gone. RegExp takes the fallback 511,908 times, so a slower shared path would cost it
  more than the code it saves.

all (REGION_OPS_BUDGET), counted from their plans before anything is emitted. A plan past the
budget costs no emission, and a refused plan still covers its operations as before. Counts

Step 4a (805fac44, 32f3fd3f): a region reads two kinds of operand in place that it used to run as
effects. Running an operation as an effect published every virtual operand to the frame's operand
stack, and the consumer reloaded the result with a Number check.
- A static name. Its binding's word goes into a register as a Number when arithmetic consumes it,
  or onto its stack slot, with nothing below it published.
- A numeric element read (GetElemLocal) whose result feeds arithmetic and whose key is a Number in
  a register. The receiver's element words, or its typed-array view, go straight into a register.

Anything else leaves the region at the read and runs the baseline's operation: a word that is not
a Number, a lexical binding in its TDZ, a hole, a key that is not an index. NavierStokes's advect:

### Region admission: step 4c, first part (record, 2026-11-03)

03a57cc4: a region tests a tail position's depth (Op::TailDeep) in place. TailDeep asks whether
a strict-mode tail call is deep enough to run as a proper tail call. The region had no lowering
for it, so it ran in the exact-PC helper, which costs about 110 instructions, while the
baseline's template reads the execution context's depth in a few. Vue's `return _e(e) ? e :
ye(...)` (ve) and similar shapes put it in admitted regions. With TRUST_JS_JIT_OPSTAT,
regions on and off differ only in TailDeep: 74,719 helper calls in the Vue fixture and 8,417 in
Djot, none without regions. That was most of what ve's region cost (about 200 instructions a
call). The region now reads the depth as the baseline does and leaves the Boolean in a register
for the JumpIfFalse that follows. Counts against 99c43d2a:

### Region admission: step 4a, third part (record, 2026-11-03)

5cbcad6a: a region stores a Number element in place. The value and the key are in registers, and
the key may be a tagged home's word, guarded as a Number. advect's `pos` is such a home: it holds
`undefined` before the loop, so it cannot be a numeric home. The receiver's element words take
the value as the baseline's store writes a Number over an existing element: the kind bits stay
exact and a frozen block's words are refused. A typed-array view takes it as the view's store
does. The element read (32f3fd3f) now takes such keys too. Anything else leaves the region before
anything is written: holes and appends (property creations), keys that are not indices, and other
receivers.

Against 2e480ada: NavierStokes -0.22%, TypeScript -0.04%, Vue +0.03%, the rest within 0.01%.
NavierStokes gained less than the store's share of advect promised. What advect's region still
loses to the baseline lies in its locals rather than its elements: an SPE profile of the region
puts 7,640 samples in LoadLocal, 3,269 in UpdateLocal and 2,374 in StoreLocal, against 6,705 in
GetElemLocal and 2,384 in SetElemLocalDrop. Locals outside the eight numeric homes, and tagged
homes such as `pos`, are read and written through the frame slot or the home with a guard at
each use.

Test: region_numeric_element_stores_stay_in_registers_and_leave_for_anything_else. It compares
with the interpreter over plain arrays, Float64Array and Int32Array views, holes, appends, frozen
arrays, NaN and -0 values, non-index keys and an array-like object. It fails with the store's
word or the view's value taken from the wrong register. Checks: rustfmt; clippy in the three
landing configurations on Crystal and zombie, and zombie's aarch64 cross clippy; the lib suite on
Crystal (2,740) and zombie (2,530); the element tests under TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1.

### Region admission: step 3's mechanism (design, 2026-11-03)

The exit sites (JSC's ExitProfile, SpiderMonkey's bailout flags) need three things the engine does
not have: a count of each region's failed speculations, a record of them that outlives the code,
and a way to replace a function's code while it runs.

1. Counts. Each bail stub of a region whose guard can fail counts its bails. That excludes a
   region's ordinary ends (Return, ReturnBare, ReturnUndef, Throw), which every entry may take.
   The counter is a word the JitCode owns, one per site, incremented after the stub has published
   the region's state, so it costs nothing on the region's own path. The census names the sites:
   inline guards (DeltaBlue, TypeScript), Number Adds on strings (React, PdfJS, Splay), a local's
   Number guard (zlib), a store of a non-Number into a numeric home (Mandreel), a call's or
   property read's Number result (TypeScript, Gameboy, Box2D), an int32 operand (Mandreel), the
   fused typed-array access's fallbacks (RegExp).
2. The record. When a site's count reaches the threshold, the stub calls a helper. The helper
   adds the site's pc to the chunk's exit profile, a set of pcs that outlives every compile of
   the chunk, and discards the chunk's code that took the bail (its fresh-frame or its OSR code).
   The threshold starts at SpiderMonkey's 10 (frequentBailoutThreshold, IonScript.h) and doubles
   with each recompile of the chunk, as JSC doubles its exit thresholds per reoptimization
   (CodeBlock.cpp:2737-2770). After eight recompiles the chunk stops recording (SpiderMonkey
   disables Ion after 50 unfixable bailouts; JSC caps its retries).
3. Replacing running code. The bail runs inside the code it discards, and an activation entered
   by a direct call holds no lease on its code. Running code is found only through activation
   records (jit::running_code); JitCode's drop asserts it is not running. So the discarded code
   leaves the slot (Cold, cooldown 0, so the next entry compiles) but not memory: it waits in a
   list of the code cache's own. A collection pass frees it once no activation runs it and nothing
   else holds it, as eviction frees code today. The call caches' epoch moves
   (invalidate_call_caches), so direct calls re-resolve to the new code, as an inline recompile
   makes them. V8 deoptimizes running frames lazily and JSC jettisons; here the running
   activation simply finishes in the old code, which is still correct.

The compile reads the profile. A recorded pc keeps its region but loses its speculation:
- an arithmetic, comparison or int32 operation runs as the generic operation (the region's effect
  path, the baseline's template);
- a producer whose Number result was demanded (LoadLocal, a static name, an element or property
  read, a call) is read without the demand;
- a store or read of a numeric home's slot takes that slot out of the homes;
- the fused typed-array access is not fused at that LoadName.

An inline guard is the one exception: its miss runs the call alternative outside the region, so
a recorded one refuses the region (refusing DeltaBlue's saved 1.69%).

Tests: each kind fails its guard past the threshold, recompiles without it, and gives the
interpreter's result. A long-running loop discards the code it is running and finishes in it, and
later calls run the new code. The cap holds under alternating speculations. All under GC stress.

### Region admission: step 3 measured, not landed (record, 2026-11-03)

Step 3's mechanism, as designed above, is on branch p2-region-s3 (e8208730). It works:
- Each region bail stub counts its site. Returns and throws are not counted.
- At the threshold the chunk records the site and discards its resident code, fresh-frame and
  OSR alike. The discarded code stays with the chunk until nothing runs it, then is swept, and
  the call caches' epoch moves.
- The recompile lowers the recorded pcs without their speculation.
- Its tests pass:
  - recorded sites converge;
  - a polymorphic inlined call refuses its region;
  - an activation discards the code it runs and finishes in it.

Two findings shaped it:
- Discarded code first went to a list in the code cache's thread-local registry. Dropping it there
  at thread exit ran JitCode's running-code check against a thread-local already destroyed, which
  aborted the test binary. The chunk keeps it instead.
- Counting the fused typed-array access's fallbacks called the helper while x14 still pointed at
  the name's value, which the value-miss path then reads. RegExp, EarleyBoyer and chai crashed.
  The fallbacks do not count, so RegExp's fusion misses are not recorded.

The counts say not to land it. Against main 5cbcad6a (fat LTO; Octane at five runs):
| | T=10 | T=100 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | +0.05% | +0.05% |
| React | +0.21% | +0.21% |
| Djot | +1.27% | +0.96% |
| chai | +0.38% | +0.26% |
| Richards | +0.06% | +0.06% |
| DeltaBlue | +3.72% | +3.46% |
| Crypto | +0.26% | -0.11% |
| RayTrace | +1.05% | +0.95% |
| EarleyBoyer | +0.40% | +0.39% |
| RegExp | +0.11% | +0.11% |
| Splay | -0.13% | -0.13% |
| NavierStokes | +0.02% | +0.02% |
| PdfJS | +0.61% | +0.37% |
| Mandreel | +0.06% | +0.06% |
| Gameboy | +1.16% | +0.51% |
| CodeLoad | +0.08% | +0.08% |
| Box2D | +0.84% | +0.19% |
| zlib | -0.29% | -0.29% |
| TypeScript | +0.08% | -0.05% |

Three variants of e8208730 separate the costs (one run each):
| | DeltaBlue | RayTrace | Djot |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| counting only (never records) | +0.24% | +0.06% | +0.20% |
| records and recompiles, profile ignored | +4.94% | +1.07% | +0.81% |
| all of it, T=100 | +3.46% | +0.95% | +0.96% |

The profile does what the census promised: about 1.5% of DeltaBlue, in the region its inline
guard refuses. The recompiles cost three times that. DeltaBlue compiles 99 times instead of 89.
Each record moves the call caches' epoch, which every call site in the program then pays for by
re-resolving. The epoch must move: a cache that kept calling discarded code would call freed
code once the chunk swept it.

The exemplars recompile code that is already hot. JSC's DFG enters at 1,000 executions and
SpiderMonkey's Ion at 1,500 warm-ups. Their invalidation reaches only the jettisoned code's
callers. Here regions compile with the baseline, after eight calls, and the only invalidation
there is reaches every call site. What would make step 3 pay is invalidating per callee, or
recording only sites whose function stays hot after the record; both are designs of their own.

The lib tests that would need changing if it landed check native residency, call-cache epochs or
one activation record a level right after a site recorded and discarded the code
(traced_tests' assert_native, the math intrinsics' epoch test, rooting_tests' census).

- step 4a, static names, numeric element reads and stores in place (805fac44, 32f3fd3f,
  5cbcad6a);
- step 4c's first part, TailDeep in place (03a57cc4).

  React -0.50%, Splay -0.37%, DeltaBlue -0.36% without them);
