### W2e: in-line allocation in generated code (census and design, 2026-11-04)

W2e's general form, on main b00bb6dc. Its first use landed with W3 item 3's step 7 (a fresh
frame's activation, in line in the prologue: jit_alloc.rs, jit_x64_alloc.rs,
gc_core::InlineAllocSite).

Census (target/p2/w2e: opstat/, micro/, opstat-table.md; main's code, fat LTO, CPUs 10-13, pacing
off):
- Allocation operations that reach a native-code helper over each run (TRUST_JS_JIT_OPSTAT=1; New
  counts only the constructions the direct sequence declined):

| workload | MakeObject | NewObject | MakeArray | MakeArrayLiteral | NewArray | MakeClosure | New |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | | | 24,444 | | 12,351 | 26,068 | 1,371 |
| React | 11,026 | 8,746 | 21 | | | 4,480 | |
| Djot | 60,443 | | 22,459 | | | 2,145 | 1,580 |
| chai | 561,923 | | 1,683,054 | 66,040 | | 702,725 | 736,167 |
| sustained | 639,484 | | 399,886 | | | 399,886 | |
| Splay | 529,192 | | | 268,798 | | | 21 |
| PdfJS | 23,212 | | 114,106 | 4,896 | | 325,350 | |
| TypeScript | 85,467 | | 512,497 | | | 245,922 | 681,917 |
| RayTrace | | | | | | 6 | 332,643 |
| Box2D | | | | | | | 110,387 |
| EarleyBoyer | | | 432 | | | 20,182 | 9,744 |

  The other Octane benchmarks make fewer than 22,000 of any kind.
- Instructions per operation, the loop excluded (2M iterations in a function, fat LTO): `{}` 309,
  `{x: i, y: i}` 348, five keys 385, `[]` 357, `[i, i]` 533, `[1, 2, 3]` (shared) 346, a function
  expression 1,061, an arrow 762; with a call each, `new P(x, y)` (two stores) 683, returning
  `arguments` 1,761, a rest array 785, against a plain call's 231.
- Where a closure's 1,061 go (instructions sampled): make_function_named 255, active_runtime_origin
  175 (a walk of the frames for the script origin), the copy of the function's template map 120,
  the cell's allocation through the active-heap lookup 120, key lookups by text about 100,
  ObjectKind::user 65, Function::runtime 46. An array's creation spends about 140 in
  array_length_layout and array_length_shape (step 4's profile), each a lookup through the active
  heap.
- From the counts and costs, in-line allocation reaches about 15% of Splay's instructions (its
  payload objects and shared arrays), about 4% of PdfJS's (closures), 3% of React's and Djot's,
  2% of chai's and 1.7% of Vue's.

What the exemplars do (target/p2/w2e/exemplars.txt; V8 c8b70908, SpiderMonkey 6bb81100, JSC
26027804, re-diffed against each engine's main of 2026-11-03/04):
- A1: the fast path is a bump against a limit (JSC: a free-list pop), with no byte budget charged
  and no collector state tested (V8 memory-optimization-reducer.h:380-393; SM
  jit/MacroAssembler.cpp:653-680; JSC jit/AssemblyHelpers.cpp:975-996).
- A2: bytes are counted and the trigger evaluated when the allocation area is refilled, never per
  object (V8 main-allocator.cc:288-293; SM gc/Allocator.cpp:483-504; JSC
  heap/LocalAllocator.cpp:130-150).
- A3: no poll follows an allocation. The miss is a call that evaluates the trigger before handing
  out the cell, and collects there (V8 heap-allocator.cc:556-580, SM gc/Allocator.cpp:76-118); a
  pending request makes the fast path fail (V8 lowers the area's limit, main-allocator.cc:372-382;
  SM moves the nursery position to the chunk end, gc/Nursery.cpp:2013-2020). TRust's step 7 polls
  after allocating, which none of them does.
- A4: the fast path writes nothing before its last check; the slow path makes the whole object in
  a call and rejoins after the operation.
- A5: the initializing stores carry no barrier (the cell is young, or unmarked in JSC).
- A6: the header comes from the hidden class, and every word the collector reads is written
  before the next point a collection can run; only V8 copies values from a template (its
  boilerplate).
- A7: the fast path needs state an earlier execution made (V8's boilerplate from a literal's third
  execution, SM's stub from the first, JSC's profile).
- Where each case allocates: V8's Sparkplug calls builtins for all of them; SM's Baseline
  allocates only its prologue's CallObject in its own code, the rest in CacheIR stubs or the VM;
  JSC's baseline allocates `{...}`, `{}` and `this` in line and calls C++ for the rest. The `this`
  of `new F()` is made on the caller's side in V8 (FastNewObject) and SM (the call IC stub, from
  MetaCreateThis data), on the callee's side in JSC (op_create_this). Closures: V8's
  FastNewClosure builtin, SM's Lambda stub that copies the canonical function's words, JSC's C++
  call.

Design. TRust has one native tier and no stubs per site, so its code allocates in line where the
exemplars' stubs and builtins do, and its helpers stay the slow path:
1. No poll after an in-line allocation, and the fast path fails while a collection is due (A3).
   Generated code compares its charge with a word of its own, `inline_check_bytes`: the Rust
   paths' due_check_bytes, but zero whenever a collection is due (Heap::refresh_due), so every
   in-line allocation then takes its helper, whose end poll collects. The activation's poll after
   its allocation goes; the entry poll before it stays. The charge stays exact per cell for now
   (step 6 takes A2's refill charge).
2. Objects' live count in line: object cells are counted for the embedder's live ceiling
   (Heap::count_live), so an object's fast path adds one to the count and compares it with
   live_check, going to the helper with nothing changed when it would pass.
3. Object literals (MakeObject with a template): a per-site InlineObject made at compile time when
   the site's template exists (A7) and its keys fit the cell: the size class, the header's fields
   (the values a helper-made object's map would hold, read from a map the template initializes
   in Rust, each stored at its field's offset and width, so no padding is read or written), the
   prototype from the realm's %Object.prototype% at run time, and the slots from the operand
   stack (V8's boilerplate copy, with the values). The helper remains for a site without a
   template, keys past the cell, and every miss.
4. `{}` (NewObject), `[]` (MakeArray(0)) and constant literals (MakeArrayLiteral): the same, with
   no slots, `length` 0, or `length` and the shared block.
5. Array literals with elements (MakeArray(n), n at most a cell's fifteen): the cell and an exact
   element block (the storage cursor's), the words copied from the stack, and the kind bits
   folded in line.
6. The `this` of `new F()`: on the caller's side, in the direct construct sequence, from the
   constructor's decided class (W2d step 3e) and its prototype through the site's cached shape and
   slot (jit_construct_this's proof), once its learning is over; the helper otherwise.
7. Closures: their helper's Rust path first (its frame walk for the origin, the runtime lease, the
   key lookups by text), measured; in line after, if the template copy and kind payload allow.
8. Refill charging (A2): the cursor's free word charged when it is taken, and refunded at a flush,
   measured on its own, since it moves every allocation's accounting.
Each step is ARM64 first; x86-64 follows where its backend already has the templates the step
needs (it has the allocation itself and the activation).

Exit (proposed): instructions per operation `{}`, `[]` and `[1, 2, 3]` at most 100, `{x, y}` and
`new P(x, y)`'s allocation at most 120 more than the loop; Splay -8% or better; no fixture or
Octane benchmark slower beyond noise; collections as before (counts, kinds), GC stress over the
new tests, TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY.

Risks: an in-line object that misses a field the collector or a Rust reader expects (debug builds
compare, at each site's compilation, the fields an in-line object gets with a helper-made object's,
and a test reads every field of in-line objects back through the Rust accessors); the live count and byte charge as the helpers keep them (unit tests
of the counts after in-line allocations); a template whose header depends on the realm or heap
(only the prototype word is patched, and a template is per chunk, whose heap is fixed).

### W2e steps 1, 3 and 4: literals in line (record, 2026-11-04)

Commits, from main b00bb6dc, on p2-w2e:
- 89e96a84: the census and design note.
- 5b16e596 (steps 1 and 3): generated code compares its byte charge with Heap::inline_check_bytes,
  zero while a collection is due, and nothing polls after an in-line allocation (the activation's
  poll after it goes); counted descriptors count their cells in line; object literals are made in
  line from their site's image.
- 81c939b9 (step 4): `{}` takes a template; `[]` and constant literals' copies are made in line.
- e546eb07: images come from the helper's fresh object, not a reference object made for them.
- 2b551bbb: in-line objects count toward the heap's allocated objects.
- 3063c642: ARM64-only constants allowed dead on x86-64.

As designed, except:
1. A site's image is read at run time, not built into the code. The design had the code embed a
   template's header at compilation; with the tier threshold at 0 (and in any function compiled
   before its literal first runs) no template exists then. The chunk holds an ObjectImage per
   object-literal and array-literal site and one for `[]` (a ready word, the nine header words,
   and an Array's `length` slot), which the helper's first execution of the site fills from the
   object it just made (header_image: each field copied at its offset into a zeroed image), and
   the code tests the ready word before it allocates, as JSC's unlinked baseline code reads its
   site's profile and V8's builtin checks its boilerplate (A7). The allocation's site, class and
   cursor are the code's own: a literal's class follows from its key count.
2. First the image was made from a reference object allocated for it: one more object per site
   moved later heap addresses, and with them the address-keyed caches (Vue +0.16% at 5b16e596).
   e546eb07 takes the helper's own fresh object instead.
3. Objects count twice in the heap: the embedder's live count (the live ceiling's) and GcState's
   allocated objects (the task boundary's collection policy reads it); the in-line path adds to
   both (2b551bbb, after tests::object_count_alone_does_not_collect caught the second).
4. The literal steps landed before steps 5-8, as their own series.

Micro-benchmarks (instructions an iteration over the loop's 18, against main 13645c27, fat LTO,
2M iterations of a function's loop):

| script | main | tip |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `{}` | 309.4 | 114.1 |
| `{x: i, y: i}` | 346.6 | 132.6 |
| five keys | 384.3 | 161.3 |
| `[]` | 356.6 | 116.9 |
| `[1, 2, 3]` (shared) | 345.6 | 116.9 |
| `[i, i]` | 532.6 | 533.6 |
| `new P(x, y)` | 682.8 | 682.8 |
| a function expression | 1,056.8 | 1,056.8 |

The design's exit asked 100 for `{}`, `[]` and `[1, 2, 3]` and 120 for `{x, y}`: 114 to 133 now.
The in-line path takes about 70 (the ready test, the allocation's 30 with its charge and two
counts, the prototype, nine header words copied, the slots, the tag and the stack), the rest is
the cursor's refills and the collections. Step 8's refill charge takes the charge and counts out
of the path.

### W2e step 8: counts at the cursor's flush (design, 2026-11-04)

Today every small cell's allocation charges its bytes and compares them with a check: Rust's
fast paths (Heap::alloc_cell, alloc_small_uninit) against due_check_bytes, generated code
(emit_inline_alloc, both backends) against inline_check_bytes. An object also adds one to the
embedder's live count, compared with live_check, and one to GcState's allocated count (Rust's
Object::alloc and alloc_empty, and generated code). In generated code these are 21 of an
in-line object allocation's 52 instructions.

None of the exemplars counts per cell (A2). V8 charges a whole LAB when it installs it
(main-allocator.cc:288-293). JSC charges a block's consumed free list at the refill that follows
its exhaustion (heap/LocalAllocator.cpp:137-139, then collectIfNecessaryOrDefer at 143): after the
fact. SpiderMonkey charges 4 KiB per new arena (gc/Allocator.cpp:483-504).

Design (JSC's after-the-fact charge, at a cursor word's granularity):
1. A cursor's cells are counted when it is flushed (Heap::flush_cursor, which already brings the
   page's `live` and `next_unused` up to date from `taken`): their bytes charged, with the
   budget's compare (charge_bytes), the embedder's live count with its compare, the objects'
   allocated count, and the statistics (count_cells). A cursor is flushed at every refill (a
   bitmap word: at most 64 cells), as a collection starts and before a sweep.
2. The fast paths take the cell (its bitmap bit, `taken`, `high`) and nothing else. Generated code
   keeps its fast_alloc test (stress, collecting) and drops the charge, the live count and the
   allocated count; Heap::inline_check_bytes goes.
3. A collection that becomes due between refills (external bytes, a request, the live ceiling
   from another path) runs at the next poll: function entry, loop back edges, a helper's end
   (A3's requested collections). A budget's trigger fires at most one word of cells late per
   cursor (64 cells, at most a 64 KiB page: 12 KiB in the 192-byte class), against today's exact
   cell; JSC's is one block (16 KiB) late per allocator, V8's one LAB early.
4. Exact reads: the engine's reads of the live and allocated counts (heap_live_objects,
   heap_allocated_objects: the task boundary's policy, the agent's trigger, tests) add the counted
   descriptor's unflushed `taken`; the sweep's checks (GC byte accounting, live-cell accounting)
   follow flush_cursors, as now. Under GC stress and during a collection every allocation takes
   the slow path, which flushes its cursor at once, so stress intervals stay exact.
5. GcState's allocated count is kept by the heap: a second count attached with the live one
   (the same descriptor), so Object::alloc and alloc_empty stop counting.

Exit: `{}`, `[]` and `[1, 2, 3]` at most 100 instructions in W2e's micro-benchmarks, `{x, y}` at
most 120; no workload slower beyond noise; collections as before (kinds and counts, within one
where a trigger falls at a word's boundary); the lib suite, TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY and GC stress over
the allocation tests.

### W2e step 8 and the shared stub's heap (record, 2026-11-04)

Commits, from main e288bfd5, on p2-w2e-refill:
- b195dd87 (step 8): a small cell's bytes, the embedder's live and allocated counts and the
  statistics are taken from a cursor's `taken` cells when it is flushed (at its next refill, as
  a collection starts and before a sweep). The fast paths, Rust's and both backends' generated
  code, take the cell and nothing else; the in-line check, live count and allocated count go
  from the code, and Heap::inline_check_bytes goes. heap_live_objects and heap_allocated_objects
  add the cursors' unflushed cells (Heap::unflushed_cells); the heap keeps GcState's allocated
  count (attach_allocated_count), so Object::alloc and alloc_empty stop counting.
- 15739ba9: generated code reads the heap through its context's interpreter
  (Interp::gc_core_raw), not an address built into the code. Step 6's construct sequence is a
  process-wide shared stub (SharedStub::DirectConstruct), and it built in the heap it was first
  assembled under: later engines and threads allocated their `this` objects there. Under the lib
  suite, call_error_name_tests (six engines in turn on one thread) crashed in 2 runs of 16 on
  main, the stub reading a dropped heap's cursors. The heap's address is now two loads from the
  context (the immediate was three or four instructions), and a test runs two live engines on
  one thread and requires each one's in-line `this` objects in its own heap (it fails when the
  code builds in the heap).
- 0f56ebc8: in test builds the stub counts its in-line `this` objects through a call (it built
  in the assembling test thread's counter's address), and Interp's memory inventory classifies
  the new field.

As designed, except:
1. The fix, which the step's crash hunt found (the lib suite's SIGSEGV was main's, not the
   step's).
2. Six tests read the counts as the step defines them: the budget and live-ceiling tests allow
   a cursor's word of cells (64) past their threshold, the published-count tests add the
   unflushed cells, and jit::poll_tests' overshoot bounds allow a word of 64-byte cells.

### W2e step 7: closures' helper, its Rust path (record, 2026-11-04)

Commits, from main 527c2db7, on p2-w2e-closures (an agent's; rebased onto main c7916f37, after
steps 5, 6 and 8, as 561622f4..c90b3889, in order: 862b5c62 561622f4, 91eb4d39 ebe1f141,
62f1ee85 635023aa, 2ded36c1 6de8558a, 80e54b25 3910c5d2, 287ab64c b2aad58d, a1f16afd 36d38cbb,
2582f306 aff2282c, b44a9e56 82cc8183, 002c2297 f9189ac2, 39c287b2 47dc061a, 4227f431 656193b2,
2f8136ce c90b3889; the measurements name the series as written, on 527c2db7, and the rebased
series is measured against c7916f37 at the end):
- 862b5c62: the deferred prototype looks `constructor` up in its template by atom, not by text.
- 91eb4d39: one runtime lease per closure (ObjectKind::user_leased), and the closure's record
  proved without the walk of the contexts (Interp::innermost_runtime_origin_identity,
  ClosureOrigin).
- 62f1ee85: object cells allocated through the Agent's tag (with_agent_heap), not the
  active-heap cell.
- 2ded36c1: one borrow (one card) for the prototype slot and the constructor bit; the function
  borrowed, not counted up and down.
- 80e54b25: a function template's copy in one pass over its record.
- 287ab64c: jit_make_closure's opt-in profile out of its line.
- a1f16afd: the test, function_runtime_tests::
  closures_of_one_function_keep_the_script_each_was_made_in.
- 2582f306: the deferred prototype told whether its template has `constructor`.
- b44a9e56: a self-name environment binds its function's atom (FunctionMaps::self_name); the
  self-environment query's scan out of line.
- 002c2297: the foreign runtime lease out of the primary lookup's line.
- 39c287b2: the function template's copy in line, any other template's out of it.
- 4227f431: Function::runtime stays a call.
- 2f8136ce: the closure's lease taken in line.

Where a function expression's 1,057 instructions went on main (SPE on a fat-LTO build with line
tables, target/p2/w2e/closures/prof; PMU sampling's skid had put 175 in active_runtime_origin,
SPE about 90): make_function_named itself about 300 (of which the deferred prototype's cell
about 100), the object's cell about 120, ObjectKind::user about 115 (its UserCallable cell
about 50), the template copy about 115, the text lookup about 120 (Props::get, its key scan and
memcmp), active_runtime_origin about 90, Function::runtime twice about 60, jit_make_closure
about 55, needs_self_environment about 17.

What changed, and why:
1. The text lookups. The "key lookups by text inlined into make_function_named" were
   LazyFunctionPrototype::new's `template.contains("constructor")`: every constructor
   function's creation asked its prototype template for the key by text. 862b5c62 took the
   atom (-64); the lookup by atom still cost about 65 (Props::get_key's general path), and
   2582f306 passes the bit, which the creation knows (the template has `constructor` unless the
   function is a generator; debug builds compare it with the lookup). A self-name environment's
   binding cloned the name's String for each closure and interned it again in the record's
   insert; the function's heap-local maps keep the atom now (b44a9e56: a named expression whose
   body uses its name, -186).
2. The origin (91eb4d39). ObjectKind::user compared the record active_runtime_origin returns with
   the function's primary entry's by identity, and shared it when they were the same: the walk
   and an Rc count up and down for each closure, most of them sharing. The walk's first step
   decides it whenever the innermost context has its own record: with no caller floor at the
   innermost depth, no job context pushed at or above it and no splice position, the record is
   the innermost code frame's, else the innermost function frame's callee's
   (UserCallable::runtime_origin). innermost_runtime_origin_identity gives that record's
   address under exactly those conditions, else null, and a closure whose function's primary
   entry holds the same record shares it (ClosureOrigin::Primary) with no walk and no count;
   anything else walks as before (ClosureOrigin::Active), and the record is boxed or shared as
   ObjectKind::user decided it. Debug builds assert, for every closure the proof shares, that
   the walk returns the same record (the lib suite runs it on each closure it makes). The test
   runs one parsed script under two bases, so one function's closures are made under two
   records: each closure keeps its creating context's record, identity and referrer, in the
   three tiers, whichever record the function's first closure had; a fast path that took the
   shared record without the proof fails it. MakeClosure is never spliced
   (inline_capability), so a closure is made in its own frame. The exemplars keep a function's
   script with its code (V8's SharedFunctionInfo, the canonical function's script that
   SpiderMonkey's Lambda stub copies, JSC's FunctionExecutable) and compute nothing at
   creation; TRust's records can differ between runs of shared parsed code, so the walk stays
   for the cases the proof does not cover.
3. One lease (91eb4d39, 002c2297, 4227f431, 2f8136ce). make_function_named took the function's
   runtime lease and ObjectKind::user took it again; user_leased takes the first. The lease's
   foreign path (another heap's entries, their pruning) sat in FunctionRuntimeCache::current's
   line and made every lease save all the callee-saved registers; it is a cold call. That made
   Function::runtime small enough for LLVM to inline at 33 of its 42 call sites, and
   Chunk::instance_class, which grew with it, left jit_construct_this's line (`new P(x, y)` +15
   at 002c2297): Function::runtime stays a call (4227f431), and make_function_named takes its
   lease through current, in line (2f8136ce).
4. The template copy (80e54b25, 39c287b2). Props::init_copy_of (only closures call it) copied
   each slot through NamedEntries::slot, which tests the index against the template's cell and
   the record's presence, stored the count at each slot and then copied the template's elements.
   A template outside any cell whose record has no index or extras, without elements, whose slots
   fit the new cell, as a function's are, now copies its record's words straight into the cell
   and sets the count once, in line; any other template takes the general copy, out of line.
5. The allocation (62f1ee85). Object::alloc_empty and Object::alloc found the heap through
   with_active_gc_heap, which borrowed and released the thread-local active-heap RefCell at
   each allocation; with_agent_heap reads the heap the Agent's tag names, as the payload,
   string and scope cells' allocations already did. Every object allocation gains (`[i, i]`,
   `arguments` and rest arrays -12 to -13, `new P(x, y)` -11).
6. Smaller cuts. One borrow (one card mark) for the prototype slot and the constructor bit, which
   only a function with that slot gets, and the function borrowed instead of an Rc count up and
   down (2ded36c1, -19 to -21). jit_make_closure's in-line jit_opstat made the helper save every
   callee-saved register (287ab64c, -11). Function::needs_self_environment answers false in line
   for every closure but a named expression's: in 287ab64c LLVM still inlined the scan back into
   the query, which then stayed a call; the scan is #[inline(never)] since b44a9e56 (-13).

Not done: the deferred prototype's cell. A constructor function's closure allocates three cells
(the object, its UserCallable and its LazyFunctionPrototype, 48 bytes); V8 (an accessor and the
hole in prototype_or_initial_map), SpiderMonkey (a resolve hook) and JSC (lazy reification)
allocate nothing for `prototype` at creation. TRust's lazy word names a per-closure cell because
materialization reads only the word, not its owner; removing the cell needs the owner found at
materialization, a change of the property words' contract. Each cell's allocation is about 45
instructions of the allocator's own (gc_core's bitmap take, the live count and the byte
charge), which step 8's refill charge and an in-line path address. The in-line closure
(step 7's second half) needs those three cells and the kind payload; its template copy is now
straight words, as the in-line literals' images are.

### W2f: a chunk-level card summary, closed by its census (census, survey and record, 2026-11-04)

W2f as planned: one more barrier store, a summary byte per chunk of pages, so that a minor
collection skips the page summaries of clean chunks. Census and survey on main e288bfd5 (whose
engine is 1037fefd's), an agent's. W2f closes with this record and no barrier change (below).

What the barrier and the scan are now:
- The barrier stores two bytes from the cell's address alone, with no load and no branch: the
  card of the cell's start in the 512-byte table that ends its 64 KiB page, and the page's summary,
  the table's last byte (gc_core.rs:76-91, 101-122). Rust calls it on every mutable borrow of an
  object or environment (gc_handle.rs:90-110, env_handle.rs:88-107). Generated code stores the
  same bytes after a store of a word that can be a reference: five instructions on ARM64
  (jit.rs:11902-11939) at ten sites, six on x86-64 (jit_x64_names.rs:374-390) at three.
- It records every written cell, young or old. A minor collection reads every page slot's
  metadata and, for a carded page, its summary byte; a page with its summary set has its cards
  read and cleared: a page that received cells since the last collection is walked by its old
  cells, any other by its set cards (gc_core.rs:1076-1144, 3398-3406). The old cells found (the
  card cells) are traced as roots.
- TRUST_JS_GC_LOG's `begin` phase holds both passes and the cursors' flush; nothing reports
  pages, summaries or dirty cards. Hence the census patch.

Census (target/p2/w2f/census: apply-patch.py, apply-patch-2.py, apply-patch-3.py, run.sh,
batch.sh, batch2.sh, runone.py, parse.py, tables.py, tables2.py, summary.txt; commits 31377809
and a7c2a448 on p2-w2f-census, from main e288bfd5; fat LTO; CPU 15, an X925 core, one run at a
time, median of 3; each run behind a gate of under 8 busy cores, its other load recorded: a median
of 2.5-4.0 cores, other sessions' test suites and builds and a game). With TRUST_JS_GC_LOG on, the
patch times the summary pass (every page slot's metadata and each carded page's summary byte,
listing the set pages) and the card pass (take_card_cells on the set pages) apart; a count mode
counts pages, carded and set pages, dirty cards, 2 MiB chunks, and the card cells and their
edges. With TRUST_JS_GC_LOG off it runs main's loop, and those runs give the run times. The two
passes' sum agrees with main's single pass timed the same way (TypeScript 1,778 against 1,763 us
a minor, chai 45.1 against 45.2).

Pacing on (the default), per minor collection: counts are means over a run's minors, times the
median of 3 runs' means; shares are of the minors' mark and sweep time and of the run's wall time
(runs without the log):

The summary:
- The summary pass costs 11-24 ns a page slot (7-22 with pacing off): 0.6-7.6 us a minor on every
  workload but TypeScript, whose 4,187 pages take 58 us (at most 359; its heap peaks at 8,979
  pages). That is 0.12-0.68% of minor time and at most 0.035% of any run at the default pacing.
  With pacing off, the same heaps collect more often: TypeScript's pass is 2.6% of its minor time
  and 0.17% of its run, the sustained workload's 2.0% and 0.11%, the rest under 0.7% and 0.17%.
- Splay's heap now averages 622 pages (1,763 at most): the 0.4 ms of P2's start, on a ~480 MB heap
  (about 7,300 pages), is 7.4 us (23 us at most).
- About half of the pass is the walk of the page slots' metadata: alone it takes 0.3-3.5 us a
  minor, TypeScript 26.5 of its 58.
- The set pages are young pages: the barrier records writes into young cells too, so a page that
  received cells since the last collection mostly has its summary set. Old pages with a set
  summary: 0 to 7 a minor, but TypeScript's 141; their dirty cards: 0 to 3, but Box2D's 879 and
  TypeScript's 17,930.
- The young pages are spread over the heap's 2 MiB chunks (allocation fills pages with free cells
  before it takes a new one, gc_core.rs:2479-2516): at the default pacing 55-100% of the chunks hold
  a set page (TypeScript 93 of 130, Splay 11 of 20, chai 7 of 8, each fixture all of its 2 or 3).
  So a chunk summary would read 1-11% more summary bytes on seven workloads (the chunk bytes on
  top of the same pages) and 4-42% fewer on four: at most 3 us a minor on Splay and 12 us on
  TypeScript. With pacing off the set pages gather in fewer chunks (TypeScript 24 of 142): 60-76%
  fewer bytes on Splay, the sustained workload and TypeScript, whose 35 us a minor come to 0.13%
  of its run [arithmetic, taking the pass's time to follow the bytes it reads: 76% of 46.6 us over
  509 minors is 18 ms of 13.6 s].

The card pass and the card cells (second build; target/p2/w2f/census/summary.txt, last tables):
- The card pass costs more than the summary pass on every workload but the sustained one, up to
  53 times (EarleyBoyer): 2.7-1,720 us a minor, 0.3-13.6% of minor time; EarleyBoyer 225 us
  (13.6%, 1.0% of its run; 14.7% and 1.6% with pacing off), TypeScript 1,720 us (9.5%, 0.49%),
  Box2D 67 us (8.6%). It reads only the set pages, so no summary above the page changes it.
- 75-99% of it is the walk of the young set pages by their old cells, at 3-9 ns a cell walked
  (the sustained workload, with 48 a minor, aside): TypeScript walks 361,539 old cells a minor on
  660 young pages (1,503 of its 1,739 us), EarleyBoyer 76,673 (222 of 224 us).
- At the default pacing the card cells are 73-100% old cells on young pages, and few hold an
  edge to a young cell: 0-36% of them (TypeScript 8% of 69,766 a minor, whose 323,104 edges hold
  7,551 young ones; EarleyBoyer 4 young edges in 9,586; chai 5 in 3,206). [The edges are the
  cells' full trace's; a minor collection's trace of an element block reports only its dirty runs,
  word_block.rs:18-23.]
- Where minor time goes: tracing (`drain`) is 84% on TypeScript and 98% on Splay; chai's 403
  minors spend 26% in the side tables and 35% in the engine's steps around the heap's phases.

What the exemplars do (target/p2/w2f/exemplars.txt; V8 c8b70908, SpiderMonkey 6bb81100, JSC
26027804, each re-diffed against its main of 2026-11-04, where the cited code is unchanged):
- A1: every barrier filters before it records, and a store into a young holder records nothing.
  V8 tests the value's and the host's page flags, a load each from the page header (V8
  macro-assembler-arm64.cc:4376-4436, 3786-3799); SM tests that the value is in a nursery chunk
  and the holder is not, a load each from the chunk header
  (SM jit/arm64/MacroAssembler-arm64.cpp:2093-2135); JSC tests the holder's state byte in its
  header against a threshold (JSC jit/AssemblyHelpers.h:1275-1285). JSC's heap is non-moving with
  sticky mark bits, like TRust's (JSC heap/BlockDirectory.cpp:304-306); so is V8's sticky-mark-bits
  build, whose barrier tests the host's and the value's mark bits (V8
  macro-assembler-x64.cc:1257-1275; off by default).
- A2: the slow path records the slot or the holder, at most once per cycle: V8 the slot, a bit in
  its page's slot set (V8 builtins-internal-gen.cc:167-245); SM's Baseline the holder, a bit in its
  4 KiB arena's bitmap behind a last-cell cache (SM jit/BaselineCodeGen.cpp:650-702;
  SM gc/StoreBuffer-inl.h:62-84); JSC the holder, appended to a mark stack as its state turns grey
  (JSC heap/Heap.cpp:1165-1221).
- A3: discovery: V8 walks every old page's metadata, 256 KiB a page, and iterates the slot sets
  of the pages that have one (V8 scavenger.cc:1689-1703, 2440-2457); SM and JSC read no heap, only
  their lists (SM gc/Nursery.cpp:1741-1804; JSC heap/MarkStackMergingConstraint.cpp:60-74).
- A4: none keeps a summary above the page; V8's walk, the only one, tests pointers in each
  page's metadata (V8 mutable-page.h:288-295).
- A5: the fast path is loads and branches; stores happen only on the slow path (SM ARM64: 3 to 9
  instructions, no store; JSC: 7 with 2 loads, plus the operands' loads) [inferred, from the
  emitters].

Options:
1. A chunk summary byte (W2f as planned). A third store in gc_core::card_mark and in both
   emitters, and a byte per 2 MiB chunk at an address computed from the cell's. No page has a free
   byte for it today: a chunk's pages are pages of cells, carded or not, so the page source would
   reserve a page a chunk (3.1% of a huge page) or a tail in every page. It saves at most 3 us a
   minor on Splay and 12 on TypeScript at the default pacing, and costs a little on the small
   heaps. No exemplar keeps one (A4).
2. A dirty-page list: the barrier tests the page's summary and, the first time in a cycle, a slow
   path sets it and appends the page to the heap's list, which the minor collection reads instead
   of the page slots. A load and a branch instead of the summary store, and a call per page per
   cycle (12-799 a minor). It saves the summary pass, 0.6-58 us a minor. It is SM's arena list
   and JSC's mark stack at a page's granularity (A2, A3), without their filter (A1).
3. A dense list of the carded pages' addresses (Rust only): the pass reads 8 bytes a carded page
   instead of every slot's metadata. It saves up to the metadata walk, 0.3-26.5 us a minor. V8's
   walk is a list of old pages (A3).
4. Leave the summary as it is.

Recommendation: option 4, and W2f closed with this census as its record. The summary pass is
under 0.7% of every workload's minor time and 0.035% of its run at the default pacing (TypeScript
2.6% and 0.17% with pacing off). A chunk summary would save at most 42% of it at the default pacing
(3 us a minor) and 76% of TypeScript's with pacing off (0.13% of its run), and cost a little on
the fixtures, for a third store at every barrier site and a page layout change. The exemplars do
not support it (A4).

What to do instead: the census points at the card pass and the card cells, not the summary.
Both come from recording every write: a write into a young cell sets its card and its page's
summary, so the minor collection walks the old cells of every young page written since the last
one (75-99% of the card pass), and it traces old cells whose cards were set but which hold no
young cell (64-100% of the card cells). The card pass alone is up to 13.6% of minor time and 1.0%
of a run at the default pacing (EarleyBoyer; 14.7% and 1.6% with pacing off); the useless cells'
tracing comes on top, untimed here. Every exemplar filters before it records (A1): V8 and SM by
the value's youth and the holder's age, JSC by the holder's state byte alone, on a non-moving heap
with sticky marks like TRust's. A holder filter would leave most young pages' summaries clear
(no walk of their old cells), and a value filter would drop the cells written with old values
[inferred]. TRust keeps its marks in side bitmaps (gc_core.rs:938-944), so either test would read
a page's mark bits (V8's sticky-mark-bits barrier) or a header byte that marking sets (JSC's
holder test). It turns the summary store into loads and a branch at every barrier, a price this
census did not count. If the owner wants this cost taken, it is its own item, with a census of
barrier executions and of the card cells written themselves (against those that only share a
card), and JSC's cell state evaluated first; W2's exit does not need it.

Change surface, if option 1 or 2 went ahead:
- Rust: gc_core::card_mark (gc_core.rs:101-122) and its callers, the handles' mutable borrows
  (gc_handle.rs:90-110, env_handle.rs:88-107).
- ARM64: emit_card_mark (jit.rs:11902-11939) at its ten sites (jit.rs: emit_set_prop_inline,
  emit_prologue_copies, emit_store_name_value, emit_word_store_body, emit_word_create,
  emit_define_elem_inline; jit_operations.rs: emit_cap_store); option 2 also needs a heap pointer
  and a slow-path call at each.
- x86-64: card_mark (jit_x64_names.rs:374-390) at its three sites (store_value,
  emit_activation_copies, emit_fixed_cap).
- The minor collection: begin_marking's loop and take_card_cells (gc_core.rs:3398-3406,
  1076-1144); for option 1 the page source, which carves pages from chunks of 2 MiB
  (gc_core.rs:495-512); for option 2 the list's reset at each collection.
- Verification: check_write_barrier's summary check (gc_core.rs:3492-3500), extended to the chunk
  byte or the list.
- Tests: card_mark_sets_exactly_its_cells_card, carded_pages_end_in_a_card_table,
  no_cell_resolves_in_a_card_table, cards_clear_when_a_collection_starts, the two write-barrier
  check tests and the minor-collection card tests
  (gc_core.rs:7715, 7746, 7844, 7893, 8034, 8057, 8146, 8196), and the backends' card tests
  (jit_names_tests.rs:182-200; jit_x64_names_tests.rs:231, 420, 563).

Exit (proposed): W2f closed by its census, with no barrier change, and the census line of the
plan's start ("~0.4 ms per minor on Splay's ~480 MB heap") superseded by this record. Were option 1
or 2 chosen instead: the summary pass's time a minor halved on TypeScript and Splay, no fixture or
Octane benchmark slower beyond noise (instructions; the barrier is on every store site), and the
backends' barrier tests and TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY's barrier check passing.

Decided (mine, reported to the owner):
1. W2f closes with this record (option 4): no barrier change. The census line of the plan's start
   ("~0.4 ms per minor on Splay's ~480 MB heap") is superseded by the tables above.
2. The filtering barrier (A1) is carried to Phase 3 (see "Carried to Phase 3"): the card pass and
   the card cells without a young edge matter to minor collections on EarleyBoyer, TypeScript and
   Box2D, not to the fixtures, whose minor collections are a small part of their runs.

### W2e's exit (record, 2026-11-04)

W2e ran from main 13645c27 (b00bb6dc's engine) to 498d98d4 (c90b3889's engine): steps 1, 3 and 4
(literals in line), 5 and 6 (array literals with elements, the `this` of a direct construction),
8 (counts at the cursor's flush) with the shared stub's heap fix, and 7 (closures' helper, an
agent's, rebased onto step 8). Step 2 (objects' live count in line) went in with step 1 and left
with step 8, which counts at the cursor's flush. x86-64 keeps its helpers for the objects (its
template backend calls helpers for most operations) and shares steps 1 and 8 and the heap's load.

iterations of a function's loop, the same session):

Against the exit (the design note's):
- Instructions per operation: `{}` 84.4, `[]` 87.2 and `[1, 2, 3]` 87.4 (bound 100); `{x, y}`
  102.9 (bound 120). `new P(x, y)` is 523.2 with its call, its constructor's two stores and its
  return (a plain call is 231.2); its allocation is one in-line cell, as `{x, y}`'s is. Met.
- Splay -12.92% (bound -8%). Met.
- No fixture or Octane benchmark slower beyond noise: every workload falls but zlib (+0.0001%).
  Met.
- Collections: majors the same on all fourteen workloads counted (target/p2/w2e/refill/
  gccount-exit.txt); minors the same on eight and fewer on six (TypeScript 509 to 484, PdfJS 139 to
  132, chai 1,030 to 1,013, one fewer on Djot, Splay and the sustained workload), from step 8's
  trigger at the refill after the budget, with the same bytes promoted. The exit asked for the
  same counts; this is the one part not met to the letter, in the direction of less work.
- GC stress and verification: W2e's 22 allocation, count and two-engine tests pass under
  TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1000 with TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY=1, each in its own process
  (target/p2/w2e/stress.sh; the in-line counters' checks skip under stress, which turns the
  in-line path off: 498d98d4). The lib suites verify the heap at every collection. Met.

Found on the way (step 8's record): a shared stub is process-wide, so generated code builds in no
heap's or thread's address; the heap is read through the context.

Left for later stages: x86-64's in-line objects; NewObject (literals with spread, computed or
accessor keys: React's 8,746) and NewArray in line; the in-line closure (three cells and the kind
payload); the deferred prototype's cell; constructions the direct sequence declines (New: chai
736,167, TypeScript 681,917, RayTrace 332,643, Box2D 110,387), W3's.

### The phase exit's gap, measured (2026-11-04)

A timing snapshot on main 0d724e3d's engine (c90b3889, fat LTO), Crystal, pinned to CPU 5, five
rounds rotating the engines, at a quiet moment (other load a median 1.02 cores, at most 2.77;
target/p2/timing/snap-1104), against Node v24.22.2 in full and limited to Sparkplug (node-sp),
the fixtures' own elapsed_ms (medians):

| fixture | TRust | node-sp | Node | TRust / node-sp | at W3's exit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | 270 | 114 | 111 | 2.37x | 287 |
| React | 32 | 16 | 18 | 2.00x | 49 |
| Djot | 95 | 39 | 51 | 2.44x | 110 |

The phase's exit (each fixture within 2x of node-sp) holds for React only, at its edge. Vue needs
about -16% and Djot about -18%. The stages left in the plan (W2g, W4, W6) are not throughput work
on these fixtures, but W2g's WeakMap and Proxy paths reach Vue.

Where the time goes (perf, user cycles or instructions sampled; TRUST_JS_PERF_METRICS):
- Djot: the native compiler is 17.2% of its user cycles (163 compiles, 3.98 MB of code, 24 KB a
  function, the largest 190 KB; 32-35 ms of compile time in a 110 ms metrics run), and lexing,
  parsing and bytecode compilation another 14 ms. Of the compiler's cycles, region planning and
  emission are about 6 points (regions::Plan::build_kind 3.1%, regions::emit 2.1%, dominators),
  operations::emit 2.9%, ExecutableBuffer::from_bytes 1.6%, finish_with_offsets 1.4%. Its
  templates are large: per operation, GetPropLocal 497 bytes, CallWithThis 526, GetMethod 460,
  StrictEq 556, SetProp 828 (region code counts toward a region's head operation).
- React: 81% of its instructions are in generated code.
- Vue: 34% in generated code; the runtime's helpers lead: Interp::try_binding_getter 5.0% (the
  bundler's export accessors), jit_get_prop 3.4%, nf_weak_map_get 2.8% (W2g), jit_call_hit 2.4%,
  get_member_recv_impl 1.9%, PropKey position scans 1.7%.

Register regions, measured by kind (instructions, fat LTO, CPUs 10-13, pacing off; regions off
by TRUST_JS_JIT_FAST without bit 32768; acyclic or loop regions off in an experiment build,
target/p2/djot-compile):

| workload | no regions | no acyclic regions | no loop regions |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Vue | +0.84% | +0.22% | +0.42% |
| React | +13.34% | +0.98% | +15.12% |
| Djot | -2.37% | -2.14% | -0.59% |
| chai | +1.06% | -0.14% | -0.15% |
| Richards | +5.44% | +4.56% | +5.95% |
| DeltaBlue | +2.04% | +1.01% | -6.60% |
| Crypto | +122.22% | -0.16% | +104.42% |
| RayTrace | +0.07% | +0.55% | -0.73% |
| EarleyBoyer | -3.34% | -1.21% | -1.75% |
| RegExp | +1.21% | +0.97% | +0.45% |
| Splay | -1.08% | -0.46% | -0.18% |
| NavierStokes | +132.00% | +1.37% | +132.53% |
| PdfJS | +31.68% | -0.37% | +27.17% |
| Mandreel | +11.97% | -0.07% | +12.33% |
| Gameboy | +16.81% | +2.94% | +2.81% |
| CodeLoad | -0.86% | +0.83% | +0.08% |
| Box2D | -0.06% | -0.74% | -0.73% |
| zlib | +41.31% | +0.19% | +41.12% |
| TypeScript | +6.24% | -0.18% | -1.19% |

Regions are a large net gain. Acyclic regions cost Djot 2.1% and EarleyBoyer 1.2% (code that
does not run enough to repay their compile) and gain Richards 4.6% and Gameboy 2.9%; a loop
region costs DeltaBlue 6.6%.

What the exemplars do with the same choices (from the plan's earlier surveys): all three compile
a function to bytecode at its first call (TRust's threshold of 8 tree-walker calls is the
outlier; at 1, W3's exit measured Djot -8.6%, and TRust's event-loop page +129% because its
platform functions compile again on each page); their baseline code calls shared IC stubs or
builtins (Sparkplug, SpiderMonkey's Baseline with CacheIR stubs, JSC's Baseline with shared
thunks) and leaves in-line fast paths to the optimizing tiers.

Candidates for the exit, beside W2g: the threshold at 1 with TRust's pages reusing their
compiled platform code; acyclic regions only for code that has run (a recompile when hot), and
DeltaBlue's loop region; compact baseline templates, with today's in-line templates for hot code
(the compile's code volume); Vue's binding getters and property-read helpers.

### W5's open items, closed (record, 2026-11-04)

`MAX_SLOTS` (4,000 capture and mark slots) and the code-size cap (`MAX_CODE_UNITS`, 2,048) keep a
pattern on the matcher; neither has a pattern to move on the acceptance set. Under
TRUST_JS_REGEXP_PROF on main c90b3889 (target/p2/w5-caps), the native tier compiled 29 of 36
attempts on Djot, 134 of 217 on Octane RegExp and 28 of 37 on chai. Of Octane RegExp's 40 patterns
with the most work, all run natively but three: two on the single-element scan (`/[+, ]/`, `/./`)
and one below the tier's 64 executions (`/.+(?:ei|vg|en|vr)[\/: ]([\d.]+)/`, 60). The refused
compilations' patterns are outside the top 40 by work. Both caps stay. W5's exit keeps its timing
(Octane RegExp's score at least 1.15x W5a's), owed to the stage-end timing on a quiet machine.

### W2g: exotic state into payload cells (census, survey and design, 2026-11-04)

W2g as planned: exotic state into payload cells, as P1 did for Map and Set: proxies, promises,
generators, `class_info`, RegExps (with W5), typed arrays. Other stages handed it four items:
WeakMap's single probe (W3 2d, Decision 10), mapped arguments' slot map (W3 item 6), String
wrappers' code units (W2c's census) and bound functions' data finalizing (W2a's record). Census
and survey on main 498d98d4, whose engine is c90b3889's (target/p2/w2g; README.txt lists the
files, census-table.md holds the full tables), an agent's. Decided (mine, reported): steps 1-7 in
the order below, each its own series; step 8 waits for an async workload's census; step 9 is not
done.

What the tables are now:
- 37 address-keyed tables on Interp hold exotic state, caches and pins. Twelve are side-slot
  tables (gc_collect.rs:328-408), whose values are edges traced once the owner is marked:
  proxies, promises, generators, `class_info`, module_ns, ta_buffer, host_indexed,
  promise_forward, routed_imports, async_gen_queue, finalization_registries and
  construct_capacity_hints (interpreter.rs:5551-5567, 4797, 4967, 4988, 5043). The others are
  FastMaps and sets: typed_arrays, array_buffers and data_views, regexps, the WeakMap index and
  data, htmldda, eval_realm_fns and gc_pins among them (interpreter.rs:4803, 4904, 4913, 4934,
  4946, 4959, 4978). The native functions' [[Realm]] table is in a RefCell beside them
  (interpreter.rs:4807).
- Reads go through accessors that test the table's emptiness first (proxy_at,
  interpreter.rs:11481-11490). For an object whose `ic_plain` byte is clear, the ordinary-get
  check tests four tables and probes each non-empty one (interpreter.rs:11396-11404). The
  [[Get]] slow path tests every hop for a deferred namespace, a proxy, a host-indexed object and
  a typed array before its own lookup (interpreter.rs:11843-11920).
- A side-slot table's write adds its owner to ClientRecords' set (gc_collect.rs:358-392), which
  each collection clears, shrinking it to 64 slots once it has passed 1,024
  (gc_collect.rs:295-306). An object with an entry in most tables is also pinned in gc_pins
  (interpreter.rs:13325-13340): every Proxy, RegExp, typed array and buffer, namespace and
  promise.
- The collector makes every side-slot owner a conditional edge. A major collection notes every
  table's keys and every pin, a minor the recorded owners (gc_collect.rs:894-944,
  gc_core.rs:3353-3366). A marked owner's edge probes gc_pins and the twelve tables
  (gc_collect.rs:947-985, 1520-1531); an owner left unmarked waits in the marker's `waiting` map
  (gc_core.rs:3827-3842). After marking, gc_evict_dead_owners removes the dead objects' entries
  from 29 tables, min(dead objects, capacity) operations each (interpreter.rs:13392-13600,
  gc_sweep.rs:186-203).
- A Proxy clears the realm-wide `inline_ic_safe` latch (builtins/proxy.rs:28), which the in-line
  `instanceof` walk tests (jit.rs:11685; eval.rs:8880). With a Proxy alive, every `instanceof`
  takes the helper: 158,205 on chai (TRUST_JS_JIT_OPSTAT).
- Already in the object: Map and Set data (ObjectKind::Collection, value.rs:2242), a typed
  array's native view (value.rs:2207, whose TaInfo and buffer object stay in typed_arrays and
  ta_buffer), a mapped arguments object's ParameterMap (value.rs:2257-2259, W3 item 6), a String
  wrapper's string (StrWrap; its code units are also materialized as properties,
  builtins/mod.rs:12599-12628) and a bound function's data (value.rs:2287-2295).

Census (target/p2/w2g: census/, census2/, prof/, micro/, gclog/, tools/; fat LTO; CPUs 10-13,
pacing off). A counting patch (p2-w2g-census, e79168e9 and ed95bb74 on main 498d98d4) wraps each
table and counts its probes by table, operation, call site (`#[track_caller]`, also on the
accessors) and whether the table was empty. SPE profiles of c90b3889 built with line tables
attribute the in-line probes and the collector's side-table work by inline chain. Costs: a probe
into a non-empty table takes 28 instructions (24-33 by SPE over the census's counts; the
out-of-line get's disassembly: 28 on a hit at the first candidate, about 22 on a miss), an insert
or owner record 50, a probe into an empty table 4, an emptiness guard 2. A kind-tag test takes
about 3 on a borrowed object.

Probes the mutator makes into non-empty tables (inserts, records and pins included):

Richards, Crypto, EarleyBoyer, Splay, NavierStokes and CodeLoad make under 10,000 of any item's.
Promises, generators and `class_info` take no measurable probe on the acceptance set: no
fixture, chai or Octane benchmark awaits, yields or constructs a class with fields in volume.

Their instructions (the model's, millions and share of the run) and the collector's side-table
work (SPE: the eviction, conditional edges, owner noting and trace probes):

| workload | instructions | Proxy | typed arrays | WeakMap | RegExp | IsHTMLDDA | native [[Realm]] | construct caches | guards, empty | mutator | collector |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| proxy-direct | 8.11G | 314.2 | | | | | | | 79.3 | 393.5 (4.85%) | 0.4 |

The other workloads' totals are at most 0.27% (DeltaBlue); React's, Splay's and EarleyBoyer's
(0.14-0.15%) are almost all the collector's.
- Proxy: Vue's probes are the [[Get]] entry's and each hop's (get_member_recv_impl 284k,
  get_from_chain 325k), the builtins' (proxy_pair 147k) and the call dispatch's (79k); chai's are
  [[Set]]'s (6.2M), [[Get]]'s (4.2M) and HasProperty's (1.6M), and it creates 371,886 proxies.
- Typed arrays: Gameboy reads `length` (or another of %TypedArray.prototype%'s accessors)
  through the Rust [[Get]] 5.1M times, two probes each (typed_arrays in typed_array_at,
  array_buffers in array_buffer_byte_length; interpreter.rs:11710, 12944-12953). perf names that
  probe `HashMap<usize, HashMap<String, NsBinding>>::get` (the owner's first pass: module_ns
  1.04%): one machine body serves every HashMap<usize, V> of its bucket size; module_ns has no
  probe there outside the collector.
- WeakMap: a get probes the brand's index, the entries index and the data
  (builtins/collections.rs:998-1017): 84 of the micro-benchmark's 535 instructions; Vue makes
  160k.
- IsHTMLDDA: every realm installs `$262` with an IsHTMLDDA object (builtins/mod.rs:3226,
  builtins/host.rs:25), so htmldda is never empty and the VM's and helpers' `typeof`, ToBoolean
  and loose equality of an object probe it (is_htmldda, interpreter.rs:12068-12076, 12082;
  eval.rs:9045, 9315-9316): TypeScript 4.7M.
- Native [[Realm]]: a native call from the VM probes eval_realm_fns and native_function_realms
  (interpreter.rs:8843-8852): RayTrace's 333k.
- Construct caches (not exotic state): TypeScript's 3.18M inserts are try_ic_create's
  unconditional re-insert of a prototype pin already present (interpreter.rs:11349).
- Guards and empty tables: the emptiness tests (Gameboy 25.7M, chai 42.5M, TypeScript 20.1M).
- The collector: chai's 338M are mostly the eviction (242M: each of its 1,013 minors finds 4,757
  dead objects on average and removes each one's key from gc_pins, about 10,100 slots, and the
  smaller tables are scanned) and the conditional edges (82M); Gameboy's 58M are its typed
  arrays' and buffers' edges and trace probes (4,001 of each live at a major).

Per operation (instructions an iteration on main, the difference of 1.2M and 200k iterations of
a function's loop over 1M; the loop alone is 13; micro/per-op.md; probes from the census, and
for five of them the side-table share from SPE, collector included):

| operation | instructions | probes (non-empty / inserts / empty / guards) | side-table work |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| an await | 4,952 | 5 / 6 / 2 / 0 | 25% (probes and record sets 15%, collector 9.7%) |
| a generator step | 4,851 | 1 / 1 / 1 / 17 | 3% |
| `new C()`, two fields | 4,346 | 5 / 0 / 10 / 15 | 4.8% (model) |
| `new Promise` + `then` | 20,250 | 8 / 9 / 3 / 12 | 12% |
| RegExp exec | 2,331 | 1 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 1.2% (model) |
| a RegExp literal | 1,124 | 0 / 2 / 0 / 0 | 21% (probes 11%, collector 9.3%) |
| a typed array's `length` and an element | 1,365 | 2 / 0 / 0 / 5 | 4.8% (model) |
| WeakMap get | 535 | 3 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 16% (model) |
| a read through a Proxy without handlers | 1,256 | 3 / 0 / 0 / 12 | 8.6% (model) |
| a 4-deep prototype miss, a Proxy alive | 2,346 | 8 / 0 / 0 / 26 | 177 more than without (2,169) |
| `typeof` of an object and a test | 430 | 1 / 0 / 0 / 0 | 6.5% (model) |
| a Proxy's creation | 2,297 | 1 / 3 / 1 / 0 | 41% (probes 7.6%, record and noting sets 13%, collector 20%) |

A Proxy's creation and collection takes about 950 instructions of side-table work, most of it
after its death: its conditional edge waits in the marker's map, the noting and record sets
regrow, and its eviction probes two tables. A generator step removes the coroutine from its
table and inserts it back with a record (builtins/mod.rs:10664-10678).

Live entries and bytes: at a major collection the tables hold at most Gameboy's 4,001 typed
arrays and buffers (13,669 slots each in four tables and 26,387 in gc_pins: about 2.1 MB of
buckets); every other workload's are under 0.5 MB (chai's 0.45 MB, most of it gc_pins' spare
capacity; census2/*.tsv, `major` lines). Memory is not W2g's case. Finalizers
(TRUST_JS_GC_LOG): bound functions' data at most 10 a run (PdfJS), Map and Set data at most 806
(chai), typed arrays' views 11,976 (Gameboy).

What the exemplars do (target/p2/w2g/exemplars-{v8,sm,jsc}.txt; V8 c8b70908, SpiderMonkey
6bb81100, JSC 26027804; each re-diffed against its main of 2026-11-04, where the cited code is
unchanged: V8 7615c0a0, SM 98bddef2, JSC c27ccd84; citations checked, target/p2/w2g/cite-*.txt):
- A1: every kind's state is fields or slots of the object itself, and no engine keys it by
  address. Proxy target and handler (V8 src/objects/js-proxy.tq:5-11; SM vm/ProxyObject.h:54-62
  and proxy/ScriptedProxyHandler.h:108-117; JSC runtime/ProxyObject.h:32-49); a promise's state,
  result and reactions (V8 src/objects/js-promise.tq:5-39; SM vm/PromiseObject.h:74-100; JSC
  runtime/JSPromise.h:37-42); a generator's suspended frame (V8 src/objects/js-generator.tq:15-39;
  SM vm/GeneratorObject.h:28-43; JSC runtime/JSGenerator.h:66-81); a RegExp's program and
  lastIndex (V8 src/objects/js-regexp.tq:65-69, src/objects/js-regexp.h:134-140; SM
  vm/RegExpObject.h:42-55; JSC runtime/RegExpObject.h:42-45); a typed array's view (V8
  src/objects/js-array-buffer.tq:61-127; SM vm/ArrayBufferViewObject.h:28-56; JSC
  runtime/JSArrayBufferView.h:374-377); a bound function's target, this and arguments (V8
  src/objects/js-function.tq:9-20; SM vm/BoundFunctionObject.h:19-99; JSC
  runtime/JSBoundFunction.h:133-140).
- A2: the property-get path tells an exotic object from an ordinary one by one test of the
  object's header or map at each holder: V8 compares the map's instance type with
  LAST_SPECIAL_RECEIVER_TYPE (V8 src/objects/objects-inl.h:901-912; src/objects/lookup.cc:
  1360-1443), SpiderMonkey tests the class's getProperty hook through the shape (SM
  vm/ObjectOperations-inl.h:104-112; vm/NativeObject.cpp:2322-2376), JSC tests a byte the cell
  header copies from its structure (JSC runtime/JSCell.h:293-302; runtime/JSObject.h:1185-1232).
  None has a "no Proxy exists" protector (V8 src/execution/protectors.h:18-106; SM
  vm/RuntimeFuses.h:51-55; JSC runtime/JSGlobalObject.h:493-547).
- A3: a proxy's callability is a property of its map or handler, not another kind: V8 picks one
  of three proxy maps from the target (V8 src/heap/factory.cc:4304-4324), SpiderMonkey asks the
  handler (SM vm/ProxyObject.h:134-137), JSC picks one of two structures (JSC
  runtime/ProxyObject.cpp:59).
- A4: the collector traces the state as the object's own fields and records stores with the
  ordinary write barrier; nothing is evicted by address: V8's visitor follows the instance type
  and an old-to-young store enters OLD_TO_NEW (V8 src/objects/map.cc:228-273;
  src/heap/heap-write-barrier.cc:373-420); SpiderMonkey traces reserved slots as slots and its
  store buffer records the slot range (SM gc/Marking.cpp:1907-1996; gc/Barrier.h:1023-1126); JSC's
  visitChildren appends the members and its barrier greys the owner (JSC
  runtime/ProxyObject.cpp:1293-1301; runtime/WriteBarrierInlines.h:34-63).
- A5: few of these kinds need anything at death: in V8 only an ArrayBuffer's extension, swept
  (V8 src/heap/array-buffer-sweeper.cc:440-445); SpiderMonkey finalizes proxies, fixed-length
  typed arrays, ArrayBuffers, WeakRefs and registries (SM proxy/Proxy.cpp:938-955;
  vm/ArrayBufferObject.cpp:310-312); JSC destroys RegExp, WeakMapImpl, module namespaces and
  registries (JSC runtime/RegExp.h:63; runtime/WeakMapImpl.h:216;
  runtime/JSModuleNamespaceObject.h:39; runtime/JSFinalizationRegistry.h:75).
- A6: a WeakMap's table hangs off the collection and a get is one lookup in it: V8's `table`
  (V8 src/objects/js-collection.tq:18-27) with the key's identity hash in the key (V8
  src/codegen/code-stub-assembler.cc:2721-2786, src/builtins/builtins-collections-gen.cc:
  2828-2879); SpiderMonkey's C++ WeakMap in a slot, hashed by pointer bits (SM
  builtin/WeakMapObject.h:13-19; gc/WeakMap.h:275-322); JSC's WeakMapImpl buckets in the cell,
  hashed by address (JSC runtime/WeakMapImpl.h:470-506; runtime/WeakMapImplInlines.h:38-41).
- A7: an undetectable object (IsHTMLDDA, document.all) is a bit of the object's type: V8's map
  bit, tested with is_callable as one mask (V8 src/objects/map.h:333-349); SpiderMonkey's class
  flag (SM js/public/Class.h:516, 691); JSC's type-info flag (JSC runtime/JSTypeInfo.h:42, 93).
- A8: a function's realm is reached from the function: V8 through its context (V8
  src/objects/js-function-inl.h:403-404), SpiderMonkey through its shape (SM vm/Shape.h:246,
  261, 393), JSC through the callee's scope (JSC runtime/JSCallee.h:56-58, 95).
- A9: class constructors are told apart by the function's kind and their fields run from the
  constructor's own code: V8 gives them an instance type its Call builtin compares (V8
  src/objects/instance-type.h:201-203; src/builtins/x64/builtins-x64.cc:2110-2125), SpiderMonkey
  a function-kind field (SM vm/FunctionFlags.h:41-45; jit/MacroAssembler-inl.h:632-640), JSC a
  constructor kind whose call code block throws (JSC bytecompiler/BytecodeGenerator.cpp:597-611);
  the field initializer is a closure on the constructor (V8
  src/interpreter/bytecode-generator.cc:3808-3815; SM frontend/ObjectEmitter.cpp:687-773; JSC
  bytecompiler/NodesCodegen.cpp:5855-5868).
- A10: a generator's live registers are copied into the generator object at a suspend (V8
  src/interpreter/interpreter-generator.cc:3530-3624; SM vm/GeneratorObject.cpp:26-57; JSC
  bytecode/BytecodeGeneratorification.cpp:142-167).
- A11: a String wrapper answers its indices from the string (V8 src/objects/elements.cc:
  5729-5800; SM builtin/String.cpp:393-449; JSC runtime/StringObject.cpp:47-62).

Design. An exotic kind's state lives in its object (A1): a tag of ObjectKind and the state in
the kind's payload word or in a PayloadBox, P1's pattern for Map and Set (value.rs:2242;
gc_storage.rs:180-217). The object's trace reports the payload and traces its edges; a change
goes through the object's mutable borrow, which sets its card; the payload dies with the object.
The [[Get]], [[Set]] and HasProperty paths test the object once per holder (A2): `ic_plain` for
an ordinary object, then the kind tag. A side table stays only for state keyed by something
other than one object. Steps, in the order of their measured value on the acceptance set
(savings by the model: a probe into a non-empty table becomes a tag test, 25 instructions saved;
an insert, record or pin goes, 50 each, for a payload cell's 30 per object; the collector's
work goes with its owners; tools/savings.py, savings.md):

1. Proxy. ObjectKind::Proxy(PayloadBox<ProxyData>): target, handler (null once revoked) and
   whether the target was callable (A3). is_callable, a range of tags today (value.rs:2529-2531),
   gains one compare for the Proxy tag; IsConstructor is already a flag bit the creation sets,
   and the call dispatch's proxy test (interpreter.rs:14185) becomes a tag test, where the
   `Native(proxy_uncallable)` kind stands in for callability today (builtins/proxy.rs:20-24).
   Revocation writes the payload through the object's borrow. The pin, the table, its record,
   the conditional edge and the eviction go; the `inline_ic_safe` latch goes, and the in-line
   `instanceof` walk tests each prototype's tag (A2). Saves Vue 23.3M (1.28%), chai 634M (0.79%,
   three quarters of its collector work with it), the Proxy micro-benchmarks 3.0-8.2%; a
   Proxy's creation about 950 instructions. Dumps: the `instanceof` template.
2. Typed arrays and buffers. A typed array's TaInfo and buffer object join its NativeTypedArray
   payload (every element type, shared buffers included), an ArrayBuffer's data moves into an
   ArrayBuffer kind's payload and a DataView's state into a DataView kind's. typed_arrays,
   ta_buffer, array_buffers, native_buffers, data_views and their pins go;
   array_buffer_versions and dirty ranges move with the data. Saves Gameboy 328M (1.53%), PdfJS
   17.3M (0.22%), Mandreel 15.0M (0.02%). Generated code reads NativeTypedArray's fields
   (JitLayout); dumps change where its offsets do. A JIT `length` of a typed array (V8 reads the
   view's field) is a later step this one makes possible.
3. Native functions' [[Realm]] (found by this census). The realm's global in the native
   function's own payload or a field beside its NativeFn, and "is a realm's eval" as a flag bit
   of the function (A8). eval_realm_fns and native_function_realms' probes leave the VM's native
   call. Saves RayTrace 8.7M (0.86%), TypeScript 15.5M (0.03%), chai 18.0M (0.02%). The table
   stays for the collector's [[Realm]] sweep until the payload holds the edge.
4. RegExp (W5a item 3, Exotic::RegExp(PayloadBox<..>) as recorded there): the program and
   flags in the payload, `lastIndex` an own data property at the initial shape's slot (SM and V8
   keep it a slot of the object, A1). The regexps table, the pin per RegExp and their eviction
   go. Saves Octane RegExp 32.7M (1.18%), chai 97.5M (0.12%, a fifth of its collector work),
   Djot 1.1M (0.17%). Built with W5a, which owns the matcher's entry.
5. WeakMap and WeakSet. ObjectKind::WeakCollection(PayloadBox<..>) with the kind, the entries'
   index by key identity and the entries; a get is one probe of that index (A6). The brand probe
   and the data probe go; the ephemeron records stay as they are (they are per entry). Saves Vue
   8.1M (0.44%), chai 5.2M. Its payload owns maps and finalizes, once per collection object.
6. IsHTMLDDA as a flag bit (found by this census): a bit of the object's flag byte set by
   make_html_dda, tested where is_htmldda probes today (A7); htmldda's weak pin can stay for the
   object's identity. Saves Vue 5.8M (0.32%), TypeScript 118M (0.20%), chai 40.5M (0.05%), the
   sustained workload 0.06%.
7. One test per holder. With 1, 2, 5 and the namespaces' kind, the [[Get]], [[Set]] and
   HasProperty walks and the ordinary-get check read `ic_plain` (already "no side-table
   behavior") and, when it is clear, the kind tag, instead of four emptiness guards and two
   probes. Saves about half of the guards' and empty tables' instructions: Gameboy 25.9M
   (0.12%), Vue 3.1M (0.17%), chai 47.9M (0.06%), the Proxy micro-benchmarks 0.4-1.1%.
8. Promises, generators and `class_info`: worth nothing on the acceptance set (no probe in
   volume on any of its workloads). Per operation they are worth more: an await's side-table
   work is a quarter of its 4,952 instructions, a generator step's 3% of 4,851, `new C()`'s
   about 5% of 4,346. The design for them: a promise's state, result and reactions in its
   payload (A1; V8 and SpiderMonkey keep reactions in one slot that turns into the result), a
   generator's coroutine in its object's payload with an executing state in place of the remove
   and re-insert (A10), a class constructor's ClassInfo with its function (A9; the kind bits are
   already in the flag byte, value.rs:2791-2808). Proposed after 1-7, measured on an async
   workload first.
9. Not done, worth nothing on the acceptance set: bound functions' data (at most 10 finalized a
   run), Map and Set data's finalizing (at most 806), String wrappers' code units (box_primitive
   is at most 0.016% of any workload: chai 2.1M, CodeLoad 0.1M; A11 is the way when a workload
   needs it), mapped arguments (done by W3 item 6).

The steps' estimates on the acceptance set (savings.md): Vue 2.3% (steps 1, 5, 6, 7), chai 1.05%,
Gameboy 1.65%, RegExp 1.18%, RayTrace 0.93%, PdfJS 0.27%, TypeScript 0.27% (step 6 and the
native realm); every other workload at most 0.25% (Djot) and React, Splay, NavierStokes and
EarleyBoyer about nothing. TypeScript's construct-cache re-inserts (0.28%) are not W2g's: an
insert that skips a present pin fixes them, as its own commit.

What stays a side table, and why:
- State keyed by something other than one object: realms (a realm's state by its global, probed
  on cross-realm calls), shadow_realms, host_settings_states, template_cache, and the
  unhandled-rejection list (ordered for the host). The weak-target index of weak_metadata (a
  target's observers, by the target) stays, as SpiderMonkey's per-zone maps do (SM
  gc/FinalizationObservers.h:151-213).
- Caches keyed by a constructor or prototype (construct_ics, construct_capacity_hints,
  creation_pins): inline-cache data with weak pins, not exotic state.
- Embedder state of host objects (host_indexed, host_state's resources) and module namespaces
  (module_ns, deferred_ns): guards only on the acceptance set; namespaces can take a kind of their
  own later (SpiderMonkey makes them proxies with their own handler, V8 and JSC give them a type).
- WeakRef and FinalizationRegistry slots, Temporal's: worth nothing measured; later, with the
  pattern.
- gc_pins, for what stays keyed by address.

The collector:
- Tracing: a moved kind's state is traced in its object's trace, as Map and Set data are (A4).
  Its objects stop being side-slot owners: no noting, no conditional edge, no `waiting` entry for
  a dead owner, no twelve-table probe when marked. That work is 0.42% of chai, 0.30% of Octane
  RegExp and 0.27% of Gameboy today.
- Minor collections: a write into a payload sets the owner's card through its borrow, as P1's
  collections do; the moved tables' records go from ClientRecords.
- Eviction: a moved kind's state dies with its object; gc_evict_dead_owners loses its tables and
  gc_pins its pins (chai's eviction: 242M instructions, 0.30%).
- Finalization: a payload cell finalizes when its value owns Rust-heap memory
  (gc_storage.rs:159-178). ProxyData and TaInfo hold none; a RegExp's Rc<Regex>, a WeakMap's
  index, a PromiseState's reactions Vec, a Coroutine and a ClassInfo do. Today eviction drops
  them, so a finalizer replaces an eviction rather than adding work; storage blocks (P1 S6) can
  replace the finalizers where a census shows volume.
- Verification: check_side_slot_records covers the tables that stay; a payload's edges are
  checked as any cell's (the write barrier's verification).

The change surface: proxies 70 lines in 12 files; typed arrays and buffers 142 lines (typed_arrays
60, array_buffers 36, ta_buffer 16, data_views 16, native_buffers 14); WeakMap 50 in 4 files;
htmldda 9 in 2; the native realm 28 in 4; RegExp 26 in 5 (W5a's); promises 84 in 12, generators 77
in 9, class_info 12 in 4 (test files excluded). ObjectKind gains Proxy, ArrayBuffer, DataView and
WeakCollection tags (value.rs:2201-2282): the JIT's range constants (KIND_CALLABLE_FIRST,
KIND_EXOTIC_NONE_LAST, KIND_ORDINARY_LAST) stay ranges if the new tags follow Array's. The
memory inventory classifies each payload.

Risks:
- A payload missed in one trace (marking, verification, the barrier's check) is a use after free
  ("Heap-held Values are unrooted"): Test262 under GC stress and TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY over each
  moved kind's tests, as P1's collections had.
- Callable and constructor proxies: typeof, IsCallable, IsConstructor, `new` and `bind` of a
  proxy through the new tag (Test262's Proxy and Reflect suites, difftest).
- Re-entrancy: a generator resumed from inside itself, a promise resolved while its reactions
  run; the payload's state must say "executing" where the table's removal said it.
- Cross-interpreter ownership: ShadowRealm interpreters route a foreign object's metadata
  through metadata_owner (agent_control.rs:274-310); with the state in the object that routing
  goes for the moved kinds, and the tests of shared heaps must still pass.
- Layout: TypedArray's payload grows; the JIT's element templates read its fields at their
  offsets (dump checks).

Exit (proposed): instruction counts against the series' base, fat LTO, pacing off: Vue -1.5% or
better (steps 1, 5, 6 and 7), Gameboy -1.2%, chai -0.7%, RayTrace -0.7%, Octane RegExp -1.0% (with
W5a item 3); the Proxy micro-benchmarks -3% (reflect) to -8% (none); a Proxy's creation at least
800 instructions fewer; no workload slower beyond noise; collections as before; Test262 identical,
GC stress and TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY over the moved kinds' tests, difftest; dumps unchanged apart from
the `instanceof` walk and the typed arrays' layout.

### W2g steps 3 and 6, and the creation pins (record, 2026-11-04)

Commits, from main 3e7d856f, on p2-w2g-small:
- 66b6bbca (step 6): Interp::is_htmldda tests the object's kind and `ic_plain` before its table.
  An [[IsHTMLDDA]] object is a native function the engine makes not ordinary, and `$262` keeps the
  table non-empty in every realm, so every ToBoolean, `typeof` and loose equality of an object
  probed it. An ordinary object, or one of another kind, is none without the probe.
- 62d55433: a creation cache's fill pins its prototype once; a fill under a prototype pinned
  already (the same live object) inserts nothing.
- 146a807c (step 3): in a one-realm interpreter, Interp::native_call_in_current_realm answers an
  ordinary native (Native or NativeData, `ic_plain`) without probing eval_realm_fns and
  native_function_realms. Every native made there is that realm's (make_callable_object registers
  it at creation) and no realm's eval is tagged (create_realm tags only the realms it makes). A
  proxy's callability sentinel and IsHTMLDDA (not `ic_plain`), a second realm and a ShadowRealm's
  interpreters take the tables.

As designed, except:
1. Step 6 has no flag bit of its own: the flag byte is full (bits 0-5 and the two class bits),
   and the kind and `ic_plain` already say what the bit would (only a native made not ordinary
   can be IsHTMLDDA). The table keeps the identity.
2. Step 3 keeps the realm out of the function: ObjectKind's payload is one word, and the
   realm's global beside a NativeFn would grow every object's header. One realm in one
   interpreter is the case the acceptance set runs; a second realm keeps today's probes.

Counts (fat LTO, CPUs 10-13, pacing off, median of 3; main c90b3889's engine, step 6 and the
pins at 62d55433 against main, step 3 at 146a807c against 62d55433):

### W4: feedback for the speculative tier (census, survey and design, 2026-11-04)

W4's remainder, as planned: always-on value classes for arithmetic and comparison sites, written
only on the VM's and the miss paths; a production read API; the schema documented in the tree;
call-target identity exported from CallSite; elements kinds; a megamorphic state; allocation-site
records with survival counted at minor collections; inlined and recompiled code's pcs mapped to
their baseline sites; Pilot C finished and a dependency registry. W3 item 7 already landed the
call cache's builtin ids and its megamorphic state (afe58a1e, 140d2d83); its per-way call count is
parked (e854636e). Census and survey on main 0d724e3d, whose engine is c90b3889's (main moved to
3e7d856f meanwhile, with plan records only); no engine change on main. Everything below is in
target/p2/w4 (untracked).

What exists:
- feedback.rs: a versioned schema (SCHEMA_VERSION 1, feedback.rs:13) of OperationKind
  (feedback.rs:46-57: named and element loads and stores, call, construct, arithmetic, branch, loop,
  allocation; no comparison), ObservationKind (62-71), ValueClass (329-339) and the states
  Uninitialized, Monomorphic, Polymorphic, Absent and Generic (373-379). A layout is built per
  chunk from its operations (bytecode.rs:6074-6221). The words exist only with
  TRUST_JS_FEEDBACK_PROFILE=1, every write finds its site by a binary search over the sites
  (feedback.rs:1247-1278), and nothing reads them in production (`read` is cfg(test)). The header
  names FEEDBACK_SCHEMA.md, which does not exist (feedback.rs:3).
- The detailed vector changes what it observes: it clears 14 template bits (arithmetic,
  comparison, branches, elements, names, stores, chains, regions' fusions, the call probe, direct
  calls; jit.rs:4868-4892), turns regions off (jit_regions.rs:634-636) and in-line allocation off
  (bytecode.rs:3411-3418). Inlined chunks get an unbound vector that records nothing
  (feedback.rs:735-741).
- What production already records, by kind:

| kind | production state | written | read |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| named load, store | 4 IcState ways a site (bytecode.rs:89-114, 153); the engine's stub cache by (shape, key) (interpreter.rs:4702-4711); no megamorphic mark | fills on the VM's and helpers' misses (interpreter.rs:10360) | the VM, both backends' templates, regions |
| element load, store | none a site; each array's kind bits in its word block (word_block.rs:35-71) | every write keeps the bits | templates and builtins at run time |
| call | CallSite: 4 CallIc ways (callee, func, builtin id, native entry, class) and a megamorphic state (bytecode.rs:984-1047, 1328-1409) | the JIT's call helpers; not the VM's Op::Call | the ARM64 probe and direct sequence; the inliner |
| construct | ConstructSite: one CallIc and the prototype's shape and slot (bytecode.rs:2005-2011) | the construct helpers | the direct construction |
| arithmetic, comparison | none | | regions speculate from SSA demand |
| branch, loop | none a site; tier-up counters a function and a VM loop (function_runtime.rs:71-74; tiering.rs:41-54) | | tier-up |
| allocation | object- and array-literal images, the empty array's, shared literal blocks (bytecode.rs:3171-3240); constructors' InstanceImage and class learning (bytecode.rs:3250-3255, 4201-4212); RegExp programs | first execution | W2e's in-line allocation |

- Region admission's exit profile is not on main: it is parked on p2-region-s3 (Decision 27).

Census (target/p2/w4: census/, counts/, perf/, scripts/; commits 537ec5f1 (the always-on record)
and 3180b163 (the census) on branch p2-w4-census, from main 0d724e3d; fat LTO through
target/p2/tools/build-lto.sh, binaries in target/p2/w4/lto/537ec5f1 and lto/census):
- Method. Each workload ran twice with TRUST_JS_W4_CENSUS. A --tier=bytecode run puts every
  compiled-code operation through the VM's loop, which counts it by pc and records the full value
  classes, element facts, allocations and survivors. The production --tier=jit run gives each
  site's production state at exit and the always-on record (below). Chunks join on a hash of
  their origin and operations, and every compiled-code execution joined (100% on every workload).
  Pacing off; census runs on the A725 cores, counts on CPUs 10-13 (setarch -R, median of 3; chai
  one run). Not counted: the tree-walker's first 8 calls of each function (it has no pcs). The
  VM-only run inlines nothing, so inlined code counts at its callee's pcs; the JIT run's inlined
  chunks keep their own state, which joins nothing (below).
- The always-on record (537ec5f1), the plan's "VM and miss paths only": one u32 per pc in the
  feedback vector, ORed and stored only when it changes. Arithmetic, unary, update and comparison
  pcs keep three ValueClass sets (operand 0, operand 1, result); element pcs keep the receiver's
  family (Array, typed array, arguments, other object, string, other), an Array's lost kind facts
  (holey, non-Number, non-int32, frozen or shared, out of bounds) and the key's kind. The VM writes
  it on every execution; generated code only where its fast path missed (jit_exec's operations,
  String + String, the strict-equality helper, the element helpers). Nothing is written in line.
- Executed operations in compiled code, by kind (% of all; target/p2/w4/census/summary1.md has
  every kind):

- The VM runs almost none of them in the JIT tier: 0.000-0.048%. It also runs the scope operations
  generated code hands it (PushLex, PopEnv and the like, 6.5% of the sustained workload's
  operations), which are no feedback sites. A function runs its first 8 calls (or 128 back edges)
  in the tree-walker, which records nothing, and its first admission compiles bytecode and native
  code together (TRUST_JS_JIT_ADMIT_RUNS defaults to 0, interpreter.rs:6064, 14846-14858). A loop
  entered from the tree-walker runs the rest of one iteration in the VM. So "the VM's paths" give
  W4 almost nothing, and the miss paths are all there is.
- Usable feedback today (the production state at exit), % of the kind's executions; element,
  arithmetic, comparison, typeof and `in` sites have none. Closures and empty literals need no
  per-site state (V8's need none either) and count as usable:
