  "All speculation kinds" counts named, element, call, construct, arithmetic, comparison,
  allocation, typeof, `in` and instanceof; branches, loops, `!` and names are apart (no exemplar
  records branch directions, A4; names mostly have static addresses, Decision 21). chai's named
  loads are 66% at sites whose ways never filled, which no state marks (receivers the ICs refuse,
  its `proxify`'s proxies [inference]).
  Calls: 0-17.7% of executions at megamorphic sites (Gameboy 17.7%), 0-81% at native targets.
- The always-on record against the full record, % of the kind's executions. "Recorded": the
  site's record is not empty. P3 can read it two ways. Rule U takes the record together with the
  classes the in-line path accepts (Number for arithmetic and relational comparison, the Array and
  typed-array words with an index key for elements; nothing for equality, whose in-line path
  covers nearly every pair). Rule R takes the record alone when it is not empty. U is safe by
  construction: every class seen outside the in-line set was recorded on 99.8-100% of arithmetic
  executions. "Coarse" compares Number, String, BigInt, Boolean, undefined, null, Symbol and Object
  (int32 and double merged); "fine" splits int32 from double:

  - Arithmetic: rule U names the coarse classes exactly for 89.7-100% of executions on 19 of 20
    workloads. Splay's 59.5% is its string-building Add sites, recorded as String and widened by
    U with Number, the in-line set. Records come from the loops' one VM iteration and from misses.
  - int32: 83-100% of arithmetic executions are int32-only on 15 workloads, 42-56% on
    NavierStokes, Splay and sustained, 5-8% on RayTrace and Box2D. The record splits them only
    where it was written (fine, rule R: 0-97%), and believing a one-iteration sample is unsafe on
    sustained (55.9%), NavierStokes (52.2%) and Box2D (75.0%): their loops' first iteration sees
    int32, later ones doubles.
  - Comparison: relational operators behave as arithmetic; equality does not. Its in-line
    template answers every pair but content-compared strings, which go to H_STRICT_EQ, and
    coercions (jit.rs:12166-12404), so its record stays empty: coarse 9.4-100% (Richards 9.4%,
    EarleyBoyer 21.5%, chai 25.3%, TypeScript 31.7%).
  - Elements: the in-line template reads Array words and typed arrays alike (jit.rs:14471-14536),
    so an empty record cannot tell them apart: coarse is the recorded share, 0-100%.
- Records in inlined chunks never reach the baseline's sites: Vue's 270,844 records went 98% to
  18 sites of its 10 inlined chunks (TypeScript has 293 inlined chunks with 22,868 sites).
- A VM warm-up (TRUST_JS_JIT_ADMIT_RUNS=4: a loop-free function's first 4 compiled runs in the
  VM) raises the recorded share where calls dominate (arithmetic: Vue 5.1 to 41.2%, Richards 0 to
  95.5%, DeltaBlue 7.7 to 29.4%; equality's samples most: comparison coarse Vue 43.1 to 91.3%,
  React 50.8 to 98.8%, Splay 43.8 to 99.2%) and makes rule R less safe where a sample drifts
  (RayTrace's arithmetic R-safe 99.2 to 20.8%). Loops gain nothing. Precision over all kinds
  (below) goes from 67.2-99.5% to 75.1-99.5%: Vue 81.7 to 92.1%, Richards 82.0 to 94.8%,
  TypeScript 88.9 to 94.2%, zlib and NavierStokes unchanged (census/summaryA4.md,
  census/coverageA4.txt).
- Coverage, % of the speculation kinds' executions (target/p2/w4/census/coverage1.txt).
  "Usable" is V8's sense (A10): P3 can compile the operation without an exit for missing
  feedback, so an executed site whose in-line path took the operation counts, as do a filled or
  megamorphic cache, an allocation image, a generic mark at a named site whose fill was refused
  (design 3), and a closure or an empty literal. "Precise": the feedback, read by rule U
  (elements by rule R), names exactly the coarse classes the operation saw:

| zlib | 138,040,683,216 | +0.08% | >16.8x | 19.02x | -0.00% | +0.08% |

  - The detailed vector costs 3.1-9.9x on the fixtures and the object-heavy benchmarks, 17-174x on
    the numeric kernels (Mandreel 29x, Crypto 125x, NavierStokes 174x, zlib more than 17x when
    cut), and nothing on CodeLoad, which mostly compiles. On Vue its observation functions take
    3.0 of its 7.99G instructions (observe_value_class 12.3%, observe_branch 10.5%, observe_call
    5.2%: each a binary search for its site and a merge; DeltaBlue 37.0%), and the templates it
    turns off most of the rest: with the same 14 template bits off and nothing observed
    (TRUST_JS_JIT_FAST=4291167192), Vue takes 2.12x. NavierStokes's 174x is every element and
    arithmetic operation in the exact-pc helper, whose profiled element path formats keys
    (observation 56.6% of its instructions). It is a diagnostic and stays one.
  - The always-on record costs what its records cost, about 10-80 instructions each (Splay 11,
    PdfJS 23, Vue 23, chai 56, Gameboy 76), and nothing where nothing misses. chai's +1.62% is
    23.4M records, 15.8M of them element misses, which borrow the receiver once more. Gameboy's
    +1.66% is 4.7M, mostly comparisons and arithmetic whose operands leave the in-line Number
    paths. PdfJS's +1.46% is 4.8M; Vue's +0.34% is 271K, 98% of them element and strict-equality
    misses in its inlined chunks. The fixtures' costs are real, not the stack scan's luck: over ten
    nursery sizes (counts/nursery.log) Vue +0.34%, React +0.26% and Djot +0.49%, the same at every
    size.
  - The VM warm-up moves counts by -6.3% to +4.1% on its own (React -6.28%, Richards -2.34%,
    DeltaBlue +3.67%, Gameboy +4.09%): it changes which functions compile and when. It is a
    tiering change, not a cost of feedback.
- Allocation sites and survival (VM-only runs with TRUST_JS_GC_LOG=1; a survivor is an object the
  next collection marked; objects are attributed to the operation that made them, a call's or a
  construction's result to that pc when a built-in made it; census/survival1.txt):

| workload | attributed allocations | survival | sites >= 0.85 (allocations, survivor bytes) | sites < 0.10 (allocations) | of the heap's promoted bytes | minor collections, % of run (pacing on / off) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Splay | 824,373 | 97.8% | 4 sites (97.8%, 100%) | 2.2% | 69.1% | 28.1 / 23.7 |
| TypeScript | 3,326,720 | 56.5% | 229 sites (53.2%, 90.9%) | 40.4% | 44.7% | 5.1 / 6.3 |
| EarleyBoyer | 1,347,209 | 40.0% | 11 sites (0.8%, 2.1%) | 31.4% | 98.0% | 6.6 / 10.9 |
| PdfJS | 579,721 | 4.4% | 19 sites (3.4%, 79.0%) | 94.3% | 7.2% | |
| chai | 4,248,105 | 1.0% | 6 sites (0.0%, 3.1%) | 98.6% | 31.9% | |
| sustained | 1,439,533 | 16.7% | 2 sites (16.7%, 99.9%) | 83.3% | 79.7% | |

  - Survival per site is bimodal where it matters: Splay's payload literals and its node
    construction survive at 100%; TypeScript's constructions split into 229 long-lived and 74
    short-lived sites; PdfJS's and chai's closures, arrays and literals die young. EarleyBoyer's
    cons cells sit between, at 57-58%.
  - Minor collections take 24-28% of Splay's run (one X925 core, the GC log's mark and sweep),
    5-6% of TypeScript's, 7-11% of EarleyBoyer's.

What the exemplars do (target/p2/w4/exemplars.txt; V8 c8b70908, SM 6bb81100, JSC 26027804, each
checked at its main of 2026-11-04, where the cited code holds; 1,370 ranges checked, 0 problems):
- A1: each first tier records value feedback on every execution, before an optimizing tier
  exists: V8's Ignition in a byte of the bytecode (V8 interpreter-generator.cc:991-1012), SM's
  Baseline Interpreter by attaching stubs (SM jit/JitScript.h:317-340), JSC's LLInt in its arith
  and value profiles (JSC LowLevelInterpreter64.asm:1236-1290). TRust's first tier, the
  tree-walker, records nothing.
- A2: in the baseline, V8's Sparkplug+ calls a typed stub chosen from that byte, whose fast path
  writes nothing; a type miss merges the byte and repatches the call
  (V8 baseline-compiler.cc:1258-1299; V8 runtime-compiler.cc:158-198). JSC's baseline writes arith
  result bits on its double paths only (JSC ArithProfile.cpp:74-93). SM's stubs count their
  entries (SM jit/BaselineCacheIRCompiler.cpp:233-236), and JSC's value profiles store on every
  baseline execution (JSC JITInlines.h:311-319).
- A3: int32-ness is a tag in all three; an integral double is a double (V8 records kNumber,
  V8 binary-op-assembler.cc:572-581; JSC's SpecAnyIntAsDouble, JSC SpeculatedType.cpp:637-648).
  Only V8 tests a double by value, for kAdditiveSafeInteger
  (V8 code-stub-assembler.cc:7626-7661).
- A4: V8 records comparisons in the same byte (V8 globals.h:2663-2703); JSC records none and
  types them from their operands' predictions (JSC DFGFixupPhase.cpp:824-904). None records branch
  directions (V8 interpreter-generator.cc:1414-1432; JSC LowLevelInterpreter64.asm:2263-2275).
- A5: calls: V8 keeps a weak target, a closure literal's FeedbackCell or the megamorphic symbol,
  with a count on every call (V8 ic-callable.tq:91-168); SM a function, then a script, then
  "scripted" stubs, whose entry count drives trial inlining (SM jit/CacheIR.cpp:6607-6624;
  SM jit/TrialInlining.cpp:779-784); JSC up to 8 callees, then a virtual call
  (JSC Repatch.cpp:171-184).
- A6: property feedback changes on misses only, up to 4 maps in V8 (then homomorphic) and 6 stubs
  in SM (V8 ic.cc:569-648; SM jit/ICState.h:26-34). Elements: V8's kinds live in the map and its
  handlers' load and store modes (V8 map.h:368-382; V8 globals.h:3131-3168); SM has none, a shape
  and a packed bit (SM jit/CacheIR.cpp:3018-3065); JSC's access paths store the last StructureID
  on every execution (JSC LowLevelInterpreter64.asm:2022-2029).
- A7: V8 removed allocation-site pretenuring on 2026-10-27 (commit 21e810452ab9; before it, 100
  mementos and a survival ratio of 0.80, V8 pretenuring-handler.cc@9e976bfb:28-69); its sites
  keep elements kinds and boilerplates, made at a literal's second execution
  (V8 runtime-literals.cc:711-734). SM keeps a site word in every nursery cell and counts
  promotions at minor collections, tenuring at 0.9 over more than 200 allocations
  (SM gc/Heap.h:589-602; SM gc/Pretenuring.cpp:175-245, 270-352). JSC, non-moving with sticky marks
  like TRust, counts no survival and never pretenured (JSC MarkedSpace.cpp:404-433).
- A8: inlined code reads its callee's own feedback: V8's (vector, slot)
  (V8 feedback-source.h:15-39), SM's per-call-site ICScript (SM jit/TrialInlining.cpp:809-861),
  JSC's CodeOrigin, whose baseline CodeBlock owns the profiles (JSC InlineCallFrame.h:196-205);
  JSC's exits record FrequentExitSite by bytecode index (JSC DFGOSRExitBase.cpp:38-60).
- A9: dependencies are recorded during compilation and checked at install: V8's
  CompilationDependencies into per-object DependentCode (V8 compilation-dependencies.cc:1450-1510),
  SM's fuses (SM jit/WarpOracle.cpp:1236-1283), JSC's DesiredWatchpoints into three-state
  watchpoint sets, whose firing jettisons the code and unlinks its incoming calls
  (JSC DFGPlan.cpp:553-600; JSC CodeBlock.cpp:2431-2433).
- A10: missing feedback is an exit, megamorphic feedback is not: V8's "insufficient type
  feedback" deopt (V8 js-type-hint-lowering.cc:774-803; V8 js-heap-broker.cc:579-584), SM's
  MBail(FirstExecution) for a never-run IC (SM jit/WarpOracle.cpp:1074-1096), JSC's ForceOSRExit
  for an empty prediction (JSC DFGByteCodeParser.cpp:1319-1330).
- A11: none reports coverage of executed operations. JSC gates DFG tier-up on 75% of a
  function's value profiles being live, after up to 5 delays (JSC CodeBlock.cpp:3049-3058;
  JSC OptionsList.h:401-404).
- A12: V8 allocates a function's vector once it has executed 8 times its bytecode length
  (V8 flag-definitions.h:1110-1111); SM makes its ICScript at a warm-up count of 10 and drops it
  with the JIT code at a major collection (SM jit/BaselineJIT.cpp:631-644; SM gc/GC.cpp:3006-3036).

Design:
1. Value classes, always on, on the VM's and miss paths only (the plan's rule; A2's Sparkplug+ and
   JSC's baseline arith):
   - For arithmetic, unary, update and comparison (relational and equality) operations: three
     ValueClass sets (operand 0, operand 1, result) in one u32, ORed and stored only on change.
     The VM writes it on every execution; generated code where its in-line path misses (the
     census's patch, 537ec5f1, measured as is).
   - The record is a u32 a pc in an array the chunk makes at its first record: only 7-76% of the
     compiled chunks recorded anything in the census's JIT runs (target/p2/w4/census/). A write
     indexes it by pc, with no search: today's detailed vector searches its sites on every write
     (feedback.rs:1247-1278). The patch's array, made with each chunk, is the measured form.
   - The in-line set is part of the schema: each template publishes the classes it takes without
     a record (Number for arithmetic and relational comparison, int32 for `%`, the element
     templates' word and typed-array receivers with index keys). P3 reads rule U: the record plus
     the in-line set. It is safe by construction, and exact at the coarse grain for 89.7-100% of
     arithmetic executions on 19 of 20 workloads.
   - int32: the record's int32 bit is a hint, written only by the VM and misses. P3 speculates
     int32 where the record has no double and the use wants an integer (bit operations, indices,
     int32 element stores), f64 elsewhere: a Number is already its f64 word, so f64 arithmetic
     needs no boxing [inference]. A wrong int32 speculation is an exit, which Phase 3's exit
     profile turns into a recompile (Carried to Phase 3).
   - Equality is not recorded in line (JSC records no comparison, A4). P3 lowers it as the
     baseline does, by tag, and narrows it from its operands' producers.
   - Cheaper records than the patch's: the element record is taken in fast_get_elem and
     fast_set_elem, which borrow the receiver and test its kind and the key anyway (chai's 15.8M
     element records then cost a few instructions each [inference]); a word already Generic is
     not reclassified.
2. Elements kinds: the element record above, from the word block's kind bits (W2c's byte,
   word_block.rs:35-71), on the VM's and miss paths. Its known gap: an in-line hit tells an
   Array from a typed array only if a miss or the VM ran there. P3 keeps the baseline's two-way
   dispatch where the record is empty, and reads the array's kind bits at run time as the
   templates do. Recording in line would cost a load, a test and a branch at every element
   access; the census's 14.6% of zlib's operations are element accesses, so it stays out of W4.
3. Megamorphic and generic states:
   - Named sites: a per-site state byte beside its 4 ways, set by a fill that is refused (an
     uncacheable receiver, chai's proxies [inference]) or that evicts a live way, as CallSite
     counts evictions in an epoch (bytecode.rs:1350-1366). It marks a site generic, which P3
     lowers to the stub cache's probe (A10: megamorphic feedback is usable). The fill path only.
   - Calls: CallSite's megamorphic state (W3 item 7) as is. The per-way count stays parked
     (e854636e) until P3's inliner reads it: P3's hotness can come from the callee's jit_runs.
4. The read API: crate::feedback::read, for a chunk and a baseline pc, returning one typed view a
   kind:
   - values: the three class sets with the in-line set applied (rule U), and whether a record
     exists;
   - element: receiver families, the Array facts, key kinds, out of bounds;
   - named: each way's receiver shape, holder shape, depth, slot location and kind (data,
     accessor, absent, creation), and the generic mark;
   - call and construct: the state (empty, monomorphic, polymorphic, megamorphic) and each way's
     target: the callee cell, its function literal (the FeedbackCell's role, as V8's one literal
     with many closures), the builtin id, the native entry, the class;
   - allocation: the image or template if made, the constructor's class, the site's survival
     record (design 6).
   It allocates nothing and interns nothing: P3 runs in the process that made the shapes. The
   versioned envelope and refresh_current_layout_feedback (bytecode.rs:6243) remain the
   diagnostics' adapters, built on the same views. The detailed vector stays a diagnostic.
5. The schema document, tracked as crates/trust-js/src/feedback-schema.txt (AGENTS.md ignores
   Markdown workfiles), and feedback.rs's header pointing at it. It holds:
   - the numeric encodings, append-only within a version: OperationKind (with Comparison, 11),
     ObservationKind, ValueClass, ObservationState, roles;
   - each kind's record word: the value-class layout, the element bits, the named state byte;
   - each template's in-line set, the rule-U reading, and that a record never shrinks;
   - the read API's fields, which are exact and which hints (int32), and when a view goes stale
     (call-cache epochs, shapes);
   - the pc map (design 7) and site identity;
   - the coverage and precision definitions (design 8) and the census tool that measures them.
6. Allocation-site records and survival:
   - A site record a pc for MakeObject, MakeArray, MakeArrayLiteral, NewObject, NewArray,
     MakeClosure, MakeRegExp and New, plus one "built-in" record a call site: allocations,
     survivors and their bytes.
   - The object's cell carries its site's 16-bit index in the two padding bytes after
     NamedEntries' len_slot [inference from the fields' sizes, to be confirmed with
     -Zprint-type-sizes]. The allocation helpers and the in-line allocation store it and increment
     the site's count, as SM's Baseline does in machine code (SM jit/MacroAssembler.cpp:708-737;
     SM gc/Nursery-inl.h:213-247): about 5 instructions an in-line allocation [inference], Splay's
     800K in-line allocations about 0.3% of its run. A minor collection's marking reads the index
     of each young object it marks and increments the survivors (SM gc/Tenuring.cpp:945-958).
   - What the records serve: P3's allocation sinking (sites that die young: PdfJS's and chai's
     94-99% of allocations), and the class and slack decisions W2d step 3e makes per constructor.
     Pretenuring is not W4's: V8 removed it and JSC never had it (A7), and in a non-moving heap it
     saves a minor collection's mark of a survivor, not a copy, while its initializing stores
     then need their cards. Splay (24-28% of its run in minor collections, 97.8% survival) is
     where an experiment would pay; it waits for the records and an owner's decision.
7. Inlined and recompiled pcs mapped to their baseline sites (A8's form):
   - The inliner's splice records, for each spliced operation, the callee's chunk and pc; the
     transformed chunk keeps the map (one u32 a pc and the callee chunks' list) beside its inline
     sites (stack_trace.rs:86-97, bytecode.rs:9107).
   - A transformed chunk's miss paths write the always-on record through the map into the
     baseline site's word, so a callee's sites see what its inlined copies saw (Vue's 98%). Its
     caches stay its own (they are its speed); the read API merges a transformed site's ways with
     its baseline site's.
   - FeedbackVector::unbound goes (feedback.rs:735-741): the map makes the "coincidentally equal
     pc" it guards against impossible.
   - P3's own exits (Phase 3) record by baseline pc through the same map, as JSC's FrequentExitSite
     does (A8).
8. Coverage and precision as standing metrics:
   - Coverage: the share of the speculation kinds' executed operations whose site P3 can compile
     without an exit for missing feedback (V8's sense, A10). Precision: the share whose feedback,
     read by rule U, names exactly the coarse classes the operation saw.
   - Measured by the census's two runs, made a tracked diagnostic under the
     architecture-diagnostics feature, as the allocation-site probes are (DIAGNOSTICS.md), so that
     ordinary builds pay nothing: TRUST_JS_FEEDBACK_CENSUS=<path> (the VM loop's counts and full
     records in a --tier=bytecode run; the production state in the --tier=jit run) and
     scripts/feedback-census.py, which joins them and prints both metrics by kind. Reported at
     every stage exit beside instruction counts, from W4's exit on.
   - JSC's live-profile share (A11) is a per-function gate, not a measure of executed operations;
     P3 may add it at its tier-up.
9. Pilot C and the dependency registry:
   - Pilot C's proof is shapes keyed by attributes, landed by W2d steps 1-2: reads trust the shape
     where a way proves data. What remains is writability: a store's way cannot prove it, because
     a read's fill and a store's share one cache (W2d's record, item 2), so generated stores
     test the attribute byte (guard_slot_writable_data, jit_storage.rs:268). Finishing Pilot C:
     a store site's fill admits only a writable data property, and a read site's fill never reaches
     a store site's ways, as the global name cache already does (c9d8babe); the three-instruction
     test then goes from the stores, and the read API reports writability as proven.
   - The dependency registry, JSC's form (A9): a watchpoint set (clear, watched, invalidated)
     on each fact compiled code may assume: a shape with no transitions out, a prototype's
     shape, a protector (array_prototypes_unshadowed, interpreter.rs:9820), a global binding's
     constancy, an allocation site's decision. A compilation collects the sets it relies on and
     installs itself in them at the end, failing if one has fired; firing marks its codes
     invalid and unlinks their callers' call caches (Carried to Phase 3 names this as the home of
     JSC's incoming-call unlinking). PROTO_EPOCH and CALL_IC_EPOCH (value.rs:8168,
     bytecode.rs:1206) stay for the baseline's caches.
   - W4 builds the registry with its first producers (protectors, prototype shapes) and a test
     consumer; P3 is its first real one.

Order (each step measured on its own, as the plan's stages):
1. The schema document and feedback.rs's header (no code).
2. The census tool as a tracked diagnostic (TRUST_JS_FEEDBACK_CENSUS and the script), so every
   later step reports coverage and precision.
3. The value-class record (design 1), with the in-line sets in the schema, ARM64 and x86-64 miss
   paths.
4. The element record (design 2), inside the receiver reads the miss paths make.
5. Named sites' generic mark (design 3).
6. The read API (design 4), with its tests against the census's records.
7. The pc map and write-through for inlined chunks (design 7).
8. Allocation-site records and survival counting (design 6), with the header's site index.
9. Pilot C's store writability (design 9), with dump checks: store templates change.
10. The dependency registry (design 9).
Region admission's step 5 (speculation from W4's value classes) follows step 6.

Change surface:
- feedback.rs (the record, the read API, the schema's encodings), a new
  crates/trust-js/src/feedback-schema.txt.
- bytecode.rs: the chunk's record; the VM's arithmetic, comparison and element paths
  (bin_num, bin_i32, bin_cmp and the unary and update paths, bytecode.rs:21617-21756; the element
  paths, 26238-26340); jit_exec's operations, jit_add_strings and jit_strict_eq (24066-24260); the
  allocation helpers; the inliner's splice (the pc map).
- interpreter.rs: fast_get_elem and fast_set_elem (the element record), ic_insert (the generic
  mark).
- value.rs and gc_core.rs: the header's site index; the minor collection's marking (survivors).
- jit.rs, jit_alloc.rs, jit_x64_alloc.rs: in-line allocation's site-index store; the store
  templates' writability test (Pilot C).
- The census tool: the VM loop's count (bytecode.rs:18155) and a dump at exit; a script.
- Tests: the record's writes per path (VM, each miss helper; never an in-line hit), rule U against
  the full record on a test program, the pc map under inlining, survival counts across minor and
  major collections, the registry's install-time check and firing.

Risks:
- x86-64 was not measured. Its backend has in-line comparisons, equality and bit operations but
  no in-line f64 Add, Sub, Mul or Div: they take jit_exec on every execution (jit_x64.rs:1048-1099),
  so the record runs there every time. Measure on zombie before landing step 3; if it is over
  budget, x86-64 records the tag's coarse class only, or gets the f64 templates first.
- A record written in line by mistake costs every execution: a test runs each template's hit
  and checks the record unchanged.
- A template whose in-line set grows without the schema saying so makes rule U wrong. The census
  tool's "complete" column (99.8-100% today) is its check, at every stage exit.
- int32 hints from one-iteration samples drift (sustained, NavierStokes, Box2D). P3 must treat
  them as hints guarded by exits, not as proofs.
- The header's site index must stay out of the way of W2d's layout; if the padding is not free,
  the index costs a word, which the census then has to price.
- Equality and element receivers stay imprecise (precision 67.2-99.5%). Raising them needs records
  on in-line paths or a recording warm-up; either is P3's call, priced by this census's numbers.

Exit (proposed):
- The record on by default at <= 2% instructions on the acceptance set (the plan's), on ARM64 and
  on x86-64 (zombie). The patch measured 0.00-1.66% on ARM64 (Gameboy 1.66%, chai 1.62%, PdfJS
  1.46%), before design 1's cheaper element record.
- Coverage >= 95% on every workload, as the census tool measures it (the plan's number; the
  census gives 99.6-100.0% for W4's design, against 0.0-83.1% today). The plan's "usable
  feedback" can mean usable or right; this note measures both, and the 95% applies to usable.
- Precision reported beside it, by kind, with no workload below this census's (67.2-99.5%), so a
  change that loses records shows. A precision target belongs to P3, whose speculation sets the
  grain.
- The detailed vector stays a diagnostic.
Why change the plan's exit: under the in-line convention every executed site whose in-line path
ran is usable, so coverage meets 95% by construction and stops measuring how good the feedback
is. Precision does, and it is where the remaining gaps are (equality, element receivers, int32).

Decided (mine, reported to the owner; the exemplars' answers taken):
1. No recording warm-up. A VM warm-up is a compile-policy change (the counts moved -6.3% to
   +4.1% for reasons other than feedback), and it belongs with the tier threshold, which the
   owner has open ("The phase exit's gap, measured"); W4 records on VM and miss paths only.
2. No pretenuring in P2: V8 removed allocation-site pretenuring (2026-10-27) and JSC never had
   it. Step 8 (allocation-site records and survival counting) then has no consumer in P2 (the
   literals' boilerplates are W2e's images, slack tracking is W2d's per constructor), and moves to
   P3 with this census's survival tables.
3. No equality feedback: P3 types equality from its operands, as JSC does.

### Export accessors' binding proof read in place (record, 2026-11-04)

A phase-exit item ("The phase exit's gap, measured": Vue's Interp::try_binding_getter, 5.0% of
its instructions). The bundler's export accessors (`get: () => binding`) read their binding
through a cached proof of the scope chain (property_read.rs), which try_binding_getter took out
of the getter's sidecar and set back on every call: two take-and-set pairs of the 48-byte proof,
about half of the function's instructions (perf annotate). The probe runs no JavaScript and
writes no cache, so UserCallable::binding_getter_proof borrows the proof in place; a stale proof
is replaced after the reference ends (f0810c79, rebased onto main as the commit before this
record).

Counts (fat LTO, CPUs 10-13, pacing off, median of 3; main 146a807c's engine):

### Branch relaxation's scan skipped for short code (record, 2026-11-04)

A phase-exit item (compile cost: "The phase exit's gap, measured"). Asm::relax_branches scanned
every patch for an out-of-range conditional on each compilation, most of
Asm::finish_with_offsets's work (2-3% of the short workloads' instructions). Code shorter than
TBZ's reach (8,192 instructions, the shortest conditional's; a label binds at most at the code's
end) has no such branch, and skips the scan (3c09e454, rebased onto main as the commit before this
record). Counts (fat LTO, CPUs 10-13, pacing off, median of 3; main f0810c79's engine):

### W2g step 1: Proxy state in the object (record, 2026-11-04)

Commits, from main 3e7d856f, on p2-w2g-proxy (an agent's; rebased onto main dffe8831 as e7aed168,
5249c358, d3843a2e and 710191fd, with a31509fe correcting step 3's comment, which named the
sentinel this series removes; the measurements below name the series as written, then the rebased
one against dffe8831):
- c26c97b7: a Proxy's target and handler in its object. ObjectKind::Proxy(PayloadBox<ProxyData>)
  holds the target, the handler (null once revoked) and whether the target was callable. The
  object's trace reports the payload cell and its two edges, and revocation nulls the handler
  through the object's mutable borrow, which sets its card. is_callable gains the Proxy tag and
  the `Native(proxy_uncallable)` kind goes; the call dispatch's proxy test is a test of the tag,
  and every other reader (proxy_pair, the [[Get]], [[Set]], HasProperty and delete walks, the
  construct dispatch, GetFunctionRealm, the ordinary-get check, for-in, the feedback kinds,
  Engine::is_proxy_value) tests the kind. IsConstructor stays the flag bit the creation sets,
  which value_is_constructor now reads instead of recursing into the target. The table, the pin,
  its ClientRecords record, the conditional edge, the eviction and the cross-interpreter routing
  go, with with_metadata_table (revocation was its only use) and call_inner's and
  construct_inner's proxy guards before callable_home (which finds no [[Realm]] for the kind).
- 9a76763f: the realm-wide inline_ic_safe latch goes. The JIT's in-line `instanceof` walk and its
  interpreter counterpart (instanceof_ic) test each holder for a proxy; OrdinaryHasInstance's walk
  shares the interpreter's (prototype_chain_has), which stops at the first proxy, from which the
  observable walk goes on. JitCtx keeps its slot [32], unused (`reserved`), so that the fixed
  offsets after it stay.
- 93b6151e: the walk in six instructions a hop, as before it tested anything (9a76763f's took
  nine): KIND_PROXY_TAG is 32, the one tag with bit 5 set, so a TBNZ tells a proxy after the
  holder's tag and prototype loads, whose offsets from the cell fold in, and the loop branches
  back while the prototype is not the target. The entry loses the latch's three instructions.
- cedee510: the call and construct dispatch take the callee's [[Call]] copy and a proxy's slots
  under one borrow, and the [[Get]] and [[Set]] entries test for a proxy in the look at the kind
  they make already (mapped arguments and string wrappers; arguments objects).

As designed: ProxyData in a payload cell without a finalizer (two Values and a bool;
`needs_drop` is false, asserted); the tag after Array's, so KIND_CALLABLE_FIRST,
KIND_EXOTIC_NONE_LAST and KIND_ORDINARY_LAST stay ranges, which a proxy now fails as it already
failed `ic_plain` (still clear: the property caches stay off it); callability in the payload, as
SpiderMonkey asks the handler (V8 and JSC choose the proxy's map or structure by it); one test
per holder in the `instanceof` walk, as V8's HasInPrototypeChain makes (V8 c8b70908
src/codegen/code-stub-assembler.cc:13663-13725: each hop's map instance type, JS_PROXY_TYPE to the
runtime). Every other side table is as it was.

Where the build differs from the design:
1. The tag is 32, not the next free one: bit 5 is set in no other kind's tag (all are below 32),
   and the walk tests it with one TBNZ. A new kind takes a tag below 32, or one with the bit if its
   [[GetPrototypeOf]] is not the field read either (value.rs; jit_layout_tests::
   only_a_proxys_kind_tag_has_the_proxy_bit checks the kinds JavaScript makes).
2. JitCtx's slot [32] stays, unused, rather than moving the five fixed offsets after it, which
   every template reads by number.
3. Revocation keeps the target, as the side table did (the spec nulls it; nothing observes it):
   typeof, IsCallable and IsConstructor of a revoked proxy come from the payload and the flag
   bit, and every operation tests the handler.
4. A native parent's graft in super() (eval.rs; %Proxy% reached as the parent through a changed
   [[Prototype]]) leaves a proxy's state with it, as the side table did, rather than making the
   new `this` a proxy whose `ic_plain` is set.
5. Tests: the side-slot record tests use a promise and a class where they used a proxy, and check
   that a proxy owns no side slot (table_record_tests); the owner-index test routes a Realm's
   record (agent_control_tests); Interp's inventory has two fields fewer (149). New:
   tests::instanceof_walks_ask_a_proxy_on_the_chain_through_its_trap (three tiers: a proxy as the
   left operand, mid-chain and revoked, a warmed site with a proxy alive elsewhere) and the tag
   test above.

Per operation (instructions an iteration: the difference of 1.2M and 200k iterations over 1M,
least of 3; target/p2/w2g/proxy-micro; main c90b3889, the tip cedee510, fat LTO):

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a Proxy's creation | 2,297.5 | 1,128.3 | -1,169.2 (-50.9%) |
| a read through a Proxy without handlers | 1,256.0 | 1,193.0 | -5.0% |
| a 4-deep prototype miss, a Proxy alive | 2,346.0 | 2,181.0 | -7.0% |
| the same miss, no Proxy | 2,169.0 | 2,168.0 | -0.0% |
| `instanceof` over a 2-deep chain, a Proxy alive | 1,709.0 | 298.0 | -82.6% |
| the same, no Proxy | 300.0 | 298.0 | -0.7% |
| `typeof` of an object and a test | 430.0 | 435.3 | +1.2% |

W1's Proxy micro-benchmarks (whole runs, median of 3): proxy-none 4,862,219,895 ->
4,471,539,196 (-8.04%), proxy-reflect 5,095,057,289 -> 4,916,261,846 (-3.51%), proxy-direct
8,105,448,483 -> 7,852,072,213 (-3.13%). A creation saves 1,169 (the design's estimate: about
950). `typeof` of an object pays is_callable's compare with the Proxy tag, an Array's and a
wrapper's (SPE: typeof_matches +2.2 an iteration; the rest is LTO's layout of that function).

Vue -1.57% and chai -1.02% (the design's estimates: 1.28% and 0.79%). EarleyBoyer's +0.52% is
its collection schedule's, not its code's: with collections suppressed it is -0.21% (the
`instanceof`s). In every workload checked, the first collection keeps one object more or fewer
than main's (EarleyBoyer 5,328 of 10,769 against 5,329; RegExp, Box2D and Gameboy one more),
the same in a build with main's Interp layout and descriptor order (below) and not moved by the
environment's size: a conservative stack word that the changed Rust frames leave or no longer
leave. EarleyBoyer's schedule then diverges (its second major collection one minor earlier), and
SPE put 9a76763f's +21M there in marking (PropWord::trace_gc_edges, Object::trace_gc_fields,
mark_unmarked, mark_phase). What stays with collections suppressed is a few hundredths: React
+0.03%, RegExp +0.04%, PdfJS +0.02%, Richards and CodeLoad +0.01%, below what SPE resolves
(RegExp's hot functions moved under LTO: regexp_exec_abstract 1,236 -> 1,332 bytes, call_dispatch
6,280 -> 5,980); is_callable's compare is one known part.

Collections: as main's in number where no proxy is made (EarleyBoyer 75, RegExp 49, Box2D 19,
Gameboy 29; TRUST_JS_GC_LOG). chai makes five minor collections more (1,019 against 1,014) for its
371,886 proxies' payload cells, and each does less: its log puts the side slots' work
(`side`) at 59 ms against 323 and the tables' (`tables`, the eviction) at 217 against 474
(times from one run each, as an indication only).

Code dumps (the gate's three modes over the fixtures, tools/dumpsum.py) differ from main's in
Interp's field offsets (the two fields' removal moves later fields by 32 bytes) and in the
object descriptor's id (the payload descriptor registered before it moves the in-line
allocation's cursor by 768 bytes). A comparison build of cedee510 that re-declares the two fields
and registers the proxy descriptor last (target/p2/w2g/proxy-layout/patch.py, for measurement
only) dumps the fixtures as main does in all three modes: the fixtures make no in-line
`instanceof`. On EarleyBoyer that build differs from main in the 39 chunks of 83 that hold the
`instanceof` template, each by three tokens a template and in at most twelve tokens a template.

GC: the object's trace is the one edge inventory every trace uses (marking, verification, the
barrier's check). Under TRUST_JS_GC_STRESS=1000 and TRUST_JS_GC_VERIFY=1, the 210 lib tests whose
bodies make proxies (target/p2/w2g/proxy-stress/select.py), each in its own process, pass at
9a76763f (the trace's last change) and at cedee510. Test262's Proxy and Reflect
suites with every other test that makes a proxy (846 tests, a dev-profile test262-runner at
9a76763f) pass at 1000 with --tier=jit threshold 0; at stress 1, in that tier and in the default
one, only timeouts fail (22 and 21, slowness).

Checks at cedee510:
- lib: ARM64 2,764 passed, 9 ignored; x86-64 (zombie) 2,545 passed, 9 ignored; clippy -D warnings
  in the three configurations on both.
- The release gate: the fixtures' masked output as the base's; difftest 138 agree, 12 skipped
  (budget); Test262 at --tier=jit threshold 8, 53,575 of 53,575 and identical to the base's
  report.
- An edge-case script (target/p2/w2g/proxy-edge/edge.js: typeof, calls and constructions of
  callable, class, arrow and revoked proxies, bind, IsArray, JSON, proxies as prototypes, in,
  delete, instanceof with a proxy on either side, Function.prototype.toString, super() with
  %Proxy% and with a proxy parent, ShadowRealm and $262.createRealm proxies, species, WeakMap
  keys, collections of live and revoked proxies) prints main's output.

### The tier threshold at 1, counted on today's main (record, 2026-11-04)

For the owner's open threshold decision ("The phase exit's gap, measured"): instructions at
TRUST_JS_TIER_THRESHOLD=1 against the default 8, main 3c09e454's engine (fat LTO, CPUs 10-13,
pacing off, median of 3; target/p2/djot-compile/counts-t1.txt): Vue +0.28%, React +5.57%, Djot
-0.87%, chai -0.19%, Richards +5.95%, DeltaBlue -3.77%, Crypto -0.71%, RayTrace +2.26%,
EarleyBoyer +2.40%, RegExp -1.01%, Splay -0.00%, NavierStokes +0.14%, PdfJS -0.55%, Mandreel
+0.21%, Gameboy -1.07%, CodeLoad +10.12%, Box2D -2.07%, zlib -0.03%, TypeScript -1.12%.
Instructions do not price the tree-walker's time per instruction, which is where threshold 1
saves, so the decision needs the timing (at W3's exit threshold 1 took Djot from 105 to 96 ms,
-8.6%); it waits for a quiet machine (the other load was 3.7 cores, over the 2 the timing rule
allows).
